| fun | `ivl_times_core` | 45 | fun ivl_times_core :: "ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl" where<br>    "ivl_times_core (Ivl (Fin l1) (Fin u1)) (Ivl (Fin l2) (Fin u2)) =<br>       Ivl (Fin (min (l1*l2) (min (l1*u2) (min (u1*l2) (u1*u2)))))<br>           (Fin (max (l1*l2) (max (l1*u2) (max (u1*l2) (u1*u2)))))"<br>  \| "ivl_times_core _ _ = ivl_top"<br><br>instantiation ivl :: times begin | Precise multiplication on non-empty intervals. For all-finite operands the product range is the min / max over the four corner products (the box product attains its extrema at the corners); any infinite bound falls back to `ivl_top`. An empty operand yields `bot` -- the proper bottom handling that keeps the operator monotone (mapping empties to `ivl_top` would destroy the ordering). |
| definition | `times_ivl` | 52 | definition times_ivl :: "ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl" where<br>  "times_ivl a b = (if ivl_nonempty a ∧ ivl_nonempty b then ivl_times_core a b else bot)"<br>instance ..<br>end |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Interval/Interval_Backward.thy

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `aval_ivl` | 9 | fun aval_ivl :: "aexp =&gt; (vname =&gt; ivl) =&gt; ivl" where<br>    "aval_ivl (N n)        σ = Ivl (Fin n) (Fin n)"<br>  \| "aval_ivl (V x)        σ = σ x"<br>  \| "aval_ivl (Plus  a b)  σ = aval_ivl a σ + aval_ivl b σ"<br>  \| "aval_ivl (Minus a b)  σ = aval_ivl a σ - aval_ivl b σ"<br>  \| "aval_ivl (Times a b)  σ = aval_ivl a σ * aval_ivl b σ" |  |
| fun | `inv_less_ivl` | 36 | fun inv_less_ivl :: "bool =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl * ivl" where<br>    "inv_less_ivl True  (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>       (Ivl l1 u1 ⊓ Ivl MinInf (u2 - Fin 1),<br>        Ivl l2 u2 ⊓ Ivl (l1 + Fin 1) PlusInf)"<br>  \| "inv_less_ivl False (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>       (Ivl l1 u1 ⊓ Ivl l2 PlusInf,<br>        Ivl l2 u2 ⊓ Ivl MinInf u1)" | Inverse operators for backward analysis over intervals. *inv_less_ivl* performs precise interval narrowing: on the true branch of *n1 < n2*, the upper bound of *n1* tightens to one below the upper bound of *n2*, and the lower bound of *n2* tightens to one above the lower bound of *n1*. On the false branch (*n1 ≥ n2*), *n1*`s` lower bound tightens to the lower bound of *n2*, and *n2*`s` upper bound tightens to the upper bound of *n1*. Plus/minus/times instantiate the shared `inv_conservative` (identity) instead of a per-domain no-op. |
| fun | `inv_eq_ivl` | 111 | fun inv_eq_ivl :: "bool =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl * ivl" where<br>    "inv_eq_ivl True  a1 a2 = (meet_ivl a1 a2, meet_ivl a1 a2)"<br>  \| "inv_eq_ivl False a1 a2 = (a1, a2)" | *inv_eq_ivl* narrows on a guard *`e1 = e2`* known true or false. The true branch narrows both operands to their intersection, the same argument as the sign instance via `meet_ivl_gamma`. The false branch is the sound identity: a precise refinement is possible in specific cases (e.g. excluding a known point value from one bound of the other operand when that point sits exactly at that bound), but `ivl`'s infinite domain makes proving that refinement's monotonicity disproportionately more expensive than for the finite sign lattice --- attempted and abandoned; the guard conditions needed access the interval's own bound values, and a boundary-matching guard is not compatible with the order-based case-split technique that closed the sign proof. This is a documented precision gap, not a soundness one: `bfilter`'s *`Eq _ _ False`* case under this instance narrows exactly as much for Interval as it already does today (not at all), while Sign gains real precision from its own instance. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Interval/Interval_Checks.thy

> Only composition lives here, mirroring `Sign_Checks`: the Interval bound tables (`interval_less_true`/`interval_less_false`/`interval_eq_true`/ `interval_eq_false`) and their `Interval_Numeric_Queries` interpretation of `abstract_numeric_queries` live in that theory. The Interval expression evaluator `aval_ivl` lives in `Interval_Backward`. The Boolean recursion over `bexp` (`Not`, `And`, `Or`), the three-way classification, and the node-indexed bridge to `checks_proven` come from interpreting `abstract_check_domain` once, below, reusing the numeric-query facts already proved sound in `interval_numeric_queries` rather than re-deriving the comparison tables --- the same way `ivl_backward_domain` in `Interval_Backward.thy` interprets `backward_domain` for guard narrowing.

| definition | `test_env_bounded` | 63 | definition test_env_bounded :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "test_env_bounded = (λ_. ivl_top)((STR ''x'') := Ivl (Fin 4) (Fin 7))" | One state per test, built as an override of an otherwise-unconstrained (`ivl_top`) environment, so each test exercises exactly the comparison it names, and one showing the precision gain over Sign: a bounded range proves both a wider upper bound and a tighter lower bound in one classification, which Sign's four-value lattice cannot distinguish from `STop`. |
| definition | `test_env_precision` | 83 | definition test_env_precision :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "test_env_precision = (λ_. ivl_top)((STR ''x'') := Ivl (Fin 4) (Fin 7))" | The precision gain over Sign: `0 < x` and `x < 8` both hold outright once `x` is known to lie strictly between `3` and `8` --- a fact only a domain that tracks numeric bounds can prove; Sign's `SPos`/`SNonNeg` would classify `x < 8` `Check_Unknown` on the same information. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Interval/Interval_DG.thy

> Interval uses the same carrier for local answers and global side effects. The diagonal specification `unit_dg_spec_for` therefore provides its D/G operations, while `sound_dg_spec` supplies collecting soundness.

The sublocale interpretation rewrites facts inherited from `sound_dg_spec` into this native vocabulary, so the exported theorem below cites the generic collecting-soundness fact directly with no manual unfold/fold bridging. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Interval/Interval_Exec_Sound.thy

> High-level vocabulary for the executable analyzer, mirroring `Sign_Exec_Sound` at the interval domain: • `ivl_exec_raw Π ps main` -- the raw solver solution (`pp + unit => ivl resolved_st_q`), code-generating, the thing @{command value} / `eval` evaluates; • `ivl_exec gs Π ps main` -- the analyzer's computed abstract state at the program exit (an `ivl abs_state`), read back through `fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs`; • `ivl_exec_terminates Π ps main` -- the single assumption: the vendored solver terminates on this program. `ivl_exec_sound_collecting` / `ivl_exec_sound_collecting_at` are the program-parametric soundness theorems; concrete examples only fix a program and instantiate them.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `ivl_exec_eqs` | 26 | definition ivl_exec_eqs ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp, unit, ivl resolved_st_q) eqsT" where<br>  "ivl_exec_eqs gs Π ps mnm main =<br>     side_cfg_T_eff_st (compile_prog Π ps mnm main) (ivl_etf_st_for gs) bot cinit_ivl_st ()" | High-level vocabulary for the executable analyzer, mirroring `Sign_Exec_Sound` at the interval domain: • `ivl_exec_raw Π ps main` -- the raw solver solution (`pp + unit => ivl resolved_st_q`), code-generating, the thing @{command value} / `eval` evaluates; • `ivl_exec gs Π ps main` -- the analyzer's computed abstract state at the program exit (an `ivl abs_state`), read back through `fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs`; • `ivl_exec_terminates Π ps main` -- the single assumption: the vendored solver terminates on this program. `ivl_exec_sound_collecting` / `ivl_exec_sound_collecting_at` are the program-parametric soundness theorems; concrete examples only fix a program and instantiate them. |
| definition | `ivl_exec_raw` | 31 | definition ivl_exec_raw ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp + unit ⇒ ivl resolved_st_q)" where<br>  "ivl_exec_raw gs Π ps mnm main =<br>     snd (TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve (ivl_exec_eqs gs Π ps mnm main)<br>            (cfg_exit (compile_prog Π ps mnm main)))" |  |
| definition | `ivl_exec_at` | 37 | definition ivl_exec_at ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ pp ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "ivl_exec_at gs Π ps mnm main v =<br>     side_env (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs ∘ ivl_exec_raw gs Π ps mnm main) v" |  |
| definition | `ivl_exec` | 42 | definition ivl_exec ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "ivl_exec gs Π ps mnm main = ivl_exec_at gs Π ps mnm main (cfg_exit (compile_prog Π ps mnm main))" |  |
| definition | `ivl_exec_terminates` | 46 | definition ivl_exec_terminates ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "ivl_exec_terminates gs Π ps mnm main =<br>     TD_side_always_join_Interp.solve_dom TYPE(unit) TYPE(ivl resolved_st_q)<br>        (ivl_exec_eqs gs Π ps mnm main) (cfg_exit (compile_prog Π ps mnm main))" |  |

The wrappers below feed those three projections to the analyzer in one step, mirroring `Sign_Exec_Sound`'s `sign_exec_prog_at` family. `prog_cfg` (`Voblint_CFG.Compile_Invariants`) is the shared, domain-independent instance of this same projection for the CFG itself, so it is imported rather than redefined here --- Sign uses the exact same constant, not an equivalent local copy. |
| definition | `ivl_exec_prog` | 174 | definition ivl_exec_prog :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "ivl_exec_prog gs mnm p = ivl_exec_prog_at gs mnm p (cfg_exit (prog_cfg mnm p))" |  |
| definition | `ivl_terminates_prog` | 177 | definition ivl_terminates_prog :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ bool" where<br>  "ivl_terminates_prog gs mnm p = ivl_exec_terminates gs (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) mnm (prog_main p)" |  |
| definition | `analyse_interval_for` | 211 | definition analyse_interval_for :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "analyse_interval_for gs p = ivl_exec_prog gs prog_main_name p" | `analyse_interval_for`/`analyse_interval` fix the main-procedure name to `prog_main_name`, matching the `imp_prog` bracket notation's convention: thin renames of `ivl_exec_prog`/`ivl_terminates_prog`, not a new proof, so callers coming from executable code generation reach the same generic collecting-soundness chain `ivl_exec_prog_sound_collecting` already gives. |
| definition | `analyse_interval` | 214 | definition analyse_interval :: "imp_prog ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "analyse_interval p = analyse_interval_for (declared_global p) p" |  |
| definition | `analyse_interval_td_raw` | 238 | definition analyse_interval_td_raw ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp + unit ⇒ ivl resolved_st_q)" where<br>  "analyse_interval_td_raw gs Pi ps mnm main =<br>     snd (TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve<br>            (ivl_exec_eqs gs Pi ps mnm main) (cfg_exit (compile_prog Pi ps mnm main)))" | `analyse_interval_td`/`analyse_interval_td_for` mirror `analyse_interval` but solve via `TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve` instead of the always-join rule, following `ivl_exec_raw`/`ivl_exec_at`/`ivl_exec_prog`'s own shape. A soundness theorem for this pipeline follows the same collecting chain `ivl_exec_sound_collecting_at` uses, with `TD_side_always_join_solve_Inr_rg` replaced by `Voblint_Core.Solver_Side_RG`'s `TD_side_warrowing_apinis_solve_Inr_rg`: the warrowing update rule reads back its own per-origin bookkeeping (`sup_over_origins`) rather than just joining, so that bridge lemma additionally needs `bot ∇ bot = bot`/`bot \<Delta> bot = bot` for the domain in play --- `ivl_widen_bot_bot`/`ivl_narrow_bot_bot` (`Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Warrowing`) discharge exactly that for `ivl`. |
| definition | `analyse_interval_td_at` | 244 | definition analyse_interval_td_at ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ pp ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "analyse_interval_td_at gs Pi ps mnm main v =<br>     side_env (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs ∘ analyse_interval_td_raw gs Pi ps mnm main) v" |  |
| definition | `analyse_interval_td_for` | 249 | definition analyse_interval_td_for :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "analyse_interval_td_for gs p =<br>     analyse_interval_td_at gs (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) prog_main_name (prog_main p)<br>       (cfg_exit (prog_cfg prog_main_name p))" |  |
| definition | `analyse_interval_td` | 254 | definition analyse_interval_td :: "imp_prog ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "analyse_interval_td p = analyse_interval_td_for (declared_global p) p" |  |
| definition | `analyse_interval_td_terminates` | 263 | definition analyse_interval_td_terminates ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "analyse_interval_td_terminates gs Pi ps mnm main =<br>     TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp.solve_dom TYPE(unit) TYPE(ivl resolved_st_q)<br>        (ivl_exec_eqs gs Pi ps mnm main) (cfg_exit (compile_prog Pi ps mnm main))" | `analyse_interval_td_terminates` mirrors `ivl_exec_terminates`, one layer down from `analyse_interval_td_raw`/`analyse_interval_td_at` exactly as `ivl_exec_terminates` sits below `ivl_exec_raw`/`ivl_exec_at`. |
| definition | `ivl_graph_config` | 405 | definition ivl_graph_config ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒<br>    (unit, unit, ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) analysis_graph_config" where<br>  "ivl_graph_config gs Π ps mnm main = compiled_domain_graph_config gs Π ps mnm main (λv. v = ivl_top)" | One-command annotated CFG rendering for the interval domain, mirroring `Sign_Exec_Sound`'s `sign_graph_config`/ `sign_annotated_dot_lit`. Typical example-file use: @{text [display] "ML_val \<open> writeln (@{code ivl_annotated_dot_prog_lit} ''main'' @{code my_prog}) \<close>"} |

[()]) @ [Inr ()];<br>           sol = ivl_graph_solution gs (ivl_exec_raw gs Π ps mnm main)<br>       in contextual_analysis_dot (ivl_graph_config gs Π ps mnm main) g domain sol)" |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Interval/Interval_Lattice.thy

> An interval *[l, u]* abstracts the integers *{n. l ≤ n ∧ n ≤ u*"}. Bounds are extended integers (*MinInf* / *Fin n* / *PlusInf*). Empty (e.g. *[+inf, -inf]*) is *bot*; *[-inf, +inf]* is *top*.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `ivl` | 15 | datatype ivl = Ivl (ivl_lower: eint) (ivl_upper: eint)   \&lt;comment&gt; \&lt;open&gt;@{text "Ivl l u = [l, u]"}\&lt;close&gt; |  |
| definition | `less_eq_ivl` | 23 | definition less_eq_ivl :: "ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; bool" where<br>  "(a::ivl) &lt;= b = (case (a, b) of (Ivl l1 u1, Ivl l2 u2) =&gt; l2 ≤ l1 ∧ u1 ≤ u2)" |  |
| definition | `less_ivl` | 25 | definition less_ivl :: "ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; bool" where<br>  "(a::ivl) &lt; b = (a &lt;= b ∧ ¬ b &lt;= a)"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instantiation ivl :: bot begin |  |
| definition | `bot_ivl` | 31 | definition bot_ivl :: ivl where<br>  "bot_ivl = Ivl PlusInf MinInf"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instantiation ivl :: order begin<br>instance proof intro_classes<br>  fix x y z :: ivl<br>  show "(x &lt; y) = (x ≤ y ∧ ¬ y ≤ x)"<br>    unfolding less_ivl_def by simp<br>  show "x ≤ x"<br>    unfolding less_eq_ivl_def by (cases x) (simp add: eint_le_refl)<br>  show "x ≤ y ⟹ y ≤ z ⟹ x ≤ z"<br>    unfolding less_eq_ivl_def by (cases x; cases y; cases z) (auto intro: eint_le_trans)<br>  show "x ≤ y ⟹ y ≤ x ⟹ x = y"<br>... (11 more lines) |  |
| definition | `ivl_top` | 58 | definition ivl_top :: ivl where<br>  "ivl_top = Ivl MinInf PlusInf"<br><br>instantiation ivl :: top begin |  |
| definition | `top_ivl` | 62 | definition "top_ivl = ivl_top"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instantiation ivl :: order_top begin<br>instance proof intro_classes<br>  fix x :: ivl<br>  show "x ≤ top"<br>    unfolding top_ivl_def by (cases x) (simp add: less_eq_ivl_def ivl_top_def)<br>qed<br>end |  |
| fun | `gamma_ivl` | 76 | fun gamma_ivl :: "ivl =&gt; int set" where<br>    "gamma_ivl (Ivl l u) = {n. l ≤ Fin n ∧ Fin n ≤ u}" |  |
| definition | `is_top_ivl` | 92 | definition is_top_ivl :: "ivl ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_top_ivl i = (i = ivl_top)" | Unlike `bot`, `top` has no other representation: \<^term>\<open>gamma_ivl (Ivl l u) = UNIV\<close> forces `l ≤ Fin n` and `Fin n ≤ u` for every `n`, which pins `l = MinInf` and `u = PlusInf` exactly --- so a direct equality test against `ivl_top` is already exact, no bound comparison needed. |
| definition | `is_bottom_ivl` | 117 | definition is_bottom_ivl :: "ivl ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_bottom_ivl i =<br>     (case i of Ivl l u ⇒ l = PlusInf ∨ u = MinInf ∨ ¬ l ≤ u)" | `bot` is one fixed empty interval (`Ivl PlusInf MinInf`), but the `≤` instance's bound comparison makes `x ≤ bot` true only for that exact representation: any other inverted bound pair (e.g. a guard refinement narrowing a variable against its own infeasible range, `Ivl (Fin 5) (Fin (-1))`) is just as empty by `gamma_ivl`, yet not `≤ bot` --- the instance never normalizes. `is_bottom_ivl` tests emptiness directly against the bounds instead of against one particular representative. Bound comparison alone is not quite enough, though: \<^term>\<open>Ivl MinInf MinInf\<close> has `l ≤ u` (reflexively), yet no integer is "at" `MinInf` itself, so its `gamma_ivl` is still empty (symmetrically for `Ivl PlusInf PlusInf`). `l = PlusInf` and `u = MinInf` are exactly the two cases where `eint_le` can hold reflexively without any integer ever satisfying it, so both are called out explicitly alongside the general inverted-bounds case. |
| fun | `join_ivl` | 143 | fun join_ivl :: "ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl" where<br>    "join_ivl (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>       Ivl (if l1 ≤ l2 then l1 else l2)<br>           (if u2 ≤ u1 then u1 else u2)" |  |
| definition | `sup_ivl` | 179 | definition sup_ivl :: "ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl" where<br>  "sup_ivl = join_ivl"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instance ivl :: semilattice_sup<br>proof intro_classes<br>  fix x y z :: ivl<br>  show "x ≤ x ⊔ y" unfolding sup_ivl_def by (rule join_ivl_le_ub1)<br>  show "y ≤ x ⊔ y" unfolding sup_ivl_def by (rule join_ivl_le_ub2)<br>  show "y ≤ x ⟹ z ≤ x ⟹ y ⊔ z ≤ x"<br>    unfolding sup_ivl_def by (rule join_ivl_le_least)<br>qed<br><br>instance ivl :: bounded_semilattice_sup_bot .. |  |
| fun | `meet_ivl` | 201 | fun meet_ivl :: "ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl" where<br>    "meet_ivl (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>       Ivl (if l2 ≤ l1 then l1 else l2)<br>           (if u1 ≤ u2 then u1 else u2)"<br><br>instantiation ivl :: inf begin | Interval intersection. Used by *assume_ivl* to refine a variable's interval on a guard (*x < n* narrows the upper bound). |
| definition | `inf_ivl` | 207 | definition inf_ivl :: "ivl ⇒ ivl ⇒ ivl" where "inf_ivl = meet_ivl"<br>instance ..<br>end<br>declare inf_ivl_def [simp] |  |
| definition | `gamma_abs_ivl` | 261 | definition gamma_abs_ivl [simp]: "gamma (a :: ivl) = gamma_ivl a" |  |
| definition | `is_bot_ivl` | 262 | definition is_bot_ivl [simp]: "is_bot (a :: ivl) = is_bottom_ivl a" |  |
| definition | `is_top_ivl'` | 263 | definition is_top_ivl' [simp]: "is_top (a :: ivl) = is_top_ivl a"<br>instance proof intro_classes<br>  show "gamma (bot :: ivl) = {}"<br>    by (simp add: gamma_ivl_bot)<br>next<br>  fix a b :: ivl<br>  assume H: "a ≤ b"<br>  show "gamma a ⊆ gamma b"<br>  proof -<br>    have "gamma_ivl a ⊆ gamma_ivl b" using H by (rule gamma_ivl_mono)<br>    then show ?thesis by simp<br>  qed<br>next<br>  fix a :: ivl<br>  show "is_bot a ↔ gamma a = {}"<br>... (7 more lines) |  |
| definition | `normalize_ivl` | 300 | definition normalize_ivl :: "ivl ⇒ ivl" where<br>  "normalize_ivl v =<br>     (case v of Ivl l u ⇒<br>        if l ≤ u ∧ l ≠ PlusInf ∧ u ≠ MinInf then v else bot)" | **Canonical empty interval.** The raw `ivl` lattice has infinitely many empty representations (any `Ivl l u` with `l > u`, or an infinite bound on the wrong side). `normalize_ivl` collapses every empty interval to the single `bot` representative `Ivl PlusInf MinInf`, leaving non-empty intervals untouched. Arithmetic feeds its operands through `normalize_ivl` first. Since the pointwise sum/difference of `bot` with anything is again `bot`, this keeps every empty result canonical. Without it, `[1,0] + [1,1] = [2,1]` would manufacture ever-new empty representations, which as context keys defeat fixpoint convergence in the recursive interval example. |
| fun | `ivl_nonempty` | 324 | fun ivl_nonempty :: "ivl =&gt; bool" where<br>  "ivl_nonempty (Ivl l u) = (l ≤ u ∧ l ≠ PlusInf ∧ u ≠ MinInf)" | Emptiness test on intervals. The raw `ivl` lattice contains many unnormalised empty intervals (any ``Ivl l u`` with *l > u*, including `bot`). *ivl_nonempty* captures exactly the intervals with non-empty concretization (proved below: implied by any witness, and monotone along *≤*); multiplication uses it for proper bottom handling, which is what makes the precise corner product monotone. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Interval/Interval_Numeric_Queries.thy

> Second-domain validation for `abstract_numeric_queries` (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Numeric_Queries`): a real, minimal Interval instance, interpreted without touching that generic theory. Entailment/refutation of `<`/`=` reduces to comparing bounds: two intervals' `<` relation is provable exactly when one interval's upper bound is strictly below the other's lower bound; `=` exactly when both are the same singleton point; `≠` exactly when the intervals are disjoint. Empty intervals (`l > u`) make every judgment vacuously true, the same role `SBot` plays for Sign.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `interval_less_true` | 59 | fun interval_less_true :: "ivl ⇒ ivl ⇒ bool" where<br>  "interval_less_true (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>     (¬ l1 ≤ u1 ∨ ¬ l2 ≤ u2 ∨ u1 &lt; l2)" |  |
| fun | `interval_less_false` | 98 | fun interval_less_false :: "ivl ⇒ ivl ⇒ bool" where<br>  "interval_less_false (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>     (¬ l1 ≤ u1 ∨ ¬ l2 ≤ u2 ∨ u2 ≤ l1)" |  |
| fun | `interval_eq_true` | 151 | fun interval_eq_true :: "ivl ⇒ ivl ⇒ bool" where<br>  "interval_eq_true (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>     (¬ l1 ≤ u1 ∨ ¬ l2 ≤ u2 ∨ (l1 = u1 ∧ l2 = u2 ∧ l1 = l2))" |  |
| fun | `interval_eq_false` | 197 | fun interval_eq_false :: "ivl ⇒ ivl ⇒ bool" where<br>  "interval_eq_false (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>     (¬ l1 ≤ u1 ∨ ¬ l2 ≤ u2 ∨ u1 &lt; l2 ∨ u2 &lt; l1)" |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Interval/Interval_Point_Digest.thy

> The point abstraction maps an integer to its singleton interval, and a slot is a *point* when it is such a singleton. The exactness obligation holds because `gamma_ivl (Ivl (Fin v) (Fin v)) = {v}`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `ivl_decode` | 13 | definition ivl_decode :: "Int.int ⇒ ivl" where<br>  "ivl_decode v = Ivl (Fin v) (Fin v)" | The point abstraction maps an integer to its singleton interval, and a slot is a *point* when it is such a singleton. The exactness obligation holds because `gamma_ivl (Ivl (Fin v) (Fin v)) = {v}`. |
| definition | `ivl_is_point` | 16 | definition ivl_is_point :: "ivl ⇒ bool" where<br>  "ivl_is_point a = (∃v. a = Ivl (Fin v) (Fin v))" |  |

Each edge tree queries the local program point and the unit global slot, applies the interval transfer, then splits the result into local and global parts. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Interval/Interval_Transfer.thy

> Both guards delegate to the generic *bfilter* proved sound in `backward_domain`. *assume_ivl b σ* filters for *bval b*, *assume_not_ivl b σ* for *¬ bval b*.

| definition | `assume_ivl` | 15 | definition assume_ivl :: "bexp =&gt; (vname =&gt; ivl) =&gt; (vname =&gt; ivl)" where<br>  "assume_ivl b σ = bfilter_ivl b True σ" | Both guards delegate to the generic *bfilter* proved sound in `backward_domain`. *assume_ivl b σ* filters for *bval b*, *assume_not_ivl b σ* for *¬ bval b*. |
| definition | `assume_not_ivl` | 23 | definition assume_not_ivl :: "bexp =&gt; (vname =&gt; ivl) =&gt; (vname =&gt; ivl)" where<br>  "assume_not_ivl b σ = bfilter_ivl b False σ" |  |
| definition | `assign_ivl` | 31 | definition assign_ivl ::<br>    "vname =&gt; aexp =&gt; (vname =&gt; ivl) =&gt; (vname =&gt; ivl)"<br>where<br>  "assign_ivl x a σ = σ(x := aval_ivl a σ)" |  |
| definition | `random_ivl` | 44 | definition random_ivl ::<br>    "vname =&gt; (vname =&gt; ivl) =&gt; (vname =&gt; ivl)"<br>where<br>  "random_ivl x σ = σ(x := ivl_top)" |  |
| definition | `enter_frame_ivl_for` | 68 | definition enter_frame_ivl_for ::<br>    "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; ivl abs_state =&gt; ivl abs_state" where<br>  "enter_frame_ivl_for gs = enter_frame_D gs ivl_top" | Procedure entry: keep globals, reset locals to the full interval, then bind the formals to the abstract values of the actuals evaluated in the caller. Generic via enter_frame_D/enter_D (Constraint_System.thy), parameterised by ivl_top as the domain's fully-imprecise reset value. Entry and combine are the only fields that consult a classifier, so the bundled transfer function is parametric in the classifier throughout. |
| definition | `enter_ivl_for` | 72 | definition enter_ivl_for ::<br>    "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; vname list =&gt; aexp list =&gt;<br>      ivl abs_state =&gt; ivl abs_state" where<br>  "enter_ivl_for gs = enter_D gs ivl_top aval_ivl" |  |
| definition | `ivl_tf_for` | 100 | definition ivl_tf_for :: "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; ivl domain_transfer" where<br>  "ivl_tf_for gs = (\| tf_assign     = assign_ivl,<br>                       tf_random     = random_ivl,<br>                       tf_assume     = assume_ivl,<br>                       tf_assume_not = assume_not_ivl,<br>                       tf_enter      = enter_ivl_for gs,<br>                       tf_combine    = combine_env^# gs \|)" |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Interval/Interval_Warrowing.thy

> Standard interval widening: keep a bound if it did not move outward, otherwise push it to the infinite end. Every widen-ascending chain therefore stabilises after at most two steps (each bound takes at most one outward jump).

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `widen_ivl_core` | 15 | fun widen_ivl_core :: "ivl =&gt; ivl =&gt; ivl" where<br>    "widen_ivl_core (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>       Ivl (if l1 ≤ l2 then l1 else MinInf)<br>           (if u2 ≤ u1 then u1 else PlusInf)" | Standard interval widening: keep a bound if it did not move outward, otherwise push it to the infinite end. Every widen-ascending chain therefore stabilises after at most two steps (each bound takes at most one outward jump). |
| fun | `narrow_ivl_td` | 168 | fun narrow_ivl_td :: "ivl ⇒ ivl ⇒ ivl" where<br>  "narrow_ivl_td (Ivl l1 u1) (Ivl l2 u2) =<br>     Ivl (if l1 = MinInf then l2 else l1) (if u1 = PlusInf then u2 else u1)"<br><br>instantiation ivl :: warrowing begin | Standard interval narrowing: when the recomputed value `b` refines the widened value `a` (`b ≤ a`), keep `a`'s finite bounds and only fill an infinite bound of `a` from `b`. A bound moves off `±inf` at most once, so the narrowing descent is finite. Combined with the backward guard filters this recovers loop bounds under *every* update rule (a bounded local counter reads `[0, 20]` under `join`, `per_origin` and `warrow` alike). It does not help a *flow-insensitive global*: there the guard never bounds the value written back to the slot, so `[0, +inf]` is a genuine fixpoint and narrowing has nothing smaller to descend to. |
| definition | `widen` | 177 | definition "widen (a :: ivl) b =<br>     (if a = bot then b else if b = bot then a else widen_ivl_core a b)" | Widening carries the standard bot-law `bot ∇ x = x`, `x ∇ bot = x`: since `bot :: ivl` is the empty interval `Ivl PlusInf MinInf`, an unguarded `widen_ivl_core` from bot would jump straight to the top interval, topping any unknown on its first stabilisation. The guard keeps the first contribution exact. |
| definition | `narrow` | 179 | definition "narrow (a :: ivl) b = narrow_ivl_td a b"<br>instance proof intro_classes<br>  fix a b :: ivl<br>  show "a ≤ widen a b"<br>  proof (cases "a = bot")<br>    case True thus ?thesis by (simp add: widen_ivl_def)<br>  next<br>    case False thus ?thesis<br>      by (cases "b = bot") (simp_all add: widen_ivl_def a_le_widen_ivl_core bot.extremum)<br>  qed<br>  show "b ≤ widen a b"<br>  proof (cases "a = bot")<br>    case True thus ?thesis by (simp add: widen_ivl_def)<br>  next<br>    case False thus ?thesis<br>... (10 more lines) |  |

Commutation lemmas hook into the generic @{theory Voblint_Core.Exec_Bridge} transport; the certified end-to-end soundness theory built on this mirror lives in `Interval_Exec_Sound`, mirroring `Sign_Exec_Sound`.

| definition | `assume_not_ivl_st_for` | 34 | definition assume_not_ivl_st_for ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; bexp =&gt; ivl resolved_st_q =&gt; ivl resolved_st_q" where<br>  "assume_not_ivl_st_for source_global b s =<br>    bfilter_ivl_st source_global b False s" |  |

(9 more lines) |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Mixed/Exec_DG_Bridge.thy

> The verified solver uses the executable association-list carrier `'a exec_dg_st`, while soundness is stated over function-valued abstract states. `fun_of_dg_st` lifts the refinement morphism `fun_of_exec_dg_st` to the D/G product and commutes with equation evaluation. D/G lattice operations are componentwise, so the product inherits the order, join, bottom, equality, and widening operations required by the solver.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `exec_dg_st` | 20 | type_synonym 'a exec_dg_st = "'a resolved_st_q" |  |
| definition | `dg_refines_on` | 36 | definition dg_refines_on ::<br>  "location set =&gt;<br>   (('a::bot) exec_dg_st, ('b::bot) exec_dg_st) dg_state =&gt;<br>   ('a abs_state, 'b abs_state) dg_state =&gt; bool"<br>where<br>  "dg_refines_on universe executable abstract_state ↔<br>    (∀location ∈ universe.<br>      lookup_resolved_st_q (locals executable) location =<br>        locals abstract_state (location_vname location)) ∧<br>    (∀location ∈ universe.<br>      lookup_resolved_st_q (globs executable) location =<br>        globs abstract_state (location_vname location))" | The executable local and side components represent their abstract counterparts pointwise on a finite set of executable locations. Scope resolution is an explicit premise of transfer lemmas: this relation itself does not infer storage from a missing declaration. |
| definition | `project_abs_on` | 116 | definition project_abs_on ::<br>  "pname =&gt; (vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; (scoped_location =&gt; bool) =&gt;<br>   'a::bot abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "project_abs_on owner source_global placed state =<br>    project_component (λx. placed (owner, location_of source_global x)) state" |  |

QueryG () (λside.<br>      let result = transfer (locals local ⊔ globs side)<br>      in Side () (DG bot<br>          (project_abs_on (owner_of write_node) source_global publish_side result))<br>        (Answer (DG<br>          (project_abs_on (owner_of write_node) source_global keep_local result)<br>          bot))))" | The abstract tree mirrors the executable placed tree. It projects function states pointwise, while the executable tree materializes the same policy over a finite scope. |
| definition | `placed_abs_dg_enter_tree` | 170 | definition placed_abs_dg_enter_tree ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; (pp =&gt; pname) =&gt;<br>   (scoped_location =&gt; bool) =&gt; (scoped_location =&gt; bool) =&gt;<br>   (vname list =&gt; aexp list =&gt;<br>     ('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state) =&gt;<br>   vname list =&gt; aexp list =&gt; pp =&gt; pp =&gt;<br>   (pp, unit, ('a abs_state, 'a abs_state) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "placed_abs_dg_enter_tree source_global owner_of keep_local publish_side<br>      enter parameters arguments caller callee =<br>    placed_abs_dg_edge_tree source_global owner_of keep_local publish_side<br>      (enter parameters arguments) caller callee" |  |

QueryL callee (λcallee_state.<br>      QueryG () (λside.<br>        let result = combine destination (locals caller_state)<br>          (locals callee_state) (globs side)<br>        in Side () (DG bot<br>... | definition | `fun_of_exec_dg_st_for` | 355 | definition fun_of_exec_dg_st_for ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; ('a::bot) exec_dg_st =&gt; 'a abs_state" where<br>  "fun_of_exec_dg_st_for gs = fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs" | The executable local/side readback, generic in the classifier: a placed executable state is written with a declaration-driven classifier, so reading it back needs the same classifier or the readback consults the wrong slot. |
| definition | `fun_of_dg_st_for` | 368 | definition fun_of_dg_st_for ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt;<br>   (('a::bot) exec_dg_st, ('b::bot) exec_dg_st) dg_state =&gt; ('a abs_state, 'b abs_state) dg_state"<br>where<br>  "fun_of_dg_st_for gs d =<br>    DG (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for gs (locals d)) (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for gs (globs d))" |  |
| definition | `complete_abs_on` | 421 | definition complete_abs_on ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; location set =&gt; (vname =&gt; 'a) =&gt;<br>    ('a::bot) exec_dg_st =&gt; 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "complete_abs_on gs universe outside s x =<br>    (if location_of gs x ∈ universe then fun_of_exec_dg_st_for gs s x<br>     else outside x)" | The two state representations disagree on what an unrepresented location means. The executable carrier is sparse: a location a placement never materializes reads back as `bot`, "no value is recorded here". The abstract carrier is total: `project_abs_on` classifies every location as local or side but never restricts *which* locations exist, so an abstract value at a location outside a node's own scope is still whatever the transfer function produced there, not `bot` -- collapsing it to `bot` would assert "no concrete state reaches here", which is false for a location the node simply never mentions. `complete_abs_on` is the correct completion: read through the scope, and complete with a caller-supplied `outside` value everywhere else. For a domain where every value is bounded by a single greatest element, `outside` can be that top element, and the completed bound is trivially large enough outside the scope; the lemma is stated against an arbitrary `outside` so it does not depend on such an element existing. |
| definition | `completed_sigma_abs` | 495 | definition completed_sigma_abs ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (pp ⇒ location list) ⇒ 'a ⇒<br>   (pp × 'c + unit ⇒ ('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot exec_dg_st, 'a exec_dg_st) dg_state) ⇒<br>   pp × 'c + unit ⇒ ('a abs_state, 'a abs_state) dg_state"<br>where<br>  "completed_sigma_abs gs locations_of outside exec_sigma key =<br>    (case key of<br>       Inl (v, ctx) ⇒ DG<br>         (complete_abs_on gs (set (locations_of v)) (λ_. outside)<br>           (locals (exec_sigma (Inl (v, ctx)))))<br>         (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for gs (globs (exec_sigma (Inl (v, ctx)))))<br>     \| Inr () ⇒ fun_of_dg_st_for gs (exec_sigma (Inr ())))" | `completed_sigma_abs` builds the abstract witness fed to a hook-generated equation system directly from the executable solution: the executable readback at every node's own scope, completed to `outside` beyond it, and the executable readback straight through elsewhere (the shared `G` side channel, which needs no completion since `complete_abs_on`'s scope argument is per-node, not per-channel). An analysis instance's own completed sigma (its `_sigma_abs`) is this constructor applied to its own executable TD solution, classifier, node-scope function, and completion value -- not a fresh case split over `Inl`/`Inr`. |
| definition | `unit_step_st` | 581 | definition unit_step_st ::<br>  "(('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st) ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st × 'a exec_dg_st"<br>where<br>  "unit_step_st f d g = (let res = f (combine_resolved_st_q d g) in (restrict_global_resolved_q res, restrict_local_resolved_q res))" | Executable diagonal step and combine operations act on `'a exec_dg_st`. Their proofs are domain-independent: any executable transfer that commutes through `fun_of_exec_dg_st` yields a commuting D/G step. |
| definition | `unit_combine_step_st_env` | 588 | definition unit_combine_step_st_env ::<br>  "('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st × 'a exec_dg_st"<br>where<br>  "unit_combine_step_st_env dc de g =<br>     (let m = combine_resolved_st_q dc g<br>      in (restrict_global_resolved_q m, restrict_local_resolved_q m))" | Executable mirror of the abstract-side `unit_combine_step_env_for`/ `unit_combine_step_assign_for` split. |
| definition | `unit_combine_step_st_assign_for` | 609 | definition unit_combine_step_st_assign_for ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ vname option ⇒ ('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st<br>   ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st × 'a exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st × 'a exec_dg_st"<br>where<br>  "unit_combine_step_st_assign_for gs dst de g merged =<br>     (let res = combine_assign_resolved_q gs dst<br>                  (lookup_resolved_st_q de (location_of gs ret_var))<br>                  (fst merged ⊔ snd merged)<br>      in (restrict_global_resolved_q res, restrict_local_resolved_q res))" | Generic combine-assign: the destination write goes through `combine_assign_resolved_q` at whatever classifier `gs` the caller's writes and reads already agree on. |
| definition | `unit_dg_spec_st_for` | 623 | definition unit_dg_spec_st_for ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool)<br>   ⇒ (edge_action ⇒ ('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st)<br>   ⇒ (vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a exec_dg_st)<br>   ⇒ ('a exec_dg_st, 'a exec_dg_st) dg_spec"<br>where<br>  "unit_dg_spec_st_for gs tf_st enter_st = ⦇<br>    dgs_nop        = unit_step_st (tf_st EA_Nop),<br>    dgs_assign     = (λx e. unit_step_st (tf_st (EA_Assign x e))),<br>    dgs_random     = (λx. unit_step_st (tf_st (EA_Random x))),<br>    dgs_assume     = (λb. unit_step_st (tf_st (EA_Assume b))),<br>    dgs_assume_not = (λb. unit_step_st (tf_st (EA_AssumeNot b))),<br>    dgs_enter      = (λxs es. unit_step_st (enter_st xs es)),<br>    dgs_combine_env    = unit_combine_step_st_env,<br>    dgs_combine_assign = unit_combine_step_st_assign_for gs<br>... | definition | `support_growth_bounded` | 1464 | definition support_growth_bounded ::<br>  "(('a::bot) exec_dg_st =&gt; 'a exec_dg_st) =&gt; location set =&gt; bool"<br>where<br>  "support_growth_bounded transfer footprint ↔<br>    (∀s. set (effective_support (rep_resolved_st (transfer s))) ⊆<br>      set (effective_support (rep_resolved_st s)) ∪ footprint)" | `project_resolved_on` already bounds the projected output by the declared scope together with whatever the raw transfer output carried (`effective_support_rep_project_resolved_on`). A hook's raw transfer only grows that carried support by its own write footprint (`support_growth_bounded`); once the footprint and the read node's own scope both fall inside the write node's scope, the projected output stays inside it too. This is the piece the classifier-parametric readback needs and the finite-scope escape hatch does not give for free: the write node's own scope must actually cover what the edge reads and writes, not merely agree with the abstract side on it. |

()) (map_ltree (λnode. (node, ctx))<br>      (placed_dg_edge_tree owner_of locations_of keep_local publish_side<br>        (transfer action) read_node write_node))" |  |

| definition | `placed_dg_gen_of` | 1882 | definition placed_dg_gen_of ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; (pp =&gt; pname) =&gt; (pp =&gt; location list) =&gt;<br>   (scoped_location =&gt; bool) =&gt; (scoped_location =&gt; bool) =&gt;<br>   (edge_action =&gt; ('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) exec_dg_st =&gt; 'a exec_dg_st) =&gt;<br>   (vname list =&gt; aexp list =&gt; 'a exec_dg_st =&gt; 'a exec_dg_st) =&gt;<br>   cfg =&gt; 'a exec_dg_st =&gt; 'a exec_dg_st =&gt; 'a exec_dg_st =&gt;<br>   (pp × unit, unit, ('a exec_dg_st, 'a exec_dg_st) dg_state) eqsT"<br>where<br>  "placed_dg_gen_of source_global owner_of locations_of keep_local publish_side<br>      transfer enter graph bot0 s0d s0g =<br>    side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_trees intra_predecessor_list (λ_. ()) (map_ltree (λw. (w, ctx))<br>         (dg_edge_tree (dgs_enter S fs as) cl))) (entry_call_list g v)" |  |

())<br>       (λ_ _ _ _. ()) (dg_cmb_of S) (dg_extra_of S g) g S bot0 s0d s0g" |  |
| definition | `dg_tree_st_commute_for` | 3253 | definition dg_tree_st_commute_for ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool)<br>   ⇒ ('u + 'k ⇒ (('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) exec_dg_st, ('b::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) exec_dg_st) dg_state)<br>   ⇒ ('u, 'k, ('a exec_dg_st, 'b exec_dg_st) dg_state) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ ('u, 'k, ('a abs_state, 'b abs_state) dg_state) strategy_tree ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "dg_tree_st_commute_for gs σ_st t_st t_abs ↔<br>     fun_of_dg_st_for gs (traverse_rhs t_st σ_st) = traverse_rhs t_abs (fun_of_dg_st_for gs ∘ σ_st)<br>   ∧ (∀k. fun_of_dg_st_for gs (sides_of_rhs t_st σ_st k) = sides_of_rhs t_abs (fun_of_dg_st_for gs ∘ σ_st) k)<br>   ∧ dep_aux σ_st t_st = dep_aux (fun_of_dg_st_for gs ∘ σ_st) t_abs" | `dg_tree_st_commute σ_st t_st t_abs` is the reusable transport contract for a single strategy tree: its executable denotation, its side-effect map, and its static dependencies all agree (through `fun_of_dg_st`) with the abstract tree read against the pushed-forward valuation `fun_of_dg_st ∘ σ_st`. It bundles the three commutation obligations the equation-system transport threads through the accumulator fold. The intra per-edge trees are discharged generically below from a componentwise analysis step. The opaque `cmb`/`extra` trees --- whose `Side` targets may be computed inside a `QueryL`/`QueryG` continuation (a routed callee seed) --- are supplied by the instance as bundled hypotheses; the bridge never assumes their side targets are syntactically fixed. The context is bound as `ctx`, never `c`: an unqualified `c` is captured by the imported constant `state.c`, which silently pins these theorems to a single context and produces a proof that only looks polymorphic. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Mixed/Mixed_Sign_Interval.thy

> The answer domain D is a flow-sensitive Sign store: every CFG point has its own Sign answer. The side domain G is one flow-insensitive Interval invariant shared by every equation. The name `globs` identifies the solver's side slot; it does not restrict G to VIMP variables satisfying the classifier `gs`. The analysis chooses which facts G contains. Every edge advances the two abstractions independently. Its Sign result becomes the successor's answer, while its Interval result is published to the shared side unknown. Solver joins therefore close G under every reachable transfer.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `mixed_si_spec` | 30 | definition mixed_si_spec :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (sign abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_spec" where<br>  "mixed_si_spec gs = indep_dg_spec gs (sign_tf_for gs) (ivl_tf_for gs)" | Sign advances the local slot, Interval the global slot, independently --- exactly `indep_dg_spec` instantiated at the two domain transfers, so no per-analysis re-derivation of the step/enter/combine fields is needed. |
| definition | `mixed_si_cmb` | 42 | definition mixed_si_cmb ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒<br>   (pp ⇒ unit ⇒ sign abs_state ⇒ call_action ⇒ unit) ⇒ unit ⇒ call_action ⇒ pp ⇒ pp<br>   ⇒ (pp × unit, unit,<br>       (sign abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "mixed_si_cmb gs = dg_cmb_of (mixed_si_spec gs)" | Mixed's combine, entry-seed, and equation-generator trees are the generic D/G executable helpers (`dg_cmb_of`, `dg_extra_of`, `dg_gen_of` in *Exec_DG_Bridge*) instantiated at `mixed_si_spec`; no per-domain reimplementation is needed. |
| definition | `mixed_si_extra` | 50 | definition mixed_si_extra ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒<br>   cfg ⇒ (pp ⇒ unit ⇒ sign abs_state ⇒ call_action ⇒ unit) ⇒ unit ⇒ pp<br>   ⇒ (pp × unit, unit,<br>       (sign abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) strategy_tree list"<br>where<br>  "mixed_si_extra gs = dg_extra_of (mixed_si_spec gs)" |  |
| definition | `mixed_si_generator` | 58 | definition mixed_si_generator ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒<br>   cfg ⇒ sign abs_state ⇒ sign abs_state ⇒ ivl abs_state<br>   ⇒ (pp × unit, unit,<br>       (sign abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) eqsT"<br>where<br>  "mixed_si_generator gs = dg_gen_of (mixed_si_spec gs)" |  |

(30 more lines) | Public DG API for the Sign/Interval mixed analysis, generic in the classifier `gs`, mirroring `Sign_DG.thy`/`Interval_DG.thy`'s pattern. The sublocale interpretation rewrites facts inherited from `sound_dg_spec` into this native vocabulary in one step, replacing the per-constant bridging lemmas above with a single rewrite obligation each; the exported theorems below then cite the generic facts directly, with no manual unfold/fold. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Mixed/Rel_Order_Domain.thy

> `relc` tracks a finite set of known pairwise-ordered variables, `(x, y)` meaning `x ≤ y` at every store the value describes. No closure: two known facts `x ≤ y` and `y ≤ z` do not automatically yield `x ≤ z` in this carrier. This is deliberately the least amount of relational structure that is still relational (a pair of variables, not one) and not `abs_state` (no `vname ⇒ 'a` function type anywhere in the carrier). The purpose of this file is not a useful analysis. It demonstrates that a non-`abs_state` carrier discharges `sound_dg_spec` with zero changes to the DG framework. Every transfer below is deliberately the most imprecise sound choice (forget on assign, havoc on call) except for a precise `assume`/ `assume_not` pair, which is enough to make the carrier genuinely relational.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `relc` | 26 | datatype relc = Bot \| RelC (relc_pairs: "(vname × vname) set") |  |
| fun | `less_eq_relc` | 47 | fun less_eq_relc :: "relc ⇒ relc ⇒ bool" where<br>  "less_eq_relc Bot _ = True"<br>\| "less_eq_relc (RelC _) Bot = False"<br>\| "less_eq_relc (RelC a) (RelC b) = (b ⊆ a)" |  |
| definition | `less_relc` | 52 | definition less_relc :: "relc ⇒ relc ⇒ bool" where<br>  "less_relc a b ↔ a ≤ b ∧ ¬ b ≤ a" |  |
| fun | `sup_relc` | 55 | fun sup_relc :: "relc ⇒ relc ⇒ relc" where<br>  "sup_relc Bot b = b"<br>\| "sup_relc a Bot = a"<br>\| "sup_relc (RelC a) (RelC b) = RelC (a ∩ b)" |  |
| definition | `bot_relc` | 60 | definition bot_relc :: relc where<br>  "bot_relc = Bot"<br><br>instance<br>proof intro_classes<br>  fix x y z :: relc<br>  show "x &lt; y ↔ x ≤ y ∧ ¬ y ≤ x" by (simp add: less_relc_def)<br>  show "x ≤ x" by (cases x) simp_all<br>  show "x ≤ y ⟹ y ≤ z ⟹ x ≤ z" by (cases x; cases y; cases z) auto<br>  show "x ≤ y ⟹ y ≤ x ⟹ x = y" by (cases x; cases y) auto<br>  show "x ≤ x ⊔ y" by (cases x; cases y) auto<br>  show "y ≤ x ⊔ y" by (cases x; cases y) auto<br>  show "y ≤ x ⟹ z ≤ x ⟹ y ⊔ z ≤ x" by (cases x; cases y; cases z) auto<br>  show "bot ≤ x" by (cases x) (simp_all add: bot_relc_def)<br>qed<br>... (2 more lines) |  |
| definition | `widen_relc` | 90 | definition widen_relc :: "relc ⇒ relc ⇒ relc" where<br>  "widen_relc a b = a ⊔ b" |  |
| definition | `narrow_relc` | 93 | definition narrow_relc :: "relc ⇒ relc ⇒ relc" where<br>  "narrow_relc a b = b"<br><br>instance<br>proof intro_classes<br>  fix a b :: relc<br>  show "a ≤ a ∇ b" by (simp add: widen_relc_def)<br>  show "b ≤ a ∇ b" by (simp add: widen_relc_def)<br>  show "b ≤ a ⟹ b ≤ a \&lt;Delta&gt; b" by (simp add: narrow_relc_def)<br>  show "b ≤ a ⟹ a \&lt;Delta&gt; b ≤ a" by (simp add: narrow_relc_def)<br>qed<br><br>end |  |
| definition | `top_relc` | 118 | definition top_relc :: relc where<br>  "top_relc = RelC {}" | `top_relc` is the empty-relation-set top element: vacuously true of every pair, so its concretization is `UNIV` (`gamma_rel_top`). |
| fun | `gamma_rel` | 123 | fun gamma_rel :: "relc ⇒ store set" where<br>  "gamma_rel Bot = {}"<br>\| "gamma_rel (RelC ps) = {s. ∀(x, y) ∈ ps. s x ≤ s y}" |  |
| fun | `relc_has` | 132 | fun relc_has :: "vname ⇒ vname ⇒ relc ⇒ bool" where<br>  "relc_has x y Bot = True"<br>\| "relc_has x y (RelC ps) = ((x, y) ∈ ps)" | Executable membership reader for downstream examples. `Bot` answers `True` for every pair: its concretization is empty, so every fact holds of it vacuously, and it never needs to materialize `UNIV`. |
| fun | `string_of_pairs` | 143 | fun string_of_pairs :: "(vname × vname) list ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_pairs [] = ''''"<br>\| "string_of_pairs [(x, y)] = String.explode x @ ''&lt;='' @ String.explode y"<br>\| "string_of_pairs ((x, y) # p # ps) =<br>     String.explode x @ ''&lt;='' @ String.explode y @ '', '' @ string_of_pairs (p # ps)" | Pretty-printer, the `relc` analogue of Interval's `string_of_ivl` for GraphViz/console display. `vname × vname` is `linorder` (via `HOL-Library.Product_Lexorder`, already imported transitively by every file in this session that touches `cfg`), so `sorted_list_of_set` gives a deterministic, executable enumeration -- the same device this project already relies on for CFG edge sets. |
| definition | `string_of_relc` | 149 | definition string_of_relc :: "relc ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_relc d =<br>     (case d of<br>        Bot ⇒ ''BOT''<br>      \| RelC ps ⇒<br>          (if ps = {} then ''(no known relations)''<br>           else string_of_pairs (sorted_list_of_set ps)))" |  |
| definition | `gammaDG_rel` | 157 | definition gammaDG_rel :: "relc ⇒ relc ⇒ store set" where<br>  "gammaDG_rel d g = gamma_rel d ∩ gamma_rel g" |  |
| fun | `forget_relc` | 179 | fun forget_relc :: "vname ⇒ relc ⇒ relc" where<br>  "forget_relc x Bot = Bot"<br>\| "forget_relc x (RelC ps) = RelC {(a, b) ∈ ps. a ≠ x ∧ b ≠ x}" |  |
| definition | `assume_step` | 190 | definition assume_step :: "bexp ⇒ relc ⇒ relc" where<br>  "assume_step b d =<br>     (case d of<br>        Bot ⇒ Bot<br>      \| RelC ps ⇒<br>          (case b of<br>             Less (V x) (V y) ⇒ RelC (insert (x, y) ps)<br>           \| _ ⇒ RelC ps))" |  |
| definition | `assume_not_step` | 227 | definition assume_not_step :: "bexp ⇒ relc ⇒ relc" where<br>  "assume_not_step b d =<br>     (case d of<br>        Bot ⇒ Bot<br>      \| RelC ps ⇒<br>          (case b of<br>             Less (V x) (V y) ⇒ RelC (insert (y, x) ps)<br>           \| _ ⇒ RelC ps))" | The negated-guard counterpart: `¬(x < y)` is `y ≤ x`, the mirror image of `assume_step`'s one precise case, recorded on the false branch instead of discarded. Every other shape falls back exactly as `assume_step` does. |
| definition | `dgs_nop_rel` | 265 | definition dgs_nop_rel :: "relc ⇒ relc ⇒ relc × relc" where<br>  "dgs_nop_rel d g = (g, d)" | Every step except the two precise `assume`/`assume_not` cases above is sound by forgetting or by leaving the carrier untouched -- deliberately imprecise: no closure, no normalization, havoc-based calls. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/NamedGlobalSign/Sign_Named_Global_Eff.thy

> The effectful transfer routes contributions to two distinct global unknowns. Its assignment tree reads the abstract value of *''Gflag''* and selects a target from its sign. Reassembling the state joins every side contribution, so routing preserves soundness while keeping unrelated fixed-point slots separate.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `gname` | 19 | datatype gname = Gpos \| Gneg |  |
| definition | `route_tree` | 65 | definition route_tree ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (sign abs_state ⇒ gname) ⇒ (sign abs_state ⇒ sign abs_state) ⇒ pp<br>   ⇒ (pp, gname, sign abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "route_tree gs route f u =<br>     read_local_cont u (λsu. read_global_cont Gpos (λgp. read_global_cont Gneg (λgn.<br>       let env = su ⊔ gp ⊔ gn; res = f env<br>       in depend_on (route env) (restrict_global_for gs res) (answer (restrict_local_for gs res)))))" | route_tree queries the local unknown at u and both global slots, reassembles the source state, applies a pure abstract step f, and emits the global part to a routed slot. Because etf_full joins all Side contributions, etf_full collapses to f on the source state regardless of the routing (route_tree_etf_full). |
| definition | `route_combine` | 81 | definition route_combine ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (sign abs_state ⇒ gname) ⇒ vname option ⇒ pp ⇒ pp<br>   ⇒ (pp, gname, sign abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "route_combine gs route dst cc ex =<br>     read_local_cont cc (λsc. read_local_cont ex (λse. read_global_cont Gpos (λgp. read_global_cont Gneg (λgn.<br>       let envc = sc ⊔ gp ⊔ gn; enve = se ⊔ gp ⊔ gn;<br>           res = combine_collect_abs gs dst envc enve<br>       in depend_on (route envc) (restrict_global_for gs res) (answer (restrict_local_for gs res))))))" |  |
| definition | `named_etf` | 251 | definition named_etf :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (gname, sign) effectful_domain_transfer" where<br>  "named_etf gs =<br>     ⦇ etf_nop        = route_tree gs (λ_. Gpos) (apply_tf (sign_tf_for gs) EA_Nop),<br>       etf_assign     = λx a. route_tree gs (λ_. Gpos) (apply_tf (sign_tf_for gs) (EA_Assign x a)),<br>       etf_random     = λx. route_tree gs (λ_. Gpos) (apply_tf (sign_tf_for gs) (EA_Random x)),<br>       etf_assume     = λb. route_tree gs (λ_. Gpos) (apply_tf (sign_tf_for gs) (EA_Assume b)),<br>       etf_assume_not = λb. route_tree gs (λ_. Gpos) (apply_tf (sign_tf_for gs) (EA_AssumeNot b)),<br>       etf_enter      = (λxs es. route_tree gs (λ_. Gpos) (tf_enter (sign_tf_for gs) xs es)),<br>       etf_combine    = route_combine gs (λ_. Gneg) ⦈" | To carry a genuinely named-global analysis through the real solver, route with CONSTANT slots: edge contributions to Gpos, combine (procedure-return) contributions to Gneg. Both named slots are populated and the routing is monotone (unlike a routing that depends on the reassembled state, which is not σ-monotone and fails the TD_side mono_sides precondition), so the three TD_side preconditions discharge from the generic per-tree lemmas in TD_Side_Eff_Bounds. |
| definition | `sideg_tree` | 480 | definition sideg_tree ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ gname ⇒ gname ⇒ (sign abs_state ⇒ sign abs_state) ⇒ pp<br>   ⇒ (pp, gname, sign abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "sideg_tree gs gread gwrite f u =<br>     QueryL u (λsu. QueryG gread (λg.<br>       let res = f (su ⊔ g)<br>       in Side gwrite (restrict_global_for gs res) (Answer (restrict_local_for gs res))))" | `sideg_tree` queries the local unknown and exactly one named global, then routes the global result to one selected slot. Its soundness statement therefore uses `side_env_g` rather than the join of every global slot. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Parity/Parity_Checks.thy

> Only composition lives here, exactly as Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Checks and Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Checks: the Parity lattice comparison tables (`parity_less_true`/`parity_less_false`/`parity_eq_true`/ `parity_eq_false`) and their `Voblint_Analysis.Parity_Numeric_Queries` interpretation of `abstract_numeric_queries` live in that theory. The Parity expression evaluator `aval_parity` lives in `Voblint_Analysis.Parity_Domain`. The Boolean recursion over `bexp`, the three-way classification, and the node-indexed bridge to `checks_proven` come from interpreting `abstract_check_domain` (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Checks`) once, below, reusing the numeric-query facts already proved sound in `parity_numeric_queries` --- no Boolean recursion or classification logic is restated.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `test_env_eo` | 61 | definition test_env_eo :: "parity abs_state" where<br>  "test_env_eo = (λ_. PTop)((STR ''x'') := PEven, (STR ''y'') := POdd)" | One state per test, built as an override of an otherwise-unconstrained (`PTop`) environment, so each test exercises exactly the comparison it names. |
| definition | `test_env_nested_proved` | 86 | definition test_env_nested_proved :: "parity abs_state" where<br>  "test_env_nested_proved = (λ_. PTop)((STR ''x'') := PEven, (STR ''y'') := POdd)" | Nested `Or`: proved through the negated-equality branch alone. |
| definition | `test_env_nested_unknown` | 98 | definition test_env_nested_unknown :: "parity abs_state" where<br>  "test_env_nested_unknown = (λ_. PTop)((STR ''x'') := PEven, (STR ''y'') := PEven)" | Nested `Or`, unknown: neither branch resolves when both sides share a parity class. |
| definition | `parity_check_report` | 114 | definition parity_check_report ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ check_report_entry list" where<br>  "parity_check_report gs mnm p =<br>     classify_checks (prog_cfg mnm p) (parity_exec_prog_at gs mnm p) parity_classify_check"<br><br>end | Thin composition, mirroring `sign_check_report`/`interval_check_report`: `classify_checks` owns the traversal and ordering, `parity_exec_prog_at` owns the node-indexed Parity environment, and `parity_classify_check` owns the per-check classification. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Parity/Parity_Domain.thy

> parity abstracts integers by their parity: Bot -- empty (unreachable) Even -- {n \| even n} Odd -- {n \| odd n} Top -- all integers Four-element lattice (Bot \<sqsubseteq> Even,Odd \<sqsubseteq> Top). Finite; widen = sup. Guards do not refine parity, so the assume transfer is the identity.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `parity` | 20 | datatype parity = PBot \| PEven \| POdd \| PTop |  |
| fun | `gamma_parity` | 22 | fun gamma_parity :: "parity =&gt; int set" where<br>    "gamma_parity PBot  = {}"<br>  \| "gamma_parity PEven = {n. even n}"<br>  \| "gamma_parity POdd  = {n. odd n}"<br>  \| "gamma_parity PTop  = UNIV" |  |
| fun | `parity_le` | 30 | fun parity_le :: "parity =&gt; parity =&gt; bool" where<br>    "parity_le PBot  _     = True"<br>  \| "parity_le _     PTop  = True"<br>  \| "parity_le PEven PEven = True"<br>  \| "parity_le POdd  POdd  = True"<br>  \| "parity_le _     _     = False" |  |
| definition | `less_eq_parity` | 49 | definition less_eq_parity :: "parity =&gt; parity =&gt; bool" where "(a::parity) ≤ b = parity_le a b" |  |
| definition | `less_parity` | 50 | definition less_parity    :: "parity =&gt; parity =&gt; bool" where "(a::parity) &lt;  b = (parity_le a b ∧ ¬ parity_le b a)"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instance parity :: preorder<br>proof<br>  fix x y z :: parity<br>  show "(x &lt; y) = (x ≤ y ∧ ¬ y ≤ x)" unfolding less_parity_def less_eq_parity_def by simp<br>  show "x ≤ x" by (simp add: less_eq_parity_def parity_le_refl)<br>  show "x ≤ y ⟹ y ≤ z ⟹ x ≤ z" unfolding less_eq_parity_def by (rule parity_le_trans)<br>qed<br><br>instantiation parity :: bot begin |  |
| definition | `bot_parity` | 63 | definition "bot_parity = PBot"<br>instance ..<br>end |  |
| definition | `is_bottom_parity` | 73 | definition is_bottom_parity :: "parity ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_bottom_parity p = (p = PBot)" | `PBot` is the only empty value a finite enumerated domain can have, the same reasoning as Sign's `is_bottom_sign`, so a direct equality test is exact here too. |
| fun | `join_parity` | 82 | fun join_parity :: "parity =&gt; parity =&gt; parity" where<br>    "join_parity PBot  b     = b"<br>  \| "join_parity a     PBot  = a"<br>  \| "join_parity PTop  _     = PTop"<br>  \| "join_parity _     PTop  = PTop"<br>  \| "join_parity PEven PEven = PEven"<br>  \| "join_parity POdd  POdd  = POdd"<br>  \| "join_parity _     _     = PTop" |  |
| definition | `top_parity` | 114 | definition "top_parity = PTop"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instantiation parity :: order_top begin<br>instance proof intro_classes<br>  fix x :: parity<br>  show "x ≤ top"<br>    unfolding less_eq_parity_def top_parity_def by (cases x) simp_all<br>qed<br>end |  |
| definition | `is_top_parity` | 128 | definition is_top_parity :: "parity ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_top_parity p = (p = PTop)" | `PTop` is the unique top of a finite enumeration, the same reasoning as Sign's `is_top_sign`. |
| definition | `sup_parity` | 135 | definition sup_parity :: "parity =&gt; parity =&gt; parity" where "sup_parity = join_parity"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instance parity :: semilattice_sup<br>proof<br>  fix x y z :: parity<br>  show "x ≤ x ⊔ y" unfolding sup_parity_def less_eq_parity_def by (rule join_parity_ub1)<br>  show "y ≤ x ⊔ y" unfolding sup_parity_def less_eq_parity_def by (rule join_parity_ub2)<br>  show "y ≤ x ⟹ z ≤ x ⟹ y ⊔ z ≤ x" unfolding sup_parity_def less_eq_parity_def by (rule join_parity_least)<br>qed<br><br>instance parity :: bounded_semilattice_sup_bot .. |  |
| definition | `widen` | 152 | definition "widen (a :: parity) b = join_parity a b" |  |
| definition | `narrow` | 153 | definition "narrow (a :: parity) b = a"<br>instance proof<br>  fix a b :: parity<br>  show "a ≤ widen a b" unfolding less_eq_parity_def widen_parity_def by (rule join_parity_ub1)<br>  show "b ≤ widen a b" unfolding less_eq_parity_def widen_parity_def by (rule join_parity_ub2)<br>  show "b ≤ a ⟹ b ≤ narrow a b" unfolding narrow_parity_def by simp<br>  show "b ≤ a ⟹ narrow a b ≤ a" unfolding narrow_parity_def by (simp add: less_eq_parity_def parity_le_refl)<br>qed<br>end |  |
| fun | `plus_parity` | 166 | fun plus_parity :: "parity =&gt; parity =&gt; parity" where<br>    "plus_parity PBot  _     = PBot"<br>  \| "plus_parity _     PBot  = PBot"<br>  \| "plus_parity PEven PEven = PEven"<br>  \| "plus_parity POdd  POdd  = PEven"<br>  \| "plus_parity PEven POdd  = POdd"<br>  \| "plus_parity POdd  PEven = POdd"<br>  \| "plus_parity _     _     = PTop"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instantiation parity :: minus begin |  |
| fun | `minus_parity` | 178 | fun minus_parity :: "parity =&gt; parity =&gt; parity" where<br>    "minus_parity PBot  _     = PBot"<br>  \| "minus_parity _     PBot  = PBot"<br>  \| "minus_parity PEven PEven = PEven"<br>  \| "minus_parity POdd  POdd  = PEven"<br>  \| "minus_parity PEven POdd  = POdd"<br>  \| "minus_parity POdd  PEven = POdd"<br>  \| "minus_parity _     _     = PTop"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instantiation parity :: times begin |  |
| fun | `times_parity` | 190 | fun times_parity :: "parity =&gt; parity =&gt; parity" where<br>    "times_parity PBot  _     = PBot"<br>  \| "times_parity _     PBot  = PBot"<br>  \| "times_parity PEven _     = PEven"<br>  \| "times_parity _     PEven = PEven"<br>  \| "times_parity POdd  POdd  = POdd"<br>  \| "times_parity _     _     = PTop"<br>instance ..<br>end |  |
| fun | `parity_of_int` | 200 | fun parity_of_int :: "int =&gt; parity" where<br>  "parity_of_int n = (if even n then PEven else POdd)" |  |
| fun | `aval_parity` | 217 | fun aval_parity :: "aexp =&gt; (vname =&gt; parity) =&gt; parity" where<br>    "aval_parity (N n)       σ = parity_of_int n"<br>  \| "aval_parity (V v)       σ = σ v"<br>  \| "aval_parity (Plus  a b) σ = aval_parity a σ + aval_parity b σ"<br>  \| "aval_parity (Minus a b) σ = aval_parity a σ - aval_parity b σ"<br>  \| "aval_parity (Times a b) σ = aval_parity a σ * aval_parity b σ" |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Parity/Parity_Exec_Sound.thy

> • `parity_exec_raw Π ps main` -- the raw solver solution (`pp + unit ⇒ parity resolved_st_q`), code-generating, the thing @{command value} / `eval` evaluates; • `parity_exec_at gs Π ps main v` -- the analyzer's computed abstract state at node `v` (a `parity abs_state`), read back through `fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs`; • `parity_exec_terminates Π ps main` -- the single assumption: the vendored solver terminates on this program. No GraphViz whole-program renderer is added here: the worked check example renders through the domain-independent `raw_cfg_dot_lit` pipeline directly, exactly as Voblint_Examples.Example_Checks_Store_Only does for Sign, so no `parity_graph_config` is needed.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `parity_exec_eqs` | 27 | definition parity_exec_eqs ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp, unit, parity resolved_st_q) eqsT" where<br>  "parity_exec_eqs gs Π ps mnm main =<br>     side_cfg_T_eff_st (compile_prog Π ps mnm main) (parity_etf_st_for gs) bot cinit_parity_st ()" | • `parity_exec_raw Π ps main` -- the raw solver solution (`pp + unit ⇒ parity resolved_st_q`), code-generating, the thing @{command value} / `eval` evaluates; • `parity_exec_at gs Π ps main v` -- the analyzer's computed abstract state at node `v` (a `parity abs_state`), read back through `fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs`; • `parity_exec_terminates Π ps main` -- the single assumption: the vendored solver terminates on this program. No GraphViz whole-program renderer is added here: the worked check example renders through the domain-independent `raw_cfg_dot_lit` pipeline directly, exactly as Voblint_Examples.Example_Checks_Store_Only does for Sign, so no `parity_graph_config` is needed. |
| definition | `parity_exec_raw` | 32 | definition parity_exec_raw ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp + unit ⇒ parity resolved_st_q)" where<br>  "parity_exec_raw gs Π ps mnm main =<br>     snd (TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve (parity_exec_eqs gs Π ps mnm main)<br>            (cfg_exit (compile_prog Π ps mnm main)))" |  |
| definition | `parity_exec_at` | 38 | definition parity_exec_at ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ pp ⇒ parity abs_state" where<br>  "parity_exec_at gs Π ps mnm main v =<br>     side_env (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs ∘ parity_exec_raw gs Π ps mnm main) v" |  |
| definition | `parity_exec` | 43 | definition parity_exec ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ parity abs_state" where<br>  "parity_exec gs Π ps mnm main = parity_exec_at gs Π ps mnm main (cfg_exit (compile_prog Π ps mnm main))" |  |
| definition | `parity_exec_terminates` | 47 | definition parity_exec_terminates ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "parity_exec_terminates gs Π ps mnm main =<br>     TD_side_always_join_Interp.solve_dom TYPE(unit) TYPE(parity resolved_st_q)<br>        (parity_exec_eqs gs Π ps mnm main) (cfg_exit (compile_prog Π ps mnm main))" |  |
| definition | `parity_exec_prog_at` | 165 | definition parity_exec_prog_at :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ pp ⇒ parity abs_state" where<br>  "parity_exec_prog_at gs mnm p v = parity_exec_at gs (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) mnm (prog_main p) v" | An @{type imp_prog} written with the `⟦ … ⟧` bracket already bundles the procedure table, procedure-name list, and main command. The wrappers below feed those three projections to the analyzer in one step, mirroring `Sign_Exec_Sound`/`Interval_Exec_Sound`'s `*_exec_prog_at` family. `prog_cfg` (`Voblint_CFG.Compile_Invariants`) is the shared, domain-independent instance of this same projection for the CFG itself, so it is imported rather than redefined here --- Sign and Interval use the exact same constant, not equivalent local copies. |
| definition | `parity_exec_prog` | 168 | definition parity_exec_prog :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ parity abs_state" where<br>  "parity_exec_prog gs mnm p = parity_exec_prog_at gs mnm p (cfg_exit (prog_cfg mnm p))" |  |
| definition | `parity_terminates_prog` | 171 | definition parity_terminates_prog :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ bool" where<br>  "parity_terminates_prog gs mnm p = parity_exec_terminates gs (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) mnm (prog_main p)" |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Parity/Parity_Numeric_Queries.thy

> Third-domain validation for `abstract_numeric_queries` (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Numeric_Queries`), hand-tuned like Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Numeric_Queries rather than derived through `backward_domain`: parity has no `inv_less`/`meet` instance (guards do not refine parity, `Voblint_Analysis.Parity_Domain`), so there is nothing for the generic derivations to read off. Parity carries no order information at all, so `parity_less_true`/`parity_less_false` are provable only in the vacuous case (one side `PBot`); every genuine pair is unknown. `PBot` is the lattice's unique empty representative (unlike Interval's raw `ivl` type, which has infinitely many non-canonical empty representations), so the vacuous case is a single clean equality test, as for Sign's `SBot`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `parity_vacuous` | 32 | definition parity_vacuous :: "parity ⇒ parity ⇒ bool" where<br>  "parity_vacuous a b ↔ (a = PBot ∨ b = PBot)" | `parity_less_true a b`/`parity_less_false a b` both hold exactly when one side concretizes to nothing: neither judgment can ever be established from parity alone, since knowing a value's evenness says nothing about its order against another value's evenness. The two coincide definitionally --- both are the same vacuity test --- which is itself the content of the "usually unknown" fact about Parity's order queries. |
| definition | `parity_less_true` | 35 | definition parity_less_true :: "parity ⇒ parity ⇒ bool" where<br>  "parity_less_true = parity_vacuous" |  |
| definition | `parity_less_false` | 38 | definition parity_less_false :: "parity ⇒ parity ⇒ bool" where<br>  "parity_less_false = parity_vacuous" |  |
| definition | `parity_eq_true` | 70 | definition parity_eq_true :: "parity ⇒ parity ⇒ bool" where<br>  "parity_eq_true = parity_vacuous" | `parity_eq_true` is vacuous-only for the same reason as the order queries: no parity class concretizes to a singleton (unlike Sign's `SZero` or an Interval point interval), so no non-vacuous pair is ever provably equal. `parity_eq_true a b` asks provable equality of every witness pair, so a reflexive-looking check such as `x == x` is *not* classified true by this interface either: `aval_abs` only ever hands both sides the same *abstract* value `PEven`/`POdd`/`PTop`, which still ranges over infinitely many concrete integers, so `eq_true` stays false there too --- correctly: this classification layer never assumes two occurrences of the same syntactic expression carry a hidden equality constraint. `parity_eq_true` is genuinely provable to be true for identical parity classes when combined at the check level with the compiler-independent evaluator; this file states it as it is, no more. |
| fun | `parity_eq_false` | 86 | fun parity_eq_false :: "parity ⇒ parity ⇒ bool" where<br>    "parity_eq_false PEven POdd = True"<br>  \| "parity_eq_false POdd PEven = True"<br>  \| "parity_eq_false a b = (a = PBot ∨ b = PBot)" | `parity_eq_false` is where parity earns its keep: two values of disjoint parity classes --- one `PEven`, the other `POdd` --- can never be equal, regardless of how imprecise Sign or Interval are about the same pair (both may see fully overlapping sign/range information). This is the one query Parity answers strictly better than a vacuity check. |

> Mirrors Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Side_Soundness at the parity domain: each edge tree queries the local program point and the unit global slot, applies the parity transfer, then splits the result into local and global parts.

| definition | `random_parity` | 30 | definition random_parity ::<br>    "vname =&gt; (vname =&gt; parity) =&gt; (vname =&gt; parity)" where<br>  "random_parity x σ = σ(x := PTop)" |  |
| definition | `assume_parity` | 60 | definition assume_parity :: "bexp =&gt; (vname =&gt; parity) =&gt; (vname =&gt; parity)" where<br>  "assume_parity b σ = σ" | No boolean guard in the language constrains the parity of a variable, so the sound and most precise parity assume keeps the incoming state unchanged. |
