### src/Analysis/Instances/Sign/Sign_Backward.thy

> The seven-element sign lattice has a well-defined glb. Adding `semilattice_inf` here gives *`inf_mono`* for free, which is needed for the monotonicity proof of *bfilter*.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `meet_sign` | 15 | fun meet_sign :: "sign =&gt; sign =&gt; sign" where<br>    "meet_sign SBot    _       = SBot"<br>  \| "meet_sign _       SBot    = SBot"<br>  \| "meet_sign STop    b       = b"<br>  \| "meet_sign a       STop    = a"<br>  \| "meet_sign SNeg    SNeg    = SNeg"<br>  \| "meet_sign SNeg    SNonPos = SNeg"<br>  \| "meet_sign SNonPos SNeg    = SNeg"<br>  \| "meet_sign SNonPos SNonPos = SNonPos"<br>  \| "meet_sign SNonPos SZero   = SZero"<br>  \| "meet_sign SZero   SNonPos = SZero"<br>  \| "meet_sign SNonPos SNonNeg = SZero"<br>  \| "meet_sign SNonNeg SNonPos = SZero"<br>  \| "meet_sign SZero   SZero   = SZero"<br>  \| "meet_sign SZero   SNonNeg = SZero"<br>... (6 more lines) | The seven-element sign lattice has a well-defined glb. Adding `semilattice_inf` here gives *`inf_mono`* for free, which is needed for the monotonicity proof of *bfilter*. |
| definition | `inf_sign` | 42 | definition inf_sign :: "sign =&gt; sign =&gt; sign" where<br>  "inf_sign = meet_sign"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>declare inf_sign_def [simp]<br><br>instance sign :: semilattice_inf<br>proof intro_classes<br>  fix x y z :: sign<br>  show "x ⊓ y ≤ x"<br>    by (cases x; cases y; auto simp: less_eq_sign_def)<br>  show "x ⊓ y ≤ y"<br>    by (cases x; cases y; auto simp: less_eq_sign_def)<br>  show "x ≤ y ⟹ x ≤ z ⟹ x ≤ y ⊓ z"<br>... (5 more lines) |  |
| fun | `inv_less_sign` | 73 | fun inv_less_sign :: "bool =&gt; sign =&gt; sign =&gt; sign * sign" where<br>    "inv_less_sign True  a1 a2 =<br>       (let a1' = if sign_le a2 SNonPos then meet_sign a1 SNeg else a1 ;<br>                a2' = if sign_le a1 SNonNeg then meet_sign a2 SPos else a2<br>        in (a1', a2'))"<br>  \| "inv_less_sign False a1 a2 =<br>       (let a1' = if sign_le a2 SPos then meet_sign a1 SPos<br>                  else if sign_le a2 SNonNeg then meet_sign a1 SNonNeg<br>                  else a1 ;<br>                a2' = if sign_le a1 SNeg then meet_sign a2 SNeg<br>                  else if sign_le a1 SNonPos then meet_sign a2 SNonPos<br>                  else a2<br>        in (a1', a2'))" | Per the backward-domain plan, *inv_less_sign* provides sign-specific refinement when a guard *e1 < e2* is known true or false. Plus/minus/times are too coarse for useful arithmetic inversion in sign, so they instantiate the shared `inv_conservative` (identity) instead of a per-domain no-op; the structural bfilter propagation (And/Or/Not/Eq) is where sign gains. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Sign/Sign_Checks.thy

> Only composition lives here: the Sign lattice comparison tables (`sign_less_true`/`sign_less_false`/`sign_eq_true`/`sign_eq_false`) and their `Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Numeric_Queries` interpretation of `abstract_numeric_queries` live in that theory. The Sign expression evaluator `aval_sign` lives in `Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Arithmetic`. The Boolean recursion over `bexp` (`Not`, `And`, `Or`), the three-way classification, and the node-indexed bridge to `checks_proven` come from interpreting `abstract_check_domain` (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Checks`) once, below, reusing the numeric-query facts already proved sound in `sign_numeric_queries` rather than re-deriving the comparison tables --- the same way `sign_backward_domain` in `Sign_Backward.thy` interprets `backward_domain` for guard narrowing.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `test_env_pos` | 61 | definition test_env_pos :: "sign abs_state" where<br>  "test_env_pos = (λ_. STop)((STR ''x'') := SPos)" | One state per test, built as an override of an otherwise-unconstrained (`STop`) environment, so each test exercises exactly the comparison it names. |
| definition | `test_env_nonneg` | 80 | definition test_env_nonneg :: "sign abs_state" where<br>  "test_env_nonneg = (λ_. STop)((STR ''x'') := SNonNeg)" | Negation: `!(x < 0)` is provable under `SNonNeg`, going through `check_false` on the un-negated `x < 0` rather than a one-sided negation of `check_true`. |
| definition | `test_env_nested_proved` | 90 | definition test_env_nested_proved :: "sign abs_state" where<br>  "test_env_nested_proved = (λ_. STop)((STR ''x'') := SPos, (STR ''y'') := SPos)" | Nested `And`/`Or`: proved through the `And` branch alone, and unknown when neither branch resolves. |
| definition | `test_env_nested_unknown` | 99 | definition test_env_nested_unknown :: "sign abs_state" where<br>  "test_env_nested_unknown = (λ_. STop)((STR ''x'') := SNonNeg, (STR ''y'') := SPos)" |  |
| definition | `sign_check_report` | 119 | definition sign_check_report ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ check_report_entry list" where<br>  "sign_check_report gs mnm p =<br>     classify_checks (prog_cfg mnm p) (sign_exec_prog_at gs mnm p) sign_classify_check" | Thin composition, not a restatement: `classify_checks` already owns the executable traversal and ordering, `sign_exec_prog_at` already owns the node-indexed Sign environment, and `sign_classify_check` above already owns the per-check classification. This wrapper only feeds a compiled program's own three projections through them, the same way `sign_exec_prog` feeds them through `sign_exec_at`. |
| definition | `analyse_sign_report_for` | 135 | definition analyse_sign_report_for :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ check_report_entry list" where<br>  "analyse_sign_report_for gs p =<br>     classify_checks (prog_cfg prog_main_name p) (analyse_sign_env_for gs p) sign_classify_check" | `analyse_sign_report_for` mirrors `sign_check_report` exactly, reading through `analyse_sign_env_for` (the native D/G pipeline `Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Exec_Sound` computes) instead of `sign_exec_prog_at` (the older `side_cfg_T_eff_st` pipeline) --- this is the report function the exported `analyse` API actually dispatches to (see `Example_Analysis_Dispatch`, downstream in Examples), fixed at `prog_main_name` rather than an arbitrary `mnm` since `analyse_sign_env_for` already is. |
| definition | `analyse_sign_report` | 167 | definition analyse_sign_report :: "imp_prog ⇒ check_report_entry list" where<br>  "analyse_sign_report p = analyse_sign_report_for (declared_global p) p" | Convenience instance at `declared_global` `p`, matching `analyse_sign`'s shape. |
| definition | `analyse_sign_report_for_with_state` | 181 | definition analyse_sign_report_for_with_state ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ (pp × bexp × check_result × (vname ⇒ sign)) list" where<br>  "analyse_sign_report_for_with_state gs p =<br>     classify_checks_with_state (prog_cfg prog_main_name p) (analyse_sign_env_for gs p)<br>       sign_classify_check"<br><br>declare analyse_sign_report_for_with_state_def [code del] | State-carrying sibling of `analyse_sign_report_for`/`analyse_sign_report`, via `classify_checks_with_state`: same D/G pipeline, same environment, with the per-check Sign environment attached to each report entry instead of discarded. The `[code]` rewrite mirrors the one above for the same reason: naive definitional unfolding would re-run the solver twice per check. |
| definition | `analyse_sign_report_with_state` | 203 | definition analyse_sign_report_with_state ::<br>    "imp_prog ⇒ (pp × bexp × check_result × (vname ⇒ sign)) list" where<br>  "analyse_sign_report_with_state p = analyse_sign_report_for_with_state (declared_global p) p"<br><br>end | Convenience instance at `declared_global` `p`, matching `analyse_sign_report`'s shape. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Sign/Sign_DG.thy

> Sign is the diagonal D/G instance: answers and side effects use the same abstract-state domain. The entry transfer therefore combines the caller answer and global side effect before binding parameters. All generator and collecting results follow from the generic locale; only transfer soundness is domain-specific.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `sign_keep_all` | 29 | definition sign_keep_all :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "sign_keep_all x = True" | Sign under a conjunctive placement: every location keeps a precise per-activation local view (`sign_keep_all` holds everywhere), while every declared global also publishes a joined summary for other activations (`publish_side = gs`). `gs` here only decides publication, never whether a location is also kept -- unlike `unit_dg_spec_for`'s exclusive `gs`/`¬ gs` split, this is the two-copies shape Goblint-style privatization needs, and it is sound by the same generic covering argument, no separate proof. |
| definition | `sign_dg_gamma` | 86 | definition sign_dg_gamma ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool)<br>   ⇒ (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (sign abs_state, sign abs_state) dg_state)<br>   ⇒ pp ⇒ store set"<br>where<br>  "sign_dg_gamma gs ≡ sound_dg_spec.dg_gamma (gamma_unit gs)" | The joint concretization at a program point and the configured equation system, named natively so consumers do not reach through the locale prefix. |
| definition | `sign_dg_generator` | 93 | definition sign_dg_generator ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ sign abs_state ⇒ sign abs_state ⇒ sign abs_state<br>   ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (sign abs_state, sign abs_state) dg_state) eqsT"<br>where<br>  "sign_dg_generator gs ≡ sound_dg_spec.dg_gen (unit_dg_spec_for gs (sign_tf_for gs))" |  |
| locale | `sign_dg_api` | 105 | locale sign_dg_api =<br>  fixes gs :: "vname ⇒ bool"<br>  assumes reserved: "reserved_ret_var gs"<br><br>sublocale sign_dg_api ⊆ sound_dg_spec_ltr_for "unit_dg_spec_for gs (sign_tf_for gs)" "gamma_unit gs" gs<br>  rewrites "sound_dg_spec.dg_gamma (gamma_unit gs) = sign_dg_gamma gs"<br>       and "sound_dg_spec.dg_gen (unit_dg_spec_for gs (sign_tf_for gs)) = sign_dg_generator gs"<br>  apply (unfold sound_dg_spec_ltr_for_def)<br>   apply (rule sound_dg_spec_unit_for[OF sign_is_sound_transfer_for reserved])<br>  apply (simp add: sign_dg_gamma_def)<br>  apply (simp add: sign_dg_generator_def)<br>  done<br><br>context sign_dg_api<br>begin | Public DG API for the Sign domain, generic in the classifier `gs`. The sublocale interpretation rewrites facts inherited from `sound_dg_spec` into this native vocabulary, so the exported theorem below cites the generic collecting-soundness fact directly with no manual unfold/fold bridging. |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Sign/Sign_Exec.thy

> `afilter_sign_st` / `bfilter_sign_st` commute with the abstract filters through `fun_of_resolved_st_q_for`; the generic executable mirror provides the shared induction.

| definition | `sign_etf_st_for` | 199 | definition sign_etf_st_for ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (unit, sign resolved_st_q) effectful_st_transfer" where<br>  "sign_etf_st_for gs = unit_etf_st_of_transfer gs (sign_tf_st_for gs) (sign_enter_st_for gs)" |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Sign/Sign_Exec_Sound.thy

> High-level vocabulary for the executable analyzer, so example statements read at the level of intent rather than solver plumbing: • `sign_exec_raw Π ps main` -- the raw solver solution (`pp + unit => sign resolved_st_q`), code-generating, the thing @{command value} / `eval` evaluates; • `sign_exec gs Π ps main` -- the analyzer's computed abstract state at the program exit (a `sign abs_state`), read back through `fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs`; • `sign_exec_terminates Π ps main` -- the single assumption: the vendored solver terminates on this program. `sign_exec_sound_collecting` / `sign_exec_sound_trace` are the program-parametric soundness theorems; concrete examples only fix a program and instantiate them.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `sign_exec_eqs` | 27 | definition sign_exec_eqs ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp, unit, sign resolved_st_q) eqsT" where<br>  "sign_exec_eqs gs Π ps mnm main =<br>     side_cfg_T_eff_st (compile_prog Π ps mnm main) (sign_etf_st_for gs) bot cinit_sign_st ()" | High-level vocabulary for the executable analyzer, so example statements read at the level of intent rather than solver plumbing: • `sign_exec_raw Π ps main` -- the raw solver solution (`pp + unit => sign resolved_st_q`), code-generating, the thing @{command value} / `eval` evaluates; • `sign_exec gs Π ps main` -- the analyzer's computed abstract state at the program exit (a `sign abs_state`), read back through `fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs`; • `sign_exec_terminates Π ps main` -- the single assumption: the vendored solver terminates on this program. `sign_exec_sound_collecting` / `sign_exec_sound_trace` are the program-parametric soundness theorems; concrete examples only fix a program and instantiate them. |
| definition | `sign_exec_raw` | 32 | definition sign_exec_raw ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp + unit ⇒ sign resolved_st_q)" where<br>  "sign_exec_raw gs Π ps mnm main =<br>     snd (TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve (sign_exec_eqs gs Π ps mnm main)<br>            (cfg_exit (compile_prog Π ps mnm main)))" |  |
| definition | `sign_exec_at` | 38 | definition sign_exec_at ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ pp ⇒ sign abs_state" where<br>  "sign_exec_at gs Π ps mnm main v =<br>     side_env (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs ∘ sign_exec_raw gs Π ps mnm main) v" |  |
| definition | `sign_exec` | 43 | definition sign_exec ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ sign abs_state" where<br>  "sign_exec gs Π ps mnm main = sign_exec_at gs Π ps mnm main (cfg_exit (compile_prog Π ps mnm main))" |  |
| definition | `sign_exec_terminates` | 47 | definition sign_exec_terminates ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "sign_exec_terminates gs Π ps mnm main =<br>     TD_side_always_join_Interp.solve_dom TYPE(unit) TYPE(sign resolved_st_q)<br>        (sign_exec_eqs gs Π ps mnm main) (cfg_exit (compile_prog Π ps mnm main))" |  |
| definition | `sign_exec_prog_at` | 181 | definition sign_exec_prog_at :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ pp ⇒ sign abs_state" where<br>  "sign_exec_prog_at gs mnm p v = sign_exec_at gs (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) mnm (prog_main p) v" | An @{type imp_prog} written with the `⟦ … ⟧` bracket already bundles the procedure table, procedure-name list, and main command. The wrappers below feed those three projections to the analyzer in one step, so example statements name the program once instead of repeating the triple. `prog_cfg` (`Voblint_CFG.Compile_Invariants`) is the shared, domain-independent instance of this same projection for the CFG itself, so it is imported rather than redefined here. |
| definition | `sign_exec_prog` | 184 | definition sign_exec_prog :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ sign abs_state" where<br>  "sign_exec_prog gs mnm p = sign_exec_prog_at gs mnm p (cfg_exit (prog_cfg mnm p))" |  |
| definition | `sign_terminates_prog` | 187 | definition sign_terminates_prog :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ bool" where<br>  "sign_terminates_prog gs mnm p = sign_exec_terminates gs (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) mnm (prog_main p)" |  |
| definition | `analyse_sign_eqs_for` | 229 | definition analyse_sign_eqs_for ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ imp_prog ⇒<br>     pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (sign exec_dg_st, sign exec_dg_st) dg_state) strategy_tree" where<br>  "analyse_sign_eqs_for gs p =<br>     dg_gen_of<br>       (unit_dg_spec_st_for gs (sign_tf_st_for gs) (sign_enter_st_for gs))<br>       (prog_cfg prog_main_name p) bot cinit_sign_st cinit_sign_st" | `sign_exec_prog` above is the older `side_cfg_T_eff_st` pipeline, mirroring `Interval_Exec_Sound`'s `ivl_exec_prog` exactly. The exported runtime API `analyse` (`Example_Analysis_Dispatch`, downstream in Examples) dispatches through a different, newer pipeline instead: the native D/G equation system (`dg_gen_of`, `Voblint_Analysis.Exec_DG_Bridge`), because that is what the `Exec_Sign_DG_Run` flagship example (`dgEx_sol`, downstream in Examples) already used before this API existed. Only the raw computation lives here --- `analyse_sign_for`'s soundness proof needs the `unit_dg_exec_analysis` locale (`Run_Analysis_Sound`, Formalization session), one session later than Analysis in the locked six-session chain, so that half cannot live in this file; it stays in `Example_Sign_Codegen` (downstream in Examples), which references these definitions with `gs`/`p` applied explicitly. |
| definition | `analyse_sign_for` | 237 | definition analyse_sign_for :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ imp_prog ⇒<br>    (pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (sign exec_dg_st, sign exec_dg_st) dg_state)" where<br>  "analyse_sign_for gs p =<br>     TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve (analyse_sign_eqs_for gs p) (cfg_exit (prog_cfg prog_main_name p), ())" |  |
| definition | `analyse_sign_env_for` | 248 | definition analyse_sign_env_for :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ pp ⇒ sign abs_state" where<br>  "analyse_sign_env_for gs p v =<br>     combine_env^# gs<br>       (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for gs (locals (snd (analyse_sign_for gs p) (Inl (v, ())))))<br>       (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for gs (globs (snd (analyse_sign_for gs p) (Inr ()))))" | `analyse_sign_env_for` reads the exit-answer/side-effect split `dg_state` keeps (`Inl (v, ())` for the local answer at `v`, `Inr ()` for the global side effect) the same way `dgEx_inspect` in `Exec_Sign_DG_Run` (downstream in Examples) does by hand. |
| definition | `analyse_sign_eqs` | 258 | definition analyse_sign_eqs :: "imp_prog ⇒<br>    pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (sign exec_dg_st, sign exec_dg_st) dg_state) strategy_tree" where<br>  "analyse_sign_eqs p = analyse_sign_eqs_for (declared_global p) p" | Convenience instances at `declared_global` `p`, matching `analyse_interval`'s shape. |
| definition | `analyse_sign` | 262 | definition analyse_sign :: "imp_prog ⇒<br>    (pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (sign exec_dg_st, sign exec_dg_st) dg_state)" where<br>  "analyse_sign p = analyse_sign_for (declared_global p) p" |  |
| definition | `analyse_sign_env` | 266 | definition analyse_sign_env :: "imp_prog ⇒ pp ⇒ sign abs_state" where<br>  "analyse_sign_env p = analyse_sign_env_for (declared_global p) p" |  |
| definition | `sign_graph_config` | 284 | definition sign_graph_config ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒<br>    (unit, unit, sign abs_state, sign abs_state) analysis_graph_config" where<br>  "sign_graph_config gs Π ps mnm main = compiled_domain_graph_config gs Π ps mnm main (λv. v = STop)" | One-command annotated CFG rendering for the sign domain through the canonical context-expanded graph model. It compiles the program, runs the solver, and adapts the unit context to the explicit presentation domain. Typical example-file use: @{text [display] "ML_val \<open> writeln (@{code sign_annotated_dot_prog_lit} ''main'' @{code my_prog}) \<close>"} |
| definition | `sign_graph_solution` | 298 | definition sign_graph_solution ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (pp + unit ⇒ sign resolved_st_q) ⇒ (pp × unit + unit ⇒ sign abs_state)" where<br>  "sign_graph_solution gs sol z =<br>    (case z of Inl (p, ()) ⇒ fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs (sol (Inl p))<br>     \| Inr () ⇒ fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs (sol (Inr ())) )" |  |
| definition | `sign_annotated_dot_lit` | 304 | definition sign_annotated_dot_lit ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ String.literal" where<br>  "sign_annotated_dot_lit gs Π ps mnm main =<br>    String.implode<br>      (let g = compile_prog Π ps mnm main;<br>           domain = contextual_graph_domain g (λ_. [()]) @ [Inr ()];<br>           sol = sign_graph_solution gs (sign_exec_raw gs Π ps mnm main)<br>       in contextual_analysis_dot (sign_graph_config gs Π ps mnm main) g domain sol)" |  |
| definition | `sign_annotated_dot_prog_lit` | 313 | definition sign_annotated_dot_prog_lit :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ String.literal" where<br>  "sign_annotated_dot_prog_lit gs mnm p =<br>     sign_annotated_dot_lit gs (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) mnm (prog_main p)"<br><br>end |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Sign/Sign_Lattice.thy

> sign abstracts integers by their sign: Bot -- empty (unreachable / undefined) Neg -- strictly negative {n \| n < 0} NonPos -- non-positive {n \| n ≤ 0} Zero -- exactly zero {0} NonNeg -- non-negative {n \| n ≥ 0} Pos -- strictly positive {n \| n > 0} Top -- all integers UNIV Seven-element lattice (Bot \<sqsubseteq> Neg,Zero,Pos \<sqsubseteq> NonPos,NonNeg \<sqsubseteq> Top). Finite; no widening needed.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `sign` | 23 | datatype sign = SBot \| SNeg \| SNonPos \| SZero \| SNonNeg \| SPos \| STop |  |
| fun | `gamma_sign` | 27 | fun gamma_sign :: "sign =&gt; int set" where<br>    "gamma_sign SBot    = {}"<br>  \| "gamma_sign SNeg    = {n. n &lt; 0}"<br>  \| "gamma_sign SNonPos = {n. n ≤ 0}"<br>  \| "gamma_sign SZero   = {0}"<br>  \| "gamma_sign SNonNeg = {n. n ≥ 0}"<br>  \| "gamma_sign SPos    = {n. n &gt; 0}"<br>  \| "gamma_sign STop    = UNIV" |  |
| fun | `sign_le` | 38 | fun sign_le :: "sign =&gt; sign =&gt; bool" where<br>    "sign_le SBot    _       = True"<br>  \| "sign_le _       STop    = True"<br>  \| "sign_le SNeg    SNeg    = True"<br>  \| "sign_le SNeg    SNonPos = True"<br>  \| "sign_le SNonPos SNonPos = True"<br>  \| "sign_le SZero   SZero   = True"<br>  \| "sign_le SZero   SNonPos = True"<br>  \| "sign_le SZero   SNonNeg = True"<br>  \| "sign_le SNonNeg SNonNeg = True"<br>  \| "sign_le SPos    SPos    = True"<br>  \| "sign_le SPos    SNonNeg = True"<br>  \| "sign_le _       _       = False" |  |
| definition | `less_eq_sign` | 70 | definition less_eq_sign :: "sign =&gt; sign =&gt; bool" where "(a::sign) &lt;= b = sign_le a b" |  |
| definition | `less_sign` | 71 | definition less_sign    :: "sign =&gt; sign =&gt; bool" where "(a::sign) &lt;  b = (sign_le a b ∧ ¬ sign_le b a)"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instance sign :: preorder<br>proof intro_classes<br>  fix x y z :: sign<br>  show "(x &lt; y) = (x ≤ y ∧ ¬ y ≤ x)"<br>    unfolding less_sign_def less_eq_sign_def by simp<br>  show "x ≤ x"<br>    by (simp add: less_eq_sign_def sign_le_refl)<br>  show "x ≤ y ⟹ y ≤ z ⟹ x ≤ z"<br>    unfolding less_eq_sign_def by (rule sign_le_trans)<br>qed |  |
| definition | `bot_sign` | 88 | definition "bot_sign = SBot"<br>instance ..<br>end | The bottom instance lifts pointwise to abstract states and enables monotone least-upper-bound iteration. |
| definition | `is_bottom_sign` | 103 | definition is_bottom_sign :: "sign ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_bottom_sign s = (s = SBot)" | `SBot` is the only empty value a finite enumerated domain can have (every other constructor denotes a nonempty set of integers), so a direct equality test is already exact --- unlike Interval's analogous fact, which cannot use equality against one representative because Interval's bound-pair representation has many empty values besides its canonical `bot`. Exposed here, at the domain's own theory, rather than inlined where a caller happens to need it, so every consumer (not just one) gets the same domain-owned fact. |
| fun | `join_sign` | 114 | fun join_sign :: "sign =&gt; sign =&gt; sign" where<br>    "join_sign SBot    b       = b"<br>  \| "join_sign a       SBot    = a"<br>  \| "join_sign STop    _       = STop"<br>  \| "join_sign _       STop    = STop"<br>  \| "join_sign SNeg    SNeg    = SNeg"<br>  \| "join_sign SNeg    SZero   = SNonPos"<br>  \| "join_sign SNeg    SNonPos = SNonPos"<br>  \| "join_sign SZero   SNeg    = SNonPos"<br>  \| "join_sign SZero   SZero   = SZero"<br>  \| "join_sign SZero   SPos    = SNonNeg"<br>  \| "join_sign SZero   SNonPos = SNonPos"<br>  \| "join_sign SZero   SNonNeg = SNonNeg"<br>  \| "join_sign SNonPos SNeg    = SNonPos"<br>  \| "join_sign SNonPos SZero   = SNonPos"<br>... (8 more lines) |  |
| definition | `top_sign` | 176 | definition "top_sign = STop"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instantiation sign :: order_top begin<br>instance proof intro_classes<br>  fix x :: sign<br>  show "x ≤ top"<br>    unfolding less_eq_sign_def top_sign_def by (cases x) simp_all<br>qed<br>end |  |
| definition | `is_top_sign` | 190 | definition is_top_sign :: "sign ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_top_sign s = (s = STop)" | `STop` is likewise the unique top of a finite enumeration. |
| definition | `sup_sign` | 197 | definition sup_sign :: "sign =&gt; sign =&gt; sign" where<br>  "sup_sign = join_sign"<br>instance ..<br>end<br><br>instance sign :: semilattice_sup<br>proof intro_classes<br>  fix x y z :: sign<br>  show "x ≤ x ⊔ y"<br>    unfolding sup_sign_def less_eq_sign_def by (rule join_sign_ub1)<br>  show "y ≤ x ⊔ y"<br>    unfolding sup_sign_def less_eq_sign_def by (rule join_sign_ub2)<br>  show "y ≤ x ⟹ z ≤ x ⟹ y ⊔ z ≤ x"<br>    unfolding sup_sign_def less_eq_sign_def by (rule join_sign_least)<br>qed<br>... (3 more lines) |  |
| definition | `narrow_sign_td` | 219 | definition narrow_sign_td :: "sign ⇒ sign ⇒ sign" where<br>  "narrow_sign_td a b = a"<br><br>instantiation sign :: warrowing begin |  |
| definition | `widen` | 223 | definition "widen (a :: sign) b = join_sign a b" |  |
| definition | `narrow` | 224 | definition "narrow (a :: sign) b = narrow_sign_td a b"<br>instance proof intro_classes<br>  fix a b :: sign<br>  show "a ≤ widen a b"<br>    unfolding less_eq_sign_def widen_sign_def by (rule join_sign_ub1)<br>  show "b ≤ widen a b"<br>    unfolding less_eq_sign_def widen_sign_def by (rule join_sign_ub2)<br>  show "b ≤ a ⟹ b ≤ narrow a b"<br>    unfolding narrow_sign_def narrow_sign_td_def by simp<br>  show "b ≤ a ⟹ narrow a b ≤ a"<br>    unfolding narrow_sign_def narrow_sign_td_def by simp<br>qed<br>end |  |
| definition | `gamma_abs_sign` | 241 | definition gamma_abs_sign [simp]: "gamma (a :: sign) = gamma_sign a" |  |
| definition | `is_bot_sign` | 242 | definition is_bot_sign [simp]: "is_bot (a :: sign) = is_bottom_sign a" |  |
| definition | `is_top_sign'` | 243 | definition is_top_sign' [simp]: "is_top (a :: sign) = is_top_sign a"<br>instance proof intro_classes<br>  show "gamma (bot :: sign) = {}"<br>    unfolding bot_sign_def by simp<br>next<br>  fix a b :: sign<br>  assume H: "a ≤ b"<br>  show "gamma a ⊆ gamma b"<br>  proof -<br>    have "gamma_sign a ⊆ gamma_sign b"<br>      using H unfolding less_eq_sign_def by (rule gamma_sign_mono)<br>    then show ?thesis by simp<br>  qed<br>next<br>  fix a :: sign<br>... ### src/Analysis/Instances/Sign/Sign_Numeric_Queries.thy

> `abstract_numeric_queries` (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Numeric_Queries`) is the reusable interface; this theory supplies its Sign instance. None of the four query functions is a hand-built table any more: all four are Sign's instance of generic derivations in the same theory --- `sign_less_true`/ `sign_less_false` from `derived_less_queries` (read off `inv_less_sign`), `sign_eq_true` from `derived_eq_true_from_less` (read off `sign_less_false` in both directions), and `sign_eq_false` from `derived_eq_false_from_meet` (read off `meet_sign` collapsing to `SBot`) --- through the `sublocale` chain every `backward_domain` instance carries, with no extra proof obligation. Exhaustive case analysis over the seven-element lattice confirms each derived pair classifies exactly the pairs a hand-written table would.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `sign_less_true` | 42 | definition sign_less_true :: "sign ⇒ sign ⇒ bool" where<br>  "sign_less_true = sign_less_true_of_inv" | `sign_less_true a b` holds when every concrete pair `i ∈ gamma_sign a`, `j ∈ gamma_sign b` satisfies `i < j`; `sign_less_false a b` when every such pair satisfies `¬ i < j`. The two are not complements of each other: overlapping abstractions (e.g. `SNonPos` against `SNonNeg`) make both false, meaning unknown. `SBot` on either side makes both vacuously true. |
| definition | `sign_less_false` | 55 | definition sign_less_false :: "sign ⇒ sign ⇒ bool" where<br>  "sign_less_false = sign_less_false_of_inv" |  |
| definition | `sign_eq_true` | 94 | definition sign_eq_true :: "sign ⇒ sign ⇒ bool" where<br>  "sign_eq_true = sign_eq_true_of_less" | Only `SZero` concretizes to a singleton, so equality is provable exactly there; two abstractions are provably unequal exactly when their concretizations are disjoint. Neither table is hand-built: `sign_eq_true` is Sign's instance of `derived_eq_true_from_less` (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Numeric_Queries`), read off `sign_less_false` in both directions (integer trichotomy); `sign_eq_false` is Sign's instance of `derived_eq_false_from_meet`, read off `meet_sign` collapsing to `SBot` (disjoint concretizations). Both sublocale under `backward_domain` with no extra proof obligation, and exhaustive case analysis over the seven-element lattice confirms each classifies exactly the pairs a hand-written table would. |
| definition | `sign_eq_false` | 112 | definition sign_eq_false :: "sign ⇒ sign ⇒ bool" where<br>  "sign_eq_false = sign_eq_false_of_meet" |  |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Sign/Sign_Side_Soundness.thy

> The Sign domain provides the effectful transfer record consumed by TD_side. `mixed_etf_of_transfer` dispatches local edges to `local_edge_tree` and global edges to `unit_edge_tree`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `sign_etf` | 13 | definition sign_etf :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (unit, sign) effectful_domain_transfer" where<br>  "sign_etf gs = mixed_etf_of_transfer gs (sign_tf_for gs)" | The Sign domain provides the effectful transfer record consumed by TD_side. `mixed_etf_of_transfer` dispatches local edges to `local_edge_tree` and global edges to `unit_edge_tree`. |
| definition | `sign_etf_unit` | 62 | definition sign_etf_unit :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (unit, sign) effectful_domain_transfer" where<br>  "sign_etf_unit gs = unit_etf_of_transfer gs (sign_tf_for gs)" | Exec soundness bridges unit-shaped executable trees to this abstract unit-only effectful record. The mixed `sign_etf` is for `side_analyse_eff`. |

| definition | `assign_sign` | 10 | definition assign_sign ::<br>    "vname =&gt; aexp =&gt; (vname =&gt; sign) =&gt; (vname =&gt; sign)"<br>where<br>  "assign_sign x a σ = σ(x := aval_sign a σ)" |  |
| definition | `random_sign` | 35 | definition random_sign ::<br>    "vname =&gt; (vname =&gt; sign) =&gt; (vname =&gt; sign)"<br>where<br>  "random_sign x σ = σ(x := STop)" |  |
| definition | `enter_frame_sign_for` | 87 | definition enter_frame_sign_for ::<br>    "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; sign abs_state =&gt; sign abs_state" where<br>  "enter_frame_sign_for gs = enter_frame_D gs STop" | Entry and combine are the only fields that consult a classifier (inside `enter_frame_D` and `combine_env_abs`); assignment and guard transfer never do, so the bundled transfer function is parametric in the classifier throughout (mirroring `ivl_tf_for` for the interval domain). |

### src/Analysis/Instances/Tooling/Analysis_GraphViz.thy

> This theory provides the canonical graph model, raw-CFG builder, CFG action printers, DOT escaping helpers, and context-expanded analysis builder.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |

| definition | `prog_cfg_edges` | 16 | definition prog_cfg_edges ::<br>    "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp × edge_action × pp) list" where<br>  "prog_cfg_edges Π ps mnm main = cfg_intra_list (compile_prog Π ps mnm main)" | This theory provides the canonical graph model, raw-CFG builder, CFG action printers, DOT escaping helpers, and context-expanded analysis builder. |

| fun | `string_of_action` | 27 | fun string_of_action :: "edge_action ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_action EA_Nop = ''nop''"<br>\| "string_of_action (EA_Assign x a) = String.explode x @ '' := '' @ string_of_aexp a"<br>\| "string_of_action (EA_Random x) = String.explode x @ '' := random()''"<br>\| "string_of_action (EA_Assume b) = ''['' @ string_of_bexp b @ '']''"<br>\| "string_of_action (EA_AssumeNot b) = ''!['' @ string_of_bexp b @ '']''"<br>\| "string_of_action (EA_Ret None p) = ''return''"<br>\| "string_of_action (EA_Ret (Some e) p) =<br>    ''return '' @ string_of_aexp e"<br>\| "string_of_action (EA_Check cnd) = ''check('' @ string_of_bexp cnd @ '')''" |  |
| fun | `string_of_call_action` | 38 | fun string_of_call_action :: "call_action ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_call_action (CallEdge None fs es) =<br>    ''call('' @ concat (map string_of_aexp es) @ '')''"<br>\| "string_of_call_action (CallEdge (Some x) fs es) =<br>    String.explode x @ '' := call('' @ concat (map string_of_aexp es) @ '')''" |  |

| definition | `nl` | 45 | definition nl :: string where "nl = [CHR 0x0A]" |  |

| fun | `join_gv_nl` | 48 | fun join_gv_nl :: "string list ⇒ string" where<br>  "join_gv_nl [] = []"<br>\| "join_gv_nl [s] = s"<br>\| "join_gv_nl (s # ss) = s @ gv_nl @ join_gv_nl ss" |  |
| fun | `graphviz_label_text` | 53 | fun graphviz_label_text :: "string ⇒ string" where<br>  "graphviz_label_text [] = []"<br>\| "graphviz_label_text (ch # rest) =<br>    (if ch = CHR 0x0A then gv_nl else [ch]) @ graphviz_label_text rest" |  |
| fun | `graphviz_html_text` | 58 | fun graphviz_html_text :: "string ⇒ string" where<br>  "graphviz_html_text [] = []"<br>\| "graphviz_html_text (ch # rest) =<br>    (if ch = CHR 0x0A then ''&lt;BR ALIGN='' @ dq @ ''LEFT'' @ dq @ ''/&gt;''<br>     else if ch = CHR 0x20 then ''&amp;#160;''<br>     else if ch = CHR 0x26 then ''&amp;amp;''<br>     else if ch = CHR 0x3C then ''&amp;lt;''<br>     else if ch = CHR 0x3E then ''&amp;gt;''<br>     else [ch]) @ graphviz_html_text rest" |  |
| definition | `ensure_trailing_nl` | 78 | definition ensure_trailing_nl :: "string ⇒ string" where<br>  "ensure_trailing_nl src =<br>    (case rev src of<br>       [] ⇒ nl<br>     \| (ch # _) ⇒ if ch = CHR 0x0A then src else src @ nl)" | A line not terminated by its own `CHR 0x0A` never gets the `<BR ALIGN="LEFT"/>` `graphviz_html_text` emits for embedded newlines, and Graphviz then renders that final line centered instead of inheriting the cell's own `ALIGN="LEFT"` --- a documented HTML-label quirk, not a property of well-formed DOT. Appending exactly one trailing newline when `src` lacks one, idempotent when it already has one, gives every line including the last its own `BR` and so its own alignment. |
| definition | `source_html_label` | 84 | definition source_html_label :: "string ⇒ string" where<br>  "source_html_label src =<br>    ''&lt;&lt;TABLE BORDER='' @ dq @ ''1'' @ dq @ '' CELLBORDER='' @ dq @ ''0'' @ dq    @ '' CELLPADDING='' @ dq @ ''8'' @ dq<br>    @ ''&gt;&lt;TR&gt;&lt;TD ALIGN='' @ dq @ ''LEFT'' @ dq @ '' WIDTH='' @ dq @ ''260'' @ dq<br>    @ '' FIXEDSIZE='' @ dq @ ''FALSE'' @ dq<br>    @ ''&gt;&lt;FONT FACE='' @ dq @ ''Menlo'' @ dq @ '' POINT-SIZE='' @ dq @ ''10'' @ dq<br>    @ ''&gt;'' @ graphviz_html_text (ensure_trailing_nl src) @ ''&lt;/FONT&gt;&lt;/TD&gt;&lt;/TR&gt;&lt;/TABLE&gt;&gt;''" |  |

| datatype | `graphviz_node_annotation` | 146 | datatype graphviz_node_annotation =<br>  Node_Annotation (annotation_label: string) (annotation_style: string) | A node annotation is presentation metadata a caller attaches to one `pp`: an optional extra label line and a DOT style-attribute string that, when present, replaces this renderer's own node styling for that point. The renderer stays agnostic to what the annotation *means* --- proof status, a debugger breakpoint, anything a caller wants to overlay on the CFG --- it only owns turning `Some` into DOT attributes and `None` into the existing entry/exit/default styling. |

| definition | `check_result_annotation` | 161 | definition check_result_annotation :: "check_result ⇒ bexp ⇒ graphviz_node_annotation" where<br>  "check_result_annotation res cnd =<br>     (case res of<br>        Check_Proved ⇒<br>          Node_Annotation (''check '' @ string_of_bexp cnd)<br>            ''shape=box,style=filled,fillcolor=darkgreen,fontcolor=white''<br>      \| Check_Unknown ⇒<br>          Node_Annotation (''check '' @ string_of_bexp cnd @ '' [unknown]'')<br>            ''shape=box,style=filled,fillcolor=gray70''<br>      \| Check_Refuted ⇒<br>          Node_Annotation (''check '' @ string_of_bexp cnd @ '' [REFUTED]'')<br>            ''shape=box,style=filled,fillcolor=red,fontcolor=white'')" | Shared status-to-style mapping for a compiled `__voblint_check(...)` condition, given its executable `check_result` classification. Domain-independent (only `check_result` and `bexp`), so every domain's check-discharge example renders proof status through this one mapping instead of restating it. `Check_Proved` renders dark green, `Check_Refuted` red, `Check_Unknown` grey. |

| fun | `compiled_proc_owner` | 197 | fun compiled_proc_owner ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ nat ⇒ nat ⇒ pname option" where<br>  "compiled_proc_owner Π [] n k = None"<br>\| "compiled_proc_owner Π (p # ps) n k =<br>    (case Π p of<br>      None ⇒ compiled_proc_owner Π ps n k<br>    \| Some decl ⇒<br>        (let (n', E, K) = compile_proc Π p decl n<br>         in if n ≤ k ∧ k &lt; n' then Some p<br>            else compiled_proc_owner Π ps n' k))" |  |
| definition | `compiled_owner_of` | 208 | definition compiled_owner_of ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ pp ⇒ pname" where<br>  "compiled_owner_of Π ps mnm main p =<br>    (case p of<br>      FunctionEntry owner ⇒ owner<br>    \| FunctionResult owner ⇒ owner<br>    \| Statement k ⇒<br>        (case compiled_proc_owner Π ps 0 k of Some owner ⇒ owner \| None ⇒ mnm))" |  |

[u, v]) (cfg_intra_list g)) @<br>       concat (map (λ(call, _, entry, cont).<br>         call # entry # cont #<br>         (case entry of FunctionEntry p ⇒ [FunctionResult p] \| _ ⇒ []))<br>         (cfg_calls_list g)))" |  |

| definition | `owner_assigned_vars` | 254 | definition owner_assigned_vars ::<br>  "cfg ⇒ (pp ⇒ pname) ⇒ pname ⇒ vname list" where<br>  "owner_assigned_vars g point_owner owner =<br>    remdups<br>      (concat (map (λ(u, a, _).<br>         if point_owner u = owner then graphviz_action_defs a else [])<br>         (cfg_intra_list g)) @<br>       concat (map (λ(call, ca, _, _).<br>         if point_owner call = owner then<br>           (case ca of CallEdge None _ _ ⇒ []<br>             \| CallEdge (Some x) _ _ ⇒ [x])<br>         else []) (cfg_calls_list g)))" |  |
| definition | `compiled_procedure_scope` | 267 | definition compiled_procedure_scope ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg ⇒ pp ⇒ procedure_scope" where<br>  "compiled_procedure_scope gs Π ps mnm main g p =<br>    (let owner = compiled_owner_of Π ps mnm main p;<br>         decl = Π owner;<br>         fs = if owner = mnm then [] else<br>           (case decl of None ⇒ [] \| Some d ⇒ formals d);<br>         ret = if owner = mnm then None else Some ret_var;<br>         ls = filter (λx. x ∉ set fs ∧ x ≠ ret_var ∧ ¬ gs x)<br>           (owner_assigned_vars g (compiled_owner_of Π ps mnm main) owner)<br>     in ⦇scope_formals = fs, scope_locals = ls, scope_return_slot = ret⦈)" |  |
| definition | `compiled_domain_graph_config` | 293 | definition compiled_domain_graph_config ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ ('a::show_val ⇒ bool) ⇒<br>    (unit, unit, 'a abs_state, 'a abs_state) analysis_graph_config"<br>where<br>  "compiled_domain_graph_config gs Π ps mnm main is_top_val =<br>    ⦇ local_of = id,<br>      route = (λ_ _ _ _. ()),<br>      show_context = (λ_. ''''),<br>      locals_for_pp = (λp.<br>        let sc = compiled_procedure_scope gs Π ps mnm main (compile_prog Π ps mnm main) p<br>        in scope_formals sc @ scope_locals sc),<br>      return_slot_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_return_slot (compiled_procedure_scope gs Π ps mnm main<br>          (compile_prog Π ps mnm main) p)),<br>      globals_to_show = compiled_global_vars gs (compile_prog Π ps mnm main),<br>... (14 more lines) | The classic abs-state route: any `show_val` instance renders its compiled program the same way -- the domain enters only through `is_top_val` (skip an untouched return slot) and `show_val` (format a value); everything else reads the compiler's own scope/owner/global bookkeeping. A per-domain config is this specialized at its abstract type, with its own concrete top test. `is_top_val` is an explicit parameter, not `is_top` from `sound_domain`: code generation for one class operation materializes that class's whole dictionary, and `sound_domain`'s `gamma` has no executable code equation for every instance (an interval domain's, for instance, is a set comprehension over `int`, which is not of sort `enum`). Taking the top test as a plain function keeps this definition's code generation independent of `sound_domain` entirely. |

ContextCluster (owner_of cfg (fst pc)) (snd pc)) covered)" |  |

| definition | `analysis_intra_edges` | 370 | definition analysis_intra_edges ::<br>  "cfg ⇒ (pp × 'ctx) list<br>    ⇒ (('ctx, 'g) analysis_node × analysis_edge_kind ×<br>       ('ctx, 'g) analysis_node) list" where<br>  "analysis_intra_edges g covered =<br>    concat (map (λsrc_ctx.<br>      concat (map (λ(u, a, v).<br>        if fst src_ctx = u ∧ (v, snd src_ctx) ∈ set covered<br>        then [(LocalNode u (snd src_ctx), IntraEdge a, LocalNode v (snd src_ctx))]<br>        else []) (cfg_intra_list g))) covered)" |  |
| definition | `analysis_enter_edges` | 381 | definition analysis_enter_edges ::<br>  "('ctx, 'g, 'a, 'd) analysis_graph_config ⇒ cfg ⇒ (pp × 'ctx) list<br>    ⇒ (pp × 'ctx + 'g ⇒ 'a)<br>    ⇒ (('ctx, 'g) analysis_node × analysis_edge_kind ×<br>       ('ctx, 'g) analysis_node) list" where<br>  "analysis_enter_edges cfg g covered sol =<br>    concat (map (λsrc_ctx.<br>      concat (map (λ(u, ca, entry, _).<br>        if fst src_ctx = u then<br>          let callee_ctx = route cfg u (snd src_ctx) ca<br>              (local_of cfg (sol (Inl src_ctx)))<br>          in if (entry, callee_ctx) ∈ set covered<br>             then [(LocalNode u (snd src_ctx), EnterEdge (owner_of cfg entry) ca,<br>                    LocalNode entry callee_ctx)]<br>             else []<br>... (1 more lines) |  |
| definition | `analysis_combine_edges` | 398 | definition analysis_combine_edges ::<br>  "('ctx, 'g, 'a, 'd) analysis_graph_config ⇒ cfg ⇒ (pp × 'ctx) list<br>    ⇒ (pp × 'ctx + 'g ⇒ 'a)<br>    ⇒ (('ctx, 'g) analysis_node × analysis_edge_kind ×<br>       ('ctx, 'g) analysis_node) list" where<br>  "analysis_combine_edges cfg g covered sol =<br>    concat (map (λsrc_ctx.<br>      concat (map (λc. case c of (call, ca, entry, cont) ⇒<br>        (case entry of FunctionEntry p ⇒<br>          if fst src_ctx = call then<br>            let callee_ctx = route cfg call (snd src_ctx) ca<br>                (local_of cfg (sol (Inl src_ctx)));<br>                result = FunctionResult p<br>            in if (result, callee_ctx) ∈ set covered ∧<br>                  (cont, snd src_ctx) ∈ set covered<br>... (7 more lines) |  |
| definition | `analysis_call_to_return_edges` | 430 | definition analysis_call_to_return_edges ::<br>  "('ctx, 'g, 'a, 'd) analysis_graph_config ⇒ cfg ⇒ (pp × 'ctx) list<br>    ⇒ (('ctx, 'g) analysis_node × analysis_edge_kind ×<br>       ('ctx, 'g) analysis_node) list" where<br>  "analysis_call_to_return_edges cfg g covered =<br>    concat (map (λsrc_ctx.<br>      concat (map (λc. case c of (call, _, entry, cont) ⇒<br>        (case entry of FunctionEntry p ⇒<br>          if fst src_ctx = call ∧ (cont, snd src_ctx) ∈ set covered<br>          then [(LocalNode call (snd src_ctx), CallToReturnEdge p,<br>                 LocalNode cont (snd src_ctx))]<br>          else []<br>        \| _ ⇒ [])) (cfg_calls_list g))) covered)" | Call sites and their continuations already share a context: `cont` is the fourth component of every `calls` tuple. This edge is purely presentational -- it draws that pairing directly, alongside the real interprocedural path through `EnterEdge` and `CombineEdge`, so a call site and its return-site stay visually linked even when the callee cluster sits elsewhere in the diagram. |
| fun | `analysis_local_domain` | 444 | fun analysis_local_domain :: "((pp × 'ctx) + 'g) list ⇒ (pp × 'ctx) list" where<br>  "analysis_local_domain [] = []"<br>\| "analysis_local_domain (Inl pc # domain) = pc # analysis_local_domain domain"<br>\| "analysis_local_domain (Inr k # domain) = analysis_local_domain domain" |  |
| fun | `analysis_global_domain` | 449 | fun analysis_global_domain :: "((pp × 'ctx) + 'g) list ⇒ 'g list" where<br>  "analysis_global_domain [] = []"<br>\| "analysis_global_domain (Inl pc # domain) = analysis_global_domain domain"<br>\| "analysis_global_domain (Inr k # domain) = k # analysis_global_domain domain" |  |
| definition | `build_analysis_graph_parts` | 454 | definition build_analysis_graph_parts ::<br>  "('ctx, 'g, 'a, 'd) analysis_graph_config ⇒ cfg<br>    ⇒ (pp × 'ctx) list ⇒ 'g list ⇒ (pp × 'ctx + 'g ⇒ 'a)<br>    ⇒ ('ctx, 'g) analysis_graph" where<br>  "build_analysis_graph_parts cfg g covered global_keys sol =<br>    (let locals = covered_local_nodes covered;<br>         globals = analysis_global_nodes cfg sol global_keys;<br>         sources = analysis_source_nodes cfg;<br>         ns = locals @ globals @ sources<br>     in (analysis_context_clusters cfg covered @ analysis_global_cluster ns<br>           @ analysis_source_cluster ns,<br>         ns,<br>         analysis_intra_edges g covered @ analysis_enter_edges cfg g covered sol<br>           @ analysis_combine_edges cfg g covered sol<br>           @ analysis_call_to_return_edges cfg g covered))" |  |
| definition | `build_analysis_graph` | 473 | definition build_analysis_graph ::<br>  "('ctx, 'g, 'a, 'd) analysis_graph_config ⇒ cfg<br>    ⇒ ((pp × 'ctx) + 'g) list ⇒ (pp × 'ctx + 'g ⇒ 'a)<br>    ⇒ ('ctx, 'g) analysis_graph" where<br>  "build_analysis_graph cfg g domain sol =<br>    build_analysis_graph_parts cfg g (analysis_local_domain (remdups domain))<br>      (analysis_global_domain (remdups domain)) sol" | The graph domain is an executable presentation choice. Solver coverage remains extensional and can be checked propositionally by clients. |

| definition | `analysis_graph_to_dot` | 748 | definition analysis_graph_to_dot ::<br>  "('ctx, 'g, 'a, 'd) analysis_graph_config ⇒ cfg<br>    ⇒ (pp × 'ctx + 'g ⇒ 'a) ⇒ ('ctx, 'g) analysis_graph ⇒ string" where<br>  "analysis_graph_to_dot cfg g sol graph =<br>    (case graph of (clusters, ns, es) ⇒<br>      if analysis_graph_wf graph then<br>        ''digraph AnalysisCFG {'' @ nl<br>        @ ''  graph [rankdir=TB,newrank=true,splines=polyline,nodesep=0.5,ranksep=0.7,fontname='' @ dq @ ''Menlo'' @ dq @ ''];'' @ nl<br>        @ ''  node [shape=box,style=filled,fillcolor=lightgreen,fontname='' @ dq @ ''Menlo'' @ dq @ ''];'' @ nl<br>        @ ''  edge [fontname='' @ dq @ ''Menlo'' @ dq @ '',fontsize=10,arrowsize=0.8];'' @ nl<br>        @ concat (map (analysis_cluster_dot cfg g clusters ns es sol) clusters)<br>        @ concat (map (analysis_edge_dot cfg g ns) es)<br>        @ ''}'' @ nl<br>      else ''digraph AnalysisCFG { invalid_graph }'' @ nl)" |  |

''      '' @ line @ nl) rest))" | A deterministic textual snapshot of an `analysis_graph`, for regression fixtures: unlike `analysis_graph_to_dot`, it carries no GraphViz styling/color/shape syntax, so a fixture only regresses on the graph's actual structure and content (clusters, node labels, check verdicts, abstract states, edge roles) and not on presentation choices that `analysis_graph_to_dot`'s own DOT-specific tests already cover. Node/cluster identifiers reuse `analysis_node_id`/ `analysis_cluster_id` rather than a second numbering scheme, so a snapshot and its DOT rendering stay cross-referenceable. Ordering is the same deterministic insertion order `build_analysis_graph` already establishes (list position, not a fresh sort) --- sorting here would paper over a real ordering regression in graph construction rather than catch it. The one omission is `SourceNode`/`SourceCluster`: the embedded pretty-printed program text is presentation convenience (the `.vimp` fixture already has that text), not graph structure. |

''    '' @ analysis_node_id cfg ns n @ nl)<br>                  (analysis_nodes_in_cluster cfg c ns))) clusters')<br>        @ nl<br>        @ ''nodes:'' @ nl<br>... (6 more lines) |  |

()),<br>      show_context = (λ_. ''''),<br>      locals_for_pp = (λ_. []),<br>      return_slot_for_pp = (λ_. None),<br>      globals_to_show = [],<br>      show_local = (λ_ _ _ _. []),<br>      format_return = (λ_ _ _ _. []),<br>      show_global = (λ_ _ _. []),<br>      show_global_key = (λ_. ''''),<br>      is_shared_global = (λ_. False),<br>... (6 more lines) |  |
