| fun | `string_of_check_result` | 888 | fun string_of_check_result :: "check_result ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_check_result Check_Proved = ''PROVED''"<br>\| "string_of_check_result Check_Refuted = ''REFUTED''"<br>\| "string_of_check_result Check_Unknown = ''UNKNOWN''" | A minimal textual rendering of a `check_report_entry` list --- kept separate from `classify_checks` itself, the same separation `check_result_annotation` keeps between classification and its own GraphViz styling. One line per entry: the check's own node, its condition, and its status. |
| definition | `string_of_check_report_entry` | 893 | definition string_of_check_report_entry :: "check_report_entry ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_check_report_entry entry =<br>     (case entry of (v, cnd, res) ⇒<br>        string_of_cfg_node v @ '': '' @ string_of_bexp cnd @ ''  '' @ string_of_check_result res)" |  |
| definition | `string_of_check_report` | 898 | definition string_of_check_report :: "check_report_entry list ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_check_report report =<br>     concat (map (λentry. string_of_check_report_entry entry @ nl) report)" |  |
| definition | `check_report_node_annotation` | 913 | definition check_report_node_annotation ::<br>    "check_report_entry list ⇒ pp ⇒ graphviz_node_annotation option" where<br>  "check_report_node_annotation report v =<br>     (case find (λentry. fst entry = v) report of<br>        Some (_, cnd, res) ⇒ Some (check_result_annotation res cnd)<br>      \| None ⇒ None)" | The GraphViz counterpart of `string_of_check_report`: looks up the report entry at a queried node and, if one exists, renders it through `check_result_annotation`. This lets a caller's `node_annotation` hook consume a whole-program `classify_checks` report directly instead of restating a manually maintained `pp`-to- `bexp` table --- the report already names every checked node once. |
| definition | `check_report_html_label` | 927 | definition check_report_html_label :: "check_report_entry list ⇒ string" where<br>  "check_report_html_label report = source_html_label (string_of_check_report report)" | A standalone DOT box listing every report entry, styled through the same `source_html_label` table `raw_cfg_dot` already uses for its source box --- one more read of the same `classify_checks` report, not a second check representation. |
| definition | `check_report_dot_cluster` | 930 | definition check_report_dot_cluster :: "check_report_entry list ⇒ string" where<br>  "check_report_dot_cluster report =<br>     ''  subgraph cluster_checks {'' @ nl<br>       @ ''    label='' @ dq @ ''Checks'' @ dq @ '';'' @ nl<br>       @ ''    style=rounded; color=gray70; penwidth=1;'' @ nl<br>       @ ''    checks [shape=plain,label='' @ check_report_html_label report @ ''];'' @ nl<br>       @ ''  }'' @ nl" |  |
| definition | `insert_dot_cluster_before_close` | 944 | definition insert_dot_cluster_before_close :: "string ⇒ string ⇒ string" where<br>  "insert_dot_cluster_before_close extra dot =<br>     take (length dot - 2) dot @ extra @ drop (length dot - 2) dot" | Every `raw_cfg_dot` output ends in the digraph's own closing brace and newline, on both its well-formed and its `invalid_graph` fallback path, so splicing one more subgraph in immediately before those final two characters is a total operation over any `raw_cfg_dot` result, not a fragile parse of DOT syntax. |
| definition | `raw_cfg_dot_with_report` | 948 | definition raw_cfg_dot_with_report ::<br>    "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp ⇒ graphviz_node_annotation option)<br>     ⇒ check_report_entry list ⇒ string" where<br>  "raw_cfg_dot_with_report Π ps mnm main annotate report =<br>     insert_dot_cluster_before_close (check_report_dot_cluster report)<br>       (raw_cfg_dot Π ps mnm main annotate)" |  |
| definition | `raw_cfg_dot_with_report_lit` | 955 | definition raw_cfg_dot_with_report_lit ::<br>    "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ (pp ⇒ graphviz_node_annotation option)<br>     ⇒ check_report_entry list ⇒ String.literal" where<br>  "raw_cfg_dot_with_report_lit Π ps mnm main annotate report =<br>     String.implode (raw_cfg_dot_with_report Π ps mnm main annotate report)"<br><br>end |  |

## CFG

### src/CFG/CFG_Def.thy

> A `cfg` is an interprocedural control-flow graph over two disjoint transition relations, one per phenomenon: • `intra` --- ordinary context-preserving flow, labelled by an `edge_action` that is a total within-context store transformer. Executed by `edge_step`. • `calls` --- context-crossing calls, labelled by a `call_action` and carrying the call-site, the callee entry node, and the continuation node. Nodes are procedure-aware: `FunctionEntry p` and `FunctionResult p` are first-class nodes, not keyed side slots. There is no global exit node and no call/return matching side relation: a call edge already names its callee entry and its continuation, and a return is an ordinary intra edge into `FunctionResult p`. Calls carry incompatible typing from intra flow, so `edge_step` has no call case and `intra` can never carry a call --- by typing, not by a side condition. Translation from VIMP to CFG and the collecting/equation layers ride on this structure.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `cfg_node` | 27 | datatype cfg_node =<br>    Statement (node_stmt: nat)<br>  \| FunctionEntry (entry_proc: pname)<br>  \| FunctionResult (result_proc: pname)<br><br>instance cfg_node :: countable<br>  by countable_datatype |  |
| datatype | `edge_action` | 52 | datatype edge_action =<br>    EA_Nop<br>  \| EA_Assign   (ea_var: vname) (ea_rhs: aexp)<br>  \| EA_Random   (ea_var: vname)<br>  \| EA_Assume   (ea_cond: bexp)<br>  \| EA_AssumeNot (ea_cond: bexp)<br>  \| EA_Ret      (ea_ret_val: "aexp option") (ea_ret_proc: pname)<br>  \| EA_Check    (ea_check_cond: bexp) | `edge_action` labels intra flow. It has no call constructor: every action is a total store transformer within one activation, so an intra edge can never denote a call. A `EA_Ret e p` writes the return value into `ret_var` in the callee's own context; its graph target is `FunctionResult p` (enforced by `wf_cfg`), which is why return summarisation is ordinary predecessor folding over `FunctionResult p`. `call_action` labels call edges. `CallEdge dst formals args` records the caller destination variable, the callee's formal parameter names, and the actual arguments; the callee identity and the continuation are the two target nodes of the `calls` tuple, not payload of the action. Carrying `formals` on the edge lets the caller-side entry transfer `call_enter` bind actuals to formals without consulting a procedure table, keeping the trace kernel independent of the source declaration environment. |
| datatype | `call_action` | 61 | datatype call_action =<br>    CallEdge (ce_dst: "vname option") (ce_formals: "vname list") (ce_args: "aexp list")<br><br>instance edge_action :: countable<br>  by countable_datatype<br><br>instance call_action :: countable<br>  by countable_datatype |  |
| record | `cfg` | 79 | record cfg =<br>  intra     :: "(cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set"<br>  calls     :: "(cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set"<br>  cfg_entry :: cfg_node<br>  checks    :: "(cfg_node × bexp) set" | `calls` is a four-place relation `(call_site, act, callee_entry, continuation)`: the callee entry and the continuation are ordinary nodes, kept explicit so no matching side relation is needed. The flat (call-free) CFG is exactly the `calls = {}` fragment, so every fact quantified over `intra` is verbatim a flat-CFG fact. |
| fun | `edge_step` | 90 | fun edge_step :: "edge_action ⇒ store ⇒ store set" where<br>  "edge_step EA_Nop s = {s}"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_Assign x a) s = {s(x := aval a s)}"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_Random x) s = {s(x := v) \|v. True}"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_Assume b) s = (if bval b s then {s} else {})"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_AssumeNot b) s = (if bval b s then {} else {s})"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_Ret e p) s =<br>     {s(ret_var := (case e of None ⇒ s ret_var \| Some a ⇒ aval a s))}"<br>\| "edge_step (EA_Check c) s = {s}" | `edge_step` is the single primitive semantics of an intra action. It is defined for every constructor and has no call case; guards are the only source of `None`. |
| definition | `cfg_intra_step` | 114 | definition cfg_intra_step :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node × store ⇒ cfg_node × store ⇒ bool" where<br>  "cfg_intra_step g p q ↔<br>     (∃a. (fst p, a, fst q) ∈ intra g ∧ snd q ∈ edge_step a (snd p))" | One intra transition on a `(node, store)` pair. `intra_path` is its `star`, so reflexivity, transitivity and the induction rule come from `HOL-IMP.Star` rather than from a bespoke closure. The name avoids `intra_step`, which is the *source*-level `pstep` fragment. |
| abbreviation | `intra_path` | 118 | abbreviation intra_path :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node × store ⇒ cfg_node × store ⇒ bool" where<br>  "intra_path g ≡ star (cfg_intra_step g)" |  |
| definition | `combine_collect` | 153 | definition combine_collect :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ vname option ⇒ store ⇒ store ⇒ store" where<br>  "combine_collect gs dst s t = combine_assign dst (t ret_var) (combine_env gs s t)" | Return-value rehydration at the caller: write the callee's `ret_var` into the destination over the combined store (callee globals, caller locals). It is fixed by the call's destination `dst`, which the `CallEdge` already records --- no side lookup. |
| definition | `call_enter` | 167 | definition call_enter :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ call_action ⇒ store ⇒ store" where<br>  "call_enter gs ca s =<br>     (case ca of CallEdge dst pars actuals ⇒<br>        bind_formals pars (map (λe. aval e s) actuals) (enter_state gs s))" | Caller-side entry transfer at a call. The actuals are evaluated in the caller store, the callee locals are reset (`enter_state`, globals preserved), and the resulting values are bound to the callee formals. All payload comes from the `CallEdge`, so the transfer needs no procedure table. This is exactly the callee-entry store produced by the source `pstep` `Call` rule (see `call_enter_eq_source_call_store`). |
| definition | `intra_successors` | 185 | definition intra_successors :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node set" where<br>  "intra_successors g u = {v. ∃a. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}" |  |
| definition | `cfg_nodes` | 195 | definition cfg_nodes :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node set" where<br>  "cfg_nodes g =<br>     {u. ∃a v. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}<br>     ∪ {v. ∃u a. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}<br>     ∪ {u. ∃act ce after. (u, act, ce, after) ∈ calls g}<br>     ∪ {ce. ∃u act after. (u, act, ce, after) ∈ calls g}<br>     ∪ {after. ∃u act ce. (u, act, ce, after) ∈ calls g}<br>     ∪ {cfg_entry g}" | `cfg_nodes` is reconstructed from the endpoints of `intra`, every node occurring in `calls`, and `cfg_entry`. It is not a record field. |
| definition | `flat_cfg` | 206 | definition flat_cfg :: "cfg ⇒ bool" where<br>  "flat_cfg g ↔ calls g = {}" |  |
| definition | `wf_cfg` | 214 | definition wf_cfg :: "cfg ⇒ bool" where<br>  "wf_cfg g ↔<br>     (∀u act ce after. (u, act, ce, after) ∈ calls g ⟶ (∃p. ce = FunctionEntry p))<br>   ∧ (∀u a v. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g ⟶ (∀p. v ≠ FunctionEntry p))<br>   ∧ (∀u e p v. (u, EA_Ret e p, v) ∈ intra g ⟶ v = FunctionResult p)" | `wf_cfg` is structural only: call edges enter procedure-entry nodes; no intra edge enters a procedure-entry node (so a callee is reached only across a call); and a return action lands on the matching procedure result. No compiler-correctness or source-semantic property is included. |

### src/CFG/CFG_Prune.thy

> `cfg_succ_rel` is the derived structural dependency graph the analysis pruning and cone proofs run on --- not the concrete execution relation. It has four sources, induced by `intra g` and `calls g`: • INTRA: an ordinary edge `(u, a, v) ∈ intra g` gives `u → v` (ordinary flow). • ENTRY: a call `(c, ca, FunctionEntry p, k) ∈ calls g` gives `c → FunctionEntry p` --- the callee entry's abstract state depends on the caller state routed through `etf_enter`. • COMB_CALLER: the same call gives `c → k` --- the continuation depends on the saved caller state via `etf_combine`. This is not a concrete execution edge; execution does not skip the callee. • COMB_RESULT: the same call gives `FunctionResult p → k` --- the continuation depends on the callee's result, the second `etf_combine` argument. `c → k` and `FunctionResult p → k` are combine dependencies of the analysis, kept visibly separate from `intra g`. They are never added to `intra g`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `cfg_succ_rel` | 28 | definition cfg_succ_rel :: "cfg ⇒ (cfg_node × cfg_node) set" where<br>  "cfg_succ_rel g =<br>     {(u, v) \| u a v. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}<br>   ∪ {(c, ce) \| c ca ce k. (c, ca, ce, k) ∈ calls g}<br>   ∪ {(c, k) \| c ca ce k. (c, ca, ce, k) ∈ calls g}<br>   ∪ {(FunctionResult p, k) \| c ca p k. (c, ca, FunctionEntry p, k) ∈ calls g}" |  |
| definition | `cfg_succ` | 68 | definition cfg_succ :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ bool" where<br>  "cfg_succ g u w ↔ (u, w) ∈ cfg_succ_rel g" |  |
| definition | `cfg_reaches` | 71 | definition cfg_reaches :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ bool" where<br>  "cfg_reaches g v v0 ↔ (v, v0) ∈ (cfg_succ_rel g)^*" |  |
| definition | `cone` | 74 | definition cone :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node set" where<br>  "cone g v0 = {v. cfg_reaches g v v0}" |  |
| definition | `cfg_exit` | 158 | definition cfg_exit :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node" where<br>  "cfg_exit g = (case cfg_entry g of FunctionEntry p ⇒ FunctionResult p \| n ⇒ n)" | Whole-program completion is the entry procedure's `FunctionResult`: the compiler sets `cfg_entry (compile_prog … mnm …) = FunctionEntry mnm`, so `cfg_exit` is `FunctionResult mnm`. |

### src/CFG/CFG_Transfer.thy

> `edge_step` (from `Voblint_CFG.CFG_Def`) is the primitive intra-edge semantics. `edge_collect` is its pointwise lift to store sets --- the successors of any source store in `S` --- so the abstract set transformer is derived from the concrete step and the two cannot drift. This is the per-`intra`-edge transfer the constraint system folds over.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `edge_collect` | 12 | definition edge_collect :: "edge_action ⇒ store set ⇒ store set" where<br>  "edge_collect a S = {t. ∃s∈S. t ∈ edge_step a s}" | `edge_step` (from `Voblint_CFG.CFG_Def`) is the primitive intra-edge semantics. `edge_collect` is its pointwise lift to store sets --- the successors of any source store in `S` --- so the abstract set transformer is derived from the concrete step and the two cannot drift. This is the per-`intra`-edge transfer the constraint system folds over. |

### src/CFG/Collecting/CFG_Local_Trace.thy

> An `ltr` represents one procedure activation and its concrete ancestry. Its local path contains `(cfg_node, store)` pairs; structural constructors record the suspended caller and, after resumption, the completed callee. Activation contexts are projections of this structure rather than fields stored in the trace. Local flow follows `intra`, including `EA_Ret` into the matching `FunctionResult`. A `calls` tuple supplies the call site, callee entry, and continuation. The call rule builds the parameter-bound entry store with `call_enter`. The resume rule combines caller locals, callee globals, and the return value with `combine_collect`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `ltr` | 31 | datatype ltr =<br>    Root "(cfg_node * store) list"<br>  \| Call (ltr_caller: ltr) "(cfg_node * store) list"<br>  \| Resume (ltr_current: ltr) (ltr_callee: ltr) "(cfg_node * store) list" | • `Root p` --- the main activation, with local path `p`. • `Call caller p` --- a callee whose local path `p` starts at the callee-entry store; `caller` is the exact suspended caller, frozen at the call node. • `Resume current callee p` --- the activation continued past a completed call. `current` is that activation frozen at its call node (the value that spawned `callee`); `callee` is the retained completed callee subtree; `p` is the continued path. |
| primrec | `path` | 41 | primrec path :: "ltr ⇒ (cfg_node * store) list" where<br>  "path (Root p)       = p"<br>\| "path (Call _ p)     = p"<br>\| "path (Resume _ _ p) = p" | `path` is the activation-local control-flow path. `sink_node` and `sink_store` return its final program point and final store. |
| definition | `entry_store` | 46 | definition entry_store :: "ltr ⇒ store" where<br>  "entry_store t = snd (hd (path t))" |  |
| definition | `sink_node` | 49 | definition sink_node :: "ltr ⇒ cfg_node" where<br>  "sink_node t = fst (last (path t))" |  |
| definition | `sink_store` | 52 | definition sink_store :: "ltr ⇒ store" where<br>  "sink_store t = snd (last (path t))" |  |
| fun | `caller_of` | 59 | fun caller_of :: "ltr ⇒ ltr option" where<br>  "caller_of (Root _)             = None"<br>\| "caller_of (Call caller _)      = Some caller"<br>\| "caller_of (Resume current _ _) = caller_of current" | `caller_of` recovers the creating caller of an activation. It descends the frozen `caller` field of a `Resume`, so it works uniformly for a returned callee of any constructor --- this is what makes nested and recursive returns compose. |
| primrec | `extend` | 68 | primrec extend :: "ltr ⇒ (cfg_node * store) ⇒ ltr" where<br>  "extend (Root p) x       = Root (p @ [x])"<br>\| "extend (Call c p) x     = Call c (p @ [x])"<br>\| "extend (Resume c d p) x = Resume c d (p @ [x])" | `extend` appends one step to the innermost local path; it never touches an outer constructor's caller/callee fields. |
| fun | `key` | 82 | fun key :: "(cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c) ⇒ 'c ⇒ ltr ⇒ 'c" where<br>  "key enterc startcontext (Root _)                  = startcontext"<br>\| "key enterc startcontext (Call parent p)           =<br>     enterc (sink_node parent) (key enterc startcontext parent) (snd (hd p))"<br>\| "key enterc startcontext (Resume current callee _) = key enterc startcontext current" | The activation context is computed from the concrete trace after the fact and is activation-stable: fixed when the activation is created and unchanged by the calls it later makes and returns from. `Root` carries the seed; a `Call` routes the caller context on the call site and the callee-entry store --- `sink_node parent` is exactly the call site, since `extend` only ever appends to the callee path and leaves `parent` frozen there; a `Resume` keeps the resumed caller's own context, so a completed call does not repartition the caller. Exposing the call site to `enterc` is what makes a call-site-keyed context (a k-call-string) expressible as an instance rather than only as a generator-side hook. |
| inductive | `ctx_key` | 103 | inductive ctx_key :: "(cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c ⇒ bool) ⇒ 'c ⇒ ltr ⇒ 'c ⇒ bool"<br>  for admiss :: "cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c ⇒ bool" and startcontext :: 'c<br>where<br>  ctx_key_Root: "ctx_key admiss startcontext (Root p) startcontext"<br>\| ctx_key_Call: "ctx_key admiss startcontext parent c<br>    ⟹ admiss (sink_node parent) c (snd (hd p)) c'<br>    ⟹ ctx_key admiss startcontext (Call parent p) c'"<br>\| ctx_key_Resume: "ctx_key admiss startcontext current c<br>    ⟹ ctx_key admiss startcontext (Resume current callee p) c"<br><br>inductive_cases ctx_key_RootE: "ctx_key admiss startcontext (Root p) c"<br>inductive_cases ctx_key_CallE: "ctx_key admiss startcontext (Call parent p) c"<br>inductive_cases ctx_key_ResumeE: "ctx_key admiss startcontext (Resume current callee p) c" | `key` fixes one context per trace by decoding the entered store exactly. `ctx_key` generalizes this: the context an activation is analyzed under is any `admiss`-admissible choice, threaded through the same recursive shape `key` has --- a `Call` activation's context is admissible relative to its caller's OWN selected context, not re-derived from the concrete store independently at each node. This is what keeps a chosen context stable across a CALL/RETURN pair: the callee's context in `Resume` resumes through `current`, so a completed call cannot repartition the caller, exactly as for `key`. `admiss_exact enterc` recovers `key` exactly (`ctx_key_exact_iff`): the deterministic decode is the special case where exactly one context is admissible per call. |
| definition | `admiss_exact` | 117 | definition admiss_exact :: "(cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c) ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c ⇒ bool" where<br>  "admiss_exact enterc u c s c' ↔ c' = enterc u c s" |  |
| inductive_set | `valid_ltr` | 166 | inductive_set valid_ltr :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ ltr set" for gs and g and S where<br>  init:<br>    "s ∈ S<br>     ⟹ Root [(cfg_entry g, s)] ∈ valid_ltr gs g S"<br>\| intra:<br>    "t ∈ valid_ltr gs g S<br>     ⟹ (sink_node t, a, v) ∈ intra g<br>     ⟹ s' ∈ edge_step a (sink_store t)<br>     ⟹ extend t (v, s') ∈ valid_ltr gs g S"<br>\| call:<br>    "caller ∈ valid_ltr gs g S<br>     ⟹ (sink_node caller, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>     ⟹ Call caller [(FunctionEntry p, call_enter gs (CallEdge dst pars args) (sink_store caller))]<br>         ∈ valid_ltr gs g S"<br>\| ret:<br>... (19 more lines) | `valid_ltr` is the least set closed under four concrete operations: an initial main activation at `cfg_entry`; an `intra` step; a call; and a return. Each rule reads exactly the relation for its phenomenon. `intra` carries no side condition --- calls are not `intra` members, so they are untraversable by typing. `call` enters the callee named by the `calls` edge at the callee-entry store `enter_state`. `ret` matches the callee's `FunctionResult p` against the `FunctionEntry p` of a concrete `calls` edge leaving the caller's node, and resumes at the continuation stored in that same edge; the resumed state is `combine_collect`. There is no `combines` lookup and no scan for a compatible context. |
| definition | `call_enter_store` | 695 | definition call_enter_store :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ store ⇒ store ⇒ bool" where<br>  "call_enter_store gs g c s t ↔<br>     (∃dst pars args p cont. (c, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>        ∧ t = call_enter gs (CallEdge dst pars args) s)" |  |
| definition | `activation_collect` | 990 | definition activation_collect ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c ⇒ bool) ⇒ 'c<br>     ⇒ cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store set" where<br>  "activation_collect gs admiss startcontext g S v c =<br>     {sink_store t \| t. t ∈ valid_ltr gs g S ∧ sink_node t = v ∧ ctx_key admiss startcontext t c}" | The activation-sensitive collecting is the sink stores of valid traces reaching `v` admissibly assigned the queried context `c`, via `ctx_key`. Exact activation matching --- one context per trace, computed by decoding the concrete entered store --- is the `admiss = admiss_exact enterc` instance; an `admiss` that ignores its concrete-store argument (for instance, one derived from an abstract routing result) instead lets one admissible context stand for every concrete execution the abstract analysis represents by it, without needing `admiss` itself to be multi-valued. |

### src/CFG/Collecting/LTR_Abstract.thy

> The smallest domain-free proof interface through which an abstract over-approximation soundly covers the stack-faithful semantics `valid_ltr`. The abstract object is a context-indexed concretization • `acc :: cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store set` --- the set of stores the analysis admits at node `v` in abstract context `c`. No `sound_domain`, `abs_state`, solver, or DG dependency appears here. The interface fixes five closure obligations --- a root seed (`ROOT`), an intra edge (`EDGE`), totality of context selection (`ADMISS_TOTAL`), a call routing (`CALL`), and a return combine (`COMB`) --- and proves that every valid trace's sink store lands in every activation slot it is admissibly assigned to. The load-bearing clause is `COMB`: the callee is read at whichever context `c2` the SAME `admiss` fact used to justify the callee's own admissibility relates to the caller's context `c1` --- so a return can only compose the callee whose context was chosen from the caller it resumes, not any context that happens to cover the callee's concrete entry. The correlation is not an extra parameter: `ctx_key_entry_invariant_iff` makes this a theorem of `valid_ltr`, so a proof-level activation record is unnecessary. `admiss u c s c'` means: given a call from `u` under caller context `c` entering at concrete store `s`, `c'` is an admissible context for the callee. This generalizes a deterministic `enterc` (`admiss_exact`, `Voblint_CFG.CFG_Local_Trace`) to a relation: several `c'` may be admissible for the same call, which is what lets an abstract analysis choose a WIDE context (e.g. covering every concrete argument to `random()`) rather than being forced to compute one exactly per concrete store. The abstract context type `'c` is arbitrary; `ctx_key` may quotient distinct activations to the same context, and several contexts may be admissible for one activation. Nothing here claims a context is an exact activation identity --- only that each valid trace lands in every admissible slot, matched at return via the SAME `admiss` derivation, not rediscovered afterward.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| locale | `ltr_gamma` | 42 | locale ltr_gamma =<br>  fixes g :: cfg and S :: "store set"<br>    and acc :: "cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store set"<br>    and admiss :: "cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c ⇒ bool"<br>    and startcontext :: 'c<br>    and gs :: "vname ⇒ bool"<br>  assumes ROOT[intro]: "⋀s. s ∈ S ⟹ s ∈ acc (cfg_entry g) startcontext"<br>    and EDGE[intro]: "⋀u a v c s s'. (u, a, v) ∈ intra g<br>        ⟹ s ∈ acc u c ⟹ s' ∈ edge_step a s ⟹ s' ∈ acc v c"<br>    and ADMISS_TOTAL: "⋀u c s. ∃c'. admiss u c s c'"<br>    and CALL[intro]: "⋀u dst pars args p cont c s c'.<br>        (u, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>        ⟹ s ∈ acc u c ⟹ admiss u c (call_enter gs (CallEdge dst pars args) s) c'<br>        ⟹ call_enter gs (CallEdge dst pars args) s ∈ acc (FunctionEntry p) c'"<br>    and COMB[intro]: "⋀cl dst pars args p cont c1 c2 s t es.<br>... (5 more lines) |  |
| abbreviation | `bnd` | 65 | abbreviation bnd :: "ltr ⇒ bool" where<br>  "bnd u ≡ ∀c. ctx_key admiss startcontext u c ⟶ sink_store u ∈ acc (sink_node u) c" | `bnd u`: the sink store of `u` is admitted at `u`'s own node under every context `admiss`/`ctx_key` admissibly assigns to `u`. Vacuously true at any non-admissible `c`. |
| definition | `gamma_ltr` | 68 | definition gamma_ltr :: "ltr set" where<br>  "gamma_ltr = {t. bnd t}" |  |

### src/CFG/Collecting/LTR_Collect.thy

> The stack-faithful concrete carrier of a procedure-aware CFG is the local-trace set `valid_ltr`. This theory presents it as a least fixed point of an explicit monotone set transformer `ltr_F`, defines the forgetful projection of a trace set to a CFG node (`ltr_collect`, the concrete collector), and proves the collecting equations, the context-sensitive/insensitive bridge to `activation_collect`. `ltr_F` has exactly the four clauses of `valid_ltr` (root, intra step, call, return), each reading exactly the relation for its phenomenon: `intra` for local extension, `calls` for entering a callee and for recovering a continuation. The return clause mirrors `valid_ltr.ret` literally --- it recovers the caller from the completed callee's own ancestry through `caller_of`, never by an independent choice, and matches the callee's `FunctionResult p` against a concrete `calls` edge. There is no global exit node: whole-program completion is collection at `FunctionResult main`, and a procedure result is an ordinary collected node, not a separate summary mechanism. No solver, DG, or abstract-domain theory is imported.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `ltr_F` | 28 | definition ltr_F :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ ltr set ⇒ ltr set" where<br>  "ltr_F gs g S T =<br>      {Root [(cfg_entry g, s)] \| s. s ∈ S}<br>    ∪ {extend t (v, s') \| t a v s'.<br>          t ∈ T ∧ (sink_node t, a, v) ∈ intra g ∧ s' ∈ edge_step a (sink_store t)}<br>    ∪ {Call caller [(FunctionEntry p, call_enter gs (CallEdge dst pars args) (sink_store caller))]<br>          \| caller dst pars args p cont.<br>          caller ∈ T ∧ (sink_node caller, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g}<br>    ∪ {Resume caller callee<br>          (path caller @ [(cont, combine_collect gs dst (sink_store caller) (sink_store callee))])<br>          \| callee caller p dst pars args cont.<br>          callee ∈ T ∧ caller_of callee = Some caller<br>          ∧ sink_node callee = FunctionResult p<br>          ∧ (sink_node caller, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g}" |  |
| definition | `ltr_collect` | 170 | definition ltr_collect :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ store set" where<br>  "ltr_collect gs g S v =<br>     {sink_store t \| t. t ∈ valid_ltr gs g S ∧ sink_node t = v}" | `ltr_collect` is the concrete collecting view: the sink stores of valid traces reaching node `v`. `activation_collect` (`CFG_Local_Trace`) is the context-indexed collector, filtering those by the structural activation key; the key type is not required finite, so a keyed bucket is not claimed to be an exact activation identity. |
| definition | `collect_result` | 176 | definition collect_result :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ pname ⇒ store set" where<br>  "collect_result gs g S p = ltr_collect gs g S (FunctionResult p)" | `collect_result` is a convenience view --- collection at a procedure result --- not a distinct mechanism. Whole-program completion is `collect_result gs g S main`. |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Compile_Invariants.thy

> The compiler preserves procedure boundaries, separates call edges from ordinary control flow, and allocates disjoint statement ranges. These properties connect source commands to activation-local traces without exposing compiler counters to semantic clients.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `wf_compile_input` | 21 | definition wf_compile_input ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; proc_table =&gt; pname list =&gt; pname =&gt; com =&gt; bool" where<br>  "wf_compile_input gs Π ps mnm main ↔<br>     distinct ps ∧<br>     set ps = {p. Π p ~= None} - {mnm} ∧<br>     mnm ∉ set ps ∧<br>     wf_source_program gs Π mnm main"<br><br>declare wf_compile_input_def [wf_compile_input_simps] | `mnm` names the distinguished entry procedure, declared in `Π` with an empty formal list and body `main`. The callee list `ps` enumerates the other declared procedures (`dom Π` minus `mnm`), so `FunctionEntry mnm` / `FunctionResult mnm` never collide with a callee's nodes, yet `FunctionEntry mnm` is an ordinary `proc_activation Π mnm main` activation. |
| definition | `wf_program_compile_input` | 36 | definition wf_program_compile_input :: "imp_prog =&gt; bool" where<br>  "wf_program_compile_input p ↔<br>    wf_compile_input (storage_global p prog_main_name) (prog_table p)<br>      (prog_procs p) prog_main_name (prog_main p)" |  |
| definition | `compile_program` | 47 | definition compile_program :: "imp_prog =&gt; cfg" where<br>  "compile_program p =<br>    compile_prog (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) prog_main_name (prog_main p)" |  |
| definition | `prog_cfg` | 64 | definition prog_cfg :: "pname =&gt; imp_prog =&gt; cfg" where<br>  "prog_cfg mnm p = compile_prog (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) mnm (prog_main p)" | `compile_prog` fixes the compiler input to `proc_table` / `pname list` / `com` separately, so every domain that only has an `imp_prog` in hand needs the same three-projection wrapper `compile_program` already provides at `prog_main_name`. The general form below (an arbitrary entry-procedure name, not just `prog_main_name`) is the constant every check-report/executable layer (Sign, Interval, Parity) actually calls; every call site in this project instantiates `mnm` at `prog_main_name` in practice, but the parameter still matches `compile_prog`'s own shape rather than hard-coding that choice into the compilation step itself. |
| fun | `returns_in` | 102 | fun returns_in :: "aexp option ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "returns_in e (Return e') = (e = e')"<br>\| "returns_in e (Seq c1 c2) = (returns_in e c1 ∨ returns_in e c2)"<br>\| "returns_in e (If _ c1 c2) = (returns_in e c1 ∨ returns_in e c2)"<br>\| "returns_in e (While _ c) = returns_in e c"<br>\| "returns_in e _ = False" |  |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Compile_Locality.thy

> An activation's own local `path` stays inside one compiled procedure fragment. A *fragment* of procedure `r` compiled over `[m, m')` is `FunctionEntry r`, `FunctionResult r`, and the `Statement` nodes in that counter range. Every intra edge keeps both endpoints in one fragment, every call edge keeps its source and continuation there (the callee `FunctionEntry` is the only cross-fragment endpoint), and distinct fragments are node-disjoint, so a single activation entered at `FunctionEntry p` reaches `FunctionResult q` only for `p = q`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `pfn` | 17 | definition pfn :: "pname ⇒ nat ⇒ nat ⇒ cfg_node set" where<br>  "pfn r m m' = insert (FunctionEntry r) (insert (FunctionResult r) (Statement ` {m..&lt;m'}))" | An activation's own local `path` stays inside one compiled procedure fragment. A *fragment* of procedure `r` compiled over `[m, m')` is `FunctionEntry r`, `FunctionResult r`, and the `Statement` nodes in that counter range. Every intra edge keeps both endpoints in one fragment, every call edge keeps its source and continuation there (the callee `FunctionEntry` is the only cross-fragment endpoint), and distinct fragments are node-disjoint, so a single activation entered at `FunctionEntry p` reaches `FunctionResult q` only for `p = q`. |
| definition | `frag_ok` | 1332 | definition frag_ok :: "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ ltr ⇒ bool" where<br>  "frag_ok Π ps mnm main u ↔<br>     (∃r d m m' Ep Kp. Π r = Some d<br>        ∧ compile_proc Π r d m = (m', Ep, Kp)<br>        ∧ (FunctionEntry r, EA_Nop, Statement m) ∈ intra (compile_prog Π ps mnm main)<br>        ∧ fst (hd (path u)) = FunctionEntry r<br>        ∧ (∀nd ∈ fst ` set (path u). nd ∈ pfn r m m'))<br>   ∨ (∃p s. path u = [(FunctionEntry p, s)] ∧ Π p = None)" | `frag_ok u`: the activation `u` either lies wholly inside a genuine procedure fragment (declared body, entry wiring, all path nodes in the fragment) or is a single-node activation stuck at an undeclared `FunctionEntry` --- which can never advance and never reaches a `FunctionResult`. |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Control_Residual.thy

> `control_at Π p c k n residual v` locates a runtime residual at node `v` inside the fragment compiled from command `c` of procedure `p` at base counter `n` with continuation `k`. The relation describes control only; the simulation states store agreement separately. The continuation is the node a completed fragment occupies. A command that has run to `SKIP` is therefore located at `k` itself rather than at a private exit node, which is why the `Done` rules below name no fragment node. A call crosses procedure fragments. The caller fragment records its call site and continuation, while the callee fragment locates the callee body. The activation stack of `cstep` connects both locations.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| inductive | `control_at` | 41 | inductive control_at ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ nat ⇒ com ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ bool"<br>  for Π :: proc_table and p :: pname<br>where<br>  Skip:<br>    "control_at Π p SKIP k n SKIP (Statement n)"<br>\| Assign:<br>    "control_at Π p (Assign x a) k n (Assign x a) (Statement n)"<br>\| AssignDone:<br>    "control_at Π p (Assign x a) k n SKIP k"<br>\| Random:<br>    "control_at Π p (Random x) k n (Random x) (Statement n)"<br>\| RandomDone:<br>    "control_at Π p (Random x) k n SKIP k"<br>\| Check:<br>... (37 more lines) | Residual coverage. For each source form the located clauses fix which node the residual occupies; runtime-only forms are handled as follows. • `SKIP`, assignment, sequence (before and after left completion), conditionals, loops, call site, entered continuation, and explicit `Return` all map to a compiled node or to the continuation. • `Restore` and `Unwind` are runtime-only activation markers, not located here: once a `Return` fires the CFG control is already at `FunctionResult p` (through the `EA_Ret` edge) and the remaining activation return is discharged by the located executor `cstep`, not by a fragment node. |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Control_Simulation.thy

> The compiled execution `cstep` simulates the source small-step `pstep`. Both run an activation stack: a source frame carries the caller store and destination, a `cframe` additionally records the CFG continuation node. The simulation relation is direct --- literal store equality, `control_at` locating the active residual at the CFG node, and `frames_match` tying the two stacks one-for-one. This layer proves the static *compiler-location* facts: a located residual whose head is a base command emits exactly the CFG edge the compiler generated for it, and the head's source step re-locates the successor residual. The intra, call, and return step simulations are built on these.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| inductive | `intra_step` | 511 | inductive intra_step ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ com × store × frame list ⇒ com × store × frame list ⇒ bool" for Π where<br>  IAssign: "intra_step Π (Assign x a, s, frs) (SKIP, s(x := aval a s), frs)"<br>\| IRandom: "intra_step Π (Random x, s, frs) (SKIP, s(x := v), frs)"<br>\| ICheck:  "intra_step Π (VIMP_Proc.com.Check b, s, frs) (SKIP, s, frs)"<br>\| ISeq1:   "intra_step Π (Seq SKIP c2, s, frs) (c2, s, frs)"<br>\| ISeq2:   "intra_step Π (c1, s, frs) (c1', s', frs) ⟹<br>            intra_step Π (Seq c1 c2, s, frs) (Seq c1' c2, s', frs)"<br>\| IIfTrue: "bval b s ⟹ intra_step Π (If b c1 c2, s, frs) (c1, s, frs)"<br>\| IIfFalse:"¬ bval b s ⟹ intra_step Π (If b c1 c2, s, frs) (c2, s, frs)"<br>\| IWhile:  "intra_step Π (While b c, s, frs) (If b (Seq c (While b c)) SKIP, s, frs)"<br><br>inductive_cases intra_SkipE:   "intra_step Π (SKIP, s, frs) y"<br>inductive_cases intra_AssignE: "intra_step Π (Assign x a, s, frs) y"<br>inductive_cases intra_RandomE: "intra_step Π (Random x, s, frs) y"<br>... (6 more lines) | `intra_step` is the fragment of `pstep` that stays inside one activation without initiating a return: assignment, sequencing (head execution and head completion), both conditionals, and the loop unfolding. It excludes `Call` (pushes a frame), `Return` (produces `Unwind`), and the runtime-only `Restore` / `Unwind` steps. Every intra step preserves the frame stack and keeps the residual source-shaped. |
| definition | `ret_store` | 847 | definition ret_store :: "aexp option ⇒ store ⇒ store" where<br>  "ret_store e s = s(ret_var := (case e of None ⇒ s ret_var \| Some a ⇒ aval a s))" | The store published by a return: the callee's `ret_var` is set to the evaluated return value (`Some e`) or left untouched (`None`). It is the common store reached by both the source `Return` step and the compiled `EA_Ret` edge. |
| definition | `proc_activation` | 885 | definition proc_activation :: "proc_table ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "proc_activation Π p c0 ↔ (∃decl. Π p = Some decl ∧ c0 = body decl)" | `proc_activation Π p c0` pins an activation's fragment origin `c0` to a genuine procedure body: `p` is declared in `Π` and `c0` is exactly its body. `csim` carries it on every activation so that preservation is stated only over real procedures --- `control_at`'s fragment parameter `c0` is otherwise free and would admit spurious (non-procedure) activations for which ordinary fall-through preservation is false. This is the *only* thing added to `csim`; all compile tuples, edge inclusions, and graph wiring stay in `procs_compiled`. |
| fun | `seq_after` | 908 | fun seq_after :: "com ⇒ com list ⇒ com" where<br>  "seq_after c [] = c"<br>\| "seq_after c (a # as) = seq_after (Seq c a) as" | A call site's caller has, after the call, a finite left-spine of residual continuations `afters`: the source commands sequenced after the call, in execution order, collected while descending the current activation's left-associated `Seq` spine. `seq_after c afters` wraps them around the active head `c`, appending each in order: `seq_after c [s3, s4] = Seq (Seq c s3) s4`. A *tail-position* call (the call is the caller's last command, including the top-level entry call) has no continuation --- `afters = []` --- and the caller resumes at bare `c`; a call with pending commands carries them in `afters` and the caller resumes at `seq_after SKIP afters`. Every case runs through the same activation machinery (the frame is pushed and popped identically); only the caller's leftover source differs. The continuation list is orthogonal to `csim`'s recursive nesting: `afters` holds pending commands *inside one activation*, while recursive nesting holds suspended *caller activations*. |
| fun | `unwinding` | 1172 | fun unwinding :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "unwinding Unwind = True"<br>\| "unwinding (Seq u c2) = (unwinding u ∧ c2 ≠ Restore)"<br>\| "unwinding _ = False" | Once a callee finishes, the CFG is already at `FunctionResult p` while the source propagates toward the frame-pop rule. `unwinding` describes an `Unwind` travelling up through the callee's residual, dropping the dead code it skips (never the activation's own `Restore`); `pop_ready` describes the whole activation body in that phase --- either the normal fall-through `Restore` or an `Unwind` spine `Seq u Restore` from an explicit `Return`. Both pop the single caller frame with one `cstep` return; `unwinding` propagation is matched by *no* CFG step (the CFG waits at `FunctionResult p`). |
| fun | `pop_ready` | 1177 | fun pop_ready :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "pop_ready Restore = True"<br>\| "pop_ready (Seq u Restore) = unwinding u"<br>\| "pop_ready _ = False" |  |
| definition | `compiled_at` | 1207 | definition compiled_at ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ cfg ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ nat ⇒ bool" where<br>  "compiled_at Π g p c0 k n ↔<br>     (∃n' en E K. compile Π p c0 k n = (n', en, E, K)<br>        ∧ E ⊆ intra g ∧ K ⊆ calls g<br>        ∧ (falls_through c0 ⟶ (k, EA_Ret None p, FunctionResult p) ∈ intra g))" | `compiled_at Pi g p c0 k n` witnesses that the fragment `c0`, compiled at the exact offset `n` with continuation `k`, is embedded in the target graph `g`: its intra edges lie in `intra g` and its call edges in `calls g`. `csim` carries it on every activation at the *same* `k` and `n` that `control_at` uses, so the located node and the fragment's edges are genuinely present in `g`. Compilation is shift-parametric --- neither the offset nor the continuation is fixed by the syntax --- so graph membership at the chosen offset is the missing fact, and this predicate supplies it. The continuation of a procedure body is its epilogue node, which is why the fall-through return edge is stated from `k`. That edge exists only when the body can actually fall through to it, so the requirement is guarded by `falls_through`; `control_at_SKIP_imp_falls_through` discharges the guard wherever a completed (`SKIP`) residual needs the edge. |
| inductive | `csim` | 1273 | inductive csim :: "proc_table ⇒ cfg ⇒ com × store × frame list<br>                    ⇒ cfg_node × store × cframe list ⇒ bool" for Π g where<br>  Base:<br>    "control_at Π p c0 k n c v ⟹ compiled_at Π g p c0 k n ⟹ proc_activation Π p c0 ⟹<br>     csim Π g (c, s, []) (v, s, [])"<br>\| Nested:<br>    "csim Π g (inner, s, frs) (v, s, stk) ⟹<br>     control_at Π pc c0c kc nc (seq_after SKIP afters) cont ⟹<br>     compiled_at Π g pc c0c kc nc ⟹<br>     proc_activation Π pc c0c ⟹<br>     csim Π g (seq_after (Seq inner Restore) afters, s, frs @ [Frame caller dst])<br>              (v, s, stk @ [(cont, dst, caller)])"<br>\| Returning:<br>    "pop_ready w ⟹<br>     control_at Π pc c0c kc nc (seq_after SKIP afters) cont ⟹<br>... (6 more lines) | `csim` relates a source configuration `(c, s, frs)` to a CFG configuration `(v, s, stk)` with literal store equality (the same `s`). It bridges the two continuation representations: the source keeps every suspended caller's continuation nested inside the single command `c` (defunctionalized as `Seq (Seq inner Restore) after` wrappings, one per activation), while the CFG keeps them as continuation nodes on the frame stack. The nesting runs outermost-first: after `main` calls `f` calls `g`, the command is `Seq (Seq (Seq (Seq C Restore) B) Restore) A` with `A` the outermost (main's) continuation and `C` the active (g's) residual. So the *top* command layer pairs with the *last* (outermost) frame, and the active node/store thread unchanged down to the `Base` case. • `Base` --- one activation: the active residual `c` is located at `v` and the frame stacks are empty. • `Nested` --- peel the outermost caller: the command's top layer `Seq (Seq inner Restore) after` and the last frame `Frame caller dst` pair with the CFG's last frame `(cont, dst, caller)`; the caller's post-call residual `Seq SKIP after` is located at the continuation node `cont`; and the remaining inner structure is related recursively. `csim` has three constructors --- one per source phase. `Base` and `Nested` cover *ordinary* execution (residuals located by `control_at` at `Statement` nodes). `Returning` covers the *return-in-progress* phase: once a callee's body has completed (fall-through), the innermost source layer is `Seq Restore after` and the CFG sits at `FunctionResult p`, one `cstep` return away from resuming the caller. Like `Base` it is an innermost constructor (an alternative to it), wrapped by any number of outer `Nested` layers. It carries exactly the *one* frame the return pops (the immediate caller's save); the outer callers' frames are consumed by the wrapping `Nested` layers. Its premise locates the *resumed caller* --- the ordinary residual `Seq SKIP after` the return will land in, at the continuation node `cont` --- so return completion pops the single frame and the caller resumes as a `Base` activation (the outer `Nested` wrapping is untouched). The active store is a free rider (`control_at` is store-independent). |
| fun | `is_returning` | 1368 | fun is_returning :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_returning Restore = True"<br>\| "is_returning Unwind = True"<br>\| "is_returning (Seq c1 c2) = is_returning c1"<br>\| "is_returning _ = False" | `is_returning c` holds when the leftmost-innermost of `c` is `Restore` or `Unwind`: the shape of every returning-phase command --- normal fall-through or an explicit-return `Unwind` spine, ordinary or `Nested`-wrapped. |
| fun | `head_call` | 1386 | fun head_call :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "head_call (Call dst q actuals) = True"<br>\| "head_call (Seq c1 c2) = head_call c1"<br>\| "head_call _ = False" | `head_call c` holds when the leftmost-innermost of `c` is a `Call`: the shape of an active residual whose next `pstep` fires the call. Like `is_returning` it descends the leftmost `Seq` spine and so is insensitive to `seq_after` wrapping and to whether the activation is a `Base` or a `Nested` inner. |
| fun | `head_return` | 1428 | fun head_return :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "head_return (Return e) = True"<br>\| "head_return (Seq c1 c2) = head_return c1"<br>\| "head_return _ = False" | `head_return c` holds when the leftmost-innermost of `c` is a source `Return`: the shape whose next `pstep` initiates a return (`Return` `-->` `Unwind`). Like the other head classifiers it descends the leftmost `Seq` spine. |
| fun | `ret_guarded` | 1467 | fun ret_guarded :: "bool ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "ret_guarded rok SKIP = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (Assign x a) = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (Random x) = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (VIMP_Proc.com.Check b) = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (Seq c1 c2) =<br>     (if c2 = Restore then ret_guarded True c1<br>      else ret_guarded rok c1 ∧ ret_guarded rok c2)"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (If b c1 c2) = (ret_guarded rok c1 ∧ ret_guarded rok c2)"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (While b c) = ret_guarded rok c"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (Call dst p actuals) = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok (Return e) = rok"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok Restore = True"<br>\| "ret_guarded rok Unwind = rok" | `ret_guarded rok c` is the deep source-only well-formedness check: an explicit `Return` (or an in-flight `Unwind`) is admissible only where `rok` holds --- inside a called activation. Permission is *granted by the activation tail*: the right child `Restore` of a `Seq` marks a callee body, so its left child is checked with `rok = True`, whereas ordinary continuations inherit the ambient `rok`. A bare `Restore` (the transient tail after the callee body reduces to `SKIP`) is always admissible. Crucially this looks *behind* `Restore` boundaries: a `Return` hidden in a base-activation continuation is rejected, which a head-only check misses --- exactly the configuration a frame pop would later surface at the base. |
| definition | `source_wf` | 1496 | definition source_wf :: "com × store × frame list ⇒ bool" where<br>  "source_wf cfg ↔ (case cfg of (c, s, frs) ⇒ ret_guarded False c)" | The runtime invariant: the active command is `ret_guarded` at the base activation (returns forbidden until a call opens one). Unlike a head-only guard this is preserved by `pstep`, because it already forbids the base-activation returns that a frame pop would expose. |
| definition | `procs_compiled` | 1920 | definition procs_compiled :: "proc_table ⇒ cfg ⇒ bool" where<br>  "procs_compiled Π g ↔<br>     (∀p decl. Π p = Some decl ⟶<br>        (∃k n n' en E K.<br>           compile Π p (body decl) k n = (n', en, E, K)<br>         ∧ E ⊆ intra g ∧ K ⊆ calls g<br>         ∧ (FunctionEntry p, EA_Nop, en) ∈ intra g<br>         ∧ (falls_through (body decl) ⟶<br>              (k, EA_Ret None p, FunctionResult p) ∈ intra g)<br>         ∧ source_com (body decl)))" | `procs_compiled Π g` is the static compiler-correctness certificate for the target graph: every declared procedure's body is compiled into `g`, with its intra/call edges included and the entry/exit wiring present (`FunctionEntry p` `--EA_Nop-->` body entry, and body exit `--EA_Ret-->` `FunctionResult p`). It is kept *separate* from `csim` (which describes only the dynamic configuration correspondence): the returning phase needs none of it, while the ordinary and call phases receive it as a single static premise and read off the concrete facts through the projection lemmas below. |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Located_Exec.thy

> `cstep` is the concrete execution of the two-relation procedure-aware CFG, in the activation-stack shape that mirrors the source `pstep` and drives the located simulation. A `cconf` pairs the current node with a store and a stack of pending activations; each `cframe` records the continuation node, the caller destination, and the saved caller store --- exactly the payload the resume transfer `combine_collect` needs. There are three transitions, one per graph phenomenon: • intra flow follows an `intra` edge and applies `edge_step` (covering `EA_Nop`, assignment, both assume forms, and `EA_Ret` into `FunctionResult`); the stack is unchanged; • a call follows a `calls` edge, applies the caller-side `call_enter`, moves to the callee `FunctionEntry`, and pushes one activation carrying the continuation; • a return/resume fires at `FunctionResult`, pops the top activation, and lands at its recorded continuation with the combined store `combine_collect`. A return does not use a `FunctionResult p --> cont` intra edge. The activation stack supplies the continuation, so one `FunctionResult` node serves every caller and the stack contains one entry per call.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `cframe` | 29 | type_synonym cframe = "cfg_node × vname option × store" | `cstep` is the concrete execution of the two-relation procedure-aware CFG, in the activation-stack shape that mirrors the source `pstep` and drives the located simulation. A `cconf` pairs the current node with a store and a stack of pending activations; each `cframe` records the continuation node, the caller destination, and the saved caller store --- exactly the payload the resume transfer `combine_collect` needs. There are three transitions, one per graph phenomenon: • intra flow follows an `intra` edge and applies `edge_step` (covering `EA_Nop`, assignment, both assume forms, and `EA_Ret` into `FunctionResult`); the stack is unchanged; • a call follows a `calls` edge, applies the caller-side `call_enter`, moves to the callee `FunctionEntry`, and pushes one activation carrying the continuation; • a return/resume fires at `FunctionResult`, pops the top activation, and lands at its recorded continuation with the combined store `combine_collect`. A return does not use a `FunctionResult p --> cont` intra edge. The activation stack supplies the continuation, so one `FunctionResult` node serves every caller and the stack contains one entry per call. |
| type_synonym | `cconf` | 30 | type_synonym cconf = "cfg_node × store × cframe list" |  |
| inductive | `cstep` | 32 | inductive cstep :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ cconf ⇒ cconf ⇒ bool" for gs and g where<br>  Intra:<br>    "(u, a, v) ∈ intra g ⟹ s' ∈ edge_step a s ⟹<br>     cstep gs g (u, s, stk) (v, s', stk)"<br>\| Call:<br>    "(u, CallEdge dst pars actuals, FunctionEntry q, cont) ∈ calls g ⟹<br>     cstep gs g (u, s, stk)<br>       (FunctionEntry q, call_enter gs (CallEdge dst pars actuals) s, (cont, dst, s) # stk)"<br>\| Return:<br>    "cstep gs g (FunctionResult q, t, (cont, dst, caller) # stk)<br>       (cont, combine_collect gs dst caller t, stk)" |  |
| fun | `act_frames` | 91 | fun act_frames :: "frame list ⇒ (store × vname option) list" where<br>  "act_frames [] = []"<br>\| "act_frames (Frame s d # frs) = (s, d) # act_frames frs" | Both stacks record procedure activations. `act_frames` removes the CFG-only continuation component and retains the caller store and destination used by the source. |
| definition | `cframe_act` | 95 | definition cframe_act :: "cframe ⇒ store × vname option" where<br>  "cframe_act cf = (case cf of (cont, d, s) ⇒ (s, d))" |  |
| definition | `frames_match` | 101 | definition frames_match :: "frame list ⇒ cframe list ⇒ bool" where<br>  "frames_match frs stk = (act_frames frs = map cframe_act stk)" | `frames_match` ties a source frame stack to a CFG activation stack: the activation frames agree, in order, on caller store and destination (the continuation node is the CFG's own bookkeeping and is not visible to the source). |

### src/CFG/Compiler/Located_LTR.thy

> The stack-faithful bridge from compiled source execution to the canonical activation-local semantics `valid_ltr`. A CFG-located configuration `cconf` (advanced one edge at a time by `cstep`) is related to a valid activation-local trace: the current activation is the trace, and the runtime `cframe` stack is its `caller_of` chain. Each `cstep` rule maps one-to-one onto a `valid_ltr` constructor (`intra` / `call` / `ret`), so a source run --- simulated into a `cstep` run by `csim` --- extends the accumulated trace.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| inductive | `stack_repr` | 29 | inductive stack_repr :: "cfg ⇒ cframe list ⇒ ltr ⇒ bool" for g where<br>  empty: "caller_of t = None ⟹ stack_repr g [] t"<br>\| frame: "caller_of t = Some c ⟹ sink_store c = caller<br>          ⟹ fst (hd (path t)) = FunctionEntry p<br>          ⟹ (sink_node c, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>          ⟹ stack_repr g stk c<br>          ⟹ stack_repr g ((cont, dst, caller) # stk) t" | `stack_repr` walks `caller_of` in lockstep with the runtime frame list: each `cframe` `(cont, dst, caller)` pins the caller activation `c` --- its frozen store `sink_store c = caller` --- and records the concrete `calls` edge that spawned the child, whose callee `FunctionEntry p` equals the child's (path-invariant) entry node. |
| definition | `ltr_repr` | 39 | definition ltr_repr :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ cconf ⇒ ltr ⇒ bool" where<br>  "ltr_repr gs g S cf t = (case cf of (v, s, stk) ⇒<br>     t ∈ valid_ltr gs g S ∧ sink_node t = v ∧ sink_store t = s ∧ stack_repr g stk t)" | `ltr_repr` pins a valid trace to a located configuration: the trace's sink is the current node/store, and its caller chain is the runtime stack. |
| definition | `located_ltr` | 43 | definition located_ltr :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ store set ⇒ cconf ⇒ bool" where<br>  "located_ltr gs g S cf = (∃t. ltr_repr gs g S cf t)" |  |

### src/CFG/VIMP_Proc_to_CFG.thy

> Compile a `com` program directly into the two-relation procedure-aware CFG. Nodes are `Statement n` (allocated from a counter), `FunctionEntry p`, and `FunctionResult p`. The compiler threads the enclosing procedure name `p`, so a source `Return` and the normal fall-through both reach `FunctionResult p` through an `EA_Ret` intra edge. Compilation is continuation passing: the node reached when a fragment falls through is an input, not a result. A node therefore denotes a program point between transfer functions, and no fragment invents a node to hold a control flow that its own source construct does not have. Layout of one procedure `p` with body `b`: • `FunctionEntry p --EA_Nop--> entry(b)`; • the compiled body edges, with the epilogue node as the body's continuation; • `epilogue --EA_Ret None p--> FunctionResult p` (normal fall-through returns without a value); • each source `Return e` inside `b` compiles to `--EA_Ret e p--> FunctionResult p` and ignores its continuation, so an early return leaves no node behind. Control effects and their source counterparts: • source lexical restoration (`Scope` / `Restore`) becomes ordinary intra flow; `Scope` is represented by transparent `EA_Nop` brackets; • source `Return` / `Unwind` become an explicit `EA_Ret` edge into `FunctionResult`; • source activation handling becomes `FunctionResult` + the `calls`-edge continuation. Calls are emitted only into `calls`; a call never appears as an executable intra edge. `Restore` / `Unwind` are runtime-only (excluded by `source_com`); their compile clauses are trivial one-node stubs that never occur for a source program.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `csize` | 43 | fun csize :: "com ⇒ nat" where<br>  "csize SKIP = 1"<br>\| "csize (Assign x a) = 1"<br>\| "csize (Random x) = 1"<br>\| "csize (Check c) = 1"<br>\| "csize (Seq c1 c2) = csize c1 + csize c2"<br>\| "csize (If b c1 c2) = 1 + csize c1 + csize c2"<br>\| "csize (While b c) = 1 + csize c"<br>\| "csize (Call dst q actuals) = 1"<br>\| "csize (Return e) = 1"<br>\| "csize Restore = 1"<br>\| "csize Unwind = 1" | `csize c` is the number of `Statement` indices the fragment of `c` allocates, fixed by the source syntax alone. `compile` needs it before it recurses: `Seq` names the entry node of `c2` --- the continuation it hands to `c1` --- and that node is `Statement (n + csize c1)`. `compile_next_id` below ties the two together, so the two definitions cannot drift apart silently. |
| fun | `falls_through` | 68 | fun falls_through :: "com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "falls_through SKIP = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Assign x a) = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Random x) = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Check c) = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Seq c1 c2) = (falls_through c1 ∧ falls_through c2)"<br>\| "falls_through (If b c1 c2) = (falls_through c1 ∨ falls_through c2)"<br>\| "falls_through (While b c) = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Call dst q actuals) = True"<br>\| "falls_through (Return e) = False"<br>\| "falls_through Restore = True"<br>\| "falls_through Unwind = True" | `falls_through c`: control can leave the fragment of `c` through its continuation, rather than only through an explicit `Return`. It is a syntactic over-approximation of the dynamic property --- `While` is counted as falling through because its guard may fail on the first test, and a branch that is never taken still contributes. The two directions `compile_reaches_falls_through` and `compile_reaches_returns` turn it into the reachability facts the procedure wrapper needs, and `compile_proc` uses it to decide whether the epilogue node is worth allocating. |
| definition | `call_formals` | 116 | definition call_formals :: "proc_table ⇒ pname ⇒ vname list" where<br>  "call_formals Π q = (case Π q of Some decl ⇒ formals decl \| None ⇒ [])"<br><br>declare call_formals_def [simp] | A call edge into an undeclared procedure carries no formals: `Call` compiles against whatever `Π q` says at compile time, declared or not, so its formal list is this classifier rather than a lookup that could fail. Naming it lets lemma statements about compiled call edges cite `call_formals` instead of restating the case split; the `[simp]` tag keeps it transparent to existing automation. |
| fun | `compile` | 121 | fun compile ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ nat<br>   ⇒ nat × cfg_node<br>        × (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set<br>        × (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set"<br>where<br>  "compile Π p SKIP k n =<br>     (Suc n, Statement n, {(Statement n, EA_Nop, k)}, {})"<br>\| "compile Π p (Assign x a) k n =<br>     (Suc n, Statement n, {(Statement n, EA_Assign x a, k)}, {})"<br>\| "compile Π p (Random x) k n =<br>     (Suc n, Statement n, {(Statement n, EA_Random x, k)}, {})"<br>\| "compile Π p (Check c) k n =<br>     (Suc n, Statement n, {(Statement n, EA_Check c, k)}, {})"<br>\| "compile Π p (Seq c1 c2) k n =<br>... (26 more lines) |  |
| definition | `compile_proc` | 187 | definition compile_proc ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname ⇒ proc_decl ⇒ nat<br>   ⇒ nat × (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set<br>        × (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set"<br>where<br>  "compile_proc Π p decl n =<br>     (let r = n + csize (body decl);<br>          (n', ben, E, K) = compile Π p (body decl) (Statement r) n<br>      in (Suc r,<br>          insert (FunctionEntry p, EA_Nop, ben)<br>            (if falls_through (body decl)<br>             then insert (Statement r, EA_Ret None p, FunctionResult p) E<br>             else E),<br>          K))" | A procedure wraps its body between `FunctionEntry p` and `FunctionResult p`. The body is compiled with the *epilogue* node `Statement r` as its continuation, and the epilogue carries the implicit `EA_Ret None p`: normal fall-through returns without a value. A value is published only by an explicit source `Return e`, which compiles to its own `EA_Ret (Some e) p` edge inside the body. The epilogue exists so that every edge into `FunctionResult p` is a return edge. Passing `FunctionResult p` directly as the body continuation would let a trailing assignment target the result node, losing `compile_result_target`. It is allocated *after* the body, so statement indices stay in source order and `r` lies inside the procedure's own counter range --- which is what keeps edge locality a statement about one fragment. The epilogue is wired only when the body can reach it, that is when `falls_through` holds. A body all of whose paths end in an explicit `Return` leaves `Statement r` with no edges at all, so it is not a node of the compiled graph --- nodes exist extensionally, through the edges that mention them. `compile_reaches_returns` supplies the reachability that the epilogue edge would otherwise have carried. The counter still advances past `r` in both cases. Reserving the index costs one `nat` and keeps every `csize` equation, fragment range and procedure interval independent of `falls_through`. |
| fun | `compile_procs` | 202 | fun compile_procs ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ nat<br>   ⇒ nat × (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set<br>        × (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set"<br>where<br>  "compile_procs Π [] n = (n, {}, {})"<br>\| "compile_procs Π (p # ps) n =<br>     (case Π p of<br>        None ⇒ compile_procs Π ps n<br>      \| Some decl ⇒<br>          (let (n1, E, K) = compile_proc Π p decl n;<br>               (n2, E', K') = compile_procs Π ps n1<br>           in (n2, E ∪ E', K ∪ K')))" |  |
| definition | `compile_prog` | 227 | definition compile_prog ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg"<br>where<br>  "compile_prog Π ps mnm main =<br>     (let (n1, Eprocs, Kprocs) = compile_procs Π ps 0;<br>          (n2, Emain, Kmain) = compile_proc Π mnm (proc_decl_of [] main) n1<br>      in ⦇ intra = Eprocs ∪ Emain, calls = Kprocs ∪ Kmain, cfg_entry = FunctionEntry mnm,<br>           checks = (λ(u, a, v). (u, ea_check_cond a))<br>             ` Set.filter (λ(u, a, v). is_EA_Check a) (Eprocs ∪ Emain) ⦈)" | `checks` is not collected by a separate counter-threading pass: it is read directly off the compiled edges, exactly the `EA_Check` edges `compile`'s own `Check c` clause emits. There is only one representation of "where a check sits and what it says" --- the compiled `intra` set itself --- so this field cannot drift from it by construction, not merely by a separately proved agreement lemma. |
| definition | `frag_stmts` | 706 | definition frag_stmts ::<br>  "(cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) set<br>   ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) set ⇒ nat set"<br>where<br>  "frag_stmts E K =<br>     {j. ∃a v. (Statement j, a, v) ∈ E} ∪ {j. ∃u a. (u, a, Statement j) ∈ E}<br>   ∪ {j. ∃act ce af. (Statement j, act, ce, af) ∈ K}<br>   ∪ {j. ∃u act ce. (u, act, ce, Statement j) ∈ K}" | The `Statement` indices a compiled fragment touches. Sources are always freshly allocated, so they lie in the fragment's own counter range; a target may in addition be the continuation, which belongs to the enclosing construct. `kstmt` names that one extra index, and is empty when the continuation is not a `Statement` node. |
| fun | `kstmt` | 715 | fun kstmt :: "cfg_node ⇒ nat set" where<br>  "kstmt (Statement j) = {j}"<br>\| "kstmt (FunctionEntry q) = {}"<br>\| "kstmt (FunctionResult q) = {}" |  |

> The solver unknown for a program point is a CFG node. Analysis-facing code keeps the short name `pp` for it; a return node is `FunctionResult p`, a callee entry `FunctionEntry p`, an ordinary location `Statement n`.

'a abs_state = vname => 'a inherits the same class pointwise via HOL's fun instances, so we never need to define lifted-bot / lifted-join. |

### src/Core/Domain/Abstract_Numeric_Queries.thy

> Any `backward_domain` instance already proves `inv_less_sound`, so it can answer `less_true`/`less_false` --- entailment/refutation of `<` over two atomic values --- for free: narrowing under the assumed truth value collapses to `bot` exactly when no witness pair could have satisfied that assumption, by `gamma_bot`. Kept as its own locale, separate from `backward_domain` itself, so a domain with only `inv_less` (no `meet`/`aval_abs`/`inv_plus`/`inv_minus`/`inv_times`) could still interpret it; `backward_domain` picks it up automatically through the `sublocale` declaration below, with no extra proof obligation. Like `backward_domain` itself, `gamma` is the `sound_domain` class operation here, not a separate locale parameter, so `gamma_bot` applies directly without an extra premise. `bfilter`'s own `Eq` case narrows through `inv_eq` directly (both branches), a separate backward-narrowing operator from the boolean query interface here; `eq_true`/`eq_false` are derived below from `less_false` and `meet` instead, not from `inv_less` or `inv_eq`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| locale | `derived_less_queries` | 24 | locale derived_less_queries =<br>  fixes inv_less :: "bool ⇒ 'a::sound_domain ⇒ 'a ⇒ 'a * 'a"<br>  assumes derived_inv_less_sound[intro]:<br>    "n1 ∈ gamma a1 ⟹ n2 ∈ gamma a2 ⟹ (n1 &lt; n2) = res<br>     ⟹ n1 ∈ gamma (fst (inv_less res a1 a2)) ∧ n2 ∈ gamma (snd (inv_less res a1 a2))"<br>begin | Any `backward_domain` instance already proves `inv_less_sound`, so it can answer `less_true`/`less_false` --- entailment/refutation of `<` over two atomic values --- for free: narrowing under the assumed truth value collapses to `bot` exactly when no witness pair could have satisfied that assumption, by `gamma_bot`. Kept as its own locale, separate from `backward_domain` itself, so a domain with only `inv_less` (no `meet`/`aval_abs`/`inv_plus`/`inv_minus`/`inv_times`) could still interpret it; `backward_domain` picks it up automatically through the `sublocale` declaration below, with no extra proof obligation. Like `backward_domain` itself, `gamma` is the `sound_domain` class operation here, not a separate locale parameter, so `gamma_bot` applies directly without an extra premise. `bfilter`'s own `Eq` case narrows through `inv_eq` directly (both branches), a separate backward-narrowing operator from the boolean query interface here; `eq_true`/`eq_false` are derived below from `less_false` and `meet` instead, not from `inv_less` or `inv_eq`. |
| definition | `less_true` | 31 | definition less_true :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool" where<br>  "less_true a b ↔ fst (inv_less False a b) = bot ∨ snd (inv_less False a b) = bot" |  |
| definition | `less_false` | 34 | definition less_false :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool" where<br>  "less_false a b ↔ fst (inv_less True a b) = bot ∨ snd (inv_less True a b) = bot" |  |
| locale | `derived_eq_true_from_less` | 93 | locale derived_eq_true_from_less =<br>  fixes less_false :: "'a::sound_domain ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>  assumes less_false_sound_for_eq[intro]:<br>    "less_false a b ⟹ i ∈ gamma a ⟹ j ∈ gamma b ⟹ ¬ i &lt; j"<br>begin | Two independent derivations, not one shared mechanism: entailment (`eq_true`) and refutation (`eq_false`) of `=` come from different existing capabilities and are proved in separate locales. `eq_true` follows from `less_false` alone, via integer trichotomy: if every pair is provably `¬ i < j` and every pair is provably `¬ j < i`, every pair is `i = j`. This needs nothing beyond `derived_less_queries`'s own `less_false_sound`, so `derived_eq_true_from_less` sublocales under it directly with no new premise. `eq_false` follows from `meet`, via the same `meet_sound`/`gamma_bot` pair `backward_domain` already assumes for narrowing: two values with no common witness meet to `bot`, whose concretization is empty. `derived_eq_false_from_meet` sublocales under `backward_domain` directly, reusing its `meet_sound` premise. Both locales fix their own capability rather than re-fixing `inv_less` or `meet` as `backward_domain` does, so a domain with only one of these operations --- a raw `less_false` query, or a raw `meet` --- could still interpret the matching locale on its own. |
| definition | `eq_true` | 99 | definition eq_true :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool" where<br>  "eq_true a b ↔ less_false a b ∧ less_false b a" |  |
| locale | `derived_eq_false_from_meet` | 118 | locale derived_eq_false_from_meet =<br>  fixes meet :: "'a::sound_domain ⇒ 'a ⇒ 'a"<br>  assumes meet_sound_for_eq[intro]:<br>    "n ∈ gamma a ⟹ n ∈ gamma b ⟹ n ∈ gamma (meet a b)"<br>begin |  |
| definition | `eq_false` | 124 | definition eq_false :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool" where<br>  "eq_false a b ↔ meet a b = bot" |  |
| locale | `abstract_numeric_queries` | 164 | locale abstract_numeric_queries =<br>  fixes gamma_num :: "'a ⇒ int set"<br>    and less_true :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and less_false :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and eq_true :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and eq_false :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>  assumes less_true_sound[intro]:<br>      "less_true a b ⟹ i ∈ gamma_num a ⟹ j ∈ gamma_num b ⟹ i &lt; j"<br>    and less_false_sound[intro]:<br>      "less_false a b ⟹ i ∈ gamma_num a ⟹ j ∈ gamma_num b ⟹ ¬ i &lt; j"<br>    and eq_true_sound[intro]:<br>      "eq_true a b ⟹ i ∈ gamma_num a ⟹ j ∈ gamma_num b ⟹ i = j"<br>    and eq_false_sound[intro]:<br>      "eq_false a b ⟹ i ∈ gamma_num a ⟹ j ∈ gamma_num b ⟹ i ≠ j"<br><br>... (1 more lines) | Entailment/refutation of `<`/`=` over an abstract numeric value is not check-specific: it is the same question a backward/guard domain answers, phrased as a query instead of a narrowing. Kept as its own locale, separate from expression abstraction and separate from the Boolean layer, so a domain that only has these four operations (no `aexp`/`store` concept at all) could still interpret it. `backward_domain` (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Domain`) refines an abstract value under an assumed truth value; this locale instead classifies an already-fixed pair of values as provably related or provably unrelated. The derivations above read the four functions off any `backward_domain` instance's own narrowing operators as a sound default --- a concrete domain interprets this locale explicitly, choosing either that derivation or its own sharper, hand-tuned predicates when the generic derivation loses precision it cannot recover. |

### src/Core/Domain/Exec_Backward.thy

> Every interpretation of `backward_domain` gets an executable `"'a resolved_st_q"` mirror of its *afilter* / *bfilter* for free, parameterized by an explicit location classifier `gs`: `afilter_st` / `bfilter_st` and their commutation with the abstract filters through `fun_of_resolved_st_q_for`, proved once here so no domain needs to repeat the induction by hand. A concrete domain names its own specialization via the `defines` clause of its existing `backward_domain` interpretation (see `Sign_Backward`, `Interval_Backward`); no per-domain proof is needed.

| fun | `afilter_st` | 22 | fun afilter_st ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; aexp =&gt; 'a =&gt; 'a resolved_st_q =&gt; 'a resolved_st_q"<br>where<br>    "afilter_st gs (V x) a s =<br>       update_resolved_st_q s (location_of gs x)<br>         (meet a (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs s x))"<br>  \| "afilter_st gs (Plus e1 e2) a s =<br>       (let (a1, a2) = inv_plus a<br>              (aval_abs e1 (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs s))<br>              (aval_abs e2 (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs s))<br>        in afilter_st gs e1 a1 (afilter_st gs e2 a2 s))"<br>  \| "afilter_st gs (Minus e1 e2) a s =<br>       (let (a1, a2) = inv_minus a<br>              (aval_abs e1 (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs s))<br>              (aval_abs e2 (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs s))<br>... (7 more lines) |  |
| fun | `bfilter_st` | 45 | fun bfilter_st ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; bexp =&gt; bool =&gt; 'a resolved_st_q =&gt; 'a resolved_st_q"<br>where<br>    "bfilter_st gs (Less e1 e2) res s =<br>       (let (a1, a2) = inv_less res<br>              (aval_abs e1 (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs s))<br>              (aval_abs e2 (fun_of_resolved_st_q_for gs s))<br>        in afilter_st gs e1 a1 (afilter_st gs e2 a2 s))"<br>  \| "bfilter_st gs (Not b) res s = bfilter_st gs b (¬ res) s"<br>  \| "bfilter_st gs (And b1 b2) True s =<br>       bfilter_st gs b1 True (bfilter_st gs b2 True s)"<br>  \| "bfilter_st gs (And b1 b2) False s =<br>       bfilter_st gs b1 False s ⊔ bfilter_st gs b2 False s"<br>  \| "bfilter_st gs (Or b1 b2) True s =<br>       bfilter_st gs b1 True s ⊔ bfilter_st gs b2 True s"<br>... | type_synonym | `scoped_location` | 7 | type_synonym scoped_location = "pname * location" |  |

| definition | `classic_keep_local` | 17 | definition classic_keep_local :: "scoped_location =&gt; bool" where<br>  "classic_keep_local scoped =<br>    (case snd scoped of Local_Location _ =&gt; True \| Global_Location _ =&gt; False)" |  |
| definition | `classic_publish_side` | 21 | definition classic_publish_side :: "scoped_location =&gt; bool" where<br>  "classic_publish_side scoped =<br>    (case snd scoped of Local_Location _ =&gt; False \| Global_Location _ =&gt; True)" |  |
| definition | `scope_locations` | 33 | definition scope_locations :: "imp_prog =&gt; pname =&gt; location list" where<br>  "scope_locations p owner =<br>    remdups (map (location_of (storage_global p owner))<br>      (scope_vnames_list p owner))" |  |
| fun | `resolved_default` | 110 | fun resolved_default :: "('a::bot) resolved_st =&gt; location =&gt; 'a" where<br>  "resolved_default (dl, dg, entries) (Local_Location x) = dl"<br>\| "resolved_default (dl, dg, entries) (Global_Location x) = dg" |  |
| fun | `raw_support` | 114 | fun raw_support :: "('a::bot) resolved_st =&gt; location list" where<br>  "raw_support (dl, dg, entries) = map fst entries" |  |
| definition | `effective_support` | 117 | definition effective_support ::<br>  "('a::bot) resolved_st =&gt; location list" where<br>  "effective_support s =<br>    filter (λloc. lookup_resolved_st s loc ≠ resolved_default s loc)<br>      (remdups (raw_support s))" |  |
| definition | `relevant_locations` | 123 | definition relevant_locations ::<br>  "imp_prog =&gt; pname =&gt; ('a::bot) resolved_st =&gt; location list" where<br>  "relevant_locations p owner s =<br>    remdups (scope_locations p owner @ effective_support s)" |  |
| definition | `project_resolved_on_raw` | 641 | definition project_resolved_on_raw ::<br>  "pname =&gt; location list =&gt; (scoped_location =&gt; bool) =&gt;<br>   ('a::bot) resolved_st =&gt; 'a resolved_st" where<br>  "project_resolved_on_raw owner universe placed s =<br>    foldr (λloc result.<br>      if placed (owner, loc)<br>      then update_resolved_st result loc (lookup_resolved_st s loc)<br>      else result)<br>    (remdups (universe @ effective_support s)) (bot, bot, [])" |  |
| definition | `project_resolved_on_strict_raw` | 812 | definition project_resolved_on_strict_raw ::<br>  "pname =&gt; location list =&gt; (scoped_location =&gt; bool) =&gt;<br>   ('a::bot) resolved_st =&gt; 'a resolved_st" where<br>  "project_resolved_on_strict_raw owner universe placed s =<br>    foldr (λloc result.<br>      if placed (owner, loc)<br>      then update_resolved_st result loc (lookup_resolved_st s loc)<br>      else result)<br>    (remdups universe) (bot, bot, [])" | `project_resolved_on` materializes `universe @ effective_support s`: a defensive escape hatch that reproduces an unplaced, unscoped source's actual support even when `universe` does not anticipate it. That behavior is what the classic-equivalence lemmas above need (comparing a placed tree against an unplaced one whose own support is not bounded by any declared scope). A generated placement equation system has no such unplaced comparison to honor; its `universe` is the write node's own declared scope, and nothing outside it should ever survive projection. `project_resolved_on_strict` drops the escape hatch, so its own support is bounded by `universe` alone, unconditionally --- no fact about the source state's support is needed. |
