### src/Core/Domain/Split_State.thy

> A split abstract state keeps the local and the global half of an abstract store in two separate components with independent value types: `'l abs_state` for locals and `'g abs_state` for globals. Each component is a total `vname => _` map; well-formedness (`wf_split`) pins the off-domain half of each component to `bot`. At the homogeneous instance `'l = 'g` the split representation is isomorphic to the plain state `'a abs_state`: `merge_state` and `split_state` are mutually inverse between well-formed split states and plain states (`merge_state_bij`), the order transports both ways (`merge_state_le_iff`), lattice operations commute with the conversions, and the split concretization `gamma_split` agrees with `gamma_state` under the isomorphism.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `split_state` | 23 | type_synonym ('l, 'g) split_state = "'l abs_state × 'g abs_state" | A split abstract state keeps the local and the global half of an abstract store in two separate components with independent value types: `'l abs_state` for locals and `'g abs_state` for globals. Each component is a total `vname => _` map; well-formedness (`wf_split`) pins the off-domain half of each component to `bot`. At the homogeneous instance `'l = 'g` the split representation is isomorphic to the plain state `'a abs_state`: `merge_state` and `split_state` are mutually inverse between well-formed split states and plain states (`merge_state_bij`), the order transports both ways (`merge_state_le_iff`), lattice operations commute with the conversions, and the split concretization `gamma_split` agrees with `gamma_state` under the isomorphism. |
| definition | `project_component` | 34 | definition project_component ::<br>  "('loc =&gt; bool) =&gt; ('loc =&gt; 'a::bot) =&gt; 'loc =&gt; 'a" where<br>  "project_component placed state =<br>     (λloc. if placed loc then state loc else bot)" | Placement controls which abstract component constrains a location. It is independent of source storage: a location can be constrained by the local component, the side component, both components, or neither component. |
| definition | `classic_split_keep_local` | 39 | definition classic_split_keep_local :: "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; vname =&gt; bool" where<br>  "classic_split_keep_local storage loc = (¬ storage loc)" |  |
| definition | `classic_split_publish_side` | 42 | definition classic_split_publish_side :: "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; vname =&gt; bool" where<br>  "classic_split_publish_side storage loc = storage loc" |  |
| definition | `wf_split` | 49 | definition wf_split :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ ('l::bot, 'g::bot) split_state ⇒ bool" where<br>  "wf_split gs lg ↔<br>     (∀x. gs x ⟶ fst lg x = bot) ∧<br>     (∀x. ¬ gs x ⟶ snd lg x = bot)" |  |
| definition | `merge_state` | 71 | definition merge_state :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ ('a, 'a) split_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "merge_state gs lg = (λx. if gs x then snd lg x else fst lg x)" |  |
| definition | `split_state` | 74 | definition split_state :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ ('a::bot) abs_state ⇒ ('a, 'a) split_state" where<br>  "split_state gs σ =<br>     ((λx. if gs x then bot else σ x),<br>      (λx. if gs x then σ x else bot))" |  |

(1 more lines) |  |
| locale | `abstract_expression_domain` | 58 | locale abstract_expression_domain =<br>  abstract_numeric_queries gamma_num less_true less_false eq_true eq_false<br>  for gamma_num :: "'a ⇒ int set"<br>    and less_true :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and less_false :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and eq_true :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and eq_false :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>  + fixes gamma_state :: "'d ⇒ store set"<br>    and aval_abs :: "aexp ⇒ 'd ⇒ 'a"<br>  assumes aval_abs_sound[intro]:<br>      "s ∈ gamma_state d ⟹ aval e s ∈ gamma_num (aval_abs e d)" | Adds the one capability every domain with an `aexp` evaluator already has: `aval_abs_sound`-shaped soundness, the same reuse point `Voblint_Core.Abstract_Domain`'s `backward_domain` locale takes for its own `aval_abs` parameter. |
| locale | `abstract_check_domain` | 105 | locale abstract_check_domain =<br>  abstract_expression_domain gamma_num less_true less_false eq_true eq_false gamma_state aval_abs<br>  for gamma_num :: "'a ⇒ int set"<br>    and less_true :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and less_false :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and eq_true :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and eq_false :: "'a ⇒ 'a ⇒ bool"<br>    and gamma_state :: "'d ⇒ store set"<br>    and aval_abs :: "aexp ⇒ 'd ⇒ 'a"<br>begin | Reusing the existing per-domain guard/assume machinery (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Domain`'s `backward_domain` locale, its `bfilter`/ `afilter`, and the Sign instances `assume_sign`/`assume_not_sign`) was investigated first: `check_true c σ ↔ bfilter c False σ = bot` is sound whenever `gamma bot = {}`, and would give `Not`/`And`/`Or` for free from `bfilter`'s own recursion. It is not usable as an executable decision procedure in this codebase, though: an `'a abs_state` is a raw function `vname ⇒ 'a` over the infinite type `vname`, so `= bot` at that level is not code-generable; the finite executable mirror `'a resolved_st_q` (`Voblint_Core.Exec_St`) is a `quotient_type` whose `≤`/`=` instance is a `lift_definition` quantifying over `location`, with no `[code]` equation --- confirmed empirically: `value "cinit_sign_st = bot"` does not reduce, echoing the unevaluated term instead of `True`/`False`. A second, narrower reuse route also exists at the atomic-value level: e.g. Sign's own `inv_less_sign` (backward inversion of `<`, already sound-proven, `Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Backward`) could derive `less_true a b` as `fst (inv_less_sign False a b) = SBot ∨ snd (inv_less_sign False a b) = SBot` --- unlike the state-level route, individual atoms have decidable equality, so this stays executable. There is no comparable existing operator for `eq_true`/`eq_false` (`bfilter`'s `Eq` case only ever narrows the `True` branch, via `meet`, never tests definite inequality), so the four query functions are defined directly per domain rather than partially reused. This layer decides entailment directly off `aval_abs` results via `less_true`/`less_false`/`eq_true`/`eq_false`. The judgments are sound but intentionally incomplete: two atomic values with overlapping concretizations are neither provably related nor provably unrelated, and are reported `Check_Unknown`, never misclassified as refuted. |
| fun | `check_true` | 126 | fun check_true :: "bexp ⇒ 'd ⇒ bool"<br>  and check_false :: "bexp ⇒ 'd ⇒ bool" where<br>    "check_true (Bc v) d = v"<br>  \| "check_false (Bc v) d = (¬ v)"<br>  \| "check_true (Not b) d = check_false b d"<br>  \| "check_false (Not b) d = check_true b d"<br>  \| "check_true (And b1 b2) d = (check_true b1 d ∧ check_true b2 d)"<br>  \| "check_false (And b1 b2) d = (check_false b1 d ∨ check_false b2 d)"<br>  \| "check_true (Or b1 b2) d = (check_true b1 d ∨ check_true b2 d)"<br>  \| "check_false (Or b1 b2) d = (check_false b1 d ∧ check_false b2 d)"<br>  \| "check_true (Less a b) d = less_true (aval_abs a d) (aval_abs b d)"<br>  \| "check_false (Less a b) d = less_false (aval_abs a d) (aval_abs b d)"<br>  \| "check_true (Eq a b) d = eq_true (aval_abs a d) (aval_abs b d)"<br>  \| "check_false (Eq a b) d = eq_false (aval_abs a d) (aval_abs b d)" | `check_true`/`check_false` are mutually recursive so `Not` can go through definitely-false reasoning on its argument rather than a one-sided negation of `check_true` (which would be unsound: "not provably true" is not "provably false"). Both may be `False` on the same `bexp`/state pair; that combination means unknown, not refuted. |
| definition | `classify_check` | 186 | definition classify_check :: "bexp ⇒ 'd ⇒ check_result" where<br>  "classify_check c d =<br>     (if check_true c d then Check_Proved<br>      else if check_false c d then Check_Refuted<br>      else Check_Unknown)" |  |
| definition | `abstract_checks_proven` | 213 | definition abstract_checks_proven :: "checks ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'd) ⇒ bool" where<br>  "abstract_checks_proven ck env ↔ (∀v c. (v, c) ∈ ck ⟶ check_true c (env v))" |  |
| type_synonym | `check_report_entry` | 252 | type_synonym check_report_entry = "pp × bexp × check_result" | A single, domain-generic report over every compiled `EA_Check` edge: one entry per check, at its own source node, classified by whatever `classify` function a domain supplies (`sign_classify_check` and its siblings, or `abstract_check_domain.classify_check` generically). Entries are read directly off `intra` through the same deterministic order `cfg_intra_list` already gives the TD bridge, rather than sorting `pp` or `bexp` values by hand: a compiled `checks` table has exactly the same `EA_Check` edges as its source, by construction, so this report is a second view of that one representation, not a parallel table. |
| definition | `classify_checks` | 254 | definition classify_checks ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ (pp ⇒ 's) ⇒ (bexp ⇒ 's ⇒ check_result) ⇒ check_report_entry list" where<br>  "classify_checks g env classify =<br>     map (λ(u, a, v). (u, ea_check_cond a, classify (ea_check_cond a) (env u)))<br>       (filter (λ(u, a, v). is_EA_Check a) (cfg_intra_list g))" |  |
| definition | `classify_checks_with_state` | 324 | definition classify_checks_with_state ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ (pp ⇒ 's) ⇒ (bexp ⇒ 's ⇒ check_result) ⇒ (pp × bexp × check_result × 's) list" where<br>  "classify_checks_with_state g env classify =<br>     map (λ(u, c, r). (u, c, r, env u)) (classify_checks g env classify)" | State-carrying sibling of `classify_checks`: the same traversal and classification, with the node's own abstract state attached to each entry. `classify_checks` stays the soundness-critical primitive every domain cites directly; the projection lemma below shows this report is exactly that one with a fourth field added, so `classify_checks_proved_sound` and `classify_checks_refuted_sound` reach it through the projection without restatement. |

### src/Core/Equations/CFG_Enumeration.thy

> Backward predecessor relations and deterministic edge enumeration over a compiled two-relation CFG. These are the equation-generation and code-generation views the TD solver reads to build and evaluate the constraint system; they are not part of the concrete CFG semantics --- `valid_ltr` never refers to them. Ordinary control flow is enumerated over `intra`; procedure calls --- entry routing and return combining --- over `calls`. There is no unified edge set and no separate combine relation: a return is recovered from the same `calls` tuple that created the activation.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `intra_predecessors` | 34 | definition intra_predecessors :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × edge_action) set" where<br>  "intra_predecessors g v = {(u, a). (u, a, v) ∈ intra g}" | The ordinary incoming transitions of a node: an intra edge is a total store transformer within one activation, so this is the transfer fold the equation system runs at every node --- including the `EA_Ret e p` edges into `FunctionResult p`, by which return values are summarised as ordinary predecessor folding. |
| definition | `entry_calls` | 58 | definition entry_calls :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action) set" where<br>  "entry_calls g v = {(c, ca). ∃k. (c, ca, v, k) ∈ calls g}" | The call tuples whose callee entry is the queried node `v` (a `FunctionEntry p`). The continuation does not affect the callee-entry state, so it is projected away; the entry contribution is the caller state routed through the call action's parameter binding (`tf_enter`). |
| definition | `return_calls` | 84 | definition return_calls ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × vname option × cfg_node) set" where<br>  "return_calls g v =<br>     {(c, dst, FunctionResult p) \| c dst formals actuals p.<br>        (c, CallEdge dst formals actuals, FunctionEntry p, v) ∈ calls g}" | The call tuples whose continuation is the queried node `v`, each paired with the caller destination and the callee's `FunctionResult p` exit node --- recovered from the callee entry `FunctionEntry p`, so no separate combine relation is needed. The return contribution is the caller state and the callee-exit state assembled by `combine_collect_abs`. The final component is returned directly so clients need not rebuild the result node. |
| definition | `cfg_intra_list` | 113 | definition cfg_intra_list :: "cfg ⇒ (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "cfg_intra_list g =<br>     (if finite (intra g) then sorted_list_of_set (intra g) else [])" | Stable list views for the TD bridge: the intra and call edge sets sorted by their structural order, and the queried-node projections built by filtering. |
| definition | `cfg_calls_list` | 121 | definition cfg_calls_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "cfg_calls_list g =<br>     (if finite (calls g) then sorted_list_of_set (calls g) else [])" |  |
| definition | `intra_predecessor_list` | 138 | definition intra_predecessor_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × edge_action) list" where<br>  "intra_predecessor_list g v =<br>     map (λ(u, a, w). (u, a)) (filter (λ(u, a, w). w = v) (cfg_intra_list g))" |  |
| definition | `entry_call_list` | 149 | definition entry_call_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action) list" where<br>  "entry_call_list g v =<br>     map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). (c, ca)) (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k). ce = v) (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |
| definition | `return_call_list` | 159 | definition return_call_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × vname option × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "return_call_list g v =<br>     map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). (c, case ca of CallEdge dst _ _ ⇒ dst,<br>                              case ce of FunctionEntry p ⇒ FunctionResult p \| _ ⇒ ce))<br>       (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k).<br>          k = v ∧ (case ce of FunctionEntry _ ⇒ True \| _ ⇒ False))<br>         (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |
| definition | `return_call_actions` | 188 | definition return_call_actions ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node) set" where<br>  "return_call_actions g v =<br>     {(c, ca, FunctionResult p) \| c ca p. (c, ca, FunctionEntry p, v) ∈ calls g}" | `return_call_list` keeps only the caller destination projected from the triggering `call_action`, which is all the flat/context-insensitive combine (`combine_collect_abs`, via `Constraint_System.rhs_combine_sources`) needs. The context-sensitive DG generator needs more: it must route both the callee-entry seed publication and the return-side context read from the *same* call action, and `entry_call_list` already keeps `ca` whole on the entry side. This is the return-side counterpart that does the same --- kept as a separate enumeration rather than widening `return_call_list` itself, since that function and its `vname option` shape are also relied on by the flat constraint system and the homogeneous effectful-transfer solver core (`TD_Side_Tree.thy`), which are out of scope for context routing. |
| definition | `return_call_action_list` | 209 | definition return_call_action_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "return_call_action_list g v =<br>     map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). (c, ca,<br>                              case ce of FunctionEntry p ⇒ FunctionResult p \| _ ⇒ ce))<br>       (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k).<br>          k = v ∧ (case ce of FunctionEntry _ ⇒ True \| _ ⇒ False))<br>         (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |
| definition | `call_successors` | 245 | definition call_successors ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node) set" where<br>  "call_successors g v = {(ce, ca, k). (v, ca, ce, k) ∈ calls g}" | The call tuples whose call site is the queried node `v`: the entry, action, and continuation of every call `v` makes. This is the caller-side counterpart of `entry_calls` (callee-indexed) and `return_call_actions` (continuation-indexed) --- routing a call-entry seed publication at its call site needs the callee entry and the whole `call_action` together, which neither of those two enumerations exposes. |
| definition | `call_successor_list` | 263 | definition call_successor_list ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ (cfg_node × call_action × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "call_successor_list g v =<br>     map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). (ce, ca, k)) (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k). c = v) (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |

### src/Core/Equations/Checks.thy

> A check table relates program points to the boolean conditions placed there; several checks may share one point. `checks_proven` states that every concrete store the caller's reachable-store function admits at a checked point satisfies that point's condition. The reachable-store function is left abstract (`pp ⇒ store set`) so an instance supplies whatever collecting semantics its own `sound_dg_spec` or `sound_dg_hooks` interpretation already proves sound: this theory adds no new soundness anchor, only the store-level check layer on top of an existing one. The table's shape, `(pp × bexp) set`, matches `checks` --- the `cfg` record field a real compiler populates (`collect_checks_prog`, `VIMP_Proc_to_CFG.thy`) --- directly: a caller instantiates `ck` with `checks g` for a compiled `g`, not with a hand-built table.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `checks` | 24 | type_synonym checks = "(pp × bexp) set" | A check table relates program points to the boolean conditions placed there; several checks may share one point. `checks_proven` states that every concrete store the caller's reachable-store function admits at a checked point satisfies that point's condition. The reachable-store function is left abstract (`pp ⇒ store set`) so an instance supplies whatever collecting semantics its own `sound_dg_spec` or `sound_dg_hooks` interpretation already proves sound: this theory adds no new soundness anchor, only the store-level check layer on top of an existing one. The table's shape, `(pp × bexp) set`, matches `checks` --- the `cfg` record field a real compiler populates (`collect_checks_prog`, `VIMP_Proc_to_CFG.thy`) --- directly: a caller instantiates `ck` with `checks g` for a compiled `g`, not with a hand-built table. |
| definition | `checks_proven` | 26 | definition checks_proven :: "checks ⇒ (pp ⇒ store set) ⇒ bool" where<br>  "checks_proven ck reach ↔ (∀v c. (v, c) ∈ ck ⟶ (∀s ∈ reach v. bval c s))" |  |

### src/Core/Equations/Constraint_System.thy

> Given: - A CFG g - An abstract domain D (instance of abstract_domain) - Per-domain transfer functions for each edge action We construct an equation system (constraint system) where each program point v has an equation: σ(v) = join_over { tf(a)(σ(u)) \| (u,a,v) in g } plus the special base case at the entry: σ(entry) includes the initial abstract state. The equation system is represented as an RHS function: rhs :: pp => (pp => D abs_state) => D abs_state This is the format expected by the top-down solver locale. All transfer functions are parameterised over the domain via a locale so the same equation-system construction works for Sign, Interval, etc.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| record | `domain_transfer` | 38 | record 'a domain_transfer =<br>  tf_assign    :: "vname =&gt; aexp =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)"<br>  tf_random    :: "vname =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)"<br>  tf_assume    :: "bexp  =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)"<br>  tf_assume_not :: "bexp =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)"<br>  tf_enter     :: "vname list =&gt; aexp list =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)"<br>  tf_combine   :: "('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state) =&gt; ('a abs_state)" | A domain_transfer bundles the per-action abstract transformers. Parameterised by the abstract value type 'a. |
| fun | `apply_tf` | 48 | fun apply_tf :: "'a domain_transfer<br>                 =&gt; edge_action<br>                 =&gt; ('a abs_state)<br>                 =&gt; ('a abs_state)" where<br>    "apply_tf tf EA_Nop              σ = σ"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_Assign x a)     σ = tf_assign tf x a σ"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_Random x)       σ = tf_random tf x σ"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_Assume b)       σ = tf_assume tf b σ"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_AssumeNot b)    σ = tf_assume_not tf b σ"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_Ret e p)        σ =<br>       (case e of None ⇒ σ \| Some a ⇒ tf_assign tf ret_var a σ)"<br>  \| "apply_tf tf (EA_Check c)        σ = σ" |  |
| locale | `action_reduces` | 92 | locale action_reduces =<br>  fixes F :: "edge_action ⇒ 'y"<br>  assumes ret_none[simp,intro]: "⋀p. F (EA_Ret None p) = F EA_Nop"<br>    and ret_some[simp,intro]: "⋀a p. F (EA_Ret (Some a) p) = F (EA_Assign ret_var a)"<br>    and check[simp,intro]: "⋀c. F (EA_Check c) = F EA_Nop" | Every family this codebase builds over `edge_action` reduces the same non-primitive constructors to an already-handled one: a void return and a check both collapse to `EA_Nop`, a value return to `EA_Assign` (`apply_tf_EA_Ret_None` / `apply_tf_EA_Ret_Some` / `apply_tf_EA_Check` give this for `apply_tf` itself; `F` is schematic, so it needs the same reduction as an explicit hypothesis). `action_reduces` packages the three reduction facts as one predicate per family `F` instead of three positional hypotheses, so `apply_tf_wrap_eqI` and its effectful siblings each take a single `action_reduces F` premise. Widening this locale is the only change a future reducible constructor needs: every wrapper lemma and call site keeps citing the same one fact. |
| definition | `abs_join_set` | 150 | definition abs_join_set ::<br>    "('a abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state)<br>     =&gt; 'a abs_state<br>     =&gt; 'a abs_state set<br>     =&gt; 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "abs_join_set join_abs bot_abs S = Finite_Set.fold join_abs bot_abs S" | Fold join_abs over a finite set of abstract states. Requires comp_fun_commute join_abs for the result to be order-independent. Finiteness of the predecessor set follows from finite (intra g) and finite (calls g). |
| definition | `combine_env_abs` | 361 | definition combine_env_abs ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>  ("combine'_env^#")<br>where<br>  "combine_env_abs gs sc se = (λx. if gs x then se x else sc x)" |  |
| definition | `bind_formals_abs` | 390 | definition bind_formals_abs ::<br>    "vname list ⇒ 'a list ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "bind_formals_abs xs avs σ = fold (λ(x, a) \&lt;tau&gt;. \&lt;tau&gt;(x := a)) (zip xs avs) σ" | Formal binding evaluates actual arguments in the caller state and assigns each abstract value to the matching callee formal. |
| definition | `enter_frame_D` | 480 | definition enter_frame_D :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ 'a ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "enter_frame_D gs top_val σ = (λx. if gs x then σ x else top_val)" | Generic procedure-entry frame: reset locals to a fixed, fully-imprecise `top_val`, keep globals, then bind the formals via `bind_formals_abs`. Every domain's own enter-frame construction (Sign's *enter_frame_sign*, Interval's *enter_frame_ivl*) is this same shape, differing only in which value stands for "unknown" -- so this is parameterised over that one value rather than duplicated per domain. |
| definition | `enter_D` | 506 | definition enter_D ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ 'a ⇒ (aexp ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a) ⇒ vname list ⇒ aexp list<br>   ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "enter_D gs top_val aval_abs xs es σ =<br>     bind_formals_abs xs (map (λe. aval_abs e σ) es) (enter_frame_D gs top_val σ)" |  |
| fun | `combine_assign_abs` | 539 | fun combine_assign_abs ::<br>    "vname option ⇒ 'a ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>    ("combine'_assign^#") where<br>    "combine_assign_abs None _ σ = σ"<br>  \| "combine_assign_abs (Some x) v σ = σ(x := v)" | Abstract counterpart of `combine_assign`: write the callee's return slot into the caller's destination, or leave the caller untouched when the call discards its result. |
| definition | `combine_collect_abs` | 550 | definition combine_collect_abs ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ vname option ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "combine_collect_abs gs dst σc σe = combine_assign^# dst (σe ret_var) (combine_env^# gs σc σe)" | Return combination joins caller locals with callee globals and then assigns the callee's `ret_var` to the optional destination. The ordinary abstract state update publishes the result without domain-specific return machinery. |
| definition | `tf_combine_collect_abs` | 585 | definition tf_combine_collect_abs ::<br>    "'a domain_transfer ⇒ vname option ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "tf_combine_collect_abs tf dst σc σe = combine_assign^# dst (σe ret_var) (tf_combine tf σc σe)" | Per-analysis return combine. `combine_collect_abs` fixes the environment merge to `combine_env_abs`; `tf_combine_collect_abs` instead reads the merge from the `'a domain_transfer` in scope, so each analysis may supply its own sound over-approximation of `combine_env` instead of the structural local/global split. The return-value write stays `combine_assign_abs`: it is already domain-agnostic under the function-based `'a abs_state` representation, so only the merge step is a per-analysis choice. |
| definition | `rhs_edge_sources` | 649 | definition rhs_edge_sources ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ 'a domain_transfer ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ pp ⇒ 'a abs_state set" where<br>  "rhs_edge_sources g tf env v =<br>     (λ(u, a). apply_tf tf a (env u)) ` intra_predecessors g v" | Each predecessor kind contributes a set of abstract states. Keeping the families separate gives downstream proofs precise introduction rules; their union is the single source of truth used by the executable right-hand side. |
| definition | `rhs_entry_sources` | 655 | definition rhs_entry_sources ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ 'a domain_transfer ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ pp ⇒ 'a abs_state set" where<br>  "rhs_entry_sources g tf env v =<br>     (λ(c, ca). case ca of CallEdge dst fs as ⇒ tf_enter tf fs as (env c))<br>       ` entry_calls g v" |  |
| definition | `rhs_combine_sources` | 662 | definition rhs_combine_sources ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ 'a domain_transfer ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ pp ⇒ 'a abs_state set" where<br>  "rhs_combine_sources g tf env v =<br>     (λ(c, dst, ex). tf_combine_collect_abs tf dst (env c) (env ex)) ` return_calls g v" |  |
| definition | `rhs_sources` | 667 | definition rhs_sources ::<br>    "cfg ⇒ 'a domain_transfer ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ pp ⇒ 'a abs_state set" where<br>  "rhs_sources g tf env v =<br>     rhs_edge_sources g tf env v ∪<br>     rhs_entry_sources g tf env v ∪<br>     rhs_combine_sources g tf env v" |  |
| definition | `rhs` | 709 | definition rhs ::<br>    "cfg<br>     ⇒ 'a domain_transfer<br>     ⇒ ('a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>     ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ pp<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "rhs g tf join_abs bot_abs s0 env v =<br>     abs_join_set join_abs bot_abs<br>       (if v = cfg_entry g then insert s0 (rhs_sources g tf env v)<br>        else rhs_sources g tf env v)" | `rhs` is the equation system's right-hand side at `v`: the join over `rhs_sources`'s abstract predecessor contributions, plus the seed `s0` exactly when `v` is the graph's entry --- the one node with no predecessor edge to supply a starting value otherwise. |
| definition | `is_post_fixpoint` | 724 | definition is_post_fixpoint ::<br>    "cfg<br>     ⇒ ('a::ord domain_transfer)<br>     ⇒ ('a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>     ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>     ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>     ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "is_post_fixpoint g tf join_abs bot_abs s0 env =<br>     (∀v. rhs g tf join_abs bot_abs s0 env v ≤ env v)" |  |
| definition | `cinit_stores` | 797 | definition cinit_stores :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ store set" where<br>  "cinit_stores gs = {s. ∀x. gs x ⟶ s x = 0}" | In C, global variables are zero-initialised before `main` starts (ISO C 6.7.9p10); local variables are uninitialized and may hold any integer value. `cinit_stores` is the corresponding set of concrete stores: those where every global is 0 and locals are unconstrained. Any analysis that uses a domain-specific abstract seed `s0` satisfying `cinit_stores ⊆ gamma_state s0` may state its soundness theorem against `cinit_stores` rather than `UNIV`, matching C program semantics. |
| locale | `sound_transfer_for` | 807 | locale sound_transfer_for =<br>  fixes gs :: "vname =&gt; bool"<br>    and tf :: "'a::sound_domain domain_transfer"<br>  assumes tf_sound_assign_for[intro]:<br>    "∀x (a::aexp) σ. ∀s ∈ ⟦σ⟧.<br>       s(x := aval a s) ∈ ⟦tf_assign tf x a σ⟧"<br>  assumes tf_sound_random_for[intro]:<br>    "∀x σ. ∀s ∈ ⟦σ⟧. ∀v. s(x := v) ∈ ⟦tf_random tf x σ⟧"<br>  assumes tf_sound_assume_for[intro]:<br>    "∀(b::bexp) σ. ∀s ∈ ⟦σ⟧. bval b s<br>       ⟶ s ∈ ⟦tf_assume tf b σ⟧"<br>  assumes tf_sound_assume_not_for[intro]:<br>    "∀(b::bexp) σ. ∀s ∈ ⟦σ⟧. ¬ bval b s<br>       ⟶ s ∈ ⟦tf_assume_not tf b σ⟧"<br>  assumes tf_sound_enter_for[intro]:<br>... (9 more lines) | Sound transfer function: a domain_transfer tf that soundly over-approximates the concrete edge actions w.r.t. a sound_domain's concretization, relative to an explicit classifier gs. Bundles the five per-action soundness obligations (assign / assume / assume-not / enter / combine) as locale assumptions. Concrete domains discharge these once via `interpretation`. |
| type_synonym | `edge_tf_tree` | 920 | type_synonym ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree =<br>  "pp ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'd abs_state) strategy_tree" | An effectful_domain_transfer bundles per-action strategy tree producers. Each field takes the edge-action parameters plus the source program point u and returns a strategy tree that may QueryL/QueryG arbitrary unknowns, emit Side contributions to named globals, and ends with Answer carrying the local result. apply_etf dispatches on edge_action to the matching field. Unit-global effectful tree constructors live in TD_Side_CFG where restrict_local / restrict_global are defined. |
| type_synonym | `combine_tf_tree` | 929 | type_synonym ('g, 'd) combine_tf_tree =<br>  "pp ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'd abs_state) strategy_tree" | A combine (procedure return) tree producer takes the caller program point and the callee-exit program point and builds a tree that queries both their local unknowns (and any globals), emits Side contributions, and ends with the combined local result. Unlike an edge, it has two local inputs. |
| record | `effectful_domain_transfer` | 932 | record ('g, 'd) effectful_domain_transfer =<br>  etf_nop        :: "('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_assign     :: "vname ⇒ aexp ⇒ ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_random     :: "vname ⇒ ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_assume     :: "bexp  ⇒ ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_assume_not :: "bexp  ⇒ ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_enter      :: "vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ ('g, 'd) edge_tf_tree"<br>  etf_combine    :: "vname option ⇒ ('g, 'd) combine_tf_tree" |  |
| fun | `apply_etf` | 941 | fun apply_etf ::<br>  "('g, 'd) effectful_domain_transfer ⇒ edge_action ⇒ pp<br>   ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'd abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "apply_etf etf EA_Nop           u = etf_nop etf u"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_Assign x a)  u = etf_assign etf x a u"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_Random x)    u = etf_random etf x u"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_Assume b)    u = etf_assume etf b u"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_AssumeNot b) u = etf_assume_not etf b u"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_Ret e p) u =<br>     (case e of None ⇒ etf_nop etf u \| Some a ⇒ etf_assign etf ret_var a u)"<br>\| "apply_etf etf (EA_Check c) u = etf_nop etf u" |  |
| fun | `local_edge_action` | 954 | fun local_edge_action :: "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; edge_action ⇒ bool" where<br>  "local_edge_action gs EA_Nop = True"<br>\| "local_edge_action gs (EA_Assign x e) =<br>    ((~ gs x) &amp; (~ aexp_mentions_where gs e))"<br>\| "local_edge_action gs (EA_Random x) = (~ gs x)"<br>\| "local_edge_action gs (EA_Assume b) = (~ bexp_mentions_where gs b)"<br>\| "local_edge_action gs (EA_AssumeNot b) = (~ bexp_mentions_where gs b)"<br>\| "local_edge_action gs (EA_Ret e p) =<br>    (case e of None ⇒ True<br>     \| Some a ⇒ ((~ gs ret_var) &amp; (~ aexp_mentions_where gs a)))"<br>\| "local_edge_action gs (EA_Check c) = True" |  |
| primrec | `all_sides` | 999 | primrec all_sides ::<br>  "(pp, 'g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ (pp + 'g ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "all_sides (Answer d) σ = ⊥"<br>\| "all_sides (QueryL y f) σ = all_sides (f (σ (Inl y))) σ"<br>\| "all_sides (QueryG y f) σ = all_sides (f (σ (Inr y))) σ"<br>\| "all_sides (Side y d t) σ = d ⊔ all_sides t σ" | all_sides totals every Side contribution of a tree, regardless of the named global it targets. Unlike sides_of_rhs (which keeps a per-name map), this is a single finite join over the tree's Side nodes -- the data needed to reassemble the full post-state across arbitrarily many named globals without an infinite Sup. At 'g = unit every Side targets (), so all_sides coincides with sides_of_rhs _ (Inr ()) (all_sides_eq_sides_Inr_unit below). |
| definition | `etf_full` | 1013 | definition etf_full ::<br>  "(pp, 'g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ (pp + 'g ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "etf_full t σ = traverse_rhs t σ ⊔ all_sides t σ" |  |
| definition | `glob_env` | 1030 | definition glob_env ::<br>  "(pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "glob_env σ = abs_join_set (⊔) ⊥ ((λg. σ (Inr g)) ` UNIV)" | glob_env joins all named-global unknowns of sigma into a single abstract state: the full flow-insensitive global value seen at any point. With a finite global-name type the join is a finite fold (abs_join_set over the image of UNIV), so it is well defined in a non-complete bounded_semilattice_sup_bot. At 'g = unit it is the single pot sigma (Inr ()) (glob_env_unit), so it generalises the unit pipeline's read of the global unknown. |
| definition | `etf_collecting_full` | 1075 | definition etf_collecting_full ::<br>  "(pp, 'g::finite, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ (pp + 'g ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "etf_collecting_full t σ = etf_full t σ ⊔ glob_env σ" | Collecting soundness for local-only trees: `etf_full` carries locals; globals are restored by joining `glob_env`. |
| definition | `se_constraint_holds` | 1163 | definition se_constraint_holds ::<br>  "('x,'g,'d::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) strategy_tree ⇒ ('x + 'g ⇒ 'd) ⇒ 'x ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "se_constraint_holds t σ u ≡<br>     traverse_rhs t σ ≤ σ (Inl u) ∧ sides_of_rhs t σ ≤ σ" | A valuation covers a strategy tree when it bounds both the tree's local answer and every side contribution produced during the same traversal. The local unknown bounds `traverse_rhs`; the complete valuation bounds `sides_of_rhs` pointwise. |
| definition | `inr_slot_locals_bot` | 1218 | definition inr_slot_locals_bot ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "inr_slot_locals_bot gs σ =<br>     (∀g. ∀x. ¬ gs x ⟶ σ (Inr g) x = bot)" |  |
| definition | `inl_slot_globals_bot` | 1232 | definition inl_slot_globals_bot ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "inl_slot_globals_bot gs σ =<br>     (∀v. ∀x. gs x ⟶ σ (Inl v) x = bot)" | Dual of `inr_slot_locals_bot`: every local program-point unknown carries bot in its global components. Holds for the effectful generator's solutions because each edge tree ends in a local-restricted Answer, so the local unknowns never accumulate globals. The keyed enter bound uses it to discard the caller's (bot) global part of a callee-entry frame. |
| definition | `inl_glob_le_glob_env` | 1247 | definition inl_glob_le_glob_env ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "inl_glob_le_glob_env gs σ =<br>     (∀v. ∀x. gs x ⟶ σ (Inl v) x ≤ glob_env σ x)" | The snapshot relaxation of `inl_slot_globals_bot`: a local unknown may carry globals, but each is bounded by the global environment. An analysis whose local Answers keep written globals @{emph `and`} publish the same values to the global slot satisfies it at any solution: every local-slot global sits below `glob_env`. This is exactly what the keyed enter bound needs: the caller's global part of a callee-entry frame is already covered by the globals. |
| locale | `sound_effectful_transfer` | 1257 | locale sound_effectful_transfer =<br>  fixes gs :: "vname =&gt; bool"<br>    and etf :: "('g::finite, 'a::sound_domain) effectful_domain_transfer"<br>  assumes etf_sound_nop[intro]:<br>    "∀u σ. inr_slot_locals_bot gs σ ⟶<br>       (∀s ∈ ⟦σ (Inl u) ⊔ glob_env σ⟧.<br>         s ∈ ⟦etf_collecting_full (etf_nop etf u) σ⟧)"<br>  assumes etf_sound_assign[intro]:<br>    "∀x e u σ. inr_slot_locals_bot gs σ ⟶<br>       (∀s ∈ ⟦σ (Inl u) ⊔ glob_env σ⟧.<br>         s(x := aval e s) ∈ ⟦etf_collecting_full (etf_assign etf x e u) σ⟧)"<br>  assumes etf_sound_random[intro]:<br>    "∀x u σ. inr_slot_locals_bot gs σ ⟶<br>       (∀s ∈ ⟦σ (Inl u) ⊔ glob_env σ⟧. ∀v.<br>         s(x := v) ∈ ⟦etf_collecting_full (etf_random etf x u) σ⟧)"<br>... (18 more lines) |  |
| definition | `local_formals` | 1307 | definition local_formals :: "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; vname list ⇒ bool" where<br>  "local_formals gs xs ↔ (∀x ∈ set xs. ¬ gs x)" | Formal parameters name locals. `bind_formals_abs` writes each formal into the callee's fresh frame, so a formal naming a global would write a caller-local value into a global slot and break the frame bound. VIMP scoping supplies this; the CFG well-formedness discharges it at each enter edge. |
| locale | `sound_effectful_transfer_framed` | 1342 | locale sound_effectful_transfer_framed = sound_effectful_transfer +<br>  fixes fresh_frame :: "'a::sound_domain abs_state"<br>  assumes etf_enter_framed_le[intro]:<br>    "∀xs (es::aexp list) u σ.<br>       local_formals gs xs ⟶<br>       inr_slot_locals_bot gs σ ⟶ inl_slot_globals_bot gs σ ⟶<br>       etf_full (etf_enter etf xs es u) σ ≤ fresh_frame ⊔ glob_env σ" |  |
| locale | `sound_effectful_transfer_framed_le` | 1358 | locale sound_effectful_transfer_framed_le = sound_effectful_transfer +<br>  fixes fresh_frame :: "'a::sound_domain abs_state"<br>  assumes etf_enter_framed_glob_le[intro]:<br>    "∀xs (es::aexp list) u σ.<br>       local_formals gs xs ⟶<br>       inr_slot_locals_bot gs σ ⟶ inl_glob_le_glob_env gs σ ⟶<br>       etf_full (etf_enter etf xs es u) σ ≤ fresh_frame ⊔ glob_env σ"<br><br>sublocale sound_effectful_transfer_framed_le ⊆ sound_effectful_transfer_framed gs etf fresh_frame<br>  by (simp add: sound_effectful_transfer_framed_def sound_effectful_transfer_axioms<br>        sound_effectful_transfer_framed_axioms_def<br>        etf_enter_framed_glob_le inl_slot_globals_bot_le_glob_env)<br><br>end | The snapshot-compatible companion of `sound_effectful_transfer_framed`: the enter bound holds under the weaker premise `inl_glob_le_glob_env`, so an analysis whose local slots carry globals can still discharge it. Since `inl_slot_globals_bot` implies `inl_glob_le_glob_env`, this contract is stronger; the sublocale below recovers the publish contract, making every `sound_effectful_transfer_framed` fact available here for free. |

### src/Core/Solver/Context/DG/Call_String_Context.thy

> The call-string route/enter-context pair: this theory owns only the call-string *data* and the one closed-term equality that makes `routed_context`'s `route_enterc_agree` obligation trivial for any bound `k` --- it deliberately does not import `Routed_Context.thy` or `DG_Ctx_Activation.thy`, and fixes no domain, no solver, and no CFG. `cs_route`/`cs_enterc` (defined below) plug into `routed_context`'s `route`/`enterc` parameters at whatever concrete instantiation a caller chooses; nothing here decides what that instantiation is.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `call_string` | 24 | type_synonym call_string = "cfg_node list" | Most recent call site first, unbounded as a type --- `k` only bounds the *values* `cs_route`/`cs_enterc` produce, not the type itself. This mirrors Goblint's `C.t` being a value-level choice under a type-level bound only because OCaml forces the bound to be a type; here `k` is ordinary `nat` data. |
| definition | `cs_route` | 30 | definition cs_route :: "nat ⇒ pp ⇒ call_string ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ call_string" where<br>  "cs_route k u ctx d ca = take k (u # ctx)" | The equation-level route: push the call site, keep only the most recent `k`. Ignores the entered abstract/executable value `d` entirely, so it is representation-independent for *any* domain without a per-domain commute lemma (`cs_route_indep_of_data` below). |
| definition | `cs_enterc` | 37 | definition cs_enterc :: "nat ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ call_string ⇒ store ⇒ call_string" where<br>  "cs_enterc k u ctx s = take k (u # ctx)" | The trace-semantic context function `routed_context`'s `enterc` parameter needs (`CFG_Local_Trace.thy`'s `key`): same closed term as `cs_route`, over the concrete `store` `key` supplies instead of an abstract/executable `'d`. |

### src/Core/Solver/Context/DG/Context_Refinement.thy

> A hand-built or seeded valuation for a coarser equation system is a `part_post_solution` exactly when it discharges the three per-unknown obligations dependency closure, right-hand-side domination, and side-effect domination on some finite unknown set containing the start unknown. `part_post_solution` already unfolds to precisely this conjunction, so the theorem below is a pure repackaging; its value is naming the seam a projection witness plugs into, so that only the three per-unknown facts need re-deriving at each instance rather than the conjunction shape itself.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| primrec | `seed_sides` | 40 | primrec seed_sides ::<br>  "'g list ⇒ ('x + 'g ⇒ 'd) ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "seed_sides [] s t = t"<br>\| "seed_sides (g # gs) s t = Side g (s (Inr g)) (seed_sides gs s t)" | A right-hand side that always joins a fixed local seed into its answer, and additionally publishes a fixed side contribution at one designated unknown for each key in a finite seed list. Built from `seqcomp_tree`, so it inherits `seqcomp_tree`'s characterization lemmas rather than adding a second strategy-tree combinator. |
| definition | `seed_rhs` | 60 | definition seed_rhs ::<br>  "('x, 'g, 'd::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) eqsT ⇒ ('x + 'g ⇒ 'd) ⇒ 'g list ⇒ 'x<br>   ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) eqsT"<br>where<br>  "seed_rhs T s gseeds x0 x =<br>     seqcomp_tree (T x)<br>       (λd. seed_sides (if x = x0 then gseeds else []) s (Answer (d ⊔ s (Inl x))))" |  |

### src/Core/Solver/Context/DG/DG_Ctx_Activation.thy

> The routed generator interprets solutions through `gamma_unit` with one shared global slot `Inr gk0`. This locale derives the edge and combine closure obligations from a `part_post_solution`, a sound D/G specification, the routing hooks, and a guarded solution reader. Concrete analyses supply the entry and callee-seed coverage obligations. The D/G layer transports abstract states at the caller, callee-result, and continuation slots. The trace semantics supplies the matched caller/callee relation, so this layer does not reconstruct activation pairing.

(11 more lines) | The routed generator interprets solutions through `gamma_unit` with one shared global slot `Inr gk0`. This locale derives the edge and combine closure obligations from a `part_post_solution`, a sound D/G specification, the routing hooks, and a guarded solution reader. Concrete analyses supply the entry and callee-seed coverage obligations. The D/G layer transports abstract states at the caller, callee-result, and continuation slots. The trace semantics supplies the matched caller/callee relation, so this layer does not reconstruct activation pairing. |

### src/Core/Solver/Context/DG/DG_Framework.thy

> An analysis chooses a flow-sensitive answer domain `D` and a flow-insensitive side-effect domain `G`. The framework keeps them opaque and stores them in separate components of `dg_state`; it never copies a global component into a local answer. `dg_edge_tree` and `dg_combine_tree` only project and repack those components, so their construction is independent of the concrete domains.

| definition | `unit_step_for` | 18 | definition unit_step_for ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt;<br>   ('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state)<br>   =&gt; 'a abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state × 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "unit_step_for gs f d g =<br>     (let res = f (combine_env^# gs d g) in<br>      (restrict_global_for gs res, restrict_local_for gs res))" |  |

| datatype | `dg_state` | 49 | datatype ('l, 'g) dg_state = DG (locals: 'l) (globs: 'g)<br><br>instantiation dg_state :: (ord, ord) ord<br>begin | The solver's single value type must order local and global halves componentwise. Raw pairs cannot: `CFG_Def` imports `HOL-Library.Product_Lexorder`, so `'l × 'g` already carries the lexicographic order everywhere in this repository, and the componentwise `HOL-Library.Product_Order` instances clash with it. `dg_state` is the componentwise-ordered copy of `('l, 'g) split_state`. |

| definition | `dg_edge_tree` | 184 | definition dg_edge_tree ::<br>  "('dl::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot ⇒ 'dg::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot ⇒ 'dg × 'dl)<br>   ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp, unit, ('dl, 'dg) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "dg_edge_tree step u =<br>     QueryL u (λd. QueryG () (λg.<br>       Side () (DG bot (fst (step (locals d) (globs g))))<br>         (Answer (DG (snd (step (locals d) (globs g)))  bot))))" | Two independent opaque domains. An analysis provides `step : D ⇒ G ⇒ G × D` (transfer plus publication); the framework reads the local unknown's `D` and the global slot's `G`, and transports the step's `Side : G` and `Answer : D`. Slot packing (`DG _ bot` / `DG bot _`) is the encoding of the two-typed unknown space into the solver's single value type; the framework never looks inside `D` or `G`. |
| definition | `dg_combine_tree` | 222 | definition dg_combine_tree ::<br>  "(vname option ⇒ 'dl::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot ⇒ 'dg × 'dl)<br>   ⇒ vname option ⇒ pp ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp, unit, ('dl, 'dg) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "dg_combine_tree comb dst cc ex =<br>     QueryL cc (λdc. QueryL ex (λde. QueryG () (λg.<br>       Side () (DG bot (fst (comb dst (locals dc) (locals de) (globs g))))<br>         (Answer (DG (snd (comb dst (locals dc) (locals de) (globs g))) bot)))))" | Procedure-return combine: two `D` inputs (caller, callee exit), one `G`. |
| record | `dg_spec` | 349 | record ('dl, 'dg) dg_spec =<br>  dgs_nop        :: "'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_assign     :: "vname ⇒ aexp ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_random     :: "vname ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_assume     :: "bexp ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_assume_not :: "bexp ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_enter      :: "vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_combine_env    :: "'dl ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>  dgs_combine_assign :: "vname option ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl ⇒ 'dg × 'dl" | Procedure-return combine is split the same way Goblint's `Spec` splits it: an environment merge (`dgs_combine_env`, caller-local + callee-exit-local + global, no destination) followed by a return-value assign (`dgs_combine_assign`, reads the callee-exit's return slot, writes the destination, and packages the two-typed `('dg, 'dl)` answer). `'dl`/`'dg` stay fully opaque -- the framework does not assume either field can share a generic assign the way the flat layer's `abs_state`-typed `combine_assign^#` can, so both halves are analysis-supplied. |
| definition | `dgs_combine` | 363 | definition dgs_combine ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname option ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg × 'dl"<br>where<br>  "dgs_combine S dst dc de g = dgs_combine_assign S dst de g (dgs_combine_env S dc de g)" | The composed combine, in the pre-split curried shape every existing caller already uses fully applied (`dgs_combine S dst dc de g`). Kept as a plain definition, not a record field, so the split above is the single source of truth and this cannot drift out of sync with it. |

| definition | `side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg` | 892 | definition side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg ::<br>  "(cfg ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp × edge_action) list)<br>   ⇒ ('c ⇒ 'k)<br>   ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c)<br>   ⇒ ((pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c) ⇒ 'c ⇒ call_action ⇒ pp ⇒ pp<br>        ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'h) dg_state) strategy_tree)<br>   ⇒ ((pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c) ⇒ 'c ⇒ pp<br>        ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'h) dg_state) strategy_tree list)<br>   ⇒ cfg<br>   ⇒ ('d::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot, 'h::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) dg_spec<br>   ⇒ 'd ⇒ 'd ⇒ 'h<br>   ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'h) dg_state) eqsT"<br>where<br>  "side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg pred_sel gkey route cmb extra g S bot0 s0d s0g =<br>     (λ(v, c).<br>... (8 more lines) | The one context-generic generator. Enter handling is routed by three hooks so that a monovariant and a context-sensitive analysis are both instances: • `pred_sel` selects the intra predecessors folded as Answers into a node. The monovariant instance uses `intra_predecessor_list` over `intra`; a callee entry over `calls` merges into the single callee context, while the context-sensitive instance instead publishes routed callee seeds. • `cmb` is the procedure-return combine tree (already fully abstract: the context-sensitive instance reads the callee exit under the routed context). • `extra c v` supplies additional per-node trees folded after the intra and combine trees. Their Answers add to the node's local accumulator and their `Side` effects are collected --- this is where a frame-entry seed *read* (a `QueryG` of the incoming seed slot) and the caller-side call-entry *publication* (a routed `Side` to the callee seed slot) live. The monovariant instance supplies `λ_ _. []`. |

### src/Core/Solver/Context/DG/DG_Soundness.thy

> Collecting soundness stated directly over an analysis's two domains `D` and `G`. The analysis supplies `gammaDG`, the joint meaning of a per-point Answer and the shared Side fact. Independent analyses use the intersection `gamma_dg d g = [[d]] Int [[g]]`; the homogeneous unit analysis uses `gamma_unit d g = [[d Sup g]]` because its transfer merges the slots. The locale `sound_dg_spec` assumes only monotonicity of `gammaDG` and semantic soundness of the analysis's edge and combine operations. It derives the structural post-fixpoint from the heterogeneous generator, then applies the collecting-semantics post-fixpoint theorem directly. No homogeneous equation system or representation transport appears in this proof.

(u, a, v) ∈ intra g ⟶ (v, ()) ∈ vars)<br>   ∧ (∀c dst fs as q k. (c, CallEdge dst fs as, FunctionEntry q, k) ∈ calls g<br>        ⟶ (FunctionEntry q, ()) ∈ vars)<br>   ∧ (∀c dst fs as q k. (c, CallEdge dst fs as, FunctionEntry q, k) ∈ calls g<br>        ⟶ (k, ()) ∈ vars)" | `vars_cover g vars` bundles the one recurring obligation every post-solution soundness theorem in this development needs: `vars` contains the CFG entry, every `intra` edge's target, and every call's callee entry and continuation. Global (not locale-local): every analysis instance and the executable pipeline cite it under the same name. |

The eight conjuncts are the two seed bounds plus a D/G pair for each of the three transfer kinds; each has its own named projection lemma below (`dg_postfix_entryD`, `dg_postfix_edgeD`, ...) so callers never navigate the conjunction positionally. |

(8 more lines) | The record an independent-transfer analysis denotes: every edge action advances each slot by its own transfer, the combine is structural in each slot. Any two sound transfers yield a `sound_dg_spec` instance; at `tfD = tfG` this is the homogeneous single-domain analysis as the diagonal. |
| definition | `gamma_unit` | 1703 | definition gamma_unit ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ 'a::sound_domain abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ store set"<br>where<br>  "gamma_unit gs d g = ⟦combine_env^# gs d g⟧" | `gamma_unit gs d g` is the meaning `unit_step_for` actually assumes of a point's `D`/`G` pair: `combine_env_abs` routes each name to the one component that owns it (`D` for locals, `G` for globals), never the raw lattice join, so an untouched name's precision in its owning component is not destroyed by the other component's unrelated default. |
| definition | `gamma_join` | 1820 | definition gamma_join ::<br>  "'a::sound_domain abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ store set"<br>where<br>  "gamma_join d g = ⟦d ⊔ g⟧" | `unit_dg_spec_placed` packages every D/G step through the raw lattice join (`d ⊔ g`) rather than `combine_env_abs`'s ownership routing: with a non-exclusive covering, a name can legitimately be tracked by both `keep_local` and `publish_side`, so there is no single owning component to route to, and the join is the only combinator that stays sound for every covering. `gamma_join` is that join-based target: \<open>gamma_join d g = ⟦d ⊔ g⟧\<close> never inspects the split, so any split whose two projections join back to the pre-split value is just as sound as any other. `unit_dg_spec_placed` exposes that split as two independent predicates, `keep_local`/`publish_side`; the only requirement is that every location is covered by at least one of them. Unlike `wf_split`, covering does not demand exclusivity: a location may sit in both, which is exactly the shape a privatized global needs (kept precisely in D for the current activation and also published to G for other activations). This is a genuinely different target from `gamma_unit`: the exclusive, storage-derived split that `unit_dg_spec_for` uses can route by ownership because it never has an overlapping name to arbitrate. |

### src/Core/Solver/Context/DG/DG_Transfer_Combinators.thy

> `dgs_enter` and `dgs_combine` already return the `'dg × 'dl` pair the framework's split demands: the global side effect and the local answer. Equations that call them read as `fst (dgs_enter S fs as d g)` / `snd (dgs_enter S fs as d g)` at every call site, which names the projection instead of the role. These abbreviations name the role; like the generic `Strategy_Tree_Combinators`, each is a plain syntax translation, so unfolding an equation's `_def` still exposes exactly `fst`/`snd` applied to `dgs_enter`/`dgs_combine`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| abbreviation | `enter_global` | 18 | abbreviation enter_global ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg"<br>where<br>  "enter_global S fs as d g ≡ fst (dgs_enter S fs as d g)" | `dgs_enter` and `dgs_combine` already return the `'dg × 'dl` pair the framework's split demands: the global side effect and the local answer. Equations that call them read as `fst (dgs_enter S fs as d g)` / `snd (dgs_enter S fs as d g)` at every call site, which names the projection instead of the role. These abbreviations name the role; like the generic `Strategy_Tree_Combinators`, each is a plain syntax translation, so unfolding an equation's `_def` still exposes exactly `fst`/`snd` applied to `dgs_enter`/`dgs_combine`. |
| abbreviation | `enter_local` | 23 | abbreviation enter_local ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dl"<br>where<br>  "enter_local S fs as d g ≡ snd (dgs_enter S fs as d g)" |  |
| abbreviation | `combine_global` | 28 | abbreviation combine_global ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname option ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dg"<br>where<br>  "combine_global S dst dc de g ≡ fst (dgs_combine S dst dc de g)" |  |
| abbreviation | `combine_local` | 33 | abbreviation combine_local ::<br>  "('dl, 'dg) dg_spec ⇒ vname option ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dl ⇒ 'dg ⇒ 'dl"<br>where<br>  "combine_local S dst dc de g ≡ snd (dgs_combine S dst dc de g)" |  |
| abbreviation | `publish_global` | 66 | abbreviation publish_global where<br>  "publish_global key x ≡ depend_on key (DG bot x) (answer (DG bot bot))" | Every value a DG equation reads or publishes is a `('d, 'd) dg_state` pair (`DG`), with the unused half fixed to `bot` by convention: a global-key publication -- the one shared `gk0` slot, or a routed seed slot, both living in the same global-key space `'g` -- carries its payload in the `globs` half; a local-key answer carries its payload in the `locals` half (`routed_extra`'s frame-entry read and `routed_cmb`'s final answer, both in `Routed_Context`, return the analysis's own local contribution this way). `publish_global`, `publish_seed`, and `answer_local` apply that convention so an equation never writes `DG`, `fst`, or `snd` directly -- each is still a plain syntax translation, so unfolding an equation's `_def` exposes exactly the `DG` term it names. `publish_global` and `publish_seed` are `depend_on` under the same `DG` convention, named for which global-key role the publication plays; a routed context-sensitive analysis (`Routed_Context`) uses one of each per call. Both are value-producing (no trailing continuation): as a `do`-block statement (no `←`), the published side effect is what matters and the answer -- `DG bot bot`, the same neutral value every `_cont` caller already supplied by hand -- is discarded by the bind. The `_cont` forms keep the explicit continuation for direct, non-`do` use. No explicit type signature is given -- as with `enter_global`/`combine_global`, an annotated signature forcing both `DG` halves to one shared type variable over-constrains unification wherever the caller and callee sides are inferred independently. |
| abbreviation | `publish_seed` | 69 | abbreviation publish_seed where<br>  "publish_seed key x ≡ depend_on key (DG bot x) (answer (DG bot bot))" |  |
| abbreviation | `publish_global_cont` | 72 | abbreviation publish_global_cont where<br>  "publish_global_cont key x cont ≡ depend_on key (DG bot x) cont" |  |
| abbreviation | `publish_seed_cont` | 75 | abbreviation publish_seed_cont where<br>  "publish_seed_cont key x cont ≡ depend_on key (DG bot x) cont" |  |
| abbreviation | `answer_local` | 78 | abbreviation answer_local where<br>  "answer_local x ≡ answer (DG x bot)" |  |
| abbreviation | `with_call` | 89 | abbreviation with_call ::<br>  "call_action ⇒ (vname option ⇒ vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'a) ⇒ 'a"<br>where<br>  "with_call ca f ≡ case ca of CallEdge dst fs as ⇒ f dst fs as"<br><br>end | `call_action` (`CFG_Def.thy`) has one constructor, so matching it is a total destructure, not a partial case split. `with_call` names that destructure once per call site instead of repeating \<open>case ca of CallEdge dst fs as ⇒ ...\<close> at every `dgs_enter`/`dgs_combine` call the site makes. |

### src/Core/Solver/Context/DG/Routed_Context.thy

> `dg_ctx_activation` already discharges EDGE (`dg_ctx_act_edge`) generically off the post-solution, independent of `route`/`cmb`/`extra`: intra edges never route. Its COMB analogue (`dg_ctx_act_comb_covered`) is generic in the same sense but still takes the tree's contribution as an assumption (`bound`) rather than deriving it, because `cmb` is an unconstrained parameter: a context-sensitive analysis whose entry-seed publication and return combine are hand-written has to rederive that same routing argument itself. This theory fixes `cmb` and `extra` to one canonical shape, parametric only in a routing function `route` and a seed-key injection `seed_key`, and discharges CALL and COMB as theorems of that shape: a k-call-string or a partial-tabulation context becomes an interpretation of this locale, not a second proof development.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `routed_cmb` | 31 | definition routed_cmb ::<br>  "('d::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot, 'd) dg_spec ⇒ 'k<br>   ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c)<br>   ⇒ 'c ⇒ call_action ⇒ pp ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'd) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "routed_cmb S gk0 route ctx ca cc ex =<br>     with_call ca (λdst _ _. do {<br>       caller_state ← read_local (cc, ctx);<br>       let ctx' = route cc ctx (locals caller_state) ca;<br>       callee_state ← read_local (ex, ctx');<br>       globals_state ← read_global gk0;<br>       let caller = locals caller_state;<br>       let callee = locals callee_state;<br>       let globals = globs globals_state;<br>       publish_global gk0 (combine_global S dst caller callee globals);<br>... (2 more lines) | Parameter order matches `dg_ctx_activation`'s `cmb` calling convention: the generator supplies `route` as `cmb`'s own first argument (`cmb route c ca cc ex` in `side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg`), so `routed_cmb S gk0`, closing over only the spec and the shared slot, is the value that instantiates `cmb`. |
| definition | `routed_extra` | 54 | definition routed_extra ::<br>  "cfg ⇒ ('d::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot, 'd) dg_spec<br>   ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'k) ⇒ 'k<br>   ⇒ (pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'c)<br>   ⇒ 'c ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'k, ('d, 'd) dg_state) strategy_tree list"<br>where<br>  "routed_extra g S seed_key gk0 route ctx v =<br>     (case v of FunctionEntry _ ⇒<br>        [do { seed_state ← read_global (seed_key v ctx); answer_local (globs seed_state) }]<br>       \| _ ⇒ [])<br>     @ map (λ(w, ca, k).<br>             with_call ca (λdst fs as. do {<br>               entry_state ← read_local (v, ctx);<br>               globals_state ← read_global gk0;<br>               let entry = locals entry_state;<br>... (7 more lines) | Per node `v`: if `v` is a callee entry, read back its own routed seed; for every outgoing call from `v`, publish the entered store into the callee's routed seed slot, reading the shared global `gk0` the same way `routed_cmb` does. The seed key is injected through an arbitrary `seed_key` function rather than fixed to any particular global-key type, so the same shape serves any routing policy. |
| locale | `routed_context` | 91 | locale routed_context =<br>  dg_ctx_activation S gs g gk0 route "routed_cmb S gk0" "routed_extra g S seed_key gk0"<br>    bot0 s0d s0g sigma vars x0 sg<br>  for S :: "('a::sound_domain abs_state, 'a abs_state) dg_spec"<br>    and gs :: "vname ⇒ bool"<br>    and g gk0 route bot0 s0d s0g sigma vars x0 sg<br>    and seed_key :: "pp ⇒ 'c ⇒ 'k" +<br>  fixes enterc :: "cfg_node ⇒ 'c ⇒ store ⇒ 'c"<br>  assumes finC[intro,simp]: "finite (calls g)"<br>    and seed_key_ne_gk0[simp]: "⋀p ctx. seed_key p ctx ≠ gk0"<br>    and route_enterc_agree:<br>    "⋀u ctx dst pars args p cont s.<br>       (u, ctx) ∈ vars<br>       ⟹ (u, CallEdge dst pars args, FunctionEntry p, cont) ∈ calls g<br>       ⟹       s ∈ gamma_unit gs (locals (sigma (Inl (u, ctx)))) (globs (sigma (Inr gk0)))<br>... (17 more lines) | Beyond `dg_ctx_activation`'s parameters: `seed_key` injects a routed `(pp, 'c)` pair into the global-key space; `enterc` is the trace-semantic context function keying the activation-local collecting semantics; and `route_enterc_agree` is the one agreement that cannot be discharged generically: at a real call edge, the equation-level `route`, evaluated on an abstract caller-local value, must agree with the semantic `enterc` on every concrete entered store the value concretizes to. Restricting the call action to an edge of `g` (rather than quantifying over every value of the `call_action` type) matches how CALL and COMB below only ever invoke this fact at a matched edge, and keeps the obligation provable for an abstract domain that is exact on edges actually present in the program without being exact everywhere. This is a per-instance proof obligation, not a locale theorem. |
| definition | `formals_context` | 395 | definition formals_context :: "vname list ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a list" where<br>  "formals_context pars d = map d pars" | A context type derived from the callee's declared formals rather than call-site history: `'a list`, one abstract value per formal, in the order `CallEdge` already carries them --- populated at compile time from the callee's own declaration, so no separate procedure-table lookup is needed here. `formals_context` is the plain per-variable projection (`'a abs_state` is `vname ⇒ 'a`, so this is just `map`); `formals_route` applies it to the entered local state via `enter_local`, the same enter transfer every other CALL obligation uses; `formals_enterc` is its trace-semantic counterpart, decoding the concrete entered store's formals the same way, given the point abstraction `decode` a domain provides for a concrete value and the CFG needed to look up a call site's own formal list. Neither definition mentions a domain-specific accessor beyond `decode` itself, so any domain reusing `routed_context` instantiates this pair once instead of hand-writing a per-formal projection, as `route_ivl`/`ivl_enterc` previously did. |
| definition | `formals_route` | 398 | definition formals_route ::<br>  "('a::sound_domain abs_state, 'a abs_state) dg_spec ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'a list"<br>where<br>  "formals_route S d ca =<br>     (case ca of CallEdge dst pars args ⇒ formals_context pars (enter_local S pars args d bot))" |  |
| definition | `formals_route_gen` | 407 | definition formals_route_gen ::<br>  "('a::sound_domain abs_state, 'a abs_state) dg_spec<br>     ⇒ pp ⇒ 'a list ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ call_action ⇒ 'a list"<br>where<br>  "formals_route_gen S u ctx d ca = formals_route S d ca" | The routing hook's exact calling convention (`dg_ctx_activation`'s `route`): generic over call site and caller context, using only the entered store, as `route_ivl_gen` already was. |
| definition | `formals_at_call_site` | 416 | definition formals_at_call_site :: "cfg ⇒ pp ⇒ vname list" where<br>  "formals_at_call_site g u =<br>     (case filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k). c = u) (cfg_calls_list g) of<br>        (_, CallEdge _ pars _, _, _) # _ ⇒ pars<br>      \| _ ⇒ [])" | The formals of the call originating at `u`: at most one, by the compiler's own invariant (`Voblint_CFG.VIMP_Proc_to_CFG` emits a single `CallEdge` per `Call`); `[]` if `u` has none. |
| definition | `formals_enterc` | 422 | definition formals_enterc ::<br>  "cfg ⇒ (int ⇒ 'a) ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ 'a list ⇒ store ⇒ 'a list"<br>where<br>  "formals_enterc g decode u ctx s = formals_context (formals_at_call_site g u) (decode ∘ s)" |  |
| definition | `call_action_at_call_site` | 436 | definition call_action_at_call_site :: "cfg ⇒ pp ⇒ call_action" where<br>  "call_action_at_call_site g u =<br>     (case filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k). c = u) (cfg_calls_list g) of<br>        (_, ca, _, _) # _ ⇒ ca<br>      \| _ ⇒ CallEdge None [] [])" | The whole matched `call_action` at a node, not only its formals: an `enterc` built purely from the caller's own solved abstract state (rather than by decoding the concrete entered store, as `formals_enterc` does) needs the callee's actuals too, to recompute the same `dgs_enter` the route itself already ran. Same convention as `formals_at_call_site`: the head of the filtered call list, `CallEdge None [] []` if `u` has no outgoing call. |

### src/Core/Solver/Context/TD_Side_Eff_Keyed_Gen.thy

> Global-key relabelling for strategy trees, the `QueryG` / `Side` analogue of `map_ltree`. The heterogeneous D/G generator relabels the local unknowns of a per-edge tree by context (`map_ltree`) and routes its global writes to the context's keyed slot with `map_gtree`; `traverse_rhs_map_gtree` shows the latter commutes with the denotation under the matching `map_sum`-pullback of the global slots, exactly as `traverse_rhs_map_ltree` does on the local side.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| primrec | `map_gtree` | 18 | primrec map_gtree ::<br>  "('g ⇒ 'h) ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree ⇒ ('x, 'h, 'd) strategy_tree" where<br>  "map_gtree r (Answer d) = Answer d"<br>\| "map_gtree r (QueryL y f) = QueryL y (λd. map_gtree r (f d))"<br>\| "map_gtree r (QueryG y f) = QueryG (r y) (λd. map_gtree r (f d))"<br>\| "map_gtree r (Side y d t) = Side (r y) d (map_gtree r t)" |  |
