### src/Core/Solver/Exec/Solver_Menu.thy

> The vendored side solver comes in several *update rules* --- disciplines for how a global's incoming contributions are combined: plain join, per-origin join, Apinis warrowing (widening on globals), and their combinations. They share one signature, so an example can run the *same* equation system under each discipline and compare the results in a single `value` or lemma, instead of duplicating a `definition` plus a read per solver. • `join` --- `TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve`: fold every contribution into one slot, join. Precise; no termination guarantee for unbounded global chains. • `per_origin` --- `TD_side_per_origin_Interp_solve`: keep each write origin's contribution separate, join per origin. Reproduces digest separation without the solver knowing about digests. • `warrow` --- `TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve`: widen globals for termination (narrow/warrow locals). Terminates on unbounded loops; over-approximates globals (collapses digest partitions). The menu omits `TD_side_warrowing_per_origin_Interp_solve` because that interpretation does not code-generate for these equation systems. It also omits `TD_side_bounded_narrowing_Interp_solve`, whose executable contract is not established here.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `side_solver` | 50 | type_synonym ('x, 'g, 'd) side_solver =<br>  "('x, 'g, 'd) eqsT ⇒ 'x ⇒ ('x set × ('x + 'g ⇒ 'd))" |  |
| definition | `solver_menu` | 53 | definition solver_menu where<br>  "solver_menu =<br>     [(STR ''join'',       TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve),<br>      (STR ''per_origin'', TD_side_per_origin_Interp_solve),<br>      (STR ''warrow'',     TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve)]" |  |
| definition | `run_menu` | 62 | definition run_menu where<br>  "run_menu gs eqs entry k var =<br>     map (λ(nm, solve).<br>       (nm, lookup_resolved_st_q (snd (solve eqs entry) k) (location_of gs var)))<br>       solver_menu"<br><br>end | Read one global/local slot's variable under every solver on the menu, in one call. `eqs` the equation system, `entry` the solver entry unknown, `k` the slot to read (e.g. `Inr ctx` for a keyed global), `var` the program variable. |

### src/Core/Solver/Exec/Solver_Side_RG.thy

> If every reachable side contribution is fixed by `restrict_global_resolved_q`, the solver keeps every global slot in that image: slots start at bottom, joins preserve the image, and only such contributions reach them. This yields `inr_slot_locals_bot` without a least-solution argument. The proof is domain-generic but intentionally phrased at the executable bridge's state projection.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `rg_val` | 13 | definition rg_val :: "('a::bot) resolved_st_q ⇒ bool" where<br>  "rg_val d ↔ (∀x. lookup_resolved_st_q d (Local_Location x) = bot)" | If every reachable side contribution is fixed by `restrict_global_resolved_q`, the solver keeps every global slot in that image: slots start at bottom, joins preserve the image, and only such contributions reach them. This yields `inr_slot_locals_bot` without a least-solution argument. The proof is domain-generic but intentionally phrased at the executable bridge's state projection. |
| abbreviation | `rg_state` | 48 | abbreviation rg_state :: "(pp, unit, ('a::bot) resolved_st_q) state ⇒ bool" where<br>  "rg_state s ≡ (∀g. rg_val (state.σ s (Inr g)))" |  |
| abbreviation | `rg_sides` | 51 | abbreviation rg_sides :: "(unit ⇒ ('a::bot) resolved_st_q) ⇒ bool" where<br>  "rg_sides sa ≡ (∀g. rg_val (sa g))" |  |
| abbreviation | `rg_ug` | 316 | abbreviation rg_ug :: "('x, 'g, ('a::bot) resolved_st_q) ug_state ⇒ bool" where<br>  "rg_ug ug ≡ (∀g orig. rg_val (rho_lookup (\&lt;rho&gt; ug) g orig))" | `update_global_warrowing_apinis` additionally reads its own per-origin bookkeeping (`\<rho>`) to compute `sup_over_origins`, which `update_global_always_join` never does --- so, unlike `TD_side_always_join_rg_ind`, the induction here must also carry an invariant on the solver's `ug_state` component alongside `rg_state`. |
| abbreviation | `rg_both` | 319 | abbreviation rg_both where<br>  "rg_both state ug ≡ rg_state state ∧ rg_ug ug" |  |

### src/Core/Solver/Strategy_Tree/Strategy_Tree_Combinators.thy

> The verified TD solver's instruction set is four constructors on `('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree`: `QueryL` reads a local unknown, `QueryG` reads a global unknown, `Side` publishes a value under a global key and continues, `Answer` yields the local result. Equation authors construct trees directly with these constructors, so an equation such as ``combine the caller state, the routed callee state, and the globals'' reads as nested nameless lambdas over `QueryL`/`QueryG` rather than as the combine it computes. The abbreviations below rename the four constructors to their equation-authoring role. Each is a plain syntax translation, not a new constant: a term written with the renamed form parses to and unfolds as exactly the constructor term it abbreviates, so every existing lemma about `QueryL`/`QueryG`/`Side`/`Answer` -- and every proof that unfolds an equation's `_def` and pattern-matches on those constructors -- applies unchanged.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| abbreviation | `answer` | 26 | abbreviation answer :: "'d ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree" where<br>  "answer d ≡ Answer d" | The verified TD solver's instruction set is four constructors on `('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree`: `QueryL` reads a local unknown, `QueryG` reads a global unknown, `Side` publishes a value under a global key and continues, `Answer` yields the local result. Equation authors construct trees directly with these constructors, so an equation such as ``combine the caller state, the routed callee state, and the globals'' reads as nested nameless lambdas over `QueryL`/`QueryG` rather than as the combine it computes. The abbreviations below rename the four constructors to their equation-authoring role. Each is a plain syntax translation, not a new constant: a term written with the renamed form parses to and unfolds as exactly the constructor term it abbreviates, so every existing lemma about `QueryL`/`QueryG`/`Side`/`Answer` -- and every proof that unfolds an equation's `_def` and pattern-matches on those constructors -- applies unchanged. |
| abbreviation | `read_local` | 40 | abbreviation read_local :: "'x ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree" where<br>  "read_local key ≡ QueryL key answer" | `read_local`/`read_global` are value-producing: the queried value itself is the tree's answer, so `seqcomp_tree` (bind for this monad, `Strategy_Tree_Monad.thy`, given `do`-notation via `Strategy_Tree_Do.thy`'s `adhoc_overloading`) sequences a read directly as `do { d ← read_local key; ... }`. Unfolding `seqcomp_tree`'s two defining equations on a bound read reduces it to exactly the `_cont` form below, so proofs that need the flat constructor shape add `seqcomp_tree.simps` to their simp set once, then proceed as before. |
| abbreviation | `read_global` | 43 | abbreviation read_global :: "'g ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree" where<br>  "read_global key ≡ QueryG key answer" |  |
| abbreviation | `read_local_cont` | 52 | abbreviation read_local_cont ::<br>  "'x ⇒ ('d ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree) ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "read_local_cont key k ≡ QueryL key k" | `_cont` suffix: the original continuation-passing shape, kept for direct, single-step low-level use (no bind/do-notation needed) and as the primitive `read_local`/`read_global` themselves compile down to. |
| abbreviation | `read_global_cont` | 57 | abbreviation read_global_cont ::<br>  "'g ⇒ ('d ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree) ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "read_global_cont key k ≡ QueryG key k" |  |
| abbreviation | `depend_on` | 74 | abbreviation depend_on ::<br>  "'g ⇒ 'd ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "depend_on key val cont ≡ Side key val cont"<br><br>end | `depend_on key val cont` publishes `val` as a side effect on the global unknown `key` and continues as `cont`. The name reflects the direction an equation author reasons in: ``this equation also depends on -- contributes to -- `key`'', not the solver-internal notion of a queued side write. Its trailing continuation is what a do-block statement (no `←`) needs, so `depend_on` itself keeps this shape rather than gaining a value-producing twin: statement-form combinators built on it (`publish_global`, `publish_seed`, `DG_Transfer_Combinators.thy`) supply `answer bot` as that continuation. |

### src/Core/Solver/Strategy_Tree/Strategy_Tree_Monad.thy

> seqcomp_tree sequences two strategy trees: run t, pass its Answer value to continuation k. Monadic bind for the strategy tree monad; the key combinator for building effectful IP folds from per-edge trees.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| primrec | `seqcomp_tree` | 13 | primrec seqcomp_tree ::<br>  "('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree<br>   ⇒ ('d ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree)<br>   ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "seqcomp_tree (Answer v)   k = k v"<br>\| "seqcomp_tree (QueryL u f) k = QueryL u  (λd. seqcomp_tree (f d) k)"<br>\| "seqcomp_tree (QueryG g f) k = QueryG g  (λd. seqcomp_tree (f d) k)"<br>\| "seqcomp_tree (Side g v t) k = Side g v  (seqcomp_tree t k)" | seqcomp_tree sequences two strategy trees: run t, pass its Answer value to continuation k. Monadic bind for the strategy tree monad; the key combinator for building effectful IP folds from per-edge trees. |
| definition | `static_deps` | 92 | definition static_deps :: "('x, 'g, 'd::order_bot) strategy_tree ⇒ bool" where<br>  "static_deps t ↔ (∀σ1 σ2. dep_aux σ1 t = dep_aux σ2 t)" | static_deps t: the set of unknowns queried in t does not depend on the environment. The TD solver's mono_deps precondition needs this. It holds for trees whose QueryL/QueryG skeleton is fixed and only the Side/Answer values vary with the environment (the safe invariant for effectful TF trees). |

### src/Core/Solver/TD_Side/TD_Side_CFG.thy

> Generic base for the side-effecting interprocedural solver. A locals/globals split on abstract states: restrict_local / restrict_global keep one component (the other set to bot), so their join recovers the original state. side_env combines the local unknown at a program point with the single global unknown. The interprocedural strategy tree and transfer functions live in TD_Side_Tree; monotonicity and solver preconditions live in TD_Side_Eff_Bounds and TD_Side_Eff_Cone_Lemmas.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `restrict_local_for` | 25 | definition restrict_local_for ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state" where<br>  "restrict_local_for gs σ = (%x. if gs x then bot else σ x)" |  |
| definition | `restrict_global_for` | 29 | definition restrict_global_for ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state" where<br>  "restrict_global_for gs σ = (%x. if gs x then σ x else bot)" |  |
| definition | `side_env` | 163 | definition side_env ::<br>  "(pp + 'g::finite =&gt; 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) =&gt; pp =&gt; 'a abs_state" where<br>  "side_env σ v = σ (Inl v) ⊔ glob_env σ" |  |
| definition | `side_env_g` | 177 | definition side_env_g ::<br>  "(pp + 'g =&gt; 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) =&gt; 'g =&gt; pp =&gt; 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "side_env_g σ g v = σ (Inl v) ⊔ σ (Inr g)" |  |
| definition | `unit_edge_tree` | 203 | definition unit_edge_tree ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool)<br>   =&gt; ('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state =&gt; 'a abs_state)<br>   =&gt; (unit, 'a) edge_tf_tree"<br>where<br>  "unit_edge_tree gs f u =<br>     QueryL u (λsu. QueryG () (λg.<br>       let res = f (su ⊔ g) in<br>       Side () (restrict_global_for gs res)<br>         (Answer (restrict_local_for gs res))))" | Unit-global effectful trees query the local program point and the unit global slot, compute one abstract post-state, then split that result between the local Answer and the global Side contribution consumed by TD_side. |
| definition | `unit_combine_tree` | 217 | definition unit_combine_tree ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; vname option =&gt; pp =&gt; pp<br>   =&gt; (pp, unit, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "unit_combine_tree gs dst cc ex =<br>     QueryL cc (λsc. QueryL ex (λse. QueryG () (λg.<br>       let res = combine_collect_abs gs dst (sc ⊔ g) (se ⊔ g) in<br>       Side () (restrict_global_for gs res)<br>         (Answer (restrict_local_for gs res)))))" |  |
| definition | `local_bot_on_locals` | 288 | definition local_bot_on_locals ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "local_bot_on_locals gs g ↔ (∀x. ¬ gs x ⟶ g x = bot)" | When an edge transfer preserves globals and only reads locals, the tree can query the source local unknown without `QueryG`. Soundness and post-fixpoint bounds use `etf_full` joined with `glob_env`. |
| definition | `local_edge_invariant` | 351 | definition local_edge_invariant ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; ('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state) ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "local_edge_invariant gs f ↔<br>     (∀su g. local_bot_on_locals gs g ⟶<br>        f (restrict_local_for gs su ⊔ g) =<br>        restrict_local_for gs (f (restrict_local_for gs su)) ⊔ g)" |  |
| definition | `local_edge_tree` | 413 | definition local_edge_tree ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; ('a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>   ⇒ (unit, 'a) edge_tf_tree"<br>where<br>  "local_edge_tree gs f u =<br>     QueryL u (λsu. Answer<br>       (restrict_local_for gs (f (restrict_local_for gs su)) ⊔ restrict_global_for gs su))" |  |
| definition | `unit_etf_of_transfer` | 586 | definition unit_etf_of_transfer ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; 'a::sound_domain domain_transfer ⇒ (unit, 'a) effectful_domain_transfer"<br>where<br>  "unit_etf_of_transfer gs tf = ⦇<br>    etf_nop        = (λu. unit_edge_tree gs (apply_tf tf EA_Nop) u),<br>    etf_assign     = (λx e u. unit_edge_tree gs (apply_tf tf (EA_Assign x e)) u),<br>    etf_random     = (λx u. unit_edge_tree gs (apply_tf tf (EA_Random x)) u),<br>    etf_assume     = (λb u. unit_edge_tree gs (apply_tf tf (EA_Assume b)) u),<br>    etf_assume_not = (λb u. unit_edge_tree gs (apply_tf tf (EA_AssumeNot b)) u),<br>    etf_enter      = (λxs es u. unit_edge_tree gs (tf_enter tf xs es) u),<br>    etf_combine    = unit_combine_tree gs<br>  ⦈" |  |
| definition | `mixed_etf_edge_tree` | 599 | definition mixed_etf_edge_tree ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; 'a::sound_domain domain_transfer ⇒ edge_action ⇒ (unit, 'a) edge_tf_tree"<br>where<br>  "mixed_etf_edge_tree gs tf a u =<br>    (if local_edge_action gs a then local_edge_tree gs (apply_tf tf a) u<br>     else unit_edge_tree gs (apply_tf tf a) u)" |  |
| definition | `mixed_etf_of_transfer` | 606 | definition mixed_etf_of_transfer ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; 'a::sound_domain domain_transfer ⇒ (unit, 'a) effectful_domain_transfer"<br>where<br>  "mixed_etf_of_transfer gs tf = ⦇<br>    etf_nop        = mixed_etf_edge_tree gs tf EA_Nop,<br>    etf_assign     = (λx e. mixed_etf_edge_tree gs tf (EA_Assign x e)),<br>    etf_random     = (λx. mixed_etf_edge_tree gs tf (EA_Random x)),<br>    etf_assume     = (λb. mixed_etf_edge_tree gs tf (EA_Assume b)),<br>    etf_assume_not = (λb. mixed_etf_edge_tree gs tf (EA_AssumeNot b)),<br>    etf_enter      = (λxs es u. unit_edge_tree gs (tf_enter tf xs es) u),<br>    etf_combine    = unit_combine_tree gs<br>  ⦈" |  |

### src/Core/Solver/TD_Side/TD_Side_Eff_Bounds.thy

> Monotonicity of the effectful equation system for an arbitrary etf follows from a per-tree contract: each edge, enter and combine tree is monotone in the environment. A genuinely effectful etf supplies this contract directly, e.g. via seqcomp_mono on its QueryL/QueryG/Side construction. The per-tree contract is stated on traverse_rhs of the trees the fold composes (apply_etf etf a u for ordinary edges, etf_enter etf fs as cl for call entries, etf_combine etf dst cc ex for caller continuations). Everything here is generic in the named-global type 'g; the global-side closure bounds (etf_combined_le_eff / etf_enter_combined_le_eff / etf_combine_combined_le_eff) route the per-name side bound through glob_env, so they require 'g::finite.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `strip_inr_globals` | 583 | definition strip_inr_globals ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) =&gt; (pp + 'g::finite ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state)<br>   ⇒ pp + 'g ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "strip_inr_globals gs σ k = (case k of Inl p ⇒ σ (Inl p) \| Inr g ⇒ restrict_global_for gs (σ (Inr g)))" |  |

### src/Core/Solver/TD_Side/TD_Side_Eff_Pipeline.thy

> The pipeline derives the solver interface from monotonicity and static dependencies of each strategy tree. A partial post-solution bounds every contribution, and a sound effectful transfer turns those bounds into collecting-semantics soundness. Effectful analyses discharge the tree contracts compositionally.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `threefold_mono` | 19 | definition threefold_mono ::<br>  "('x, 'g, 'd::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) eqsT ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "threefold_mono T ≡ is_mono_eq T ∧ mono_sides T ∧ mono_deps T" | `threefold_mono` bundles the conditions required by the optimized solver: equation values and side effects are monotone in the environment, while dependency sets can only shrink as the environment grows. Together they guarantee a least partial post-solution. |
| definition | `cone_compatible_etf` | 86 | definition cone_compatible_etf ::<br>  "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ ('g::finite, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) effectful_domain_transfer ⇒ bool"<br>where<br>  "cone_compatible_etf gs etf ≡<br>     (∀b z σ'. Inl z ∈ dep_aux σ' (apply_etf etf b z)) ∧<br>     (∀c2 e2 d2 σ'. Inl c2 ∈ dep_aux σ' (etf_combine etf d2 c2 e2)) ∧<br>     (∀c2 e2 d2 σ'. Inl e2 ∈ dep_aux σ' (etf_combine etf d2 c2 e2)) ∧<br>     (∀cl fs as σ'. Inl cl ∈ dep_aux σ' (etf_enter etf fs as cl)) ∧<br>     (∀a u. static_deps (apply_etf etf a u)) ∧<br>     (∀cc ex dst. static_deps (etf_combine etf dst cc ex)) ∧<br>     (∀cl fs as. static_deps (etf_enter etf fs as cl)) ∧<br>     (∀a u σ' g. local_bot_on_locals gs (sides_of_rhs (apply_etf etf a u) σ' (Inr g))) ∧<br>     (∀cc ex dst σ' g. local_bot_on_locals gs (sides_of_rhs (etf_combine etf dst cc ex) σ' (Inr g))) ∧<br>     (∀cl fs as σ' g. local_bot_on_locals gs (sides_of_rhs (etf_enter etf fs as cl) σ' (Inr g)))" | cone_compatible_etf bundles the five structural conditions that guarantee the solver's stable set reaches the program entry (the cone argument in side_cone_in_vars_eff). The first three are self-reference conditions: each edge/combine tree queries its own source program points. The last two say the queried unknowns are independent of the environment (static_deps), so the cone is determined structurally rather than by the abstract state. |

### src/Core/Solver/TD_Side/TD_Side_RHS_Generator.thy

> `unit_etf_of_transfer` and `mixed_etf_of_transfer` share combine-tree shape and differ only in per-edge tree dispatch. These locales bundle the transfer-tree shape, dependency-staticness facts, and solver-side monotonicity packaging for each shape.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| locale | `sound_rhs_generator_base` | 16 | locale sound_rhs_generator_base =<br>  fixes gs :: "vname ⇒ bool"<br>    and etf :: "(unit, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) effectful_domain_transfer"<br>  assumes comb[simp]:<br>    "⋀cc ex dst.<br>       etf_combine etf dst cc ex =<br>       unit_combine_tree gs dst cc ex"<br>begin |  |
| locale | `sound_rhs_generator_static` | 39 | locale sound_rhs_generator_static = sound_rhs_generator_base<br>begin |  |
| locale | `mixed_rhs_generator` | 70 | locale mixed_rhs_generator = sound_rhs_generator_static +<br>  fixes F :: "edge_action ⇒ 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>    and Fe :: "vname list ⇒ aexp list ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>  assumes edge:<br>      "⋀a u.<br>         apply_etf etf a u = local_edge_tree gs (F a) u<br>       ∨ apply_etf etf a u = unit_edge_tree gs (F a) u"<br>    and enter[simp]:<br>      "⋀cl fs as.<br>         etf_enter etf fs as cl = unit_edge_tree gs (Fe fs as) cl"<br>begin | Each per-edge tree is a local or a unit edge tree. The disjunction form subsumes both the pure-unit generator (always the unit disjunct) and a genuine local/unit mix, so one locale covers both. |
| locale | `mixed_rhs_generator_mono` | 120 | locale mixed_rhs_generator_mono = mixed_rhs_generator +<br>  assumes F_mono:<br>      "⋀a s1 s2. s1 ≤ s2 ⟹ F a s1 ≤ F a s2"<br>    and Fe_mono:<br>      "⋀fs as s1 s2. s1 ≤ s2 ⟹ Fe fs as s1 ≤ Fe fs as s2"<br>begin |  |

### src/Core/Solver/TD_Side/TD_Side_Tree.thy

> Each equation right-hand side folds three contribution families: ordinary CFG edges, procedure-entry transfers, and return/combine transfers. The accumulator joins their local results, while each strategy tree may emit side contributions to named global slots. A return/combine tree joins caller locals with callee-exit globals. Its local result flows to the resume point, and its global result flows through side effects.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `fold_rhs_trees` | 27 | fun fold_rhs_trees ::<br>  "'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot<br>   ⇒ ('k, 'g, 'a) strategy_tree list<br>   ⇒ ('k, 'g, 'a) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "fold_rhs_trees acc [] = Answer acc"<br>\| "fold_rhs_trees acc (t # ts) =<br>     seqcomp_tree t (λres. fold_rhs_trees (acc ⊔ res) ts)" | The generic fold composes a list of contribution trees with `seqcomp_tree`. `side_contribution_trees` assembles the three source families in their executable order; both construction and denotation use that same list. |
| definition | `side_contribution_trees` | 36 | definition side_contribution_trees ::<br>  "('g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) effectful_domain_transfer<br>   ⇒ (pp × edge_action) list<br>   ⇒ (pp × vname list × aexp list) list<br>   ⇒ (pp × vname option × pp) list<br>   ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'a abs_state) strategy_tree list"<br>where<br>  "side_contribution_trees etf es ens cs =<br>     map (λ(u, a). apply_etf etf a u) es @<br>     map (λ(cl, fs, as). etf_enter etf fs as cl) ens @<br>     map (λ(cc, dst, ex). etf_combine etf dst cc ex) cs" |  |
| definition | `side_rhs_fold_eff` | 48 | definition side_rhs_fold_eff ::<br>  "('g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) effectful_domain_transfer<br>   ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>   ⇒ (pp × edge_action) list<br>   ⇒ (pp × vname list × aexp list) list<br>   ⇒ (pp × vname option × pp) list<br>   ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'a abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "side_rhs_fold_eff etf acc es ens cs =<br>     fold_rhs_trees acc (side_contribution_trees etf es ens cs)" |  |
| definition | `entry_seed_list` | 87 | definition entry_seed_list :: "cfg ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp × vname list × aexp list) list" where<br>  "entry_seed_list g v =<br>     map (λ(c, ca). case ca of CallEdge dst fs as ⇒ (c, fs, as)) (entry_call_list g v)" | The callee-entry seed list: each incoming call at callee entry `v` contributes its formals/actuals so the fold can invoke `etf_enter` on the caller state. |
| definition | `make_side_rhs_tree_eff` | 91 | definition make_side_rhs_tree_eff ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ ('g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) effectful_domain_transfer<br>   ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'g ⇒ pp<br>   ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'a abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "make_side_rhs_tree_eff gs g etf bot0 s0 gseed v =<br>     (let acc0 = (if v = cfg_entry g then bot0 ⊔ restrict_local_for gs s0 else bot0);<br>          t    = side_rhs_fold_eff etf acc0<br>                   (intra_predecessor_list g v) (entry_seed_list g v)<br>                   (return_call_list g v)<br>      in if v = cfg_entry g then Side gseed (restrict_global_for gs s0) t else t)" |  |
| definition | `side_cfg_T_eff` | 103 | definition side_cfg_T_eff ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool) ⇒ cfg ⇒ ('g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) effectful_domain_transfer<br>   ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'g<br>   ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'a abs_state) eqsT"<br>where<br>  "side_cfg_T_eff gs g etf bot0 s0 gseed = make_side_rhs_tree_eff gs g etf bot0 s0 gseed" |  |
| fun | `fold_rhs_values` | 112 | fun fold_rhs_values ::<br>  "'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot<br>   ⇒ ('k + 'g ⇒ 'a)<br>   ⇒ ('k, 'g, 'a) strategy_tree list<br>   ⇒ 'a"<br>where<br>  "fold_rhs_values acc σ [] = acc"<br>\| "fold_rhs_values acc σ (t # ts) =<br>     fold_rhs_values (acc ⊔ traverse_rhs t σ) σ ts" |  |
| definition | `side_acc_eff` | 122 | definition side_acc_eff ::<br>  "('g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) effectful_domain_transfer<br>   ⇒ 'a abs_state<br>   ⇒ (pp + 'g ⇒ 'a abs_state)<br>   ⇒ (pp × edge_action) list<br>   ⇒ (pp × vname list × aexp list) list<br>   ⇒ (pp × vname option × pp) list ⇒ 'a abs_state"<br>where<br>  "side_acc_eff etf acc σ es ens cs =<br>     fold_rhs_values acc σ (side_contribution_trees etf es ens cs)" |  |
| definition | `edge_constraint_tree` | 181 | definition edge_constraint_tree ::<br>  "('g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) effectful_domain_transfer<br>   ⇒ pp ⇒ edge_action ⇒ pp ⇒ (pp, 'g, 'a abs_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "edge_constraint_tree etf u a v = apply_etf etf a u" | Each incoming CFG edge contributes the transfer of its source unknown to the target equation. The transfer depends on the source point and edge action; the target point selects the equation that receives the contribution. `edge_constraint_tree` exposes this contribution as a strategy tree. |
| primrec | `map_ltree` | 404 | primrec map_ltree ::<br>  "('x ⇒ 'y) ⇒ ('x, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree ⇒ ('y, 'g, 'd) strategy_tree" where<br>  "map_ltree h (Answer d) = Answer d"<br>\| "map_ltree h (QueryL y f) = QueryL (h y) (λd. map_ltree h (f d))"<br>\| "map_ltree h (QueryG y f) = QueryG y (λd. map_ltree h (f d))"<br>\| "map_ltree h (Side y d t) = Side y d (map_ltree h t)" | Context-sensitivity reindexes the local unknown `pp` to `pp × 'c`. A per-edge tree `apply_etf etf a u` queries only the single predecessor `u`, and a combine tree `etf_combine etf dst cc ex` queries the caller `cc` and the callee exit `ex`; so the context routing of either is captured by a position-aware relabelling `h :: pp ⇒ pp × 'c` of the `QueryL` targets (intra: `u ↦ (u, c)`; combine: caller `↦ (cc, c)`, callee `↦ (ex, c')`). `map_ltree` performs that relabelling; `traverse_rhs_map_ltree` shows it commutes with the denotation under the matching `map_sum`-pullback of the unknown environment, so a context-indexed equation system's denotation is the original one read against the relabelled environment. Globals (`QueryG` / `Side`) are untouched. |
| definition | `side_cfg_T_eff_ctx` | 427 | definition side_cfg_T_eff_ctx ::<br>  "(vname =&gt; bool)<br>   ⇒ ('c ⇒ vname option ⇒ pp ⇒ pp<br>     ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'g, 'a abs_state) strategy_tree)<br>   ⇒ cfg ⇒ ('g, 'a::bounded_semilattice_sup_bot) effectful_domain_transfer<br>   ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'a abs_state ⇒ 'g<br>   ⇒ (pp × 'c, 'g, 'a abs_state) eqsT"<br>where<br>  "side_cfg_T_eff_ctx gs cmb g etf bot0 s0 gseed =<br>     (λ(v, c).<br>        let acc0 = (if v = cfg_entry g then bot0 ⊔ restrict_local_for gs s0 else bot0);<br>            intra = map (λ(u, a). map_ltree (λw. (w, c)) (apply_etf etf a u))<br>                        (intra_predecessor_list g v);<br>            enter = map (λ(cl, fs, as). map_ltree (λw. (w, c)) (etf_enter etf fs as cl))<br>                        (entry_seed_list g v);<br>... (3 more lines) | The equation for `(v, c)` statically relabels ordinary-edge and entry trees to context `c`. Return/combine trees come from the supplied builder `cmb`, which may route the callee-exit query through a context computed from the caller state. Keeping `cmb` explicit separates context routing from the domain transfer record. |

## Examples

### src/Examples/CFG/Example_Compile_Baseline.thy

> Executable diagnostics for the compiled CFG, recorded before any compiler change. The structural successor relation `cfg_succ_rel` is set-valued; `succ_list` is its list view, with one clause per source of that relation: INTRA, ENTRY, COMB_CALLER, and COMB_RESULT.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `succ_list` | 20 | definition succ_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "succ_list g u =<br>     map (λ(s, a, v). v) (filter (λ(s, a, v). s = u) (cfg_intra_list g))<br>   @ concat<br>       (map (λ(c, ca, ce, k).<br>               (if c = u then [ce, k] else [])<br>             @ (case ce of<br>                  FunctionEntry q ⇒ (if u = FunctionResult q then [k] else [])<br>                \| _ ⇒ []))<br>         (cfg_calls_list g))" |  |
| definition | `all_nodes_list` | 36 | definition all_nodes_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "all_nodes_list g =<br>     remdups<br>       (concat (map (λ(u, a, v). [u, v]) (cfg_intra_list g))<br>      @ concat (map (λ(c, ca, ce, k). [c, ce, k]) (cfg_calls_list g))<br>      @ [cfg_entry g])" | Every node mentioned by the graph, deduplicated: intra endpoints, call sites, callee entries, call continuations, and the graph entry. |
| definition | `entry_roots` | 46 | definition entry_roots :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "entry_roots g =<br>     filter (λv. case v of FunctionEntry q ⇒ True \| _ ⇒ False) (all_nodes_list g)" | Roots for pruning: every procedure entry node in the graph, so that a procedure never called from `main` still counts as reachable rather than as compiler debris. |
| definition | `reach_step` | 50 | definition reach_step :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "reach_step g vs = remdups (vs @ concat (map (succ_list g) vs))" |  |
| definition | `reach_from` | 56 | definition reach_from :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "reach_from g roots = (reach_step g ^^ length (all_nodes_list g)) roots" | One round of `reach_step` adds at least one node until closure, so iterating as often as there are nodes saturates. |
| definition | `reach_list` | 59 | definition reach_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "reach_list g = reach_from g (entry_roots g)" |  |
| definition | `dead_list` | 62 | definition dead_list :: "cfg ⇒ cfg_node list" where<br>  "dead_list g = filter (λv. v ∉ set (reach_list g)) (all_nodes_list g)" |  |
| definition | `nop_edge_list` | 65 | definition nop_edge_list :: "cfg ⇒ (cfg_node × edge_action × cfg_node) list" where<br>  "nop_edge_list g = filter (λ(u, a, v). a = EA_Nop) (cfg_intra_list g)" |  |
| definition | `cfg_report` | 73 | definition cfg_report :: "cfg ⇒ nat × nat × nat × nat × nat" where<br>  "cfg_report g =<br>     (length (all_nodes_list g), length (dead_list g), length (cfg_intra_list g),<br>      length (nop_edge_list g), length (cfg_calls_list g))" | `(nodes, dead nodes, intra edges, nop edges, call edges)` --- the row recorded for each regression program, so a compiler change shows up as a diff of these numbers. |
| definition | `prog_cfg` | 80 | definition prog_cfg :: "imp_prog ⇒ cfg" where<br>  "prog_cfg P = compile_prog (prog_table P) (prog_procs P) (STR ''main'') (prog_main P)" |  |
| definition | `p01_skip` | 88 | definition p01_skip :: imp_prog where<br>  "p01_skip = program { void f() { skip } void main() { f() } }" | Each shape is the body of `f`, with `main` reduced to a single call, so the `main` contribution to every row is constant and the differences isolate the shape. |
| definition | `p02_assign` | 91 | definition p02_assign :: imp_prog where<br>  "p02_assign = program { void f() { x := 1 } void main() { f() } }" |  |
| definition | `p03_return` | 94 | definition p03_return :: imp_prog where<br>  "p03_return = program { void f() { return 1 } void main() { f() } }" |  |
| definition | `p04_return_then_dead` | 97 | definition p04_return_then_dead :: imp_prog where<br>  "p04_return_then_dead = program { void f() { return 1; x := 2 } void main() { f() } }" |  |
| definition | `p05_if_both_return` | 100 | definition p05_if_both_return :: imp_prog where<br>  "p05_if_both_return =<br>     program { void f() { if (x &lt; 1) { return 1 } else { return 2 } } void main() { f() } }" |  |
| definition | `p06_if_one_returns` | 104 | definition p06_if_one_returns :: imp_prog where<br>  "p06_if_one_returns =<br>     program { void f() { if (x &lt; 1) { return 1 } else { y := 2 } } void main() { f() } }" |  |
| definition | `p07_while_body_returns` | 108 | definition p07_while_body_returns :: imp_prog where<br>  "p07_while_body_returns =<br>     program { void f() { while (x &lt; 1) { return 1 } } void main() { f() } }" |  |

| definition | `factorial_program` | 119 | definition factorial_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "factorial_program = program {<br><br>     void fac(n) {<br>       if (n &lt; 2) {<br>         return 1<br>       } else {<br>         tmp := fac(n - 1);<br>         return n * tmp<br>       }<br>     }<br><br>     void main() {<br>       N := 8;<br>       r := fac(N)<br>... (2 more lines) |  |
| definition | `factorial_cfg` | 137 | definition factorial_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "factorial_cfg =<br>     compile_prog<br>       (prog_table factorial_program)<br>       (prog_procs factorial_program)<br>       (STR ''main'')<br>       (prog_main factorial_program)" |  |
| definition | `p09_one_call` | 147 | definition p09_one_call :: imp_prog where<br>  "p09_one_call = program { void g() { y := 1 } void main() { g() } }" |  |
| definition | `p11_nested_calls` | 150 | definition p11_nested_calls :: imp_prog where<br>  "p11_nested_calls =<br>     program { void h() { y := 1 } void g() { h() } void main() { g() } }" |  |
| definition | `p12_two_call_sites` | 154 | definition p12_two_call_sites :: imp_prog where<br>  "p12_two_call_sites =<br>     program { void g() { y := 1 } void f() { g(); g() } void main() { f() } }" |  |
| definition | `p13_after_guaranteed_return` | 158 | definition p13_after_guaranteed_return :: imp_prog where<br>  "p13_after_guaranteed_return =<br>     program { void f() { if (x &lt; 1) { return 1 } else { return 2 }; z := 9 }<br>               void main() { f() } }" |  |

### src/Examples/CFG/Example_Inc_Proc.thy

> Shared witness program: a single procedure p increments the declared global counter, and main sets a same-named-convention local before calling p once. `counter` carries no naming hint of its storage class, and `Glocal` carries the classic hint for the opposite class, so every classifier call below is driven by `declared_global` alone -- see `inc_program_counter_global`/`inc_program_glocal_not_global`. Examples reuse this theory when they need a small interprocedural program with a concrete run-to-collecting witness.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `inc_program` | 19 | definition inc_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "inc_program = program {<br>     global counter;<br>     void p() { counter := counter + 1 }<br>     void main() { Glocal := 1; p() }<br>   }" | Shared witness program: a single procedure p increments the declared global counter, and main sets a same-named-convention local before calling p once. `counter` carries no naming hint of its storage class, and `Glocal` carries the classic hint for the opposite class, so every classifier call below is driven by `declared_global` alone -- see `inc_program_counter_global`/`inc_program_glocal_not_global`. Examples reuse this theory when they need a small interprocedural program with a concrete run-to-collecting witness. |
| definition | `inc_pi` | 26 | definition inc_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "inc_pi = prog_table inc_program" |  |
| definition | `inc_g` | 51 | definition inc_g :: cfg where<br>  "inc_g = compile_prog (prog_table inc_program) (prog_procs inc_program) (STR ''main'') (prog_main inc_program)" |  |

### src/Examples/CFG/Example_Proc_Recursion_CFG.thy

> Source-level regression for procedure layout. The procedure list is known before any body is compiled, so calls may target the current procedure, a later procedure, or a mutually recursive sibling.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `proc_layout_regression_prog` | 13 | definition proc_layout_regression_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "proc_layout_regression_prog = program {<br>     global G;<br>     void f() {<br>       if (G &lt; 1) { f() } else { G := G }<br>     }<br>     void g() { h() }<br>     void h() { g() }<br>     void inc() { G := G + 1 }<br>     void main() { f(); g(); inc() }<br>   }" | Source-level regression for procedure layout. The procedure list is known before any body is compiled, so calls may target the current procedure, a later procedure, or a mutually recursive sibling. |
| definition | `proc_layout_regression_cfg` | 25 | definition proc_layout_regression_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "proc_layout_regression_cfg =<br>     compile_prog (prog_table proc_layout_regression_prog)<br>       (prog_procs proc_layout_regression_prog)<br>       (STR ''main'') (prog_main proc_layout_regression_prog)" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/CallString/Call_String_Solver_Refinement.thy

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `merged_result_g` | 72 | definition merged_result_g :: "(ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state" where<br>  "merged_result_g = snd nest_2_sol (Inl (FunctionResult (STR ''g''), [Statement 2, Statement 5]))<br>                        ⊔ snd nest_2_sol (Inl (FunctionResult (STR ''g''), [Statement 2, Statement 6]))" | Named closed forms for the downstream-cone values, so `project_sigma` plugs in one constant per node instead of re-inlining the same subterm at every site that reads it (`merged_result_g` alone would otherwise appear once in `project_sigma`'s own `FunctionResult (STR ''g'')` case and again inside every downstream case that reads it). None of these may mention `project_sigma` itself -- a `definition` cannot recurse -- so each is built directly from `nest_2_sol` and the constants already defined earlier in the chain, mirroring exactly what `eq` would compute at that node once its own inputs are fixed. |
| definition | `statement3_val` | 76 | definition statement3_val :: "cfg_node list ⇒ (ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state" where<br>  "statement3_val ctx1 = DG (combine_local Spoly (Some (STR ''t''))<br>        (locals (snd nest_2_sol (Inl (Statement 2, ctx1))))<br>        (locals merged_result_g)<br>        (globs (snd nest_2_sol (Inr Global2)))) bot" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/CallString/Example_Interval_DG_CallString.thy

> `SeidlEtAl2026`, Example 7: for 1-call-string context-sensitivity, the context set `C` is the call sites of the program plus a distinguished `_main` element, and `context\<^bsub>u,f,args\<^esub> _ _ = u` --- the new context is exactly the call site, ignoring both the calling context and the entered value. `Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Collect` routes `twice`'s two calls by *decoding* the entered formal (`ivl_enterc`, partial tabulation, the paper's Example 8). This theory routes the same program by call site instead: the context type is `cfg_node`, and `route_cs u ctx d ca = u` is exactly the paper's Example 7. Before `enterc` carried the call site (`CFG_Local_Trace.key`, widened for issue `#66`/G1), a call-site-keyed context had no semantic key to be proved sound against; this theory witnesses that the gap is closed.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `route_cs` | 32 | definition route_cs :: "pp ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ 'd ⇒ call_action ⇒ cfg_node" where<br>  "route_cs u ctx d ca = u" | `route_cs` ignores the calling context and the entered data entirely, so it commutes with *any* representation map on `'d` --- in particular the executable/abstract refinement `fun_of_exec_dg_st_for` --- for free. One definition serves both the executable and the abstract routing hook below. |
| datatype | `gk_cs` | 43 | datatype gk_cs = GlobalCS \| SeedCS (seedcs_pp: pp) (seedcs_call: cfg_node) | Distinct constructor names from `gk` (`Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Flagship`): both theories are imported here, and `Seed` would otherwise be ambiguous. |
| definition | `twice_cs_eqs` | 55 | definition twice_cs_eqs :: "(pp × cfg_node, gk_cs, (ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state) eqsT" where<br>  "twice_cs_eqs =<br>     side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg intra_predecessor_list (λ_. GlobalCS) route_cs<br>       (routed_cmb Spoly GlobalCS) (routed_extra twice_cfg Spoly SeedCS GlobalCS)<br>       twice_cfg Spoly bot cinit_ivl_st (restrict_global_resolved_q cinit_ivl_st)" | The root context is `cfg_entry` `twice_cfg` --- `main`'s own entry node, the paper's distinguished `_main` element. It is never a call site inside `twice`, so it cannot collide with a routed context. |

This is the `enterc` the paper's Example 7 asks for, and the widened `key` (`CFG_Local_Trace.thy`, G1) is what lets it be stated at all. |

GlobalCS) route_cs<br>       (routed_cmb Sabs GlobalCS) (routed_extra twice_cfg Sabs SeedCS GlobalCS) twice_cfg Sabs<br>       (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for twice_gs (bot::ivl exec_dg_st)) (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for twice_gs cinit_ivl_st)<br>       (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for twice_gs (restrict_global_resolved_q cinit_ivl_st))" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/CallString/Example_Interval_DG_CallString_K1.thy

> A `cs_route`/`cs_enterc` instance at `k = 1`. Unlike the flat `twice` program used elsewhere, `nest` chains two procedures: `main` calls `f` from two distinct sites, and `f` calls `g` from one site inside its own body. `g`'s immediate call site is therefore identical for both activations, so a 1-call-string context cannot separate them --- only a longer call string, keying on which `f` call led there, can.

Unlike the flat `twice` program used elsewhere, `nest` chains two procedures: `main` calls `f` from two distinct sites, and `f` calls `g` from one site inside its own body. `g`'s immediate call site is therefore identical for both activations, so a 1-call-string context cannot separate them --- only a longer call string, keying on which `f` call led there, can. |

### src/Examples/Interval/CallString/Example_Interval_DG_CallString_K2.thy

### src/Examples/Interval/Ctx/Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Collect.thy

> The connecting theorem between the routed executable post-solution (`twice_ctx_pp_abs`) and the semantic activation-indexed collecting semantics. It instantiates the generic `activation_collect_sound` and discharges its four obligations --- `ENTRY_G`, `EDGE`, `SEED_G`, `COMB` --- as separate lemmas. Coverage is internalised in the guarded reader `ivl_ctx_sg` (uncovered unknowns denote `{}`), real globals are shared through the single `Global` slot, and the return combine reads the callee exit at the routed context (`ivl_ctx_sg_comb`).

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `ivl_enterc` | 28 | definition ivl_enterc :: "cfg_node ⇒ ivl list ⇒ store ⇒ ivl list" where<br>  "ivl_enterc = formals_enterc twice_cfg ivl_decode" | A call selects the point abstraction of the callee formal in the entered store. Returns resume the caller context, and the root uses `bot`. `ivl_ctx_sg` joins the routed local slot with the shared global slot expected by `activation_collect_sound`. |
| definition | `ivl_ctx_sg` | 38 | definition ivl_ctx_sg :: "pp × ivl list + gk ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "ivl_ctx_sg k =<br>     (case k of<br>        Inl (v, ctx) ⇒<br>          (if (v, ctx) ∈ fst twice_ctx_sol<br>           then combine_env^# twice_gs<br>                  (locals ((fun_of_dg_st_for twice_gs ∘ snd twice_ctx_sol) (Inl (v, ctx))))<br>                  (globs ((fun_of_dg_st_for twice_gs ∘ snd twice_ctx_sol) (Inr Global)))<br>           else bot)<br>      \| Inr _ ⇒ bot)" | The reader is guarded by the *solved domain* `fst twice_ctx_sol`: the solver returns a partial solution, so an unknown outside `vars` is an artefact of the total implementation function and must denote no states. A covered `Inl` slot reads the transported local slot joined with its context's real-global slot; every other key (uncovered local, or any `Inr` global key, which `activation_collect_sound` never consults) denotes `⊥`. Uncovered points thus have empty concretization by construction, with no appeal to solver leastness. |
| abbreviation | `sigma_abs` | 58 | abbreviation sigma_abs :: "pp × ivl list + gk ⇒ (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state" where<br>  "sigma_abs ≡ fun_of_dg_st_for twice_gs ∘ snd twice_ctx_sol" | One name for the transported abstract solution and the abstract routed generator that `twice_ctx_pp_abs` is a post-solution of. The two projections below --- the per-slot value bound (`eq ≤ σ(Inl …)`) and the side-effect bound (`sides_of_rhs ≤ σ`) --- are the single elimination of `part_post_solution` that `EDGE`, `SEED_G`, and `COMB` all read through; the post-solution is never unfolded again inside a semantic obligation. |
| abbreviation | `gen_abs` | 61 | abbreviation gen_abs :: "(pp × ivl list, gk, (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) eqsT" where<br>  "gen_abs ≡ side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg intra_predecessor_list (λ_. Global) route_abs_gen<br>       (routed_cmb Sabs Global) (routed_extra twice_cfg Sabs Seed Global) twice_cfg Sabs<br>       (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for twice_gs (bot::ivl exec_dg_st)) (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for twice_gs cinit_ivl_st)<br>       (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for twice_gs (restrict_global_resolved_q cinit_ivl_st))" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/Ctx/Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Flagship.thy

> The generalized D/G generator is instantiated with routed context hooks. Each call to `twice` receives the abstract entry value of formal `p` as its context: • `twice(3)` uses context `[3,3]` and computes `#ret = [6,6]` and `x = [6,6]`; • `twice(10)` uses context `[10,10]` and computes `#ret = [20,20]` and `y = [20,20]`. The two repeated calls remain separate, whereas unit context joins their formal and return values. This entry-value key is finite for the two constant call sites. A general interval analysis needs a finite canonical context representation because arbitrary interval states may grow under recursion and widening.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `gk` | 30 | datatype gk = Global \| Seed (seed_pp: pp) (seed_ivl: "ivl list") | The global-key type separates shared analysis globals from context-sensitive callee-entry seeds. `Global` denotes the single flow-insensitive slot; `Seed` keys an entry seed by program point and context. Distinct constructors prevent the two roles from sharing a solver unknown. |
| abbreviation | `twice_gs` | 40 | abbreviation twice_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "twice_gs ≡ declared_global twice_program" | Storage here is decided from `twice_program`'s own declarations (`declared_global`) rather than a name-based naming convention. `twice_program` declares no globals, so `twice_gs` classifies every variable this chain touches as local --- unlike a G-prefix convention, which would still treat any `G`-prefixed name as global by naming convention alone. |
| abbreviation | `twice_lookup_exec_dg_st` | 43 | abbreviation twice_lookup_exec_dg_st :: "('a::bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ vname ⇒ 'a" where<br>  "twice_lookup_exec_dg_st s x ≡ lookup_resolved_st_q s (location_of twice_gs x)" |  |

(ce, ca, k)) (filter (λ(c, ca, ce, k). c = v) (cfg_calls_list g))" | Outgoing call edges of a node, as callee entry / call action / continuation. The procedure-aware `calls` relation records all three on the edge, so no separate matching relation is consulted. |
| fun | `is_function_entry` | 59 | fun is_function_entry :: "cfg_node ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_function_entry (FunctionEntry _) = True"<br>\| "is_function_entry _ = False" | A frame entry is a `FunctionEntry` node: the seed slot lives there. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Ctx/Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Sound.thy

> The executable routed equations are transported to an abstract context-indexed post-solution. Entry publication, activation selection, and callee-result lookup use one route function. Proving that route commutes with representation transport therefore discharges both strategy-tree commutation obligations.

### src/Examples/Interval/Ctx/Example_Interval_DG_EntryState_Base.thy

> `rc_program` is the acceptance witness for the entry-state coverage instance: a procedure `p` returning its formal unchanged, called once with an argument whose concrete value is unconstrained (`random()`). Unlike a program that calls its callee with distinct literal arguments and so routes to distinct exact contexts, this program's single call site is entered from infinitely many distinct concrete stores, all sharing one caller-local abstract value (`Top`). The routed context this file's siblings compute for that call is therefore `Top` itself: one abstract context that concretizes every one of those infinitely many concrete entries, not a family of contexts covering them.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `rc_program` | 26 | definition rc_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "rc_program = program {<br>     void p(a) { return a }<br>     void main() { x := random(); y := p(x) }<br>   }" | `rc_program` is the acceptance witness for the entry-state coverage instance: a procedure `p` returning its formal unchanged, called once with an argument whose concrete value is unconstrained (`random()`). Unlike a program that calls its callee with distinct literal arguments and so routes to distinct exact contexts, this program's single call site is entered from infinitely many distinct concrete stores, all sharing one caller-local abstract value (`Top`). The routed context this file's siblings compute for that call is therefore `Top` itself: one abstract context that concretizes every one of those infinitely many concrete entries, not a family of contexts covering them. |
| definition | `rc_pi` | 32 | definition rc_pi :: proc_table where "rc_pi = prog_table rc_program" |  |
| definition | `rc_procs` | 33 | definition rc_procs :: "pname list" where "rc_procs = prog_procs rc_program" |  |
| definition | `rc_main` | 34 | definition rc_main :: "VIMP_Proc.com" where "rc_main = prog_main rc_program" |  |
| abbreviation | `rc_gs` | 38 | abbreviation rc_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "rc_gs ≡ declared_global rc_program" | The storage classifier: `rc_program` declares no globals, so `rc_gs` classifies every variable this chain touches as local. |
| abbreviation | `rc_lookup` | 41 | abbreviation rc_lookup :: "('a::bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ vname ⇒ 'a" where<br>  "rc_lookup s x ≡ lookup_resolved_st_q s (location_of rc_gs x)" |  |
| definition | `rc_cfg` | 44 | definition rc_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "rc_cfg = compile_prog rc_pi rc_procs (STR ''main'') rc_main" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/Ctx/Example_Interval_DG_EntryState_Collect.thy

> The connecting theorem between the routed executable post-solution and the semantic activation-indexed collecting semantics, instantiating the generic `activation_collect_sound` exactly as a companion "twice" example does for a program with literal call arguments. The difference is `rc_enterc`: instead of decoding the concrete entered store (as `ivl_enterc` does for `twice`'s literal-argument calls), `rc_enterc` ignores its store argument and always returns the caller's already-solved routed value. That single design choice is what turns the per-value exact-context story into the one-context coverage story: every concrete `random()` outcome enters under the very same admissible context `ctx_call`, because `rc_enterc` never looks at which outcome it was.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `rc_ctx_sg` | 27 | definition rc_ctx_sg :: "pp × ivl list + gk ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "rc_ctx_sg k =<br>     (case k of<br>        Inl (v, ctx) ⇒<br>          (if (v, ctx) ∈ fst rc_ctx_sol<br>           then combine_env^# rc_gs<br>                  (locals ((fun_of_dg_st_for rc_gs ∘ snd rc_ctx_sol) (Inl (v, ctx))))<br>                  (globs ((fun_of_dg_st_for rc_gs ∘ snd rc_ctx_sol) (Inr Global)))<br>           else bot)<br>      \| Inr _ ⇒ bot)" |  |
| abbreviation | `sigma_abs` | 38 | abbreviation sigma_abs :: "pp × ivl list + gk ⇒ (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state" where<br>  "sigma_abs ≡ fun_of_dg_st_for rc_gs ∘ snd rc_ctx_sol" |  |
| abbreviation | `gen_abs` | 41 | abbreviation gen_abs :: "(pp × ivl list, gk, (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) eqsT" where<br>  "gen_abs ≡ side_cfg_T_eff_keyed_seed_dg intra_predecessor_list (λ_. Global) route_abs_gen<br>       (routed_cmb Sabs Global) (routed_extra rc_cfg Sabs Seed Global) rc_cfg Sabs<br>       (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for rc_gs (bot::ivl exec_dg_st)) (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for rc_gs cinit_ivl_st)<br>       (fun_of_exec_dg_st_for rc_gs (restrict_global_resolved_q cinit_ivl_st))" |  |
| definition | `rc_enterc` | 57 | definition rc_enterc :: "cfg_node ⇒ ivl list ⇒ store ⇒ ivl list" where<br>  "rc_enterc u ctx s =<br>     route_abs (locals (sigma_abs (Inl (u, ctx))))<br>       (CallEdge (Some (STR ''y'')) [(STR ''a'')] [V (STR ''x'')])" | The trace-semantic context function for the one call in `rc_program`. Unlike `formals_enterc` (which decodes the concrete store `s` through a domain's point abstraction), `rc_enterc` discards `s` entirely and recomputes the routed value from the caller's own solved abstract state --- the same value `route_abs_gen` already selected when the executable solver ran. This is `admiss_exact`'s functional shape, specialized so that coverage of infinitely many concrete stores comes from the caller's own value being `Top`, not from `rc_enterc` being genuinely multi-valued. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Ctx/Example_Interval_DG_EntryState_Sound.thy

> The executable routed equations are transported to an abstract context-indexed post-solution. Entry publication, activation selection, and callee-result lookup use one route function. Proving that route commutes with representation transport therefore discharges both strategy-tree commutation obligations. This is mechanical in the compiled program's own shape: none of the commutation lemmas depend on it, only on the routed hooks' generic structure.

These mirror `entered_ivl` / `route_ivl` on `ivl abs_state`. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Guard_Refinement.thy

> The identity assumption transfer is sound but imprecise. Backward guard refinement intersects the incoming interval with the states satisfying the guard, which preserves the bounded loop invariant through one body step. Sign cannot express the numeric bounds; interval filtering exposes the gain.

| abbreviation | `sigma_loop_head` | 21 | abbreviation "sigma_loop_head ≡ sigma_x (Ivl (Fin 0) (Fin 20))" |  |
| definition | `assume_ivl_identity` | 24 | definition assume_ivl_identity :: "bexp ⇒ ivl abs_state ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "assume_ivl_identity _ sigma = sigma" | The identity baseline leaves the abstract state unchanged. |
| definition | `body_after_refined` | 53 | definition body_after_refined :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "body_after_refined =<br>     assign_ivl (STR ''x'') (Plus (V (STR ''x'')) (N 1))<br>       (assume_ivl (Less (V (STR ''x'')) (N 20)) sigma_loop_head)" |  |
| definition | `body_after_identity` | 58 | definition body_after_identity :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "body_after_identity =<br>     assign_ivl (STR ''x'') (Plus (V (STR ''x'')) (N 1))<br>       (assume_ivl_identity (Less (V (STR ''x'')) (N 20)) sigma_loop_head)" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_DG_Flagship.thy

> **The complete story in one self-contained theory.** A VIMP program (given inline below) is compiled to a CFG; the generic D/G framework generates an equation system; the *verified* top-down solver *computes* an interval solution inside Isabelle (with interval widening for termination); and the single registered endpoint `flagship_ex_reg.run_source_sound` turns that computed solution directly into a source-level guarantee. \<^verbatim>\<open> VIMP source \| compile_prog v CFG \| dg_gen_of, executable D/G specification v D/G equation system \| verified solver, by eval v computed interval solution sigma \| flagship_ex_reg.run_source_sound v every VIMP source run is bounded by sigma, read through flagship_ex_reg.gamma \<close> **Every step is machine-checked, and the result is informative:** the analysis discovers the loop invariant `x in [0,20]`, the body bound `x in [0,19]`, and the exact exit value `x in [20,20]` --- not `top`. `flagship_ex_reg.run_source_sound` bundles executable/pure commutation, post-solution transport, D/G collecting soundness, and the compiler-correctness simulation into one application, so none of those intermediate obligations appear in this file's own proofs. A later subsection exhibits an explicit reachable state so the guarantee is visibly *not vacuous*, reusing the native interval D/G locale from `Interval_DG`; the final subsection emits an analysis-annotated GraphViz rendering. It reuses, without duplicating: the executable interval transfer `ivl_tf_st_for` (`Ivl_Exec`), the executable D/G bridge (`Exec_DG_Bridge`), the registration locale `unit_dg_exec_analysis` (`Run_Analysis_Sound`), and the vendored warrowing solver.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `flagship_prog` | 65 | definition flagship_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "flagship_prog = program { void main() { x := 0; while (x &lt; 20) { x := x + 1 } } }" | A bounded counting loop: initialise `x` to `0`, increment while `x < 20`. On exit `x = 20`. No procedures, no globals; `x` is a single flow-sensitive local. The analysis must *discover* the bound, not assume it. |
| abbreviation | `flagship_gs` | 71 | abbreviation flagship_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "flagship_gs ≡ declared_global flagship_prog" | The storage classifier: `flagship_prog` declares no globals, so `flagship_gs` classifies `x` as local, matching the `declared_global` pattern used by every other flagship rather than the `is_global` naming convention. |
| abbreviation | `flagship_lookup` | 90 | abbreviation flagship_lookup :: "('a::bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ vname ⇒ 'a" where<br>  "flagship_lookup s x ≡ lookup_resolved_st_q s (location_of flagship_gs x)" | Local shorthand for the executable state's lookup and refinement projections, fixed at this file's own `flagship_gs` classifier. |
| abbreviation | `flagship_fun_of` | 93 | abbreviation flagship_fun_of :: "('a::bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ 'a abs_state" where<br>  "flagship_fun_of ≡ fun_of_resolved_st_q_for flagship_gs" |  |
| definition | `flagship_pi` | 96 | definition flagship_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "flagship_pi = prog_table flagship_prog" |  |

Locals seed at `cinit_ivl_st` (globals `[0,0]`, locals `top`); the flow-insensitive global slot seeds at `restrict_global_resolved_q cinit_ivl_st` (bottom on locals, so it never pollutes the local `x` through the diagonal read). |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_Loop_Coverage.thy

> A full program carried end to end through the interval analyzer: `x := 0; while (x < 20) { x := x + 1 }` The interval domain proves the bounded invariant `0 <= x <= 20` (i.e. `x ∈ [0, 20]`) at the loop head over every reaching store. Both bounds are interval-specific: the lower bound `0` is the joined initial value, and the upper bound `20` comes from guard refinement -- `assume_ivl` narrows `x` to `[.., 19]` on entering the body (`x < 20`), so after `x := x + 1` and the join with the initial `[0,0]` the loop head stabilises at `[0,20]` with no widening needed. The Sign lattice expresses neither bound (it collapses `[0,0]` joined with `[1,1]` to sign top). Certified backward-analysis story: *Example_Guard_Refinement* isolates the guard step; this theory carries the same `assume_ivl` transfers through the full CFG to the trace-native post-fixpoint soundness theorem. Executable mirror (Kleene / warrowing TD on *ivl st*, eval only): *Exec_Ivl_Run*.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `loop_prog` | 34 | definition loop_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "loop_prog = program {<br>     void main() { x := 0; while (x &lt; 20) { x := x + 1 } }<br>   }" | A full program carried end to end through the interval analyzer: `x := 0; while (x < 20) { x := x + 1 }` The interval domain proves the bounded invariant `0 <= x <= 20` (i.e. `x ∈ [0, 20]`) at the loop head over every reaching store. Both bounds are interval-specific: the lower bound `0` is the joined initial value, and the upper bound `20` comes from guard refinement -- `assume_ivl` narrows `x` to `[.., 19]` on entering the body (`x < 20`), so after `x := x + 1` and the join with the initial `[0,0]` the loop head stabilises at `[0,20]` with no widening needed. The Sign lattice expresses neither bound (it collapses `[0,0]` joined with `[1,1]` to sign top). Certified backward-analysis story: *Example_Guard_Refinement* isolates the guard step; this theory carries the same `assume_ivl` transfers through the full CFG to the trace-native post-fixpoint soundness theorem. Executable mirror (Kleene / warrowing TD on *ivl st*, eval only): *Exec_Ivl_Run*. |
| abbreviation | `loop_cfg` | 41 | abbreviation "loop_cfg ≡<br>  compile_prog (prog_table loop_prog) (prog_procs loop_prog) prog_main_name (prog_main loop_prog)" |  |
| definition | `loop_s0` | 74 | definition loop_s0 :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "loop_s0 = (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)" |  |
| definition | `loop_env` | 87 | definition loop_env :: "pp ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "loop_env v =<br>     (if v = Statement 2 then (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)((STR ''x'') := Ivl (Fin 0) (Fin 19))<br>      else if v ∈ {Statement 1, Statement 3}<br>        then (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)((STR ''x'') := Ivl (Fin 0) (Fin 20))<br>      else (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf))" | The loop head (node 1) stabilises at `[0,20]`: the join of `x := 0` flowing in directly from node 0 and `x := x + 1` flowing back from the body. Guard refinement narrows the body entry (node 2) to `[0,19]`; applying the body's own assignment to that value gives `[1,20]`, which joined with the initial `[0,0]` is exactly the loop-head value -- a finite (non-widened) fixpoint. Neither intermediate join operand needs its own node: the continuation-passing compiler routes both edges directly into the loop head, so `rhs` computes the join on the fly. Other variables stay at the full interval. |
| abbreviation | `loop_body_entry` | 144 | abbreviation "loop_body_entry ≡ Statement 2" | Edge from node 2 to node 3 is `EA_Assume` on *x < 20*. The body-entry interval *[0,19]* in `loop_env` is exactly `assume_ivl` applied at the loop head --- not widening and not join alone (identity assume would keep *[0,20]*; see *Example_Guard_Refinement*). |
| abbreviation | `loop_head` | 159 | abbreviation "loop_head ≡ Statement 1" | The loop head is the assume node where ``x < 20`` is checked. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_Placement.thy

> The program has two declared globals with independent placement. The procedure call binds a formal, introduces the implicit local `tmp`, and returns into the caller-local `answer`. Neither global name relies on the historical `G` prefix convention. `balance` and `request_count` are placed as a static analogue of Goblint's own protected/unprotected privatization split (`VojdaniPriv` in `basePriv.ml`): `balance` is `keep_local`-only, never `publish_side` -- the protected case, whose write updates the local `CPA` but skips `sideg`. `request_count` is `publish_side`-only, never `keep_local` -- the unprotected case, whose write goes straight to the shared side. This is a static per-variable policy, fixed for the whole program; it does not model the dynamic transition a real lock/unlock would drive (a protected global's write becoming visible to `G` only once the critical section it was written under is released). Modelling that transition needs an action-specific publish hook at the unlock edge, which `unit_dg_spec_placed`'s generic per-edge transfer does not have; it is a framework extension, not an example, and is out of scope here. Even this static split already needs `gamma_join`, not `gamma_unit`: `balance`'s and `request_count`'s live values sit on different sides regardless of the declared-global classifier, so no single-bit ownership routing can recover both.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `placement_prog` | 34 | definition placement_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "placement_prog = program {<br>     global balance, request_count;<br>     void add(x) {<br>       tmp := balance + x;<br>       balance := tmp;<br>       request_count := request_count + 1;<br>       return balance<br>     }<br>     void main() { answer := add(3) }<br>   }" | The program has two declared globals with independent placement. The procedure call binds a formal, introduces the implicit local `tmp`, and returns into the caller-local `answer`. Neither global name relies on the historical `G` prefix convention. `balance` and `request_count` are placed as a static analogue of Goblint's own protected/unprotected privatization split (`VojdaniPriv` in `basePriv.ml`): `balance` is `keep_local`-only, never `publish_side` -- the protected case, whose write updates the local `CPA` but skips `sideg`. `request_count` is `publish_side`-only, never `keep_local` -- the unprotected case, whose write goes straight to the shared side. This is a static per-variable policy, fixed for the whole program; it does not model the dynamic transition a real lock/unlock would drive (a protected global's write becoming visible to `G` only once the critical section it was written under is released). Modelling that transition needs an action-specific publish hook at the unlock edge, which `unit_dg_spec_placed`'s generic per-edge transfer does not have; it is a framework extension, not an example, and is out of scope here. Even this static split already needs `gamma_join`, not `gamma_unit`: `balance`'s and `request_count`'s live values sit on different sides regardless of the declared-global classifier, so no single-bit ownership routing can recover both. |
| definition | `placement_cfg` | 46 | definition placement_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "placement_cfg =<br>    compile_prog (prog_table placement_prog) (prog_procs placement_prog)<br>      prog_main_name (prog_main placement_prog)"<br><br>value "intra placement_cfg"<br>value "calls placement_cfg" |  |
| definition | `placement_owner` | 54 | definition placement_owner :: pname where<br>  "placement_owner = (STR ''add'')" |  |
| fun | `placement_keep_local` | 59 | fun placement_keep_local :: "scoped_location =&gt; bool" where<br>  "placement_keep_local (owner, Local_Location x) = True"<br>\| "placement_keep_local (owner, Global_Location x) = (x = (STR ''balance''))" | `balance`: protected, kept only in the flow-sensitive local answer. `request_count`: unprotected, published only to the flow-insensitive side. |
| fun | `placement_publish_side` | 63 | fun placement_publish_side :: "scoped_location =&gt; bool" where<br>  "placement_publish_side (owner, Local_Location x) = False"<br>\| "placement_publish_side (owner, Global_Location x) = (x = (STR ''request_count''))" |  |
| fun | `placement_node_owner` | 75 | fun placement_node_owner :: "pp =&gt; pname" where<br>  "placement_node_owner (FunctionEntry p) = p"<br>\| "placement_node_owner (FunctionResult p) = p"<br>\| "placement_node_owner (Statement n) =<br>    (if n &lt; 4 then (STR ''add'') else prog_main_name)" |  |
| definition | `placement_locations_of` | 81 | definition placement_locations_of :: "pp =&gt; location list" where<br>  "placement_locations_of node =<br>    scope_locations placement_prog (placement_node_owner node)" |  |
| definition | `placement_ivl_etf_st` | 85 | definition placement_ivl_etf_st ::<br>  "(unit, ivl resolved_st_q) effectful_st_transfer" where<br>  "placement_ivl_etf_st =<br>    unit_etf_st_of_transfer_placed<br>      (declared_global placement_prog)<br>      placement_node_owner placement_locations_of<br>      placement_keep_local placement_publish_side<br>      (ivl_tf_st_for (declared_global placement_prog))<br>      (ivl_enter_st_for (declared_global placement_prog))" |  |
| definition | `placement_state` | 256 | definition placement_state :: "ivl resolved_st_q" where<br>  "placement_state =<br>    update_resolved_st_q<br>      (update_resolved_st_q cinit_ivl_st (Global_Location (STR ''balance''))<br>        (Ivl (Fin 10) (Fin 10)))<br>      (Global_Location (STR ''request_count'')) (Ivl (Fin 4) (Fin 4))" |  |
| definition | `placement_local_state` | 263 | definition placement_local_state :: "ivl resolved_st_q" where<br>  "placement_local_state =<br>    project_resolved_on placement_owner<br>      (scope_locations placement_prog placement_owner)<br>      placement_keep_local placement_state" |  |
| definition | `placement_side_state` | 269 | definition placement_side_state :: "ivl resolved_st_q" where<br>  "placement_side_state =<br>    project_resolved_on placement_owner<br>      (scope_locations placement_prog placement_owner)<br>      placement_publish_side placement_state" |  |
| definition | `placement_dg_eqs` | 348 | definition placement_dg_eqs ::<br>  "pp × unit =&gt;<br>    (pp × unit, unit, (ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "placement_dg_eqs =<br>    placed_dg_gen_of_strict (declared_global placement_prog)<br>      placement_node_owner placement_locations_of<br>      placement_keep_local placement_publish_side<br>      (ivl_tf_st_for (declared_global placement_prog))<br>      (ivl_enter_st_for (declared_global placement_prog))<br>      placement_cfg bot cinit_ivl_st<br>      (project_resolved_on_strict prog_main_name<br>        (scope_locations placement_prog prog_main_name)<br>        placement_publish_side cinit_ivl_st)" | The placed D/G generator receives the intra, entry, and return hooks as explicit node-aware tree constructors. The generator itself does not select scopes or placement. |

| definition | `placement_abs_edge_tree` | 441 | definition placement_abs_edge_tree ::<br>  "pp =&gt; edge_action =&gt; pp =&gt;<br>    (pp × unit, unit, (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "placement_abs_edge_tree =<br>    placed_abs_dg_edge_of (declared_global placement_prog)<br>      placement_node_owner placement_keep_local placement_publish_side<br>      (apply_tf (ivl_tf_for (declared_global placement_prog))) ()" |  |
| definition | `placement_abs_enter_tree` | 450 | definition placement_abs_enter_tree ::<br>  "pp =&gt; call_action =&gt; pp =&gt;<br>    (pp × unit, unit, (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "placement_abs_enter_tree =<br>    placed_abs_dg_enter_of (declared_global placement_prog)<br>      placement_node_owner placement_keep_local placement_publish_side<br>      (tf_enter (ivl_tf_for (declared_global placement_prog))) ()" |  |
| definition | `placement_abs_combine_tree` | 459 | definition placement_abs_combine_tree ::<br>  "pp =&gt; call_action =&gt; pp =&gt; pp =&gt;<br>    (pp × unit, unit, (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "placement_abs_combine_tree =<br>    placed_abs_dg_combine_of (declared_global placement_prog)<br>      placement_node_owner placement_keep_local placement_publish_side ()" |  |
| definition | `placement_sigma_abs` | 708 | definition placement_sigma_abs ::<br>  "pp × unit + unit =&gt; (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state"<br>where<br>  "placement_sigma_abs =<br>    completed_sigma_abs (declared_global placement_prog) placement_locations_of<br>      ivl_top (snd placement_dg_td_sol)" | `placement_sigma_abs` is now a single call to the generic completed- readback constructor (`completed_sigma_abs`), not a fresh case split over `Inl`/`Inr`: the executable TD solution, read back through `declared_global placement_prog`'s classifier, completed to `ivl_top` beyond each node's own scope. |
| definition | `placement_s0d_abs` | 2097 | definition placement_s0d_abs :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "placement_s0d_abs = fun_of_resolved_st_q_for (declared_global placement_prog) cinit_ivl_st" | The abstract seed values that make `placement_sigma_abs` a post-solution of `hook_gen` at the entry node: the readback of the executable seeds through `fun_of_resolved_st_q_for`, already total, so no completion is needed here (unlike the per-node locals, which are completed only because the executable side is sparse). |
| definition | `placement_s0g_abs` | 2100 | definition placement_s0g_abs :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "placement_s0g_abs = fun_of_resolved_st_q_for (declared_global placement_prog)<br>    (project_resolved_on_strict prog_main_name<br>      (scope_locations placement_prog prog_main_name) placement_publish_side cinit_ivl_st)" |  |
| definition | `placement_nodes` | 2233 | definition placement_nodes :: "(pp × unit) set" where<br>  "placement_nodes =<br>    {(FunctionEntry (STR ''add''), ()), (FunctionResult (STR ''add''), ()),<br>     (FunctionEntry prog_main_name, ()), (FunctionResult prog_main_name, ())}<br>    ∪ (λn. (Statement n, ())) ` {0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6}" |  |
| definition | `placement_eqs` | 3035 | definition placement_eqs :: "(pp, unit, ivl resolved_st_q) eqsT" where<br>  "placement_eqs =<br>    side_cfg_T_eff_st placement_cfg placement_ivl_etf_st bot cinit_ivl_st ()" | `placement_eqs` below compiles the same source program under the same placement policy (`placement_keep_local`/ `placement_publish_side`) as `placement_dg_eqs` above, but through the flat, single-tree route (`side_cfg_T_eff_st` over one executable transfer function per edge) instead of the D/G split route (`placed_dg_gen_of_strict`, separate local/side unknowns per node). Both routes instantiate the identical policy; only the equation-system architecture differs. `placement_td_values` reads `answer = Ivl (Fin 0) (Fin 3)` from the flat route, against `placement_dg_td_values`'s exact `answer = Ivl (Fin 3) (Fin 3)` from the D/G route. The precision difference is associated with the D/G split itself -- the separate, per-node local unknown that the flat route's single shared unknown does not have -- not with the placement policy, which is held fixed across both computations. This comparison does not by itself attribute the gap to any one lower-level mechanism inside the D/G route (for instance, strict projection alone, or scoped combine alone); isolating a single mechanism would need a further controlled experiment that varies that mechanism independently of the others. `balance = Ivl (Fin 3) (Fin 3)` and `answer = Ivl (Fin 3) (Fin 3)` are the observed end-to-end results of the full D/G pipeline for this example. |
| definition | `placement_sig0` | 3039 | definition placement_sig0 ::<br>  "pp + unit =&gt; ivl resolved_st_q" where<br>  "placement_sig0 key =<br>    (case key of<br>      Inl node =&gt; bot<br>    \| Inr () =&gt;<br>        project_resolved_on prog_main_name<br>          (scope_locations placement_prog prog_main_name)<br>          placement_publish_side cinit_ivl_st)" |  |
| definition | `placement_kleene_step` | 3049 | definition placement_kleene_step ::<br>  "(pp + unit =&gt; ivl resolved_st_q) =&gt; pp + unit =&gt; ivl resolved_st_q" where<br>  "placement_kleene_step sig key =<br>    (case key of<br>      Inl node =&gt; eq placement_eqs node sig<br>    \| Inr () =&gt; sig (Inr ()))" |  |
| fun | `placement_iter_sig` | 3056 | fun placement_iter_sig ::<br>  "nat =&gt; (pp + unit =&gt; ivl resolved_st_q) =&gt;<br>   pp + unit =&gt; ivl resolved_st_q" where<br>  "placement_iter_sig 0 sig = sig"<br>\| "placement_iter_sig (Suc n) sig =<br>    placement_iter_sig n (placement_kleene_step sig)" |  |
| definition | `placement_sol` | 3063 | definition placement_sol ::<br>  "pp set * (pp + unit =&gt; ivl resolved_st_q)" where<br>  "placement_sol =<br>    ({FunctionEntry (STR ''add''), FunctionResult (STR ''add''),<br>      FunctionEntry prog_main_name, FunctionResult prog_main_name}<br>      ∪ Statement ` {0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6},<br>     placement_iter_sig 20 placement_sig0)" |  |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Interval_Side_Proc_Global.thy

> Interval instance of the side-effecting interprocedural witness: the same `inc_pi` program (a single call to procedure p incrementing the global ``(STR ''counter'')``) carried through the `side_analyse_eff` solver at the interval domain. Demonstrates the soundness scaffold is domain-generic by reusing it on a second, infinite-height numeric domain.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
