| definition | `side_proc_global_ivl_s0` | 21 | definition side_proc_global_ivl_s0 :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "side_proc_global_ivl_s0 = (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)" | Initial state: every variable -- including the globals -- starts at the full interval ``Ivl MinInf PlusInf`` (top), the interval analogue of sign top. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_M4_Acceptance.thy

> The three Phase 1 acceptance cases named in the M4 design doc (section 6, "Analysis roadmap"): A1 (explicitly declared mixed interval analysis), A2 (fully flow-sensitive sequential globals), A3 (selectively flow-sensitive variables). Each subsection below computes, not asserts, the values the design doc's own success criteria name.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `a1_node_owner` | 36 | fun a1_node_owner :: "pp ⇒ pname" where<br>  "a1_node_owner (FunctionEntry p) = p"<br>\| "a1_node_owner (FunctionResult p) = p"<br>\| "a1_node_owner (Statement n) = (if n &lt; 2 then (STR ''p'') else prog_main_name)" | Reuses `inc_program` (`Example_Inc_Proc`) rather than inventing a second witness: `counter` is the declared global with no naming hint, `Glocal` the `G`-prefixed local, and both classifications (`inc_program_counter_global`/`inc_program_glocal_not_global`) are already proven there off `declared_global` alone. What's new here is running the mixed local/global program through the executable, placement- aware D/G pipeline (classic placement: `counter` side-effected, `Glocal` flow-sensitive because it is local) and reading off real computed values. |
| definition | `a1_locations_of` | 41 | definition a1_locations_of :: "pp ⇒ location list" where<br>  "a1_locations_of node = scope_locations inc_program (a1_node_owner node)" |  |
| definition | `a1_dg_eqs` | 44 | definition a1_dg_eqs ::<br>  "pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "a1_dg_eqs =<br>    placed_dg_gen_of_strict (declared_global inc_program) a1_node_owner a1_locations_of<br>      classic_keep_local classic_publish_side<br>      (ivl_tf_st_for (declared_global inc_program)) (ivl_enter_st_for (declared_global inc_program))<br>      inc_g bot cinit_ivl_st<br>      (project_resolved_on_strict prog_main_name<br>        (a1_locations_of (cfg_entry inc_g)) classic_publish_side cinit_ivl_st)" |  |
| definition | `a1_sol` | 55 | definition a1_sol ::<br>  "(pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state)"<br>where<br>  "a1_sol = TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve a1_dg_eqs (cfg_exit inc_g, ())" |  |
| definition | `a2_program` | 108 | definition a2_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "a2_program = program {<br>     global x;<br>     void main() { x := 0; x := 1; __voblint_check(x == 1) }<br>   }" | `global x; x := 0; x := 1; __voblint_check(x == 1)`, exactly as specified. Two placements of the same program, same classifier, same check: classic (`x` exclusively side-effected) against all-flow-sensitive (`keep_local = True`, `publish_side = False` for every location, so the shared `G` unknown never receives a write and stays `bot` for the whole solve -- `d ⊔ g = d ⊔ bot = d`, so nothing dilutes the flow-sensitive read). This is not assumed: both solves are run and both check results are computed via `interval_classify_check`, the same node-local classifier `Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Checks` already proves sound and `Example_Checks_Store_Only` exercises on locals. |
| abbreviation | `a2_gs` | 114 | abbreviation a2_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "a2_gs ≡ declared_global a2_program" |  |

| definition | `a2_locations_of` | 129 | definition a2_locations_of :: "pp ⇒ location list" where<br>  "a2_locations_of node = scope_locations a2_program (a2_node_owner node)" |  |
| definition | `a2_keep_flowsens_sl` | 132 | definition a2_keep_flowsens_sl :: "scoped_location ⇒ bool" where<br>  "a2_keep_flowsens_sl loc = True" |  |
| definition | `a2_publish_flowsens_sl` | 135 | definition a2_publish_flowsens_sl :: "scoped_location ⇒ bool" where<br>  "a2_publish_flowsens_sl loc = False" |  |
| definition | `a2_dg_eqs_classic` | 138 | definition a2_dg_eqs_classic ::<br>  "pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state) strategy_tree"<br>where<br>  "a2_dg_eqs_classic =<br>    placed_dg_gen_of_strict a2_gs a2_node_owner a2_locations_of<br>      classic_keep_local classic_publish_side<br>      (ivl_tf_st_for a2_gs) (ivl_enter_st_for a2_gs)<br>      a2_cfg bot cinit_ivl_st<br>      (project_resolved_on_strict prog_main_name<br>        (a2_locations_of (cfg_entry a2_cfg)) classic_publish_side cinit_ivl_st)" |  |

What's new here is running the identical program under classic and all-flow-sensitive placement too, so all three policies A3 names are compared on the same source, holding the program fixed and varying only placement -- the opposite axis from that theory's own `section 3090` flat-vs-D/G-split comparison, which holds placement fixed and varies architecture. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Example_Proc_Call.thy

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `main_cfg_name` | 13 | definition main_cfg_name :: pname where<br>  "main_cfg_name = (STR ''main'')" |  |
| definition | `proc_call_gs` | 29 | definition proc_call_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "proc_call_gs x ↔ x = (STR ''Gx'')" | Two parameterless procedures communicate through the global variable ``(STR ''Gx'')``. `proc_call_gs` is this program's explicit storage classifier: `Gx` is global by declaration, not by its leading letter, so it survives call-frame restore while locals are reset to zero. • `inc`: adds 1 to `Gx`. • `sqr`: replaces `Gx` with its square. Main program: `Gx := 4; call inc; call sqr` terminates with `Gx = 25` since `(4 + 1)^2 = 25`. |
| definition | `inc_body` | 32 | definition inc_body :: "VIMP_Proc.com" where<br>  "inc_body = imp ⟦ Gx := Gx + 1 ⟧" |  |
| definition | `sqr_body` | 35 | definition sqr_body :: "VIMP_Proc.com" where<br>  "sqr_body = imp ⟦ Gx := Gx * Gx ⟧" |  |
| definition | `proc_pi` | 38 | definition proc_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "proc_pi = (λ_. None)((STR ''inc'') := Some (proc_decl_of [] inc_body), (STR ''sqr'') := Some (proc_decl_of [] sqr_body))" |  |
| definition | `main_prog` | 41 | definition main_prog :: "VIMP_Proc.com" where<br>  "main_prog = imp ⟦<br>     Gx := 4;<br>     inc();<br>     sqr()<br>   ⟧" |  |
| definition | `main_procs` | 48 | definition main_procs :: "pname list" where<br>  "main_procs = [(STR ''inc''), (STR ''sqr'')]" |  |
| abbreviation | `main_cfg` | 150 | abbreviation "main_cfg ≡ compile_prog proc_pi [(STR ''inc''), (STR ''sqr'')] main_cfg_name main_prog" | Compile `main_prog` together with its two procedure bodies. `compile_prog` first lays out `inc_body` at statements 0--1 and `sqr_body` at statements 2--3, then compiles the main body starting at statement 4. Each procedure is bracketed by its own `FunctionEntry` / `FunctionResult`; each call edge names its callee entry and its continuation, so the two calls are *5 -> inc, continue at 6* and *6 -> sqr, continue at 7*. |
| definition | `main_prog_s0` | 201 | definition main_prog_s0 :: "ivl abs_state" where<br>  "main_prog_s0 = (λ_. Ivl MinInf PlusInf)" | The initial abstract state maps every variable to the full interval. |
| definition | `main_prog_env` | 221 | definition main_prog_env :: "pp ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "main_prog_env v x =<br>     (if v ∈ {Statement 5, FunctionEntry (STR ''inc''), Statement 0} ∧ x = (STR ''Gx'')<br>        then Ivl (Fin 4) (Fin 4)<br>      else if v ∈ {Statement 1, FunctionResult (STR ''inc''), Statement 6,<br>                   FunctionEntry (STR ''sqr''), Statement 2} ∧ x = (STR ''Gx'')<br>        then Ivl (Fin 5) (Fin 5)<br>      else if v ∈ {Statement 3, FunctionResult (STR ''sqr''), Statement 7,<br>                   FunctionResult (STR ''main'')} ∧ x = (STR ''Gx'')<br>        then Ivl (Fin 25) (Fin 25)<br>      else Ivl MinInf PlusInf)" | Abstract environment: each program point is assigned an interval state. The assignment propagates *Gx = [4,4]* after *Gx := 4*, *Gx = [5,5]* after *inc* executes, and *Gx = [25,25]* after *sqr* executes. All local variables remain unconstrained throughout. Node groupings: *4* -- main body entry, before *Gx := 4*; *5* -- after *Gx := 4*, which is also the call site to inc; *0* -- inc body entry (reached through the call edge from 5); *1* -- inc body exit, feeding ``FunctionResult (STR ''inc'')``; *6* -- continuation after inc, which is also the call site to sqr; *2*, *3* -- sqr body entry and exit; *7* -- continuation after sqr, feeding ``FunctionResult (STR ''main'')``. |

### src/Examples/Interval/Exec_Ivl_Run.thy

> Same program as *Example_Interval_Loop_Coverage* in session `Voblint_Formalization`: *x := 0; while (x < 20) { x := x + 1*"}. The executable transfer `ivl_tf_st_for` applies the same backward guard filters as `assume_ivl` (via `assume_ivl_st_for` / `bfilter_ivl_st`) on `EA_Assume` edges. Node~2 therefore reads *[0,19]* because *x < 20* refines *x* at the loop head --- not because of widening. This theory evaluates two fixpoint engines on `side_cfg_T_eff_st`: bounded Kleene iteration on `eq`, and `TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp_solve` (pointwise interval widening on `"ivl resolved_st_q"` for solver termination). The example uses the trace-native post-fixpoint soundness theorem.

A different program from `loop_prog`, run through the very same `analyse_interval`: the entry point is not specialized to one hard-coded example. |

### src/Examples/Mixed/Example_Analysis_Dispatch.thy

> `analyse_sign_report` (`Voblint_Examples.Example_Sign_Codegen`) and `interval_td_check_report`/`analyse_interval_td_report` (`Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Checks`) already share one observable result type, `check_report_entry list` (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Checks`), even though the two domains' internal abstract states (`sign abs_state` vs `ivl abs_state`) genuinely differ. `analyse` below is therefore a thin dispatcher, not a new proof: each branch reuses the domain's own already-generic, already-sound report function unchanged. The `Interval_Analysis` branch dispatches to `analyse_interval_td_report`, the widening/warrowing-backed report, not the always-join `analyse_interval_report`: the always-join backend's flow-insensitive global-summary update has no widening, so it can fail to terminate on a finite program whose global-writing transfer depends on the summary's own current value (`total := total + n` is the minimal reproducer, isolated below to *not* require a call). `analyse_interval_td_report` now carries its own soundness theorems (`Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Checks`'s `analyse_interval_td_report_sound_proved`/ `_refuted`, built on `Voblint_Core.Solver_Side_RG`'s generic `TD_side_warrowing_apinis_solve_Inr_rg`), so this is a like-for-like swap, not a precision or soundness downgrade.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `analysis_kind` | 37 | datatype analysis_kind = Sign_Analysis \| Interval_Analysis | `analyse_sign_report` (`Voblint_Examples.Example_Sign_Codegen`) and `interval_td_check_report`/`analyse_interval_td_report` (`Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Checks`) already share one observable result type, `check_report_entry list` (`Voblint_Core.Abstract_Checks`), even though the two domains' internal abstract states (`sign abs_state` vs `ivl abs_state`) genuinely differ. `analyse` below is therefore a thin dispatcher, not a new proof: each branch reuses the domain's own already-generic, already-sound report function unchanged. The `Interval_Analysis` branch dispatches to `analyse_interval_td_report`, the widening/warrowing-backed report, not the always-join `analyse_interval_report`: the always-join backend's flow-insensitive global-summary update has no widening, so it can fail to terminate on a finite program whose global-writing transfer depends on the summary's own current value (`total := total + n` is the minimal reproducer, isolated below to *not* require a call). `analyse_interval_td_report` now carries its own soundness theorems (`Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Checks`'s `analyse_interval_td_report_sound_proved`/ `_refuted`, built on `Voblint_Core.Solver_Side_RG`'s generic `TD_side_warrowing_apinis_solve_Inr_rg`), so this is a like-for-like swap, not a precision or soundness downgrade. |
| fun | `analyse` | 39 | fun analyse :: "analysis_kind ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ check_report_entry list" where<br>  "analyse Sign_Analysis p = analyse_sign_report p"<br>\| "analyse Interval_Analysis p = analyse_interval_td_report p" |  |
| datatype | `context_mode` | 61 | datatype context_mode = Ctx_None \| Ctx_EntryState | `Ctx_None` is today's flow-insensitive, call-site-insensitive behaviour; `Ctx_EntryState` selects the value-derived entry-state context analysis (`Voblint_Formalization.Interval_Exec_Ctx_Sound`, #108). Deliberately not a wider `analyse`: `analyse`/`analyse_with_state` stay untouched (the CLI's no-`--context` path, the GraphViz report, and every existing `codegen/regression` consumer already pin their exact two-argument shape as a trust boundary), and Sign has no context-sensitive branch to route to yet, so widening `analyse` itself would force a fabricated Sign case. `analyse_ctx` is the additional export for this one new dimension, reusing `analyse`'s own branches unchanged at `Ctx_None` rather than duplicating their logic. `None` is a real, checkable unsupported-combination result -- todays's only unsupported pairing is `Sign_Analysis`/`Ctx_EntryState` -- not a silent fallback to context-insensitive behaviour. |

Each branch reuses `analyse_sign_report_with_state`/ `analyse_interval_td_report_with_state` (`Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Checks`, `Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Checks`) unchanged, just as `analyse` reuses their state-free counterparts. |

(u, c, r, SignValue ∘ s)) (analyse_sign_report_with_state p)"<br>\| "analyse_with_state Interval_Analysis p =<br>     map (λ(u, c, r, s). (u, c, r, IntervalValue ∘ s)) (analyse_interval_td_report_with_state p)" |  |
| definition | `dispatch_demo_prog` | 219 | definition dispatch_demo_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "dispatch_demo_prog =<br>     program {<br>       void main() {<br>         y := 1;<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; y);<br>         y := 0 - 1;<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; y)<br>       }<br>     }" | `y := 1` then check `0 < y` (should hold), then `y := 0 - 1` and check `0 < y` again (should now fail): Interval's numeric bounds settle both checks precisely, and so does Sign, at the coarser sign-only granularity --- `SPos` proves the first check, `SNeg` refutes the second. |
| definition | `proc_demo_prog` | 351 | definition proc_demo_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "proc_demo_prog =<br>     program {<br>       global total;<br>       void inc(n) {<br>         total := total + n<br>       }<br>       void main() {<br>         total := 0;<br>         inc(3);<br>         inc(4);<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; total);<br>         __voblint_check(total &lt; 100)<br>       }<br>     }" | `total` is a declared global; `inc` takes one formal and adds it to `total`; `main` calls `inc` twice, then checks the accumulated total --- exercising `imp_prog.make`'s procedure list, `proc_decl_of`'s formals, and `com.Call` together, not just straight-line assignment/check as `dispatch_demo_prog` does above. |

| definition | `one_call_prog` | 477 | definition one_call_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "one_call_prog =<br>     program {<br>       global total;<br>       void inc(n) {<br>         total := total + n<br>       }<br>       void main() {<br>         total := 0;<br>         inc(3);<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; total)<br>       }<br>     }" | Acceptance regression B (interprocedural global self-feedback): a single call to a procedure that reads and grows the same global `total` reads back through the same flow-insensitive `Inr ()` summary as the zero-call case below --- so this checks the fix also survives `dgs_enter`/`dgs_combine` (entry/return) handling, not just a straight-line global write. |
| definition | `no_global_call_prog` | 501 | definition no_global_call_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "no_global_call_prog =<br>     program {<br>       void noop(n) {<br>         skip<br>       }<br>       void main() {<br>         x := 0;<br>         noop(3);<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; x + 1)<br>       }<br>     }" | A call that touches no global at all: contrasts with `one_call_prog` to show the call/return machinery itself is not the hazard --- only a global read-and-grow is. |
| definition | `harmless_global_prog` | 529 | definition harmless_global_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "harmless_global_prog =<br>     program {<br>       global g;<br>       void main() {<br>         g := 1;<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; g)<br>       }<br>     }" | A harmless global write --- a plain constant assignment, no self-reference through the summary --- always terminated, under *either* backend: distinguishes "a program has a global" from "a program has a self-dependent global side contribution" (the actual hazard class, isolated below). The result is `Check_Unknown` here (this older `side_cfg_T_eff_st` pipeline's local/global environment read loses precision on a global read back right after its own write, independent of solver choice), not `Check_Proved` --- termination, not precision, is the property this case demonstrates. |
| definition | `no_call_global_self_ref_prog` | 748 | definition no_call_global_self_ref_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "no_call_global_self_ref_prog =<br>     program {<br>       global total;<br>       void main() {<br>         total := 0;<br>         total := total + 3;<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; total)<br>       }<br>     }" | Acceptance regression A (no-call global self-feedback, task #39): no procedure, no call, just one global write that reads its own prior value through the flow-insensitive global summary, plus one independent base-case write. Isolates global-summary widening on its own, without any interprocedural (`dgs_enter`/`dgs_combine`) machinery in play at all --- this is the minimal case that first proved the call/return summary was never the actual hazard. |
| definition | `state_wiring_ex_prog` | 772 | definition state_wiring_ex_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "state_wiring_ex_prog =<br>     program {<br>       void main() {<br>         x := 5;<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; x)<br>       }<br>     }" | Wiring regression for `analyse_with_state`: a single exact assignment, no widening or narrowing in play, so the reported state at the check is independently known. This exercises the node environment, the global/local merge, and the domain constructor `analyse_with_state` wraps its result in --- not merely that the function type-checks. |
| definition | `no_call_two_step_prog` | 819 | definition no_call_two_step_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "no_call_two_step_prog =<br>     program {<br>       void main() {<br>         x := 5;<br>         y := 1;<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; x)<br>       }<br>     }" | Root-cause fix witness for the Sign native D/G pipeline, pinned at its actual source rather than at the final report. `unit_step_st` (`Voblint_Analysis.Exec_DG_Bridge`), which `unit_dg_spec_st_for` uses for every ordinary transfer step (nop, assign, random, assume, enter), computes `f (combine_resolved_st_q d g)` before splitting the result back into local/global halves: each name is routed to the exec state that owns it, never joined against the other's unrelated default. `y := 1` here never reads or writes `x`, and there is no call, no global, and no widening --- `x` now stays exactly `SPos` by the check, confirming the fix reaches the transfer/combine layer itself, not merely `analyse_sign_report_for_code`'s readback. |
| definition | `dg_probe_prog` | 850 | definition dg_probe_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "dg_probe_prog =<br>     program {<br>       global g;<br>       void foo() {<br>         g := 5;<br>         return 2<br>       }<br>       void main() {<br>         x := 1;<br>         y := foo();<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; x);<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; g);<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; y)<br>       }<br>... (1 more lines) | The same fix carries across an actual call boundary: `foo` neither reads nor writes the caller-local `x`, and `x` now stays precise at the check after the call, for exactly the same reason (the call's own combine step, `unit_combine_step_st_env`, routes the caller's locals from `dc` directly instead of joining them against `g`). Kept as a second, more realistic witness alongside `no_call_two_step_prog_locals_precise`, which isolates the same mechanism without any interprocedural machinery at all. |
| definition | `global_initial_value_remains_in_summary_prog` | 889 | definition global_initial_value_remains_in_summary_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "global_initial_value_remains_in_summary_prog =<br>     program {<br>       global g;<br>       void main() {<br>         g := 5;<br>         __voblint_check(0 &lt; g)<br>       }<br>     }" | `dg_probe_prog`'s `g` above is `Check_Unknown` not because any D/G routing loses precision, but because `cinit_sign_st` seeds every declared global at `SZero` (`Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Exec`), and the flow-insensitive global summary (`TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve`, the `join` discipline) folds that seed in as a real contribution alongside every `Side` a write publishes --- it cannot tell "before this write" from "after" apart, so soundness requires covering both. \<open>join_sign SZero SPos = SNonNeg\<close>, and `0 < g` is genuinely undecided at `SNonNeg`: this is the flow-insensitive model working as designed, not a residual gap in the same fix as `no_call_two_step_prog`/`dg_probe_prog` above. The witness below pins the actual semantic reason (the joined summary value itself), not merely the report's `Check_Unknown`, which follows from it. |

### src/Examples/Mixed/Example_Relational_DG_Demo.thy

> @{theory Voblint_Analysis.Rel_Order_Domain} interprets `sound_dg_spec` over `relc`, a non-`abs_state` relational carrier, with zero DG-framework changes -- the mathematical half of the claim. This file is the executable half: the same CFG, the same generic `dg_gen_of` generator, and the same vendored solver that runs Interval also run `relc`, end to end, with a genuinely different observable result. **Program.** `if (x < y) { z := 1 } else { z := 0 }`, with `x`/`y` left entirely unconstrained at entry (no prior assignment). This is deliberately the case where Interval learns nothing from the guard: `x < y` narrows `x`'s interval only using `y`'s *current* upper bound, and vice versa; with both at `[-inf,+inf]`, the guard supplies no new bound for either variable. `relc` instead records the pair `(x,y)` directly, because `assume_step` reads the guard's syntactic shape, not either variable's current abstract value.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `demo_program` | 37 | definition demo_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "demo_program = program { <br>   void main() { <br>     if (x &lt; y) <br>        { z := 1 } <br>     else <br>        { z := 0 }<br>     } <br>}" |  |
| abbreviation | `demo_gs` | 51 | abbreviation demo_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "demo_gs ≡ declared_global demo_program" | No `global` declarations, so the classifier this program's own source gives is trivially false everywhere. |
| abbreviation | `demo_lookup` | 60 | abbreviation demo_lookup :: "('a::bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ vname ⇒ 'a" where<br>  "demo_lookup s x ≡ lookup_resolved_st_q s (location_of demo_gs x)" | Local shorthand for the executable state's lookup projection, fixed at this file's own `demo_gs` classifier. |
| definition | `demo_pi` | 63 | definition demo_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "demo_pi = prog_table demo_program" |  |
| definition | `demo_cfg` | 66 | definition demo_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "demo_cfg = compile_prog demo_pi (prog_procs demo_program) prog_main_name (prog_main demo_program)" |  |
| definition | `demo_ivl_eqs` | 79 | definition demo_ivl_eqs ::<br>  "pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state) strategy_tree" where<br>  "demo_ivl_eqs =<br>     dg_gen_of (unit_dg_spec_st_for demo_gs (ivl_tf_st_for demo_gs) (ivl_enter_st_for demo_gs)) demo_cfg<br>       bot top_ivl_st (restrict_global_resolved_q top_ivl_st)" |  |
| definition | `demo_ivl_sol` | 85 | definition demo_ivl_sol ::<br>  "(pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state)" where<br>  "demo_ivl_sol = TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve demo_ivl_eqs (cfg_exit demo_cfg, ())" |  |
| definition | `demo_rel_eqs` | 100 | definition demo_rel_eqs ::<br>  "pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (relc, relc) dg_state) strategy_tree" where<br>  "demo_rel_eqs = dg_gen_of rel_order_spec demo_cfg bot top_relc top_relc" | `rel_order_spec` is already both the sound *and* the executable specification -- `relc` needed no `Exec_St.thy`-style refinement layer, so `dg_gen_of` is applied to it directly, with no bridging step and no parallel generator. |
| definition | `demo_rel_sol` | 104 | definition demo_rel_sol ::<br>  "(pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (relc, relc) dg_state)" where<br>  "demo_rel_sol = TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve demo_rel_eqs (cfg_exit demo_cfg, ())" |  |

None)"<br><br>ML_val \&lt;open&gt;writeln (@{code demo_dot})\&lt;close&gt; | The plain compiled CFG, rendered through the same GraphViz backend the other examples use -- no analysis annotation, just the structure the equation system above was generated from: entry, the guard's two branches, the two assignments, and the merge into `main`'s exit. |

()),<br>      show_context = (λ_. ''unit''),<br>      locals_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_locals (compiled_procedure_scope demo_gs demo_pi (prog_procs demo_program)<br>          prog_main_name (prog_main demo_program) demo_cfg p)),<br>      return_slot_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_return_slot (compiled_procedure_scope demo_gs demo_pi (prog_procs demo_program)<br>          prog_main_name (prog_main demo_program) demo_cfg p)),<br>      globals_to_show = [],<br>      show_local = (λ_ _ _ d. (10 more lines) | Same CFG, same rendering backend, this time with each node labelled by the `relc` value the solver computed there -- `string_of_relc` is the `relc` analogue of `string_of_ivl`, and `local_of = locals` is the same projection the flagship Interval example uses. |

### src/Examples/Parity/Example_Parity_Checks_Store_Only.thy

> Third-domain worked example, mirroring Voblint_Examples.Example_Checks_Store_Only (Sign) and Voblint_Examples.Example_Interval_Checks_Store_Only, discharged node-locally through `Voblint_Analysis.Parity_Checks` rather than by forwarding stores to the procedure exit. `x` is read from `random()`, so neither Sign nor Interval learns anything about it. `y := x * 2` is *always even*, regardless of `x`'s sign or range --- a fact only Parity expresses. `z := y + 1` is then always odd. `y` and `z` therefore land in disjoint parity classes no matter what `x` or `w` (a second, independent `random()` read) turn out to be: the first check, `!(y == z)`, is `Check_Proved`; the second, `y == z` again, is `Check_Refuted`; the third, `y == w` against the unconstrained `w`, is `Check_Unknown` (Parity has no singleton representation, so it can never prove a positive equality).

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `parity_ex_program` | 26 | definition parity_ex_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "parity_ex_program = program {<br>     void main() {<br>       x := random();<br>       y := x * 2;<br>       z := y + 1;<br>       __voblint_check(! (y == z));<br>       __voblint_check(y == z);<br>       w := random();<br>       __voblint_check(y == w)<br>     }<br>   }" | Third-domain worked example, mirroring Voblint_Examples.Example_Checks_Store_Only (Sign) and Voblint_Examples.Example_Interval_Checks_Store_Only, discharged node-locally through `Voblint_Analysis.Parity_Checks` rather than by forwarding stores to the procedure exit. `x` is read from `random()`, so neither Sign nor Interval learns anything about it. `y := x * 2` is *always even*, regardless of `x`'s sign or range --- a fact only Parity expresses. `z := y + 1` is then always odd. `y` and `z` therefore land in disjoint parity classes no matter what `x` or `w` (a second, independent `random()` read) turn out to be: the first check, `!(y == z)`, is `Check_Proved`; the second, `y == z` again, is `Check_Refuted`; the third, `y == w` against the unconstrained `w`, is `Check_Unknown` (Parity has no singleton representation, so it can never prove a positive equality). |
| abbreviation | `parity_ex_gs` | 50 | abbreviation parity_ex_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "parity_ex_gs ≡ declared_global parity_ex_program" | No `global` declarations, so the classifier this program's own source gives is trivially false everywhere. |
| definition | `parity_ex_reach` | 60 | definition parity_ex_reach :: "pp ⇒ store set" where<br>  "parity_ex_reach v = ltr_collect parity_ex_gs (prog_cfg (STR ''main'') parity_ex_program) (cinit_stores parity_ex_gs) v" |  |
| definition | `parity_ex_env` | 66 | definition parity_ex_env :: "pp ⇒ parity abs_state" where<br>  "parity_ex_env v = parity_exec_prog_at parity_ex_gs (STR ''main'') parity_ex_program v" | The computed Parity environment at an arbitrary node, not fixed to the exit: `parity_exec_prog_at` queries the same solver result `parity_exec_prog` reads only at the exit. |
| definition | `parity_ex_node_annotation` | 369 | definition parity_ex_node_annotation :: "pp ⇒ graphviz_node_annotation option" where<br>  "parity_ex_node_annotation v =<br>     (case check_report_node_annotation<br>             (parity_check_report parity_ex_gs (STR ''main'') parity_ex_program) v of<br>        Some ann ⇒ Some ann<br>      \| None ⇒<br>          if v = FunctionResult (STR ''main'') then<br>            Some (Node_Annotation ''''<br>              ''shape=doublecircle,color=gray40,style=filled,fillcolor=lightgray'')<br>          else None)" | The compiled CFG is rendered through the same generic `Voblint_Analysis.Analysis_GraphViz` pipeline every other example uses (`raw_cfg_dot_lit`), through the same check-agnostic `graphviz_node_annotation` hook. There is no manually maintained `pp`-to-`bexp` table: `check_report_node_annotation` looks each node up directly in the computed `parity_check_report`. `Check_Proved` renders dark green, `Check_Refuted` red, `Check_Unknown` grey. The unrelated `FunctionResult (STR ''main'')` exit node gets its own neutral-grey annotation through the same hook. |
| definition | `parity_ex_dot_lit` | 399 | definition parity_ex_dot_lit :: String.literal where<br>  "parity_ex_dot_lit =<br>     raw_cfg_dot_with_report_lit (prog_table parity_ex_program) (prog_procs parity_ex_program)<br>       (STR ''main'') (prog_main parity_ex_program) parity_ex_node_annotation<br>       (parity_check_report parity_ex_gs (STR ''main'') parity_ex_program)"<br><br>ML_val \&lt;open&gt;<br>  writeln (@{code parity_ex_dot_lit})<br>\&lt;close&gt;<br><br>end |  |

### src/Examples/Parity/Example_Parity_DG_Flagship.thy

> **Second domain, same registration.** This theory is the E1 validation of the domain-registration API: parity registers through the `unit_dg_exec_analysis` locale (as `parity_ex_reg` below, at this file's own storage classifier `parity_gs`) with *no* copied `Hstep`, `Hcomb`, `strategy_tree`, `Inl`/`Inr`, or manual post-solution transport lemmas. An IMP2 program is compiled to a CFG; the generic D/G framework generates the equation system; the *verified* always-join solver *computes* a parity solution inside Isabelle (finite lattice, no widening needed); and the single registered endpoint `parity_ex_reg.run_source_sound` lifts the result to actual source runs. The result is informative: the analysis *discovers* that `x` is even at every program point (`x = 0` initially, then `x := x + 2` preserves parity), so the loop invariant `x` even holds without any guard refinement --- parity ignores the guard.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `parity_program` | 45 | definition parity_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "parity_program = program {<br><br>      global total;<br><br>      void main() {<br>        x := 0;<br>        Gcount:=1;<br>        while (x &lt; 20) {<br>          x := x + 2;<br>          Gcount:=Gcount + 1<br>        };<br>        total:= x + Gcount<br>      }<br>}" | A bounded counting loop that increments by two: initialise `x` to `0`, add `2` while `x < 20`. No procedures beyond `main`; `x` is a single flow-sensitive local, `Gcount` a second, `G`-prefixed local, and `total` the one declared global despite carrying no naming hint. The parity of `x` stays even at every reachable point regardless of the guard, which the analysis must discover, not assume. |
| definition | `parity_prog` | 61 | definition parity_prog :: "VIMP_Proc.com" where<br>  "parity_prog = prog_main parity_program" |  |
| abbreviation | `parity_gs` | 69 | abbreviation parity_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "parity_gs ≡ declared_global parity_program" | The storage classifier: `total` is declared global despite its plain name, and `Gcount` stays local despite its `G` prefix, so `parity_gs` is the M4 entry point for a family that actually exercises global/local separation through the program's own declaration, not a naming convention (see `parity_total_global`/`parity_gcount_not_global` below). |
| abbreviation | `parity_lookup` | 80 | abbreviation parity_lookup :: "('a::bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ vname ⇒ 'a" where<br>  "parity_lookup s x ≡ lookup_resolved_st_q s (location_of parity_gs x)" | Local shorthand for the executable state's lookup projection, fixed at this file's own `parity_gs` classifier. |
| definition | `parity_pi` | 83 | definition parity_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "parity_pi = prog_table parity_program" |  |
| definition | `parity_cfg` | 93 | definition parity_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "parity_cfg = compile_prog parity_pi [] (STR ''main'') parity_prog" | The source compiles to an interprocedural CFG by `compile_prog`, exactly like the interval flagship's counting loop --- same topology, only the increment differs. |
| definition | `parity_eqs` | 116 | definition parity_eqs :: "pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (parity exec_dg_st, parity exec_dg_st) dg_state) strategy_tree" where<br>  "parity_eqs = dg_gen_of (unit_dg_spec_st_for parity_gs (parity_tf_st_for parity_gs) (parity_enter_st_for parity_gs)) parity_cfg<br>     bot cinit_parity_st (restrict_global_resolved_q cinit_parity_st)" |  |
| definition | `parity_sol` | 126 | definition parity_sol :: "(pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (parity exec_dg_st, parity exec_dg_st) dg_state)" where<br>  "parity_sol = TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve parity_eqs (cfg_exit parity_cfg, ())" |  |
| definition | `parity_graph_config` | 279 | definition parity_graph_config ::<br>  "(unit, unit, (parity exec_dg_st, parity exec_dg_st) dg_state, parity exec_dg_st) analysis_graph_config" where<br>  "parity_graph_config =<br>    ⦇ local_of = locals,<br>      route = (λ_ _ _ _. ()),<br>      show_context = (λ_. ''unit''),<br>      locals_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_locals (compiled_procedure_scope parity_gs parity_pi [] (STR ''main'') parity_prog<br>          parity_cfg p)),<br>      return_slot_for_pp = (λp.<br>        scope_return_slot (compiled_procedure_scope parity_gs parity_pi [] (STR ''main'') parity_prog<br>          parity_cfg p)),<br>      globals_to_show = [(STR ''total'')],<br>      show_local = (λ_ _ vars d.<br>        map (λx.<br>... (13 more lines) | A DOT rendering of `parity_cfg` with each node annotated by the computed parity for `x`. The `ML_val` below prints the DOT as a plain string; paste it into any GraphViz renderer (`dot -Tpng`) to view the analysed loop, each node labelled with the loop-invariant parity the verified solver computed. |
| definition | `parity_graph_domain` | 308 | definition parity_graph_domain :: "(pp × unit + unit) list" where<br>  "parity_graph_domain =<br>    contextual_graph_domain parity_cfg (λ_. [()])<br>    @ [Inr ()]" |  |
| definition | `parity_dot` | 313 | definition parity_dot :: String.literal where<br>  "parity_dot =<br>     String.implode<br>       (case TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve_c parity_eqs (cfg_exit parity_cfg, ()) of<br>          None ⇒ ''solver did not terminate''<br>        \| Some sol ⇒ contextual_analysis_dot parity_graph_config parity_cfg<br>            parity_graph_domain (snd sol))"<br><br>ML_val \&lt;open&gt;writeln (@{code parity_dot})\&lt;close&gt;<br><br>end |  |

### src/Examples/Regression/Example_Interval_DG_Ctx_Multi_Call_Regression.thy

> `twice_cfg`'s two calls share one callee entry and never share a call site, so it cannot exercise the case `routed_cmb`'s routing comment warns about: a call site with more than one outgoing call edge, where reconstructing the triggering call action from the call site alone (`call_successor_list`, taking its head) is ambiguous, while `return_call_action_list` --- keyed on the return's continuation, not the call site --- picks the exact edge unambiguously. `multi_call_cfg` is a minimal hand-built witness: one call site, `Statement 0`, with two outgoing edges to two different callees and two different continuations.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `mc_f1` | 19 | definition mc_f1 :: pname where "mc_f1 = (STR ''f1'')" | `twice_cfg`'s two calls share one callee entry and never share a call site, so it cannot exercise the case `routed_cmb`'s routing comment warns about: a call site with more than one outgoing call edge, where reconstructing the triggering call action from the call site alone (`call_successor_list`, taking its head) is ambiguous, while `return_call_action_list` --- keyed on the return's continuation, not the call site --- picks the exact edge unambiguously. `multi_call_cfg` is a minimal hand-built witness: one call site, `Statement 0`, with two outgoing edges to two different callees and two different continuations. |
| definition | `mc_f2` | 20 | definition mc_f2 :: pname where "mc_f2 = (STR ''f2'')" |  |
| definition | `mc_ca1` | 22 | definition mc_ca1 :: call_action where<br>  "mc_ca1 = CallEdge (Some (STR ''x'')) [(STR ''p'')] [VIMP_Syntax.N 3]" |  |
| definition | `mc_ca2` | 24 | definition mc_ca2 :: call_action where<br>  "mc_ca2 = CallEdge (Some (STR ''y'')) [(STR ''p'')] [VIMP_Syntax.N 10]" |  |
| definition | `multi_call_cfg` | 27 | definition multi_call_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "multi_call_cfg =<br>     ⦇ intra =<br>         { (FunctionEntry mc_f1, EA_Ret None mc_f1, FunctionResult mc_f1),<br>           (FunctionEntry mc_f2, EA_Ret None mc_f2, FunctionResult mc_f2) },<br>       calls =<br>         { (Statement 0, mc_ca1, FunctionEntry mc_f1, Statement 1),<br>           (Statement 0, mc_ca2, FunctionEntry mc_f2, Statement 2) },<br>       cfg_entry = Statement 0,<br>       checks = {} ⦈"<br><br>lemmas mc_defs = multi_call_cfg_def mc_ca1_def mc_ca2_def mc_f1_def mc_f2_def |  |

### src/Examples/Regression/Example_LTR_Collect_Regression.thy

> These witnesses build hand-written CFGs rather than compiling a source program, so there is no declared-globals table to read a classifier off; `(STR ''Gx'')` is the only store variable any witness below reads or writes, so it is fixed as the sole global.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| abbreviation | `demo_gs` | 10 | abbreviation demo_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "demo_gs ≡ (λx. x = STR ''Gx'')" | These witnesses build hand-written CFGs rather than compiling a source program, so there is no declared-globals table to read a classifier off; `(STR ''Gx'')` is the only store variable any witness below reads or writes, so it is fixed as the sole global. |
| definition | `dmain` | 18 | definition dmain :: pname where "dmain = (STR ''main'')" | Procedure `dpf` reaches one result from two nodes and is called from two sites that share a continuation. Result nodes and continuations are join points. |
| definition | `dpf` | 19 | definition dpf :: pname where "dpf = (STR ''f'')" |  |
| definition | `demo_cfg` | 21 | definition demo_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "demo_cfg =<br>     ⦇ intra =<br>         { (FunctionEntry dpf, EA_Ret None dpf, FunctionResult dpf),<br>           (Statement 0,      EA_Ret None dpf, FunctionResult dpf) },<br>       calls =<br>         { (Statement 10, CallEdge None [] [], FunctionEntry dpf, Statement 99),<br>           (Statement 20, CallEdge None [] [], FunctionEntry dpf, Statement 99) },<br>       cfg_entry = FunctionEntry dmain,<br>       checks = {} ⦈"<br><br>lemmas demo_defs = demo_cfg_def dmain_def dpf_def |  |
| definition | `mn` | 58 | definition mn :: pname where "mn = (STR ''main'')" | A two-level program `main -> f -> g` where `g` returns into `f` and `f` returns into `main`. It exercises every rule and shows `g`'s return resumes `f` (the nearest activation), while `f`'s return recovers `main` through the resumed `f`. |
| definition | `pf` | 59 | definition pf :: pname where "pf = (STR ''f'')" |  |
| definition | `pg` | 60 | definition pg :: pname where "pg = (STR ''g'')" |  |
| definition | `nest_cfg` | 62 | definition nest_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "nest_cfg =<br>     ⦇ intra =<br>         { (FunctionEntry pg, EA_Ret None pg, FunctionResult pg),<br>           (Statement 200,    EA_Ret None pf, FunctionResult pf) },<br>       calls =<br>         { (FunctionEntry mn, CallEdge None [] [], FunctionEntry pf, Statement 100),<br>           (FunctionEntry pf, CallEdge None [] [], FunctionEntry pg, Statement 200) },<br>       cfg_entry = FunctionEntry mn,<br>       checks = {} ⦈"<br><br>lemmas nest_defs = nest_cfg_def mn_def pf_def pg_def |  |
| definition | `bpos` | 141 | definition bpos :: bexp where "bpos = Less (N 0) (V (STR ''Gx''))" | Procedure `pf` reaches `FunctionResult pf` through two distinct intra branches; `main` calls it with continuation `Statement 100`. Both branches resume through that same continuation. |
| definition | `mret_cfg` | 143 | definition mret_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "mret_cfg =<br>     ⦇ intra =<br>         { (FunctionEntry pf, EA_Assume bpos,    Statement 0),<br>           (Statement 0,      EA_Ret None pf,    FunctionResult pf),<br>           (FunctionEntry pf, EA_AssumeNot bpos, Statement 1),<br>           (Statement 1,      EA_Ret None pf,    FunctionResult pf) },<br>       calls =<br>         { (FunctionEntry mn, CallEdge None [] [], FunctionEntry pf, Statement 100) },<br>       cfg_entry = FunctionEntry mn,<br>       checks = {} ⦈"<br><br>lemmas mret_defs = mret_cfg_def mn_def pf_def bpos_def |  |
| definition | `pr` | 243 | definition pr :: pname where "pr = (STR ''r'')" | A self-recursive procedure `pr`: at its entry it returns (`Gx <= 0`) or calls itself (`Gx > 0`) at continuation `Statement 200`. Two activations of `pr` are distinct and correctly nested via `caller_of`. |
| definition | `rec_cfg` | 245 | definition rec_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "rec_cfg =<br>     ⦇ intra =<br>         { (FunctionEntry pr, EA_AssumeNot bpos, Statement 0),<br>           (Statement 0,      EA_Ret None pr,    FunctionResult pr),<br>           (FunctionEntry pr, EA_Assume bpos,    Statement 1) },<br>       calls =<br>         { (Statement 1, CallEdge None [] [], FunctionEntry pr, Statement 200) },<br>       cfg_entry = FunctionEntry pr,<br>       checks = {} ⦈"<br><br>lemmas rec_defs = rec_cfg_def pr_def bpos_def |  |
| definition | `flat_demo` | 362 | definition flat_demo :: cfg where<br>  "flat_demo =<br>     ⦇ intra = { (Statement 0, EA_Nop, Statement 1), (Statement 1, EA_Nop, Statement 2) },<br>       calls = {},<br>       cfg_entry = Statement 0,<br>       checks = {} ⦈" | Flat CFG: for a `calls = {}` graph the collector agrees with ordinary intra reachability. `flat_demo` is a two-edge straight line; both edge targets are collected exactly as intra propagation yields, and no call-derived activation exists. |

### src/Examples/Regression/Example_Placement_Regression.thy

> Six compact cases validating that a variable's declared storage class (`declared_global`) and its D/G placement (`keep_local`/`publish_side`) are independent axes: neither is a function of spelling, and placement soundness never inspects storage beyond the covering side-condition. One small program carries cases 1-3; cases 4-6 instantiate three different placement policies over that same program and its same soundness obligation.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `m48_program` | 21 | definition m48_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "m48_program = program {<br>     global total;<br>     void main() { Glocal := 1; total := total + Glocal }<br>   }" |  |
| abbreviation | `m48_gs` | 27 | abbreviation m48_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "m48_gs ≡ declared_global m48_program" |  |
| definition | `m48_keep_all` | 72 | definition m48_keep_all :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "m48_keep_all x = True" | `total` is both kept locally (a private, precisely strong-updated per-activation view) and published (a joined summary for other activations) -- the two-copies shape exclusivity cannot express, and the exact case `Sign_DG.sign_dg_privatized` validates concretely for an assignment step. |
| definition | `m48_keep_swapped` | 93 | definition m48_keep_swapped :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "m48_keep_swapped x = m48_gs x" | A placement policy that inverts the classic split relative to storage -- globals kept, locals published -- is exactly as sound as the classic split or the conjunctive one, for the same reason: `sound_dg_spec_unit_placed` never consults `m48_gs` when checking a `keep_local`/`publish_side` pair, only that the pair covers every location. Placement soundness is storage-independent by construction, not by coincidence of this example. |
| definition | `m48_publish_swapped` | 96 | definition m48_publish_swapped :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "m48_publish_swapped x = (¬ m48_gs x)" |  |

### src/Examples/Sign/CallString/Example_Sign_DG_CallString_K1.thy

> Sign counterpart of the Interval `Example_Interval_DG_CallString_K1` example: same `nest` shape (`main` calls `f` from two distinct sites, `f` calls `g` from one site inside its own body), but with one positive and one non-positive argument so the Sign domain actually separates the two `f`-activations. `TD_side_mono` has no executable refinement in the vendored solver (only the widening/`abort=False` path does), so `sigma`/`vars` are hand-witnessed here rather than obtained by running a solver, in the style of `projected_part_post_solution` (`Context_Refinement.thy`).

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

### src/Examples/Sign/CallString/Example_Sign_DG_CallString_K2.thy

> The `k = 2` sibling of `Example_Sign_DG_CallString_K1.thy`, same `sign_nest` program: `g`'s single call site is reached from two different `f` activations. At `k = 1` both collapse into one merged context and `g`'s entry parameter joins to `STop`; at `k = 2` the call string also records which `f` call led there, so the two activations stay separate and `g`'s entry parameter is `SPos` in one context, `SNeg` in the other.

### src/Examples/Sign/Example_Checks_Store_Only.thy

> Exercises `checks_proven` against a computed (not hand-built) Sign post-solution, discharged node-locally through the generic `Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Checks` interface rather than by forwarding each check node's stores to the procedure exit. The compiled `checks` field comes from a real compiler run (`collect_checks_prog`, `VIMP_Proc_to_CFG.thy`); `y` is overwritten (`y := 0`) between the first and second check, and `z` is set by a nondeterministic `random()` read, so the three checks land in each of the three possible outcomes: the first is `Check_Proved`, the second --- checking `0 < y` again after `y := 0` --- is `Check_Refuted`, and the third --- `z = 1` against an unconstrained `z` --- is `Check_Unknown`. `sign_exec_prog_sound_collecting_at` (`Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Exec_Sound`) connects the computed node-indexed environment back to `ltr_collect` at each check's own node, for any node the solver's query seed can reach --- not only the seed itself. No ghost or trace-projection content: the check condition is a plain `bexp`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `checks_ex_program` | 26 | definition checks_ex_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "checks_ex_program = program {<br>     void main() {<br>       y := 5;<br>       __voblint_check(0 &lt; y);<br>       y := 0;<br>       __voblint_check(0 &lt; y);<br>       z := random();<br>       __voblint_check(z == 1)<br>     }<br>   }" | Exercises `checks_proven` against a computed (not hand-built) Sign post-solution, discharged node-locally through the generic `Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Checks` interface rather than by forwarding each check node's stores to the procedure exit. The compiled `checks` field comes from a real compiler run (`collect_checks_prog`, `VIMP_Proc_to_CFG.thy`); `y` is overwritten (`y := 0`) between the first and second check, and `z` is set by a nondeterministic `random()` read, so the three checks land in each of the three possible outcomes: the first is `Check_Proved`, the second --- checking `0 < y` again after `y := 0` --- is `Check_Refuted`, and the third --- `z = 1` against an unconstrained `z` --- is `Check_Unknown`. `sign_exec_prog_sound_collecting_at` (`Voblint_Analysis.Sign_Exec_Sound`) connects the computed node-indexed environment back to `ltr_collect` at each check's own node, for any node the solver's query seed can reach --- not only the seed itself. No ghost or trace-projection content: the check condition is a plain `bexp`. |
| abbreviation | `checks_ex_gs` | 49 | abbreviation checks_ex_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "checks_ex_gs ≡ declared_global checks_ex_program" | No `global` declarations, so the classifier this program's own source gives is trivially false everywhere. |
| definition | `checks_ex_reach` | 59 | definition checks_ex_reach :: "pp ⇒ store set" where<br>  "checks_ex_reach v = ltr_collect checks_ex_gs (prog_cfg (STR ''main'') checks_ex_program) (cinit_stores checks_ex_gs) v" |  |

### src/Examples/Sign/Example_Mixed_Flow_Sign.thy

> Demonstrates the top-level collecting-semantics theorem applied to the sign domain on the `inc_pi` program (procedure p increments the declared global `counter`). The effectful transfer record is the domain interface here. Its soundness, cone compatibility, and threefold monotonicity are discharged by the Sign domain theory. Two obligations remain program- or run-specific: dom: solver termination -- no static proof from the equation system S_sound: S <= gamma(s0) -- caller's initial stores must fit the abstract entry

### src/Examples/Sign/Example_Random_Sign_Showcase.thy

> `x := random()` is nondeterministic. `random_sign` answers it by forgetting `x` entirely -- setting it to `STop`, the sign abstraction of the infinite concrete successor set `{s(x := v) \| v. True}` -- regardless of what was known about `x` beforehand. A subsequent guard still recovers precision. For `x := 0; x := random(); if (x > 0) y := x else y := 0 - x`, the leading `x := 0` is overwritten by `random()` -- whatever was known about `x` beforehand is gone -- yet each branch narrows `x` before assigning `y`, and the two branches join to a single tight sign at the merge point: the branch guards recover enough information to derive `y = SNonNeg`, rather than losing all useful information after propagating the arbitrary value of `x` into `y`. The equation-system generator and the vendored TD solver compute this directly; no hand-derived abstract state is asserted here.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `random_guard_program` | 29 | definition random_guard_program :: imp_prog where<br>  "random_guard_program = program {<br>     void main() {<br>       x := 0;<br>       x := random();<br>       if (0 &lt; x) { y := x } else { y := 0 - x }<br>     }<br>   }" |  |
| abbreviation | `random_guard_gs` | 40 | abbreviation random_guard_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "random_guard_gs ≡ declared_global random_guard_program" | No `global` declarations, so the classifier this program's own source gives is trivially false everywhere. |
| definition | `random_guard_mnm` | 160 | definition random_guard_mnm :: pname where "random_guard_mnm = (STR ''main'')"<br><br>ML_val \&lt;open&gt;<br>  writeln (@{code sign_annotated_dot_prog_lit} (@{code declared_global} @{code random_guard_program}) @{code random_guard_mnm} @{code random_guard_program})<br>\&lt;close&gt;<br><br>end | `sign_annotated_dot_prog_lit` renders the compiled CFG with every node labelled by its computed sign abstract state, confirming visually that `x` shows `STop` right after `random()`, narrows to `SPos`/`SNonPos` on each branch, and that `y` is `SNonNeg` at the exit. |

### src/Examples/Sign/Example_Side_Branch_Calls.thy

> A richer end-to-end witness than the single-statement `Example_Side_Execute`: a procedure `compute` with an `if`/`else` on a global input, called twice from `main` with different inputs, followed by an arithmetic use of the result. In C: \begin{verbatim} int input_val; int result_val; int out_val; // globals, no naming hint void compute() { int Glocal = 1; // local, G-prefixed but not global if (input_val > 0) Glocal = Glocal + 1; else Glocal = Glocal + 2; result_val = Glocal; } int main() { int r = 0; // local call counter input_val = 5; compute(); r = r + 1; input_val = -3; compute(); r = r + 1; out_val = 100 * result_val; // language has no '/', so use '*' } \end{verbatim} Two modelling notes. The expression language @{type aexp} has `Plus` / `Minus` / `Times` but no division, so `100 / result_val` is written as the multiplication `100 * result_val`. `input_val`, `result_val`, and `out_val` are declared `global` in the source below despite carrying no naming hint; *Glocal* is local to *compute* despite the classic `G` prefix, and *r* to *main* -- `declared_global` reads storage off the declaration list, never off spelling (see `branch_prog_glocal_not_global` below).

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `branch_prog_mnm` | 38 | definition branch_prog_mnm :: pname where "branch_prog_mnm = (STR ''main'')" | A richer end-to-end witness than the single-statement `Example_Side_Execute`: a procedure `compute` with an `if`/`else` on a global input, called twice from `main` with different inputs, followed by an arithmetic use of the result. The expression language @{type aexp} has `Plus` / `Minus` / `Times` but no division, so `100 / result_val` is written as the multiplication `100 * result_val`. `input_val`, `result_val`, and `out_val` are declared `global` in the source below despite carrying no naming hint; *Glocal* is local to *compute* despite the classic `G` prefix, and *r* to *main* -- `declared_global` reads storage off the declaration list, never off spelling (see `branch_prog_glocal_not_global` below). |

(5 more lines) |  |

### src/Examples/Sign/Example_Sign_Codegen.thy

> `gEx`/`dgEx_eqs`/`dgEx_sol` in `Exec_Sign_DG_Run` fix one hard-coded example. This section widens that same native D/G chain to an arbitrary classifier `gs` and program `p`, reusing `prog_cfg` for the compiled CFG. The locale interpretation below is the classifier-generic twin of `sign_ex_reg` in `Exec_Sign_DG_Run`: the same five transfer facts discharge `unit_dg_exec_analysis` at any classifier, not only at `sign_ex_gs`, because `sign_is_sound_transfer_for`, `sign_tf_st_for_commute`, and `sign_enter_st_for_commute` are already stated for an arbitrary `gs`. `gs` stays an explicit parameter (rather than hard-wired to `declared_global` applied to `p`) so a caller can override which variables count as global; `analyse_sign` below is the convenience instance at `declared_global` `p`.

### src/Examples/Sign/Exec_Sign_DG_Run.thy

> An end-to-end certified run on the carrier-opaque D/G equation system, registered through the `unit_dg_exec_analysis` locale (interpreted as `sign_ex_reg` below, at this file's own storage classifier `sign_ex_gs`, from just `sign_is_sound_transfer_for` and `sign_tf_st_for_commute`). A concrete call-free Sign program is compiled to a CFG; the executable D/G generator (`dg_gen_of (unit_dg_spec_st sign_tf_st)`, values in `(sign exec_dg_st, sign exec_dg_st) dg_state`) is handed to the vendored always-join TD-side solver; the solver *computes* a partial post-solution. The final theorem `dgEx_source_run_sound` turns the single `by eval` solver success directly into a source-level guarantee, matching the pattern in `Example_Interval_DG_Flagship`: no transport lemma, `part_post_solution`, `solve_dom`, or `fun_of_dg_st` appears in this file's own proofs. It depends on the *computed* solver result `snd dgEx_sol`, not on any hand-written candidate solution.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| definition | `sign_ex_prog` | 41 | definition sign_ex_prog :: imp_prog where<br>  "sign_ex_prog = program { void main() { x := 1; y := x } }" | A minimal call-free program `x := 1; y := x` inside `main`: the body occupies `Statement 0`--`Statement 2` between `FunctionEntry (STR ''main'')` and `FunctionResult (STR ''main'')`, and `calls` is empty. `gEx_eq` proves the compilation equals the explicit graph, matching the source-soundness pattern in `Example_Interval_DG_Flagship`. |
| abbreviation | `sign_ex_gs` | 48 | abbreviation sign_ex_gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "sign_ex_gs ≡ declared_global sign_ex_prog" | The storage classifier: `sign_ex_prog` declares no globals, so `sign_ex_gs` classifies every variable this chain touches as local. This validates domain-independence of the generic transport, matching `parity_gs`'s role for the parity flagship, rather than global/local separation. |
| abbreviation | `sign_ex_lookup` | 53 | abbreviation sign_ex_lookup :: "('a::bot) exec_dg_st ⇒ vname ⇒ 'a" where<br>  "sign_ex_lookup s x ≡ lookup_resolved_st_q s (location_of sign_ex_gs x)" | Local shorthand for the executable state's lookup projection, fixed at this file's own `sign_ex_gs` classifier. |
| definition | `sign_ex_pi` | 56 | definition sign_ex_pi :: proc_table where<br>  "sign_ex_pi = prog_table sign_ex_prog" |  |
| definition | `gEx` | 59 | definition gEx :: cfg where<br>  "gEx = compile_prog sign_ex_pi (prog_procs sign_ex_prog) prog_main_name (prog_main sign_ex_prog)" |  |
| definition | `dgEx_eqs` | 67 | definition dgEx_eqs :: "pp × unit ⇒ (pp × unit, unit, (sign exec_dg_st, sign exec_dg_st) dg_state) strategy_tree" where<br>  "dgEx_eqs = dg_gen_of (unit_dg_spec_st_for sign_ex_gs (sign_tf_st_for sign_ex_gs) (sign_enter_st_for sign_ex_gs)) gEx bot cinit_sign_st cinit_sign_st" |  |
| definition | `dgEx_sol` | 70 | definition dgEx_sol :: "(pp × unit) set × (pp × unit + unit ⇒ (sign exec_dg_st, sign exec_dg_st) dg_state)" where<br>  "dgEx_sol = TD_side_always_join_Interp_solve dgEx_eqs (cfg_exit gEx, ())" |  |

| datatype | `pp` | 43 | datatype pp = P0 \| P1 \| P2 | A two-point equation system over sign resolved_st_q: P0 answers the state x \|-> SPos, P1 queries P0 and propagates it. Solved by the always-join (precise) update rule via the vendored global interpretation, code-generated and evaluated. |

Answer (assign_st s (STR ''x'') (N 1)))"<br>\| "sign_eqs P2 = QueryL P1 (λs. Answer<br>     (assign_st s (STR ''y'') (Plus (V (STR ''x'')) (V (STR ''x'')))))" | Program x := 1; y := x + x. Forward equations seeded from bot at entry; the analysis derives the signs -- it does not assert them. |

### src/Examples/Tooling/Example_Mixed_Sign_Interval_GraphViz.thy

> The program compiles through the ordinary VIMP-to-CFG pipeline. The variable *(STR ''x'')* is local: this program declares no `global` variables, so its classifier is trivially false everywhere. Per-point answer unknowns carry Sign stores, while the single side unknown carries an Interval store chosen by this analysis. The side slot is called `globs` by the generic routing layer, but it is not an VIMP global-variable store. Starting from *x = 0*, execution visits *x = -1* and finishes at *x = 2*. The exit Sign answer is therefore positive. The shared Interval invariant joins all three values and is *[-1, 2]*. The DOT rendering displays the flow-sensitive Sign answers at CFG nodes and the flow-insensitive Interval invariant in a separate cluster.

The variable *(STR ''x'')* is local: this program declares no `global` variables, so its classifier is trivially false everywhere. Per-point answer unknowns carry Sign stores, while the single side unknown carries an Interval store chosen by this analysis. The side slot is called `globs` by the generic routing layer, but it is not an VIMP global-variable store. Starting from *x = 0*, execution visits *x = -1* and finishes at *x = 2*. The exit Sign answer is therefore positive. The shared Interval invariant joins all three values and is *[-1, 2]*. The DOT rendering displays the flow-sensitive Sign answers at CFG nodes and the flow-insensitive Interval invariant in a separate cluster. |

False)" | No `global` declarations, and this file compiles directly through a bare proc table rather than `mixed_graphviz_prog` itself, so the classifier is fixed trivially false rather than derived via `declared_global`. |
| definition | `mixed_graphviz_cfg` | 34 | definition mixed_graphviz_cfg :: cfg where<br>  "mixed_graphviz_cfg =<br>     compile_prog (λ_. None) [] prog_main_name (prog_main mixed_graphviz_prog)" |  |

### src/Examples/Tooling/Example_State_Report_GraphViz.thy

> `raw_cfg_dot`'s `node_annotation` hook already renders a verdict-only `classify_checks` report through `check_report_node_annotation`. This is the same idea over `analyse_with_state`'s richer report: the rendered label gains one line per queried variable, printed through the same domain print functions (`string_of_sign`, `string_of_ivl`) the standalone `Sign_Print`/`Interval_Print` theories already export, so the DOT rendering shows a real solved state rather than a hand-built one.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `string_of_abstract_value` | 19 | fun string_of_abstract_value :: "abstract_value ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_abstract_value (SignValue s) = string_of_sign s"<br>\| "string_of_abstract_value (IntervalValue i) = string_of_ivl i" | `raw_cfg_dot`'s `node_annotation` hook already renders a verdict-only `classify_checks` report through `check_report_node_annotation`. This is the same idea over `analyse_with_state`'s richer report: the rendered label gains one line per queried variable, printed through the same domain print functions (`string_of_sign`, `string_of_ivl`) the standalone `Sign_Print`/`Interval_Print` theories already export, so the DOT rendering shows a real solved state rather than a hand-built one. |
| fun | `is_bottom_abstract_value` | 44 | fun is_bottom_abstract_value :: "abstract_value ⇒ bool" where<br>  "is_bottom_abstract_value (SignValue s) = is_bot s"<br>\| "is_bottom_abstract_value (IntervalValue i) = is_bot i" | Dispatches to each domain's own `is_bot` (`sound_domain`, `Voblint_Core.Abstract_Domain`) rather than a bespoke test per branch: `is_bot` is exact by the class's own `is_bot_correct` axiom for every instance, Sign and Interval today and any future domain added to `abstract_value` without this dispatcher changing at all. |
| definition | `program_vars` | 67 | definition program_vars :: "imp_prog ⇒ vname list" where<br>  "program_vars p =<br>     remdups (concat (map (scope_vnames_list p) (prog_main_name # prog_procs p)))" | `program_vars` is the union of `scope_vnames_list` over every procedure in the program (including `main`), a safe program-wide superset of any single activation's scope. Querying a variable outside a point's own activation through `analyse_with_state`'s state function is harmless: it returns that name's ordinary default, never a false bottom witness. Probing every in-scope variable rather than one fixed name (`ret_var` alone, tried first) matters because Sign and Interval are both non-relational --- `gamma` of a pointwise abstract state is the conjunction of every variable's own `gamma`, so it is empty **iff** some variable's is, not only when one particular fixed variable's is. A single fixed probe is a sound but incomplete reachability test: it can miss a program point whose local state has collapsed to bottom through a variable other than the one being asked about. Probing every in-scope variable closes that gap; for a non-relational domain, no variable outside that scope can add a witness `scope_vnames_list` does not already cover, so the test is exact, not merely broader. |
| definition | `state_line` | 71 | definition state_line :: "(vname ⇒ abstract_value) ⇒ vname ⇒ string" where<br>  "state_line f x = String.explode x @ ''='' @ string_of_abstract_value (f x)" |  |
| definition | `state_report_node_annotation` | 74 | definition state_report_node_annotation ::<br>    "vname list ⇒ (pp × bexp × check_result × (vname ⇒ abstract_value)) list<br>     ⇒ pp ⇒ graphviz_node_annotation option" where<br>  "state_report_node_annotation vars report v =<br>     (case find (λentry. fst entry = v) report of<br>        None ⇒ None<br>      \| Some (_, cnd, res, f) ⇒<br>          (case check_result_annotation res cnd of<br>             Node_Annotation lbl style ⇒<br>               Some (Node_Annotation (join_gv_nl (lbl # map (state_line f) vars)) style)))" |  |
| definition | `state_report_dot` | 85 | definition state_report_dot ::<br>    "analysis_kind ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ vname list ⇒ String.literal" where<br>  "state_report_dot kind p vars =<br>     raw_cfg_dot_lit (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) prog_main_name (prog_main p)<br>       (state_report_node_annotation vars (analyse_with_state kind p))" |  |
| definition | `bexp_vnames_list` | 111 | definition bexp_vnames_list :: "bexp ⇒ vname list" where<br>  "bexp_vnames_list b = sorted_list_of_set (bexp_vnames b)" | `report_vars` and `bexp_vnames_list` turn `bexp_vnames`'s set of variable occurrences into a sorted list, the same idiom `scope_vnames_list` already uses over `vname` via `sorted_list_of_set`. `state_report_dot_auto` is the CLI-facing sibling of `state_report_dot`: instead of a caller-supplied variable list, it renders exactly the variables occurring in some check condition in the report, solving the program once rather than once per call. |
| definition | `report_vars` | 114 | definition report_vars ::<br>    "(pp × bexp × check_result × (vname ⇒ abstract_value)) list ⇒ vname list" where<br>  "report_vars report =<br>     sorted_list_of_set (\&lt;Union&gt; ((λ(_, c, _, _). bexp_vnames c) ` set report))" |  |
| definition | `state_report_dot_auto` | 119 | definition state_report_dot_auto :: "analysis_kind ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ String.literal" where<br>  "state_report_dot_auto kind p =<br>     (let report = analyse_with_state kind p<br>      in raw_cfg_dot_lit (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) prog_main_name (prog_main p)<br>           (state_report_node_annotation (report_vars report) report))" |  |

| definition | `state_report_demo_dot` | 138 | definition state_report_demo_dot :: String.literal where<br>  "state_report_demo_dot =<br>     state_report_dot Interval_Analysis state_wiring_ex_prog [STR ''x'']"<br><br>ML_val \&lt;open&gt;writeln (@{code state_report_demo_dot})\&lt;close&gt; |  |
| fun | `analyse_env_for` | 169 | fun analyse_env_for :: "analysis_kind ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ pp ⇒ abstract_value abs_state" where<br>  "analyse_env_for Sign_Analysis p v =<br>     SignValue ∘ analyse_sign_env_for (declared_global p) p v"<br>\| "analyse_env_for Interval_Analysis p v =<br>     IntervalValue ∘<br>       analyse_interval_td_at (declared_global p) (prog_table p) (prog_procs p)<br>         prog_main_name (prog_main p) v" | `state_report_dot_auto` anchors every node label to a report entry (`state_report_node_annotation` looks a point up in `analyse_with_state`'s report, which `classify_checks_with_state` only ever populates at check nodes), so every non-check node renders with no state at all. `full_state_dot_auto` instead queries the solved environment directly at *every* `pp` via `analyse_sign_env_for` / `analyse_interval_td_at` -- the same per-point lookup `analyse_sign_report_for_code`/`interval_td_check_report_code` already use to build a check's own state -- so the annotation exists independently of whether that point happens to carry a check. |

| definition | `full_state_graph_snapshot_auto` | 199 | definition full_state_graph_snapshot_auto :: "analysis_kind ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ String.literal" where<br>  "full_state_graph_snapshot_auto kind p =<br>     raw_cfg_canonical_text_lit (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) prog_main_name (prog_main p)<br>       (full_state_node_annotation (program_vars p) (analyse_env_for kind p))" | Canonical-text sibling, the same DOT-free relationship `state_report_graph_snapshot_auto` already has to `state_report_dot_auto`. |
| definition | `entry_state_env_at` | 218 | definition entry_state_env_at ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ cfg_node ⇒ abstract_value abs_state" where<br>  "entry_state_env_at gs Pi ps mnm main v x =<br>     (let sol = entry_state_sol gs Pi ps mnm main;<br>          sg = entry_state_sg_exec gs Pi ps mnm main;<br>          ctxs = snd ` Set.filter (λ(v', ctx). v' = v) (fst sol)<br>      in IntervalValue<br>           (if ctxs = {} then sg (Inl (v, [])) x<br>            else Sup_fin ((λctx. sg (Inl (v, ctx)) x) ` ctxs)))" | Entry-state siblings of `state_report_dot_auto`/`full_state_dot_auto` (`#108`), Interval-only (`analyse_ctx` has no Sign entry-state branch, so there is no `analysis_kind` parameter here). A `pp` may be covered by several entry-state contexts at once; `entry_state_env_at` joins every covered context's reading of a variable through `ivl`'s own `semilattice_sup` (`Sup_fin` over `Set.filter` on the solver's own already-finite solution set, the same non-comprehension idiom `entry_state_classify_at` uses and for the same reason -- \<^typ>\<open>ivl list\<close> has no `enum` instance). An uncovered point falls back to the seeded default context, mirroring `entry_state_classify_at`'s own uncovered case. |

| definition | `entry_state_full_state_graph_snapshot_auto` | 234 | definition entry_state_full_state_graph_snapshot_auto :: "imp_prog ⇒ String.literal" where<br>  "entry_state_full_state_graph_snapshot_auto p =<br>     raw_cfg_canonical_text_lit (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) prog_main_name (prog_main p)<br>       (full_state_node_annotation (program_vars p)<br>          (entry_state_env_at (declared_global p) (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) prog_main_name (prog_main p)))" |  |

(v, cnd, res,<br>            entry_state_env_at (declared_global p) (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) prog_main_name (prog_main p) v))<br>       (entry_state_check_report_prog prog_main_name p)" | `entry_state_report_for_annotation` is `entry_state_check_report_prog` (already the context-aggregated verdict, `entry_state_classify_at`) paired with `entry_state_env_at`'s own, separately joined, per-variable state reading, giving `state_report_node_annotation` the same `(pp × bexp × check_result × (vname ⇒ abstract_value)) list` shape `analyse_with_state`'s report already has. |

### src/Examples/Tooling/Example_Strategy_Tree_Demo.thy

> A minimal, domain-independent demonstration that `strategy_tree` is a small effect language on its own -- no abstract domain, CFG, or context involved. `answer` returns a value and `seqcomp_tree` sequences two trees: exactly `return`/`>>=` for this monad (`Strategy_Tree_Monad.thy`), written below with `do` notation (`Strategy_Tree_Do.thy` registers `seqcomp_tree` as this monad's bind, so `do { x ← t; k x }` parses to exactly `seqcomp_tree t k`). `fib_tree n` builds the whole finite computation tree for `fib n` before any evaluation. `strategy_tree` itself gains no recursion primitive -- the recursion lives in the Isabelle function that builds the tree, exactly as every equation-authoring definition in this codebase builds a finite tree per unknown ahead of the solver run.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `fib` | 26 | fun fib :: "nat ⇒ nat" where<br>  "fib 0 = 0"<br>\| "fib (Suc 0) = 1"<br>\| "fib (Suc (Suc n)) = fib (Suc n) + fib n" | A minimal, domain-independent demonstration that `strategy_tree` is a small effect language on its own -- no abstract domain, CFG, or context involved. `answer` returns a value and `seqcomp_tree` sequences two trees: exactly `return`/`>>=` for this monad (`Strategy_Tree_Monad.thy`), written below with `do` notation (`Strategy_Tree_Do.thy` registers `seqcomp_tree` as this monad's bind, so `do { x ← t; k x }` parses to exactly `seqcomp_tree t k`). `fib_tree n` builds the whole finite computation tree for `fib n` before any evaluation. `strategy_tree` itself gains no recursion primitive -- the recursion lives in the Isabelle function that builds the tree, exactly as every equation-authoring definition in this codebase builds a finite tree per unknown ahead of the solver run. |
| fun | `fib_tree` | 31 | fun fib_tree :: "nat ⇒ ('x, 'g, nat) strategy_tree" where<br>  "fib_tree 0 = answer 0"<br>\| "fib_tree (Suc 0) = answer 1"<br>\| "fib_tree (Suc (Suc n)) =<br>     do {<br>       a ← fib_tree (Suc n);<br>       b ← fib_tree n;<br>       answer (a + b)<br>     }" |  |

## Formalization

### src/Formalization/Pipeline/Interval_Exec_Ctx_Sound.thy

> Promotes the routed D/G machinery a fixed-program example (an entry-state acceptance case such as `void p(a) { return a }` / \<open>void main() { x := random(); y := p(x) }\<close>) exercises to an executable analysis over an arbitrary `imp_prog`: the context at a call is the entered abstract value of the callee's declared formals (`formals_route`/`formals_context`), never call-site history, so a call whose argument is unconstrained (e.g. `random()`) is analyzed once under one wide context rather than diverging over every concrete value. Mirrors `Voblint_Analysis.Interval_Exec_Sound`'s `analyse_interval_td` family and naming convention, adding one context dimension: every quantity here is keyed on `pp × ivl list`, not `pp` alone.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| datatype | `gk` | 32 | datatype gk = Global \| Seed (seed_pp: pp) (seed_ivl: "ivl list") |  |
| definition | `ectx_spec` | 40 | definition ectx_spec :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ (ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_spec" where<br>  "ectx_spec gs = unit_dg_spec_st_for gs (ivl_tf_st_for gs) (ivl_enter_st_for gs)" | The one D/G spec every hook below shares. |
| definition | `entry_state_entered` | 53 | definition entry_state_entered ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ ivl exec_dg_st ⇒ call_action ⇒ ivl exec_dg_st" where<br>  "entry_state_entered gs d ca =<br>     (case ca of CallEdge dst fs as ⇒ snd (dgs_enter (ectx_spec gs) fs as d bot))" | `formals_route`/`formals_route_gen` (`Voblint_Core.Routed_Context`) read the entered callee formals off an arbitrary `CallEdge` generically, but only at the semantic `'a abs_state` carrier, not the executable `'a exec_dg_st` one this equation system solves over: the entered callee store is materialized here as an `dgs_enter` result and read back through `lookup_resolved_st_q`, then `formals_context` -- the same generic per-variable projection -- reads off the formals. |
| definition | `entry_state_route` | 58 | definition entry_state_route :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ ivl exec_dg_st ⇒ call_action ⇒ ivl list" where<br>  "entry_state_route gs d ca =<br>     (case ca of CallEdge dst pars args ⇒<br>        formals_context pars<br>          (λx. lookup_resolved_st_q (entry_state_entered gs d ca) (location_of gs x)))" |  |
| definition | `entry_state_route_gen` | 64 | definition entry_state_route_gen ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pp ⇒ ivl list ⇒ ivl exec_dg_st ⇒ call_action ⇒ ivl list" where<br>  "entry_state_route_gen gs u ctx d ca = entry_state_route gs d ca" |  |

Global)<br>       (entry_state_route_gen gs)<br>       (routed_cmb (ectx_spec gs) Global)<br>       (routed_extra (compile_prog Pi ps mnm main) (ectx_spec gs) Seed Global)<br>       (compile_prog Pi ps mnm main) (ectx_spec gs) bot cinit_ivl_st<br>       (restrict_global_resolved_q cinit_ivl_st)" |  |

| definition | `entry_state_terminates` | 88 | definition entry_state_terminates ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ bool" where<br>  "entry_state_terminates gs Pi ps mnm main =<br>     TD_side_warrowing_apinis_Interp.solve_dom TYPE(gk) TYPE((ivl exec_dg_st, ivl exec_dg_st) dg_state)<br>       (entry_state_eqs gs Pi ps mnm main) (cfg_exit (compile_prog Pi ps mnm main), [])" |  |
