| definition | `entered_state_abs` | 163 | definition entered_state_abs :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ ivl abs_state ⇒ call_action ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "entered_state_abs gs d ca =<br>     (case ca of CallEdge dst fs as ⇒ snd (dgs_enter (ectx_abs_spec gs) fs as d bot))" |  |
| definition | `entry_state_route_abs` | 167 | definition entry_state_route_abs :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ ivl abs_state ⇒ call_action ⇒ ivl list" where<br>  "entry_state_route_abs gs d ca =<br>     (case ca of CallEdge dst pars args ⇒ formals_context pars (entered_state_abs gs d ca))" |  |
| definition | `entry_state_route_abs_gen` | 171 | definition entry_state_route_abs_gen ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ pp ⇒ ivl list ⇒ ivl abs_state ⇒ call_action ⇒ ivl list" where<br>  "entry_state_route_abs_gen gs u ctx d ca = entry_state_route_abs gs d ca" |  |
| definition | `entry_state_sigma_abs_exec` | 442 | definition entry_state_sigma_abs_exec ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com<br>       ⇒ pp × ivl list + gk ⇒ (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state" where<br>  "entry_state_sigma_abs_exec gs Pi ps mnm main =<br>     fun_of_dg_st_for gs ∘ snd (entry_state_sol gs Pi ps mnm main)" | Executable twins of the context's own `entry_state_sigma_abs`/`entry_state_sg` (below), defined here before that `context` so their equations are unconditional. Neither reads anything but `entry_state_sol`: none of the context's soundness obligations (`wf`, `solves`, `entry_cov`, `fwd_ok`, `call_fwd_ok`, `comb_fwd_ok`) are needed to compute them, but the code generator cannot discharge those obligations as a runtime side-condition, so the context-local names -- kept, unchanged in shape, for the soundness development below -- are simply defined as aliases of these. |
| definition | `entry_state_sg_exec` | 448 | definition entry_state_sg_exec ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com<br>       ⇒ pp × ivl list + gk ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "entry_state_sg_exec gs Pi ps mnm main k =<br>     (case k of<br>        Inl (v, ctx) ⇒<br>          (if (v, ctx) ∈ fst (entry_state_sol gs Pi ps mnm main)<br>           then combine_env^# gs (locals (entry_state_sigma_abs_exec gs Pi ps mnm main (Inl (v, ctx))))<br>                  (globs (entry_state_sigma_abs_exec gs Pi ps mnm main (Inr Global)))<br>           else bot)<br>      \| Inr _ ⇒ bot)"<br><br>context<br>  fixes gs :: "vname ⇒ bool" and Pi :: proc_table and ps :: "pname list" and mnm :: pname and main :: com<br>  assumes wf: "wf_compile_input gs Pi ps mnm main"<br>... (18 more lines) |  |
| definition | `entry_state_sigma_abs` | 484 | definition entry_state_sigma_abs ::<br>    "pp × ivl list + gk ⇒ (ivl abs_state, ivl abs_state) dg_state" where<br>  "entry_state_sigma_abs = entry_state_sigma_abs_exec gs Pi ps mnm main" |  |
| definition | `entry_state_sg` | 488 | definition entry_state_sg :: "pp × ivl list + gk ⇒ ivl abs_state" where<br>  "entry_state_sg = entry_state_sg_exec gs Pi ps mnm main" |  |
| definition | `entry_state_enterc` | 500 | definition entry_state_enterc :: "cfg_node ⇒ ivl list ⇒ store ⇒ ivl list" where<br>  "entry_state_enterc u ctx s =<br>     entry_state_route_abs_gen gs u ctx (locals (entry_state_sigma_abs (Inl (u, ctx))))<br>       (call_action_at_call_site (compile_prog Pi ps mnm main) u)" | The trace-semantic context function: ignores its store argument entirely and recomputes the routed value from the caller's own solved abstract state, using `call_action_at_call_site` for the one call at `u`. This is `admiss_exact`'s functional shape, specialized so coverage of infinitely many concrete stores comes from the caller's own value being imprecise, not from `entry_state_enterc` being multi-valued. |
| definition | `entry_state_classify_at` | 715 | definition entry_state_classify_at ::<br>    "cfg_node ⇒ bexp ⇒ (pp × ivl list) set ⇒ (pp × ivl list + gk ⇒ ivl abs_state)<br>       ⇒ check_result" where<br>  "entry_state_classify_at v cnd vars sg =<br>     (let ctxs = snd ` Set.filter (λ(v', ctx). v' = v) vars<br>      in if ctxs = {} then interval_classify_check cnd (sg (Inl (v, [])))<br>         else Sup_fin ((λctx. interval_classify_check cnd (sg (Inl (v, ctx)))) ` ctxs))" | For each check node, aggregates the verdict across every context the solver actually covers there, using `semilattice_sup`'s flat join on `check_result` (`Check_Unknown` is the top: two contexts agreeing keep that verdict, any disagreement collapses to `Check_Unknown`) rather than joining the underlying interval states first. A node with no covered context is classified directly against the uncovered reading of `entry_state_sg_exec` (`bot` at the seeded default context `[]`, since `entry_state_sg_exec` returns `bot` at every context for an uncovered node) -- the same `bot` classification the flat, context-insensitive report already gives dead code, not a fabricated new case. `entry_state_sg_exec` (not the context-scoped `entry_state_sg` the soundness theorem above is stated about) is used here directly because the two are the same function unconditionally, by `entry_state_sg_def` inside that context, and only the top-level definition carries an unconditional code equation -- the context's own soundness obligations are not runtime-decidable side-conditions the code generator could discharge. Enumerating the contexts at a node reads `Set.filter` over the solver's own already-finite `fst sol`, never a raw set comprehension: since `ivl`'s only order is the abstract-domain lattice (interval containment, not total -- `[0,5]` and `[3,10]` are incomparable), `ivl list` has no `enum` instance, so a comprehension `{ctx. ...}` is not executable here; filtering an already-finite set needs no such instance. |
| definition | `entry_state_check_report` | 723 | definition entry_state_check_report ::<br>    "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ pname ⇒ com ⇒ check_report_entry list" where<br>  "entry_state_check_report gs Pi ps mnm main =<br>     (let sol = entry_state_sol gs Pi ps mnm main<br>      in map (λ(u, a, v). (u, ea_check_cond a,<br>                entry_state_classify_at u (ea_check_cond a) (fst sol) (entry_state_sg_exec gs Pi ps mnm main)))<br>           (filter (λ(u, a, v). is_EA_Check a) (cfg_intra_list (compile_prog Pi ps mnm main))))" |  |
| definition | `entry_state_check_report_prog` | 748 | definition entry_state_check_report_prog :: "pname ⇒ imp_prog ⇒ check_report_entry list" where<br>  "entry_state_check_report_prog mnm p =<br>     entry_state_check_report (declared_global p) (prog_table p) (prog_procs p) mnm (prog_main p)" |  |
| definition | `analyse_interval_entry_state` | 752 | definition analyse_interval_entry_state :: "imp_prog ⇒ check_report_entry list" where<br>  "analyse_interval_entry_state p = entry_state_check_report_prog prog_main_name p"<br><br>end |  |

### src/Formalization/Pipeline/Run_Analysis_Sound.thy

> One reusable theorem replaces the per-example chain \<open>solve_c success -> solve_dom -> partial_post_solution -> executable-to-abstract transport -> collecting soundness -> source-level soundness\<close>. \<open>This theorem bundles partial correctness and source-level soundness. It does not establish that `solve_c` returns.\<close> The caller supplies `solve_c ... ≠ None` (typically a code-generated `by eval` fact), and the bundle turns that single success into a source-level guarantee. Termination of the vendored solver is not a theorem of this development. The result is *query-parametric*: the reached program point `v` is existential and matched to where the run actually is, and the D/G collecting endpoint `dg_post_solution_collect_sound_ltr` holds at every covered point, so no premature specialisation to `cfg_exit` is needed. **Why the solver step stays a one-line adapter.** The step \<open>solve_c success -> part_post_solution\<close> lives in the vendored locale `TD_side_upd_rule` as `part_post_solution_of_solve_c`, whose unknown type is rigid inside that locale and cannot be tied to the D/G unknown type `pp × unit`. So the executable-to-abstract transport lives in the *global* theorem below, and the caller feeds it the executable `part_post_solution` obtained from a single `by eval` success via that adapter.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

It obtains a reusable executable source-soundness theorem `run_source_sound` and a semantic concretization accessor `gamma`, with no `dg_spec_step` trees, `fun_of_dg_st_for`, `part_post_solution`, or transport lemmas exposed to callers. |

This is the accessor `run_source_sound` states its soundness guarantee in terms of, so no `dg_spec_step`/`fun_of_dg_st`/`part_post_solution` plumbing reaches the caller. |

## VIMP

### src/VIMP/VIMP_Expr.thy

> This theory also re-exports the reflexive-transitive closure used by the procedure small-step relation.

| fun | `aval` | 12 | fun aval :: "aexp =&gt; store =&gt; int" where<br>    "aval (N n)     s  = n"<br>  \| "aval (V x)     s  = s x"<br>  \| "aval (Plus  a b) s  = aval a s + aval b s"<br>  \| "aval (Minus a b) s  = aval a s - aval b s"<br>  \| "aval (Times a b) s  = aval a s * aval b s" |  |
| fun | `bval` | 19 | fun bval :: "bexp =&gt; store =&gt; bool" where<br>    "bval (Bc v)      s  = v"<br>  \| "bval (Not b)     s  = (¬ bval b s)"<br>  \| "bval (And b1 b2) s  = (bval b1 s ∧ bval b2 s)"<br>  \| "bval (Or  b1 b2) s  = (bval b1 s ∨ bval b2 s)"<br>  \| "bval (Less a b)  s  = (aval a s &lt; aval b s)"<br>  \| "bval (Eq   a b)  s  = (aval a s = aval b s)" |  |
| fun | `aexp_mentions_where` | 38 | fun aexp_mentions_where :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ aexp ⇒ bool" where<br>  "aexp_mentions_where P (N _) = False"<br>\| "aexp_mentions_where P (V x) = P x"<br>\| "aexp_mentions_where P (Plus a b) = (aexp_mentions_where P a ∨ aexp_mentions_where P b)"<br>\| "aexp_mentions_where P (Minus a b) = (aexp_mentions_where P a ∨ aexp_mentions_where P b)"<br>\| "aexp_mentions_where P (Times a b) = (aexp_mentions_where P a ∨ aexp_mentions_where P b)" | Both "does this expression mention a global?" and "does this expression mention variable x?" are the same syntax-directed walk, differing only in the leaf predicate on the mentioned name. Defining the walk once as *_where P* and instantiating *P* to a global-variable classifier (below) or to an equality test (`VIMP_Proc.aexp_mentions`) avoids two copies of the same recursion. |

### src/VIMP/VIMP_Globals.thy

> The store is a single `vname => int` function; the locals/globals split is a classifier `gs :: vname => bool` layered on top of it, not a separate representation. `combine_env` `<s\|t>` takes locals from `s` and globals from `t`, as decided by `gs`. This is the splitting a procedure call needs on both ends: on entry the caller's store is saved and locals reset (`enter_state`), on exit the callee's globals are kept and the caller's locals restored (`<caller\|callee>`). Self-contained: depends only on the store, not on `com`, small-step, or the CFG.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| type_synonym | `pname` | 21 | type_synonym pname = String.literal | The store is a single `vname => int` function; the locals/globals split is a classifier `gs :: vname => bool` layered on top of it, not a separate representation. `combine_env` `<s\|t>` takes locals from `s` and globals from `t`, as decided by `gs`. This is the splitting a procedure call needs on both ends: on entry the caller's store is saved and locals reset (`enter_state`), on exit the callee's globals are kept and the caller's locals restored (`<caller\|callee>`). Self-contained: depends only on the store, not on `com`, small-step, or the CFG. |
| definition | `combine_env` | 23 | definition combine_env :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ store ⇒ store ⇒ store" where<br>  "combine_env gs s t = (λn. if gs n then t n else s n)" |  |
| definition | `enter_state` | 32 | definition enter_state :: "(vname ⇒ bool) ⇒ store ⇒ store" where<br>  "enter_state gs s = (λn. if gs n then s n else 0)" | Concrete call/scope entry: globals persist from `s`, locals reset to `0` -- the store a callee starts execution in. |

### src/VIMP/VIMP_Grammar_Generated.thy

> GENERATED FILE. Source: `grammar/vimp.yaml`; generator: `scripts/gen_vimp_isabelle.py`. Regenerate with the generator rather than hand-editing; a CI drift check compares regenerated output against this file. Owns the grammar-level nonterminals, syntax, and lowering (`Vimp_Grammar_Tr`) shared by every VIMP quotation: expressions, statements, statement lists, actuals, and the two identifier-list shapes (`formals`/`ids`). Does not register `imp ⟦ ... ⟧` or the whole-program `program { ... }` quotation itself, and has no notion of `imp_prog` -- those are `VIMP_Notation`'s concern (main-selection, `proc_rep` construction, and everything else specific to that record shape), which imports this theory and calls into `Vimp_Grammar_Tr` for the pieces that are the same regardless of what the surrounding quotation ultimately builds. Two lowering rules stay hand-written inside `Vimp_Grammar_Tr` rather than being generated from `grammar/vimp.yaml` directly: Isabelle's binary `Num` literal decoding (`dest_num`/`read_num_const`, no Menhir analogue) and the compositional unary-minus rule (folds a numeral operand into a negative `N`, otherwise `Minus (N 0) x` -- deliberately does NOT cancel a nested unary minus; `--x` lowers to `Minus (N 0) (Minus (N 0) (V x))`, matching the shipped CLI frontend, not to `x`). `_aexp_zero`/`_aexp_one` and `_stmt_call0`/`_stmt_callret0` are Isabelle-target realizations of a canonical rule that Isabelle's own mixfix grammar cannot express directly: `num_const` has no derivation for the literal `0` or `1` (`Num.num`'s `One`/`Bit0`/`Bit1` encoding has no zero, and `1` is the unwrapped base case), and mixfix lists have no empty derivation at all -- both confirmed empirically, not just by inspection: an isolation load test with only the generic `actuals` production failed to parse `f()`.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `K` | 118 | fun K name = Const (name, dummyT) |  |
| fun | `dest_num` | 123 | fun dest_num (Const (c, _)) =<br>        let val name = Long_Name.base_name c<br>        in if name = "One" then 1<br>           else case Int.fromString name of<br>                  SOME n =&gt; n<br>                \| NONE =&gt; raise TERM ("Vimp_Grammar_Tr: not a num leaf", [Const (c, dummyT)])<br>        end<br>    \| dest_num (Const (c, _) $ t) =<br>        let val name = Long_Name.base_name c<br>            val n    = dest_num t<br>        in if name = "Bit0" then 2 * n<br>           else if name = "Bit1" then 2 * n + 1<br>           else raise TERM ("Vimp_Grammar_Tr: not a num constructor", [Const (c, dummyT) $ t])<br>        end<br>    \| dest_num t = raise TERM ("Vimp_Grammar_Tr: dest_num catchall", [t]) |  |
| fun | `read_num_const` | 139 | fun read_num_const (Const ("_constify", _) $ t) = read_num_const t<br>    \| read_num_const (Const ("_position", _) $ t) = read_num_const t<br>    \| read_num_const ((Const ("_constrain", _) $ t) $ _) = read_num_const t<br>    \| read_num_const (Free (s, _)) =<br>        (case Int.fromString s of<br>           SOME n =&gt; n<br>         \| NONE =&gt; raise TERM ("Vimp_Grammar_Tr: not a numeral", [Free (s, dummyT)]))<br>    \| read_num_const (Const (s, _)) =<br>        (case Int.fromString (Long_Name.base_name s) of<br>           SOME n =&gt; n<br>         \| NONE =&gt; raise TERM ("Vimp_Grammar_Tr: not a numeral", [Const (s, dummyT)]))<br>    \| read_num_const t = dest_num t |  |
| fun | `neg_num` | 152 | fun neg_num n = K c_N $ HOLogic.mk_number HOLogic.intT (~ n) |  |

### src/VIMP/VIMP_Notation.thy

> The `imp ⟦ ... ⟧` quotation produces an `VIMP_Proc.com` without HOL string quotes or qualified constructor names. The `imp` keyword distinguishes command quotation from Pure premises; whole programs use the explicit `program` prefix. Design is inspired by: https://awslabs.github.io/AutoCorrode/Unsorted/AutoCorrode/Micro_Rust_Examples.Basic_Micro_Rust.html and https://github.com/awslabs/AutoCorrode/blob/e234addc5e67f78cbff63defd24199578e8e1af3/Micro_Rust_Parsing_Frontend/Micro_Rust_Syntax.thy#L5 Inside the bracket: - bare identifiers become `V` literals (HOL string literals via @{type vname}) - numerals become `N` - arithmetic: +, -, * map to `Plus`, `Minus`, `Times` unary minus *-n* on numerals becomes `N` with a negative int - boolean comparisons: <, == map to `Less`, `Eq` - constants: true/false map to `Bc` Example: @{verbatim [display] "definition loop_prog :: VIMP_Proc.com where \"loop_prog = imp ⟦ x := 0; while (x < 20) { x := x + 1 } ⟧\""}

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| record | `imp_prog` | 54 | record imp_prog =<br>  proc_rep :: "(pname * proc_decl) list"<br>  declared_global_vars :: "vname list" | `declared_global_vars` is the program's declared-global list, exactly as the source wrote it. Program-context concrete operations use this list through `declared_global`; generic interfaces take any classifier of type `vname => bool` as an explicit argument instead of a fixed instance. A list, not a set: the field is finite by its type, not by an assumption that would otherwise have to be threaded through every lemma about `imp_prog`. |
| definition | `declared_global` | 68 | definition declared_global :: "imp_prog ⇒ vname ⇒ bool" where<br>  "declared_global p x ↔ x ∈ set (declared_global_vars p)" | `declared_global` is the program-relative classifier: a name is declared-global exactly when it is in the program's declared list. The program-context concrete operations below use this classifier. |
| definition | `storage_of` | 74 | definition storage_of :: "imp_prog =&gt; pname =&gt; vname =&gt; source_location" where<br>  "storage_of p owner x =<br>    (if declared_global p x then GlobalVar else LocalVar owner)" |  |
| definition | `storage_global` | 78 | definition storage_global :: "imp_prog =&gt; pname =&gt; vname =&gt; bool" where<br>  "storage_global p owner x =<br>    (case storage_of p owner x of GlobalVar =&gt; True \| LocalVar _ =&gt; False)" |  |
| definition | `prog_main_name` | 107 | definition prog_main_name :: pname where<br>  "prog_main_name = STR ''main''" | `prog_main_name` is the entry procedure's name. The `program` parser identifies the entry by exactly this name and rejects formals on it, so it is fixed by construction. |
| definition | `prog_procs` | 116 | definition prog_procs :: "imp_prog =&gt; pname list" where<br>  "prog_procs p = filter (%n. n ~= prog_main_name) (map fst (proc_rep p))" | `prog_procs` is the non-entry declared names: every `proc_rep` key except the distinguished entry. This is exactly the complement `wf_compile_input` asks for -- the entry is declared in `prog_table`, and this list is everything else. |
| definition | `prog_table` | 125 | definition prog_table :: "imp_prog =&gt; proc_table" where<br>  "prog_table p = map_of (proc_rep p)" | `prog_table` is the declaration environment read directly off `proc_rep`: `main` is an ordinary entry now, not a field folded in separately, so a program whose `proc_rep` omits `prog_main_name` is a legal (if not well-formed) `imp_prog` value. |
| definition | `prog_main` | 135 | definition prog_main :: "imp_prog =&gt; com" where<br>  "prog_main p = body (the (prog_table p prog_main_name))" | `prog_main` reads the entry's body back out of `proc_rep`. It stays total by `the`'s convention on an absent entry; `wf_source_program`'s `Π mnm = Some (proc_decl_of [] main)` conjunct is what makes that lookup meaningful, not the record's shape. |
| definition | `mk_program` | 146 | definition mk_program :: "(pname * proc_decl) list =&gt; com =&gt; vname list =&gt; imp_prog" where<br>  "mk_program ps m gv = imp_prog.make ((prog_main_name, proc_decl_of [] m) # ps) gv" | `mk_program` is the constructor every caller actually wants: procedure list, entry body, declared globals -- the same three arguments `imp_prog` took before `main` moved into `proc_rep`. It conses the entry onto `proc_rep` so a program built through it satisfies `wf_source_program`'s entry conjunct unconditionally, matching `program` syntax's own contract that `ps` excludes `main` by construction. |
| definition | `scope_vnames` | 154 | definition scope_vnames :: "imp_prog =&gt; pname =&gt; vname set" where<br>  "scope_vnames p owner =<br>    set (declared_global_vars p) ∪ {ret_var} ∪<br>    (case prog_table p owner of<br>      None =&gt; {} \|<br>      Some decl =&gt; set (formals decl) ∪ com_vnames (body decl))" | The finite variable scope of an activation contains every declared global, the activation's formals and body occurrences, and the reserved return location. |
| definition | `scope_vnames_list` | 165 | definition scope_vnames_list :: "imp_prog =&gt; pname =&gt; vname list" where<br>  "scope_vnames_list p owner = sorted_list_of_set (scope_vnames p owner)" |  |
| definition | `wf_program_source` | 174 | definition wf_program_source :: "imp_prog =&gt; bool" where<br>  "wf_program_source p ↔<br>    wf_source_program (storage_global p prog_main_name) (prog_table p)<br>      prog_main_name (prog_main p)" |  |
| definition | `prog_enter_state` | 186 | definition prog_enter_state :: "imp_prog =&gt; store =&gt; store" where<br>  "prog_enter_state p s = enter_state (storage_global p prog_main_name) s" | Program-context concrete operations classify each variable through the program declaration. Identifiers absent from the global declaration are implicitly local to the supplied owner. |
| definition | `prog_combine_env` | 189 | definition prog_combine_env :: "imp_prog =&gt; store =&gt; store =&gt; store" where<br>  "prog_combine_env p s t =<br>    combine_env (storage_global p prog_main_name) s t" |  |
| definition | `prog_pstep` | 193 | definition prog_pstep ::<br>    "imp_prog =&gt; (com × store × frame list) =&gt;<br>      (com × store × frame list) =&gt; bool" where<br>  "prog_pstep p = pstep (storage_global p prog_main_name) (prog_table p)" |  |
| definition | `prog_pcompletes` | 198 | definition prog_pcompletes ::<br>    "imp_prog =&gt; com =&gt; store =&gt; store =&gt; bool" where<br>  "prog_pcompletes p =<br>    pcompletes (storage_global p prog_main_name) (prog_table p)" |  |

### src/VIMP/VIMP_Proc.thy

> A call evaluates actual arguments in the caller store, binds them in a fresh callee activation, and saves the caller store with the optional destination. The runtime marker `Restore` identifies the activation boundary inside sequential syntax. An explicit `Return` publishes its value through `ret_var` and replaces pending callee commands with `Unwind`. Restoration keeps callee globals, restores caller locals, and writes the published value when the call has a destination. `Return` is the source-level early return. It publishes an optional value into `ret_var` and unwinds the current activation. `Unwind` is a runtime-only marker (like `Restore`, never in source programs): once a `Return` has fired, the computation is in `Unwind` state, discarding pending statements up to the nearest enclosing activation frame.

The runtime marker `Restore` identifies the activation boundary inside sequential syntax. An explicit `Return` publishes its value through `ret_var` and replaces pending callee commands with `Unwind`. Restoration keeps callee globals, restores caller locals, and writes the published value when the call has a destination. `Return` is the source-level early return. ### src/VIMP/VIMP_Source_Print.thy

> Executable source rendering for VIMP commands and procedure tables.

| Kind | Name | Line | Definition | Docstring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fun | `string_of_nat` | 7 | fun string_of_nat :: "nat ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_nat n =<br>     (if n &lt; 10 then [char_of (n + 48)]<br>      else string_of_nat (n div 10) @ [char_of (n mod 10 + 48)])" | Executable source rendering for VIMP commands and procedure tables. |
| definition | `string_of_int` | 12 | definition string_of_int :: "int ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_int i =<br>     (if i &lt; 0 then ''-'' @ string_of_nat (nat (- i))<br>      else string_of_nat (nat i))" |  |
| fun | `string_of_aexp` | 33 | fun string_of_aexp :: "aexp ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_aexp (N n) = string_of_int n"<br>\| "string_of_aexp (V x) = String.explode x"<br>\| "string_of_aexp (Plus a b) = string_of_aexp a @ ''+'' @ string_of_aexp b"<br>\| "string_of_aexp (Minus a b) = string_of_aexp a @ ''-'' @ string_of_aexp b"<br>\| "string_of_aexp (Times a b) = string_of_aexp a @ ''*'' @ string_of_aexp b" | No wrapping parens on `Plus`/`Minus`/`Times`: unlike `bexp`, VIMP's source grammar has no parenthesized `aexp` form at all (`VIMP_Notation.thy`'s `imp2_aexp` productions never declare one), so a parenthesized rendering here would print text this analyzer's own parsers cannot read back -- actively misleading for a "this is what the source looks like" printer. Precedence is exactly `*` tighter than `+`/`-`, both left-associative (`VIMP_Notation.thy`'s `_ + _ [65,66] 65` / `_ * _ [70,71] 70`), so this prints correctly for any `aexp` actually reachable by parsing source text. A tree built some other way (e.g. `Times (Plus a b) c`, not obtainable from any concrete VIMP source, since nothing can demote a `Plus` back under a `*` without parens) still prints as *some* string, just not one that means the same tree back -- an inherent limit of the source grammar itself, not a printer defect. |
| fun | `string_of_bexp` | 40 | fun string_of_bexp :: "bexp ⇒ string" where<br>  "string_of_bexp (Bc True) = ''true''"<br>\| "string_of_bexp (Bc False) = ''false''"<br>\| "string_of_bexp (Not b) = ''!('' @ string_of_bexp b @ '')''"<br>\| "string_of_bexp (And b1 b2) =<br>    ''('' @ string_of_bexp b1 @ ''&amp;&amp;'' @ string_of_bexp b2 @ '')''"<br>\| "string_of_bexp (Or b1 b2) =<br>    ''('' @ string_of_bexp b1 @ ''\|\|'' @ string_of_bexp b2 @ '')''"<br>\| "string_of_bexp (Less a1 a2) =<br>    string_of_aexp a1 @ ''&lt;'' @ string_of_aexp a2"<br>\| "string_of_bexp (Eq a1 a2) =<br>    string_of_aexp a1 @ ''=='' @ string_of_aexp a2" |  |

| fun | `append_last` | 94 | fun append_last :: "string ⇒ string list ⇒ string list" where<br>  "append_last suffix [] = []"<br>\| "append_last suffix [s] = [s @ suffix]"<br>\| "append_last suffix (s # ss) = s # append_last suffix ss" | `append_last` puts the `;` VIMP's grammar requires between sequential statements onto the final rendered line of the first, rather than between every line unconditionally -- a multi-line `if`/`while` block's closing `}` is one rendered "statement" spanning several list entries, and only its last line is where the following statement's separator belongs. |

| definition | `pretty_string_of_program` | 141 | definition pretty_string_of_program ::<br>  "proc_table ⇒ pname list ⇒ com ⇒ vname list ⇒ string" where<br>  "pretty_string_of_program Π ps main globals =<br>    join_source source_nl<br>      ((if globals = [] then []<br>        else [''global '' @ join_source '', '' (map String.explode globals) @ '';''])<br>      @ concat (map (λp. case Π p of<br>          None ⇒ [''procedure '' @ String.explode p @ '' &lt;missing&gt;'']<br>        \| Some decl ⇒ pretty_source_lines_proc 0 p decl) ps)<br>      @ [''void main() {''] @ pretty_source_lines_com 2 main @ [''}''])"<br><br>end | Matches VIMP's canonical concrete syntax exactly: no `program { ... }` wrapper (a standalone source file is delimited by its own end, not by an enclosing keyword -- see grammar/vimp.yaml), procedures rendered as `void NAME(args) { ... }`. `globals` is the program's declared-global list; passing `[]` omits the `global ...;` line entirely, matching how an unwrapped program with no globals reads. |
